# The decision log of app

> Why app is built the way it is. Each entry: what was decided, why, what was rejected, and in its tail "— *Consequence:*" the files, by their paths, that a change of it edits.
>
> **Rule:** do not propose again what was rejected without new facts. If a decision has to change, ask the author and rewrite its entry in its place, and every entry the change touches: an entry is its text in force — what was decided, why, what was rejected and what a change of it edits —, git keeps the old text, and the commit says why. An entry's number stands in its heading alone: an entry cites no other entry and no section, says in words what it rests on, and carries no date of who decided it and no amendment; a fact that no run of ours checks cites its one record of [sources.md](sources.md). An entry that still has dated amendments, or one marked "Superseded", stays as it is until a task touches it, and that task rewrites it whole, its amendments folded in.
>
> The repository was set up on 2026-10-19 from the template of `spec` v0.1.0; a move to a tag of `spec` is its commit, not an entry. The tag in force is the submodule's, which CLAUDE.md and RUN.md name: what the entries of the earlier moves say of the tag they stood on and of the versions of `spec`'s packages is history. How it comes to differ from the template — tools in the devcontainer, rules in RUN.md — is recorded here as a decision. Next number — R86.
>
> Open questions that concern several repositories — О-, П-, Т- — are in the registers of `spec`, in `spec/docs/product.md`, `spec/docs/privacy.md` and `spec/docs/repositories.md`. What is still to decide in the component itself is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The `spec` tag

**R01. `app` stands on `spec` v0.2.9: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, the links to `spec` pinned to a tag name it, and what came from the template is as in the template v0.2.9. Since then the documents are checked by `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo`.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.1.0 v0.2.9`, read 2026-10-20; Д6 of `spec`):
  - three questions that this repository passed «в реестр spec» are decided:
    - one account can hold both the owner profile as a learner and the profiles of the owner's children: a parent who is learning Spanish does not create a second sign-in. Whom the own key on such an account is for, `spec` does not decide — that is still О-4, П-4 and Т-9;
    - both questions about `app`'s build now name it. Т-17 — where it is hosted: the hosting is chosen by `infra` by what the hosting writes to its logs. Т-19 — who deploys it: `infra`, by the same list of exact versions and digests as the services;
    - the card of `app` in `spec` writes «доступность интерфейса», and `spec`'s glossary gives it a row — accessibility — and tells it apart from the opening of a skill and access to content. Beyond this repository's recommendation — it left the glossary's item of the opening of a skill as it was — that item was renamed too: it was «Доступность», it became «Открытие навыка и доступ к содержанию»;
  - П-17 is a new question that the account with both kinds of profiles opened: how a child on a shared device does not get into the profile of a parent who has the category 18+, AI routes for adults and maybe an own key. The recommendation — the parent's profile behind the same PIN as the section for adults, and an own key on a shared device only after О-4 and Т-9. It is decided by `api` and by this repository's own key;
  - the same decision also left open the export and the deletion by profile and of the whole account: `api` details them, and what remains of the consent after the deletion of a child's profile is П-14, extended for such an account. The export is assembled by `app`, and it also erases its own data on the device: this is an input of the storage on the device and of the screens;
  - Т-27 — what checks the signature of a package or a build that the shared release workflow of `.github` puts on it. `infra` deploys `app`'s build only with a verified signature (Т-19), and Т-27 is decided with the first release of `module-kit`. This is an input of the framework and build;
  - Т-7 is closed for the API: API contracts are protobuf and buf. The messages and services of the protocol `app` ↔ `api` are an item of `api`'s detailing, and the contract is written by an epic of `spec`. What remained open in Т-7 is the module format;
  - the widget domain is a separate registrable domain, and Т-17 no longer asks about it. From stage 4 it is one of the settings of `app`'s deployment;
  - the list of the engine's reason codes is a contract in `spec`, written by an epic of `spec`. The adult sees the reason code in `app`;
  - the samples page for the first measurement is not part of `app`: its code starts after the measurement. The page moves to the hosting of `app`'s build once that exists (Т-17);
  - the CI checks are the same for all repositories, and `app` will have them even before code: secret scanning, DCO, CodeQL, Scorecard and the documents check are shared checks from `.github`. The merge is held by all checks except Scorecard, the SonarCloud quality gate included, and merges are made only by a merge commit. `spec`'s session connects them: it writes the CI files here, with `dependabot.yml` and the exclusion of `spec/**` for SonarCloud, and it also sets the ruleset of the branch `main`. The language checks with the license check and the signed release are shared workflows of `.github` for code: they appear with the first code of `module-kit`, and come to `app` with its first code;
  - an analyser's finding that a fix cannot close stays open, and its reason is recorded; the reasons shared by all repositories are in `spec`'s log. Findings on the previous task are closed by the new rule 12 of RUN.md;
  - a release of `spec` comes out by itself, after green CI on `main`: the next patch of the line, immutable, with a GitHub attestation. A repository still moves to a new tag by a task of its own;
  - the documents are checked by the `docs-check` of the `spec` the submodule stands on: links, the repository's own sections and log, the tables «В» and «Д», and the citations of `spec` by the submodule at its tag. An example of a citation is written in inline code;
  - the rule "only compatible with Apache-2.0" is about what ships. A development or CI tool that ships nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships may also carry another OSI-approved license, or free terms for open source, but only as a named exception: in the license check or in the decision that brings it in. This is an input of the framework and build;
  - the success corridor, the key for eval, the randomness generator and the derivation of seeds, the graph types — about other components. They do not change `app`'s documents;
  - devcontainer — the base image `devcontainers/base` 3.0.10 by digest; Claude Code is installed without package scripts, its `install.cjs` is run by name, and `claude --version` stops the build on a stub; `git.alwaysSignOff` — a commit from Source Control is signed off by DCO.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.2.9, commit 5eafb1a. The links to `spec` in README.md and the tag in CLAUDE.md and RUN.md are v0.2.9. That the repository was set up from the template v0.1.0, the opening of the log still says: that is history;
  - five examples of citations of the old template are in inline code: two in CLAUDE.md, two in RUN.md and one at the opening of the log. RUN.md and CLAUDE.md name `docs-check-repo`, and rule 12 came into RUN.md. These places are as in the template v0.2.9;
  - `.devcontainer/` is the one that the recipe of `spec` v0.2.9 (`editDevcontainer`) builds for an `app` of the kind `typescript`. The names of the container and the network and `postStartCommand` are its own, as on v0.1.0, and the kind `typescript` adds no extensions beyond those of `spec`. The move introduces no differences from the template;
  - what the tag decided for `app` is named in its documents: three rows «в реестр spec» left table 3 of RUN.md, and its opening says what closed them; the tasks of RUN.md and [architecture.md](architecture.md) name what the tag decided for the own key and П-17, the export and the deletion with П-14, the hosting of the build with Т-17 and Т-27, the license rule, the widget domain, the term "accessibility", the API contracts instead of Т-7 and the shared CI checks before code.

— *Why now:* `spec`'s session connects the shared checks from `.github` here, among them the required "Documents / docs-check-repo". The shared `documents.yml` builds the stage `toolchain` from the `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` of its caller and runs `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` in it ([`documents.yml`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/.github/blob/ff2a359/.github/workflows/documents.yml), the release v0.1.0 of `.github`, commit ff2a359, read 2026-10-20). And this recipe exists in `spec` only from v0.2.3: on v0.1.0 the required check would be red and would hold any merge. v0.2.9 is the latest release of `spec` on 2026-10-20, and immutable (`gh release view -R altnovatrail/spec`); `gh release verify v0.2.9 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it by its attestation, and the tag is on commit 5eafb1a. Moving to the latest release at the time of the move is also what Д6 of `spec` requires.

— *Rejected:*
- to stay on v0.1.0 until the first code: the required documents check would hold any merge, the own key would be planned without the account that holds both kinds of profiles and without П-17, and a link to a section of `spec` that the tag lacks, or a gap in the log, would pass unnoticed;
- a tag older than v0.2.9, even v0.2.3, where `docs-check-repo` already exists: Д6 of `spec` requires taking the latest release, and rule 12 of RUN.md came into the template only with v0.2.7, and the rule on analysers' findings with v0.2.8;
- to move without the devcontainer or to carry over its changes selectively: `app`'s container is `spec`'s container, and a difference would have to be recorded as a decision, and there is no reason for one;
- examples of citations in guillemets, as before: `docs-check-repo` checks a number in guillemets against the log, and `spec` rejected not checking them.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R02. `app` stands on `spec` v0.6.2: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, the links to `spec` pinned to a tag name it, README, CLAUDE.md, the rules of RUN.md and the devcontainer are as in the template and the recipe of v0.6.2, and the tasks of epic E1 and [architecture.md](architecture.md) are checked against what the tag decided for `app`.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.2.9 v0.6.2`, read 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-02; Д53 and Д54 of `spec`):
  - the card of `app` in `spec` got an eighth item of "To decide when detailed" — the lesson player: the segments of a lesson, the demo in parts, its state by `demochange` and `demoState`, the mark "sequential" from the graph. The items on the framework, the storage, the solving steps and the languages got additions, and "Does" and "Data and links" name the learner's grade, the choice of an option, a lesson by the open view, taking a task only from `api`, the context of a submission, the types from `@altnovatrail/spec`, packages from npm with a signature check, and the manifest by the package digest of the attempt;
  - the questions that the tasks of E1 relied on are closed: Т-21 — the packages come in as web components, `canvas`'s is `<novatrail-canvas>`; Т-26 — npm, the scope `@altnovatrail`; Т-27 — the signature is checked by the recipe `release-verify`, and packages from npm by `release-verify-npm`; Т-7 for the format too — JSON Schema 2020-12; О-1 — three modules of the first slice, without a text canvas and language modules; О-8 — the work is read only on "Check" and "Step done"; О-19 — the order of lessons by the skill graph. The decisions of `spec` matched what the tasks were going to pass «в реестр spec», or cancelled their example — Spanish A1 in the solving steps and in voicing; there are no questions for the author;
  - new questions that name `app`: О-21 — a hint on the learner's request; О-23 — what the presentation style consists of, decided by `app` and `tutor`; О-24 — the level of accessibility, which waits for the strings of the interface; П-19 and П-20 — essays; Т-31 — the group boxes of stroke groups in a submission;
  - format v0 decided what the steps need in the open view: `solving-steps.yaml` is open, the exit conditions are `givens_ticked`, `answer_slots_filled` and `none`, "givens and unknown" are open fields of the task, and the technique is closed. The solving steps decide no longer what is needed from the format, but how the application leads the steps by it;
  - while the format is v0, `app` and `api` move to a new format version together. The types are the package `@altnovatrail/spec` in `devDependencies`, by an exact version, the same as the submodule's tag, and the move to a tag moves it. There is no code and no lock file here yet, and this move does not touch the package;
  - the cross-repository plan COORDINATION.md and the first application: the author locally, with handwriting, lessons and the engine, without children, deployment, an own key and tariffs. The export format went from one epic of `spec` to a later one, the detailing of `infra` is put off until the first deployment, and the Iconify icons of `spec` wait for the framework;
  - RULES.md, which CLAUDE.md imports: code comes with tests, and the merge waits for `codecov/patch`; an archive in the Dockerfile comes with its sha256 sum, an `apt` package from a snapshot; a file of a NovaTrail release enters code or CI only through `release-verify`; the normative texts for authors are in English; the free terms of a tool are also for everyone, within two bounds;
  - the README badges, the first of them from day one; the licenses of the lock file are checked by the recipe `licenses-check-repo`; Google's models are closed, with an own key too, and providers beyond OpenAI and Anthropic wait until their terms are checked. One round of review already applies here (commit a91ced0);
  - what `app` has to know about its neighbours, though it does not change its tasks: nothing from the writing history goes to the AI; a place on the canvas is in fractions 0–1000 of the crop, and from a workbook go the crops of the filled boxes; the tutor's words come whole and pass the form check; every slot has a verdict; a template's instance has no field `template`; a withdrawn version leaves the cache; there is no reading on the device in the first slice; an essay gets feedback by a rubric, not a verdict. `app`'s documents write "solution" without a qualifier only where the context shows whose it is;
  - the rest of the tag is about other components and about `spec` itself. It does not change `app`'s documents;
  - devcontainer — Go 1.27.3 in the stage `toolchain`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, a sha256 sum for each archive, and the comment on `install.cjs` is corrected — the fix that the previous move waited for "with the next tag".
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.6.2, commit 49d0fb6. The tag in CLAUDE.md, RUN.md and the links of README is v0.6.2. That the repository was on v0.1.0 and v0.2.9, the opening of the log and the entry of the previous move still say: that is history;
  - README starts with the badges of day one — CI, CodeQL, OpenSSF Scorecard, the license and five measures of SonarCloud, as in the template v0.6.2. All of them respond: CI and CodeQL — passing, Scorecard — 6.7, the license — Apache-2.0, SonarCloud — 0 issues and ratings A (checked 2026-11-02);
  - CLAUDE.md names Go, and RUN.md names the normative texts for authors in English. The rules of RUN.md match the template v0.6.2 line by line; AGENTS.md, LICENSE and `.claude/settings.json` are the same as in the template;
  - `.devcontainer/` is the one that the recipe of `spec` v0.6.2 (`editDevcontainer`) builds for an `app` of the kind `typescript`: only the Dockerfile changed. The move introduces no differences from the template;
  - RUN.md: the task of the move and the task of the lesson player; the tasks of the framework, the storage and the solving steps depend on the move, and the screens also on the lesson player; the tasks' bodies — by what the tag decided for them; the epic's «Не входит» — the shared CI checks are already in place. Table 4: Д1 is closed — the container was rebuilt —, and Д2 — the new image has not been built here yet;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) — its purpose by the new card, the items to decide — eight items and seven questions about the screens —, and a new item, the lesson player.

— *Why now:* the move to a tag of the line 0.6 is every repository's first task on the way to the first application, once, by a task of its own and a decision of its own (Д53 of `spec`). By the same move the repository gets the signature rule of NovaTrail's releases (Д54) and corrects the tasks that went out of date: the framework referred to Т-21 as a recommendation, and lessons and the path of assessment by a rubric come into the detailing with the tag. A move takes the latest release. At the start of the move it was v0.6.1, and while it ran v0.6.2 came out — it differs only by `spec`'s glossary entry on solving steps and RUN.md of `spec`, and the design of the solving steps rests on that entry. The release is immutable and the latest (`gh release view -R altnovatrail/spec`), `gh release verify v0.6.2 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it by its attestation, the tag is on commit 49d0fb6, and `spec`'s CI on it is green.

— *Rejected:*
- to stay on v0.2.9 until the framework is chosen: it would be chosen without web components, the types' package and the signature check of packages, the solving steps would plan what the format has already decided, and the icons of `spec`, which wait for the framework, would get an answer by the old tag;
- v0.6.0 or v0.6.1 — the line is the same, but a move takes the latest release, and v0.6.2 corrects what the glossary says of solving steps;
- to renumber the items of what is to decide and the tasks in the order of the new card: other entries, `spec`'s log and its debt cite them by number. The lesson player is an item and a task of its own, last;
- the lesson player inside the solving steps: the card gives it an item of its own, and the epic decides by a task per item;
- to put off voicing and the own key until after the first application: it has no own key, and it names no voicing. But `app`'s code waits for `api`'s code, and that waits for `module-kit`, `modules`, `learner-model`, `engine` and `tutor`, so putting them off speeds nothing up. On the shared path of E1, `app` stands only through the choice of the framework: the icons of `spec` wait for it, and an epic of `spec` waits for the icons;
- to move without the devcontainer — for the same reasons as on the previous move.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Framework and build

**R03. `app` is written in React 19 and built by Vite 8. TypeScript 7 checks the types, Biome the format and lint, and Vitest runs the tests in Node and in the browser engines Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. esbuild minifies the CSS, a script of its own on `@iconify/utils` embeds the icons of Material Symbols, and the packages are installed by npm, which comes with Node.** The versions, licenses and configuration rules are in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The framework — React 19.3.1.** The element `<novatrail-canvas>` gets its data by properties, and React 19 "passes all tests on Custom Elements Everywhere" and assigns as a property what the element's class has ([React 19](https://react.dev/blog/2024/12/05/react-19); the trial test `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx`). For a consumer on React 19, `canvas` has already recorded how to handle the element: properties go in the order of the JSX, events in lower case, a new element per lesson segment by a new `key`, the entry point before the first render ([`canvas`'s decisions](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/e90e210/docs/decisions.md) at commit e90e210). MathTrail's canvas-api made the same choice ([canvas-api](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/decisions.md) at commit 0901af0).
- **The bundler — Vite 8.3.5 with `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.2**, without Babel. esbuild 0.28.2 minifies the CSS: `build.cssMinify: 'esbuild'`. Vite 8 installs `lightningcss` 1.33.0 under MPL-2.0 as a required dependency and by default minifies CSS with it ([Announcing Vite 8](https://vite.dev/blog/announcing-vite8); [`build.cssMinify`](https://github.com/vitejs/vite/blob/v8.3.8/docs/config/build-options.md#build-cssminify) of Vite v8.3.8). With esbuild it stays in the lock file but processes nothing of what ships: it is a development tool that ships nowhere and puts in no code of its own — the named exception `dev` in `licenses.json`, with its reason, as `canvas` did ([`canvas`'s decisions](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/e90e210/docs/decisions.md) at commit e90e210).
- **The types — TypeScript 7.0.2**, as in `canvas` and in `spec`'s types package: `tsc --noEmit` checks, and Vite builds.
- **Format and lint — Biome 2.6.4.** typescript-eslint 8.72.1 requires `typescript` below 6.1 ([`typescript-eslint` 8.72.1](https://registry.npmjs.org/typescript-eslint/8.72.1), `peerDependencies`), and TypeScript 7 has no API ([Announcing TypeScript 7.0](https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/)); ESLint 9 went out of support on 2026-08-06 (sources 2.1). Biome alone does the format and the lint and needs no TypeScript — so `canvas` and canvas-api decided too ([`canvas`'s decisions](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/e90e210/docs/decisions.md) at commit e90e210; [canvas-api](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/decisions.md) at commit 0901af0).
- **Tests — Vitest 5.0.5.** The logic runs in Node, the screens with the real canvas element in the browser, by the provider `@vitest/browser-playwright` with `headless: true`, an instance per engine: Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. `ci-test` runs Node and Chromium with the coverage alone, and the recipe `ci-engines` runs WebKit and Firefox without it, as the check "TypeScript / engines": Vitest's v8 coverage refuses any other engine ([Vitest v5.1.0](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/blob/v5.1.0/packages/vitest/src/node/projects/resolveProjects.ts#L989-L1029)). The screens are rendered by Testing Library for React, and the input is driven by `userEvent` from `vitest/browser` — through the Chrome DevTools Protocol, not by faking events ([`userEvent`](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/blob/v5.1.0/docs/api/browser/interactivity.md) of Vitest v5.1.0). Coverage is `@vitest/coverage-v8`, the report `coverage/lcov.info` for `codecov/patch`. The browser is Chrome Headless Shell from the Playwright image by tag and digest, as in `canvas`: the Google Chrome for Testing build ([Playwright's release notes](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/v1.64.1/docs/src/release-notes-js.md) at v1.64.1, version 1.57) under Google's free terms is a named exception, within the two bounds of `spec`'s license rule: NovaTrail does not distribute it and is built, run and deployed without it. In DOM emulators the canvas element does not work: happy-dom without an adapter returns `null` from `getContext()`, and jsdom without the package `canvas` draws `<canvas>` as a `<div>` ([happy-dom v21.0.4](https://github.com/capricorn86/happy-dom/blob/v21.0.4/packages/happy-dom/src/nodes/html-canvas-element/HTMLCanvasElement.ts); [jsdom v30.1.3](https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom/blob/v30.1.3/README.md#canvas-support)). A third configuration, `walk/vitest.config.ts`, runs the path of the first slice in Node through `playwright`, on Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, by `just walk` by hand and not in CI.
- **The tests in the browser run with `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` off, `src/test/browser.setup.ts`, and take `act` from Testing Library alone: `noRestrictedImports` of `biome.json` refuses the named import of `act` from `react` and any import of `react-dom/test-utils`.** `userEvent` of `vitest/browser` gives real events of the browser, which no `act()` wraps, and with the flag on React warned of each update made outside an `act` — 10 958 warnings in the log of one run of CI (measurements 3.1 spec). React reads the flag only to warn, and its work goes by the queue of `act`, not by the flag; Testing Library's `render` and `act` turn the flag on for their own time ([`isConcurrentActEnvironment`](https://github.com/facebook/react/blob/v19.3.1/packages/react-reconciler/src/ReactFiberAct.js#L40-L62) and [`scheduleCallback`](https://github.com/facebook/react/blob/v19.3.1/packages/react-reconciler/src/ReactFiberRootScheduler.js#L626-L639) of React v19.3.1, [`act-compat.js`](https://github.com/testing-library/react-testing-library/blob/v16.3.3/src/act-compat.js#L36-L83) of Testing Library v16.3.3). So the tests flush as before, and only the warnings went, those of an update a test truly left unwrapped too — among 10 958 nobody read them.
- **Icons — Material Symbols, the package `@iconify-json/material-symbols` by an exact version, in `dependencies`: its paths go into the build.** A script of its own, as in `canvas` and `skill-map`, `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils` 3.1.7, writes the module `src/app/icons.ts` — the viewBox and paths of only the icons that `app` takes, by the rule «только пути `fill="currentColor"`» —, and `ci-lint` fails if the module is not the one the script writes. The component `Icon` draws them by SVG elements, without inserting markup as a string. The application has no Iconify components, which load icons from the Iconify API at run time (sources 2.1). The version of the set is the same as in the versions of `canvas` and `skill-map` in `app`'s lock file: a test in Node checks it by the `devDependencies` of the installed packages `canvas` and `skill-map`, and the set's notice — Material Symbols, Google, Apache-2.0, its address and version — goes into the build's `NOTICE`.
- **npm installs the packages**, from the devcontainer's Node 24.21.0: `licenses-check-repo` reads only npm's lock file, and npm itself is named as an exception in `spec`. `.npmrc` has `save-exact=true` and `ignore-scripts=true`, as the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` requires ([its `docs/architecture.md`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/.github/blob/1704f782e76cee50ea5392cc0ea0d4b76c4b3672/docs/architecture.md) at the commit `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins). With the code come the package `@altnovatrail/contracts` — the code of the contracts, of the same version as the submodule's tag — and the client connect-es.
- **The versions** of third-party packages are the latest released no later than three days before the installation, as in `canvas` ([`canvas`'s decisions](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/e90e210/docs/decisions.md) at commit e90e210); the lock file is built by `npm install --before=…`. The packages of `@altnovatrail` do not fall under the pause: `@altnovatrail/spec` has the same version as the submodule's tag and comes out on the day of the tag, and the signature of all of them is checked by `release-verify-npm`.
- **The devcontainer's stage with the test browser and the Biome extension** come from the template, by the recipe `editDevcontainer`, not as a copy of its own.

— *Why:* TypeScript 7, Biome and npm are not a choice: they are set by `spec`, the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` and TypeScript 7 itself. The set of tools is the same as `canvas`'s, and both have one contract with the wrapper and one stage with the browser. The icons are embedded by a script, not by `unplugin-icons`: its `compiler: 'jsx'` requires `@svgr/core` and `@svgr/plugin-jsx` 8.1.0, both depend on `@babel/core`, that one — through `@babel/helper-compilation-targets` — on `browserslist`, and that one on `caniuse-lite` under CC-BY-4.0 ([unplugin-icons v24.0.0](https://github.com/unplugin/unplugin-icons/blob/v24.0.0/README.md); in the npm registry [`@svgr/core` 8.1.0](https://registry.npmjs.org/@svgr/core/8.1.0), [`@svgr/plugin-jsx` 8.1.0](https://registry.npmjs.org/@svgr/plugin-jsx/8.1.0), [`@babel/helper-compilation-targets` 7.27.2](https://registry.npmjs.org/@babel/helper-compilation-targets/7.27.2), [`browserslist` 4.28.0](https://registry.npmjs.org/browserslist/4.28.0) and [`caniuse-lite` 1.0.30001827](https://registry.npmjs.org/caniuse-lite/1.0.30001827)). This license is not in `spec`'s `licenses-allowed`, and the exception for tools that do not ship does not hold for it: without the package the build does not build. A script on `@iconify/utils` is a way that `spec` names on a par with the plugin, and the same as in `canvas` and `skill-map`.

— *Rejected:*
- Preact 11: 11.0.0 came out on 2026-09-30, Custom Elements Everywhere tested only Preact 10, and `@preact/preset-vite` 2.10.6 requires Babel and was tested on Preact 10. Preact 10.29.8 is the previous major version (sources 2.1; [`preact`](https://registry.npmjs.org/preact) and [`@preact/preset-vite` 2.10.6](https://registry.npmjs.org/@preact/preset-vite/2.10.6) in the npm registry);
- Lit 3.3.3 is a library of components, not an application framework: the state, the routes and the tests of the screens would have to be assembled by hand. Vue, Svelte and Solid — `canvas` has no rules for them, and Custom Elements Everywhere did not test Svelte 5 (sources 2.1);
- ESLint 10 with typescript-eslint and Prettier — for the reason above;
- happy-dom alone with a stub of the canvas: the order of properties, the events and `exportWork()` would be checked on the stub. happy-dom for the screens and a separate suite in the browser: the browser and the exception are needed anyway, and there are more Vitest projects;
- the provider WebdriverIO — it downloads the browser itself during a run, and `canvas` takes it from the image by digest; the provider `preview` "relies on simulating events"; `@testing-library/user-event` — for the same reason: it fakes events in JavaScript (sources 2.1; Vitest's [browser guide](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/blob/v5.1.0/docs/guide/browser/index.md) and [`userEvent`](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/blob/v5.1.0/docs/api/browser/interactivity.md) at v5.1.0);
- hiding the act warnings by `onConsoleLog` — they would still be made for nothing; wrapping `userEvent` in `act()`, by a helper or call by call — an update that comes once the event has ended, from an answer of `api` or a timer, would still warn, and an open `act` holds React's work in its queue until it ends, which a real event does not;
- `lightningcss` as the minifier, on the argument "our CSS goes into what ships" — wider than `canvas`, which put this case off; CSS without minification;
- `unplugin-icons` with `@svgr/core` and an exception for `caniuse-lite` — the license is outside the list, and the build cannot do without it; `unplugin-icons` with a compiler of its own — the type `CustomCompiler` is in its `dist/types.d.mts`, but the README has no such way; `unplugin-icons` with raw SVG — the markup would be inserted as a string; Iconify components at run time — a request from the learner's device to the Iconify API;
- pnpm and yarn: `licenses-check-repo` does not read their lock file;
- building by `tsc` alone, as in `canvas`: `tsc` is enough for a package, and an application needs a bundler — modules, CSS, assets and hashes in file names.

— *Consequence:* `package.json` and its lock file; `.npmrc`; `vite.config.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`, `walk/vitest.config.ts`; `biome.json`; `tsconfig.json`; `licenses.json`; `scripts/icons.ts`, `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/test/browser.setup.ts`; `justfile`; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Storage on the device

**R04. `app` keeps learners' data in IndexedDB, a database per profile. A task's work with the contexts of its submissions lives until the end of the attempt, an essay's too, and an unfinished one for 30 days without changes. The account owner's sign-out erases nothing, and the same account's next sign-in opens it all; a sign-in of another account erases what is left of the previous one; and the adult sees what lies on the device, and erases it, on the page "This device".** The table "what — where — how much — when it is erased — into the export" is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The storage is IndexedDB, by code of its own behind a narrow interface, without a library.** Each profile on the device has its own database: the attempts with the writing history and the submissions, an essay's among them, the state of lessons and, at the owner profile, the own key the adult keeps on this device. Besides them there are a shared cache of open views by package digest; the device's registry — whose account signed in, which databases of profiles lie there, Firebase's `projectId` and `apiKey` the sign-in went with, the voices of the device and the period after which the owner profile closes; and the device settings, a small database of its own with the device's language an adult chose. The sign-in session is kept by Firebase Auth in its own IndexedDB database — `app` sets it up through `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alone. The media files of the device part `app` takes from `api` by `fetch` and gives to the canvas as a `Blob`, by the canvas's function `media`, and shows the pictures of a lesson's explanations by a `blob:` address; the responses lie in the browser's HTTP cache, which `fetch` goes through. `app` puts no data of its own in localStorage and sessionStorage; what Firebase Auth itself writes there at a sign-in through Google, its documentation does not say, so an erasure of the account clears them whole.
- **Terms.** A task's work — the writing history, and for each submission its context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission and the submitted work itself — lives until the end of the attempt; the work of a correct submission, until the correct answer words have come or their request has ended. Unfinished work is erased after 30 days without changes, and `app` checks the term at start in all databases of profiles. An essay — a task's work with an answer slot bound by `sheet` — goes by the same terms, its submissions kept with their contexts alone: the crop, the text read and the feedback are the screen's while it is open. A lesson's state — the open view, the place and the state of the demo of its examples — is kept by the lesson's ID and the package digest it was started on until the lesson has ended, however it ended, for 30 days without changes, until the withdrawal of the version, or until `api` gives the same lesson on another package; the place and the state of the demo go into the export. The cache by package digest — 30 days without access, and a withdrawn version leaves it at once.
- **The sign-out and the account's erasure.** The account owner's sign-out is the sign-in service's `signOut()` alone, which removes the session, and it asks nothing: the databases of the profiles, the cache, the registry and the web storage stay, and the same account's next sign-in opens them. A sign-in of another account — the registry names another account, or `api` refuses with `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS`, "no access", while the registry names one, as the comment of `AccountService` in `novatrail.api.v1` requires — erases what is left of the previous account before it opens anything: the registry holds one account (Д44). "Erase everything on this device" and an account that is gone — `CAUSE_ACCOUNT_GONE` or its deletion — sign out and erase everything of the account: the databases of the profiles, the cache, the web storage, and the registry last. A `projectId` or `apiKey` in `/deployment.json` other than the registry's erases the Firebase Auth database and the web storage before the sign-in, and the learners' data stays until another account signs in. The device settings are neither the learner's data nor the account's: only "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself erase them. The voices, the closing period and the own key are not the learner's data and go into no export.
- **The page "This device"** is `#/adults/device`, from the first page of the section for adults. For each profile, the owner first: its unfinished tasks and "about" how much space its data takes, by `app`'s estimate from the records of its database, the own key not counted. A profile without a database on the device — "Nothing of this profile is kept"; a database that cannot be read — "can't be read", and it can be erased even then; a database whose erasure waits for the browser — "Not all of it was erased", with a button to erase again. At each profile, the warning that its works are only on this device while the server keeps no copy of them. For the whole device: the `estimate()` of the site, with the caveat that it is the whole site's; whether the browser granted persistent storage — `persisted()` — and the button `persist()` while it has not, on a browser that has the API; the warning that the browser may erase the data by itself, and Safari after seven days without a touch, and the advice about the Home Screen, under persistent storage too: whether `persist()` saves a Safari tab from the seven days, the primary source does not say (sources 3.1). "Voicing" follows the profiles, and at the owner profile the own key kept here, with "Remove key". "Erase the profile's data on this device" erases its database through the registry, and on the server it changes nothing. "Erase everything on this device" signs out, then erases everything of the account, the device settings and the heartbeat database of `@firebase/app`: its deletion is `blocked` while the SDK's connection holds it, IndexedDB does not cancel it, and `app` says "not erased" only of what failed; if the settings were not erased — "Not all of it was erased", and a start does not repeat it, since the registry that would remember it is gone. Every erasure asks first (WCAG 3.3.4). At the account's first sign-in on this device the choice of the learner calls the same `persist()` itself, with no line or button.

— *Why:* the model is MathTrail's canvas-api: a draft on the device in IndexedDB, erased by a solved canvas, a new canvas of the same task, 30 days without changes and the deletion of the profile or the account ([canvas-api](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/decisions.md) at commit 0901af0). A database per profile is deleted by one `deleteDatabase` and leaves no remnants in shared stores. `canvas` requires keeping the context of a submission «пока могут прийти пометки» ([`canvas`'s architecture](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/e90e210/docs/architecture.md) at commit e90e210), and anything longer is for `app` to decide: marks come within the attempt, and the application sends the work for the analysis once more; the request `GetCorrectAnswerWords` carries the work of a correct submission, because the server does not keep the crop, and a repeat of it brings no words. An essay is a task's work like any other: no screen reads it again, and an essay kept with no screen for it would be data with no use. The sign-out erases nothing and asks nothing, so as not to lose work at a re-sign-in: a sign-out that erased would take the unfinished work the server does not keep from an adult who only signs out and in again (П-8); kept, it opens only after a sign-in of the same account, and another account's sign-in erases them. Without erasing at "no access", `app` would keep on the device the data of an account that is gone, at a sign-in of another. "Erase everything" signs out first: without it the sign-in session would stay, and a sign-in without a registry, though the button promises everything. The warning of loss is per profile, because storage on the server is turned on per profile. Firefox shows the `persist()` prompt to whoever holds the device, and at the first stroke that is a child, at the first sign-in the adult who has just signed in; Chrome and Safari decide without a prompt (sources 3.1), so the first sign-in calls it silently. Safari erases a site's data after seven days without a touch, and the adult has to know this in advance (sources 3.1).

— *Rejected:*
- one database per device with the profile's ID in each key: the deletion of a profile would become a selective erasure across all stores, and nobody would notice a forgotten remnant in one of them;
- a library over IndexedDB — `idb` or Dexie: a little less code of our own, but a new dependency in what ships, and `app` needs only a few stores and cursors;
- localStorage: 5 MiB per origin, synchronous, and it does not escape Safari's seven days (sources 3.1);
- keeping solved work with marks for 30 more days, or counting any work as an artifact, an essay among them: the device would hold more writing history — with time and pressure — than the platform needs;
- erasing everything of the account at the sign-out, with a screen that names the loss first: the adult who only signs out and in again loses the unfinished work the server does not keep, and "Sign out and erase" is one press too many. A device given away keeps the account's data until "Erase everything on this device" or another account's sign-in, so the adult who gives a device away uses "Erase everything on this device", where canvas-api advised signing out;
- `persist()` at the first stroke, as canvas-api did ([its `docs/spec.md`](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/spec.md) at commit 0901af0): in Firefox a child would see the prompt. Without a page of its own the adult would not know what lies on the device and that the browser may erase it;
- the line that learners' work is kept here and the button "Ask the browser to keep it" on the choice of the learner at the first sign-in — the screen keeps the mockup's `who`, and the call needs no press: the page "This device" says the rest;
- erasing the account's data by the header Clear-Site-Data: it clears the storage of the response's origin (sources 3.3), `app` has no server process of its own, and a response of `api` from another origin will not touch `app`'s storage;
- caching a task's content by its ID: the instances of a template have one ID;
- "Erase everything" without a sign-out — the argument above; the profile's space from `estimate()` — it measures the whole origin, with the cache and the SDK's databases; the warning about the seven days only in Safari, by the browser's user-agent string — the string is unreliable, and an application on the Home Screen does not change it; hiding the warning under persistent storage — the argument above.

— *Does not decide:* the export of the device — an epic of `spec`; several accounts kept apart on one device — Д44; the list of the works on the server and the erasure of one — Д26; the size of the writing history, an estimate in [architecture.md](architecture.md) — Д4.

— *Consequence:* `src/storage/` — `schema.ts`, `registry.ts`, `profile.ts`, `cache.ts`, `settings.ts`, `erase.ts`, `start.ts`; `src/account/session.ts` — `signOut`, `eraseEverything`; `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Solving steps

**R05. `app` leads the solving steps on the device, without the key and without AI: the exit condition enables the button "Step done", and "Step done" only leads to the next step — in format v0 the work is read on "Check". The learner ticks the chips "givens and unknown" by a tap, returns to a step already passed, and may always ask for a hint. The default mode is "by steps": the learner switches it on the task screen, and the adult sets the default.** The design and the path by steps of the three modules of the first slice are in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The steps** `app` takes from the open view of the solving steps of the attempt's package — it lies in the cache by package digest — and leads them in order. All tasks of a module go through the same steps, and in a module without steps the tasks are solved without them, as the module format says. The canvas is open on every step: a step does not lock the work.
- **The end of a step.** Each step but the last has the button "Step done", and the exit condition enables it: `none` — at once; `givens_ticked` — when all chips of the task are ticked; `answer_slots_filled` — when each answer slot has something in it: for `choice` the chosen option, for other slots the canvas's `filled`, which `app` rereads on each `historychange`. The step does not end by itself: for a slot `anywhere` or `sheet` the condition is met already with the first stroke on the sheet. There are no timers and no automatic advance. A step with `givens_ticked` in a task without chips passes by itself, as the format requires. The last step ends on "Check": it has no next step, and its exit condition locks nothing — `app` shows its line, and `answer_slots_filled` enables "Check". In the format examples the last step is `none`.
- **"Step done" does not read the work:** it leads to the next step on the device, without a call to AI, and the work is read on "Check". `spec` allows reading on "Step done" too, but in format v0 a reading has nothing to give: it has no check by steps, `module-kit` gives the verdict by the observation as a whole, and an early verdict «ждёт вида проверки по шагам в новой версии формата». Reading on "Step done" will come with that kind.
- **The chips** — the open fields of the task `givens` and `unknown` — are shown and ticked by `app`, as `canvas` leaves them to it ([`canvas`'s architecture](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/7401439/docs/architecture.md) at commit 7401439): in the tutor's panel, `givens` as a list, `unknown` apart. A tap ticks a chip, a second tap unticks it. There is no wrong tick: the chips retell the task statement, and they need no key.
- **Return** — to any step already passed, by a tap on the strip of steps; forward — only "Step done". A return resets nothing: the ticks, the technique received and the work stay, and the technique is not asked of `api` again — only the hint asks for it, below.
- **The words on a step** are the module's texts and `app`'s own strings, without AI: the step's title, the chips and the techniques, and for the exit condition a line of the interface on what will enable the button, without grammatical gender. The technique is closed, and `app` does not know whether the task has one. When a step whose `gives` gives techniques starts, `app` names to `api` the attempt and the step's place, and `api` answers with the IDs of the task's techniques — empty if there are none — and records what the learner saw. The names and descriptions of the techniques `app` takes from the open technique catalog in the cache. The words of AI come only after a submission: the question of a rung of the hint ladder and "Explain differently", below.
- **A hint on request** — always, in both modes, from the start of the task. It sends no work, and the request always goes to `api`. Before the first wrong answer `app` shows the chips "givens and unknown" from the open view and the technique that `api` returned; `api` does not record that the learner saw the chips. After it — the words of the current rung once more, without AI; "Explain differently" — the same rung in other words, one AI call per rung, and in the mode without AI there is none.
- **Showing the solution** — to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, and under the adult's setting "ask before showing", on by default, by an adult action behind the PIN; otherwise `api` answers "adult needed". Showing closes the attempt with the outcome "Solution shown", and only then or after a correct answer does the task's solution come to the device.
- **"Check"** — in both modes and on any step. It is enabled when each answer slot has something in it — the same event as `answer_slots_filled`: an empty sheet and empty boxes do not go to the AI. A tap on an option of `choice` only chooses, and "Check" sends. On a pause, after 6 s without a stroke, "Check" blinks if it is enabled, in both modes, once per pause: shorter than 5 s and no more often than 3 times a second, and then the button stays as it was. It blinks again only after a new stroke or the choice of an option, not after every next 6 s. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` a still highlight replaces the blinking, and the blinking says nothing to a screen reader. "Step done" does not blink on a pause: at the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` it is enabled from the first stroke, and the blinking would call onward before the answer.
- **The mode.** The adult sets the default mode in the section for adults, and for a new profile it is "by steps". It is a field of its own in the learner's profile on the server, "default mode", not part of the presentation style, and it does not go to the AI; it follows the learner to any device: every task starts with it. The learner switches the mode on the task screen at any moment; the learner's choice holds until the end of the attempt and is kept with its state of the steps, and it does not change the default mode. In the mode "self", on the button "On my own", no steps are seen, the technique comes only on request, and the tutor answers only when called, as the format says. A module without steps has no switch.
- **The state of the steps** — the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips and the IDs of the techniques received — `app` keeps with the attempt in the profile's database and erases together with it. After a restart the learner goes on in the same mode and from the same step. Each task of a lesson starts from the first step.

— *Why:*
- The format's examples of steps put a self-check after "solve" and before the submission: in olympiad maths `Solve it` → `Check the answer`, in school maths `Fill in the boxes` → `Check each line` — "with the task's technique to check it by", and in essays `Read it again` is the last step. If "Step done" on a step with `answer_slots_filled` sent the work, the mode "by steps" would itself give the verdict before the step of the check. As it is, the steps lead to the self-check before the submission, and "Check" before the last step is the choice of a learner who skips the steps, as in the mode "self".
- `spec` rejected a reading without a verdict for a hint request, and `tutor` has no capability "words on a step" — no prompt, no eval, no form check. A reading of unfinished work gave a verdict before the answer in 94 calls of 196.
- The steps are a frame, not a check: they have no key, and returning to the plan after "not yet" is an ordinary move in solving. A tick and a return cost only a tap.
- Before the first wrong answer the hint gives what the steps give, without AI. Closing it in the mode "by steps" would leave without help someone stuck on a step.
- "Check" with an empty slot is a call to AI for the sake of "can't read: empty" and a spending of the quota, and `api` will say nothing about the emptiness faster than the device.
- The threshold of the pause of 6 s is the only threshold with a precedent in the project: MathTrail's canvas-api counted a line as finished after 6 s, and the long pause of `tutor`'s measurement log is also from 6 s ([`tutor`'s decisions](https://github.com/altnovatrail/tutor/blob/be607e5/docs/decisions.md) at commit be607e5). One blink per pause, its length and its rate are what WCAG requires: 2.2.2 requires a way to stop blinking longer than 5 s, and 2.3.1 forbids more often than 3 times a second (sources 8.1). "On my own" names the mode "self" because in Russian «сам» gives away the learner's gender.
- Both the adult and the learner choose the mode, and the learner also learns on other devices. The adult's default holds, and the learner's choice lives in the attempt: so the adult knows what each task will start with, and the learner does not lose their choice in the middle of one. The default mode is a field of the learner's profile of its own, as `spec`'s profile has it, not part of the presentation style, and so it does not go to the AI.
- A step start tells `api` only what it has to record — the techniques the learner saw; the server has nothing to know about steps without them.

— *Rejected:*
- "Step done" on a step with `answer_slots_filled` doing the same as "Check": the mode "by steps" would itself give the verdict before the step of the self-check;
- "Step done" with a reading on every step and words of AI without a verdict: a new capability of `tutor` with eval, a call and money on every press, and `spec` rejected a reading without a verdict;
- an automatic advance by the exit condition: the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` would end with the first line. For the same reason "Step done" does not blink on a pause;
- steps only forward, as in the prototype, or one step back: after "not yet" the learner would not return to the plan and the technique, or would return step by step;
- a hint in the mode "by steps" only after the first submission — the argument above;
- "Check" always enabled — the argument above;
- the mode on the device: on another device it would be different, and the adult would set it on each; the mode only for the adult behind the PIN, as in the prototype — narrower than the platform, where both the adult and the learner choose it; a learner's choice that changes the profile's mode: the adult's default would not hold; the mode as part of the presentation style — the profile has a field of its own for it;
- chips that the learner puts in their place in the task statement: the format has no places for chips;
- a "step start" in `api` for every step: the server has nothing to know about steps without techniques.

— *Does not decide:* how the request and the chips enter the evidence — `api` with `learner-model`; what comes after feedback by a rubric — `api`; a widget's state as an exit condition — stage 4; the step "pronounce" — with voice input (О-6).

— *Consequence:* `src/task/`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The lesson player

**R06. `app` runs a lesson on the device by its open view: the segments in the author's order, forward by "Continue", back to the explanations and examples already gone through. The place in the lesson and the demo state of its examples belong to the lesson run on its package — the key is the lesson ID and the package digest —, so the state of another version of the module does not reach the canvas element. The lesson runs by `LearningService`, under the lesson's top bar of the mockup. `app` derives the mark "sequential" from the skill graph and shows it on a module's card — the kind "Sequential" on Home, "In order" on Subjects — and in the section for adults.** The design and the learner's path through a lesson are in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The lesson** `app` gets from `api` whole, together with the digest of the package on which the lesson was begun: all the tasks of the lesson are attempts on this package. The types are `Lesson`, `Segment` and `WorkedExample` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`. A segment and a part of a demo have no ID: their place names them, from 1, as the module format says.
- **The lesson screen.** In the top bar, on every segment, is the lesson's title, so the learner sees it before each task too, as the format requires, and under it the line of the segment shown, which opens the segments in a pop-over; Back, "Leave the lesson", and the pop-over's "Leave the lesson" are the exit. `app` draws an explanation itself, without the canvas, and an example with a new canvas element per segment: `workedExample`, then the `demoState` of this run, and the new state comes by the event `demochange`. The learner opens the parts of the demo and hides the whole demo with the element's buttons "Next" and "Demo". A lesson's task is the task screen under the lesson's bar.
- **"Continue"** is the button of `app` to the next segment, `ContinueButton` of `src/lesson/LessonScreen.tsx`; it is not called "Next", so as not to clash with the canvas's button. Its icon, after its word, is `material-symbols:arrow-circle-right-outline-rounded`, without a fill, mirrored right to left: a value of `app`'s own, not in `spec`'s table of shared values. On an explanation and a task it stands in a row of glass at the bottom; on a worked example in the canvas's own row, after its "Next" and "Demo" — a child `<button slot="actions">` of the element, which canvas 0.3.26 and newer shows in its slot `actions` —, in the order of Tab after them. On an explanation and an example it is enabled at once: the demo does not lock it. On a task it appears when `api` has said that the task has ended — so the format requires; a task with a rubric is closed by the learner's "Done", enabled after at least one submission, and "Continue" appears after it. It is `aria-disabled` while "lesson done" is under way. There is no automatic move on.
- **Back** — to any explanation and example already gone through, by a row of the segments' pop-over; an example opens with its demo state, and the lesson's place does not change: "Continue" leads through the explanations and examples gone through back to it, past the segments of ended tasks. The segment of a task that has ended does not open again: the attempt is closed, and to solve the task again is a new attempt; the work and the open view are erased too, an essay's as well. From an unfinished task the learner looks into an example and comes back: the task's work lies in the profile database, and the new element gets it by the property `history`.
- **The lesson state** — the open view, the place and the demo state of each example by its place — `app` keeps in the profile database under the key "lesson ID, package digest", with the run ID that `OpenLesson` gives beside it; the state of another run under the same key is discarded. The place is the segment the learner has reached, or a consolidation task after a segment; when `api` has said that the task at the place has ended, the place moves on at once. The end of the run for any reason erases the state — "passed", "unchecked" on a withdrawn version and on a version outside the window, "not suitable" and "abandoned", in an answer or in the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` —, and so do the withdrawal of the version and the same lesson that `api` gave on another package. A lesson on another package is a new run, from the first segment and with the demos from the start. A record that the element would reject — `opened` not a whole number from 0 to the number of parts, or `hidden` neither `true` nor `false` — means a damaged database: `app` does not give it, and the demo begins from the start.
- **Opening a lesson.** Which attempts of the lesson have ended and which has not, `api` knows — they are its attempts. The lesson opens on the later of two places: its own, from the lesson state, and the one that comes after the last ended task. On another device, where there is no lesson state, this is the place after the last ended task, and if none has ended, the first segment; the demos from the start, and an unfinished task by the same attempt with a clean sheet: the work lies on the device where it was written.
- **The end of a lesson.** The ordinary end is always "Continue" on the last segment, whatever its kind: `app` tells `api` that the lesson is passed, `CompleteLesson`, repeated until `api` answers, and erases the lesson state; the refusal `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED`, with the places of the task segments not ended, opens the lesson on the first of them. `api` itself ends a lesson only by an unusual path — "unchecked", "not suitable" or "abandoned" —, and then `app` does not send "lesson passed". The end screen is the mockup's card: the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose, without a score or a comparison; only the adult sees the reason code. A lesson that ended "unchecked" is a neutral phrase.
- **The engine choice.** `engine` chooses the next lesson or task, and does not reorder the segments. A task outside a lesson is the task screen without the lesson's bar. A lesson of the first slice has no consolidation and no early end: at the end of a segment `api` does not call the engine. What comes next `app` learns from the field `next` at the end of an attempt — the next segment, "no more segments" or the engine choice: the skills of the lesson are closed, and whether a verdict assesses them, `app` does not know.
- **A task with a rubric** ends with feedback, "unreadable" or "unchecked", not with a verdict; feedback without words is `app`'s own phrase "No feedback this time", and the adult sees the marks of the criteria in the progress.
- **The mark "sequential"** is on a module whose skill graph is a chain: each skill of the module stands in a tier of its own, and there are at least two skills. Anchor skills give no tiers and are not counted; a module without a graph and with one skill has no mark. `app` takes the tiers ready — the `skill_tiers` that `module-kit` computes and `api` gives to the skill map: a module is a chain when each tier holds one skill. The mark is seen on the module's card — the kind "Sequential" on Home, "In order" on Subjects — and on the module's page in the section for adults.

— *Why:*
- Back — for the sake of the example: `spec` rejected a demo over a lesson's task, and to reread an example while stuck on a task is possible only by going back to it. A task, though, is an attempt: to solve an ended task again is a new attempt, evidence and an engine choice, and the lesson names the task once. To open it read-only would mean keeping the task's open view after the end of the attempt, longer than the storage on the device keeps it; an essay is kept no longer than its attempt either, and no screen reads it again.
- The place in the lesson moves on as soon as the task has ended, and the lesson opens on the later of its own place and the answer of `api`: otherwise a restart or another device would open a task that has ended and does not open.
- Within one run the version does not change: the lesson holds the digest of its package. The state of another version occurs only when the former run has ended — most often "unchecked" on a withdrawn version —, and the lesson is begun anew. The key with the digest tells such runs apart without comparing the content, and the element will not make this comparison: a state that fits by the number of parts it takes silently, even if the parts are different.
- The place on the device adds no data on the server: `api` knows the attempts of the lesson anyway. The price: on another device the learner loses the place among the explanations and examples after the last ended task and the opened parts of the demo, and that is a few taps.
- The mark speaks of the order of the module's lessons, not of the segments: the segments go in the author's order in any lesson. So its place is where a module is chosen. The graph orders the lessons of different skills wholly exactly when each skill stands in a tier of its own, and an extra link — a skill needs both `a` and `b`, and `b` needs `a` — does not change the order (`spec/format/vectors/tiers/chain.yaml`). The chain is counted by `skill_tiers`, without a second count of the tiers.
- An example is neither a task nor evidence: to lock "Continue" with the demo would mean pressing the learner, and the learner can come back to the example.
- Without "lesson passed" from `app`, `api` would not learn that the learner has read the explanation after the last task to the end, and a lesson without tasks — for example, `reading-model-essays.yaml` among the format examples — would never end.
- "Continue" has an icon of its own: the arrow in a circle looks different from the arrow of the element's "Next", `arrow-forward`, and from the chevron of "Demo", `keyboard-arrow-right`, and on the screen of a worked example all three are seen at once; there it stands in the canvas's row, where the learner's eye already is.

— *Rejected:*
- only forward, and back to all segments, to the tasks too — the reasons above;
- carrying the demo state over if the example is the same by content, as `sameExample` of `canvas` does — code for a case that does not occur within a run; giving `opened` no greater than the number of parts of the new example — the element will take it silently, and the learner will see open the parts they have not read;
- the place in the lesson on the server: each "Continue" is a request and a new row of the learner's data on the server; the place only on the device: on another device the lesson would begin from the start, and the ended tasks would have to be gone through again or skipped without explanation;
- the mark in the lesson's header too, as in the prototype — the reason above; not showing it in the first slice: the rule of derivation is needed anyway, and the places for it are the same;
- "Continue" on an example, locked until all parts are opened — the reason above;
- one button "Next" for the parts of the demo and the segments, with the canvas's buttons turned off, as `canvas` allows: the element makes the demo's buttons accessible and captions them by `lang`, and `app` would have to repeat this and run the parts itself;
- a chain as a strict path, where a skill has at most one prerequisite of the module and at most one skill after it: a graph with an extra link orders the lessons the same way, and would get no mark;
- its own count of the tiers by `prerequisites` of the open view of the graph: a second count beside `module-kit` could diverge from the tiers of the skill map, and the mark would contradict the map;
- the icons `double-arrow` and `east` for "Continue" — the same bare arrow to the right as that of "Next", only drawn otherwise; `skip-next` — a button of a sound player;
- a strip of chips of the segments under the title — the mockup lists them in a pop-over.

— *Does not decide:* when a lesson is passed and what `api` records about it, and whether a lesson begun on another device continues the same run — `api`; when the engine gives a lesson and when a task, consolidation and an early end — the detailing of `engine`; how a lesson's task ends by the hint ladder and the showing of the solution — `api`.

— *Consequence:* `src/lesson/LessonScreen.tsx` — `ContinueButton` —, held as the element's child in the slot by `src/lesson/Lesson.browser.test.tsx`; `src/lesson/`; `src/storage/profile.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Voicing

**R07. `app` voices the tutor's words by the device's speech synthesis and always names the voice itself: `localService` is true, and the name has no "Online". On Android and Linux, where the browser does not know whether the system synthesizes on the device, an adult turns voicing on for this device. No voice — no voicing, with a note, and the text always stays on the screen. Whether voicing is on, and its speed, are part of the presentation style in the learner's profile, and the voice is chosen on the device. Voicing is in the first slice, but not in the first app.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The boundary** is `tutor`'s: the same text that passed the checks of `api` is voiced, the model writes no separate text for speech, and `tutor` takes no part in voicing by the device. Signs in words, if the synthesis needs them, are written by the code of `app`, after the checks (В63 `tutor`).
- **What is voiced.** By itself, if voicing is on, the tutor's words from `api`: a rung's question, a hint, feedback by a rubric, a fallback text — and the lines of the panel that come with them: the verdict, the reason "unreadable", the neutral phrase. Voicing begins when the whole text is on the screen. By the speaker button, always when there is a voice: a repeat of the tutor's words and the open texts of the module that the learner reads: the statement, the chips, a step, a technique, a lesson's explanation and the opened parts of an example. The buttons and captions of the interface and the `alt` of pictures are not voiced: that is the screen reader's.
- **The language** is each text's own: the module's texts and the tutor's words are the module's explanation language, the strings of `app` the interface language.
- **The voice** `app` names itself, `utterance.voice`, and leaves the choice to the browser not at all. The candidates are the voices whose `localService` is true, whose name has no "Online" and whose `lang` matches the text's language by the basic filtering of RFC 4647: `en-US` matches `en`. First the voice the adult chose on this device, then the voice with `default` if it is the language's only one, then the first. `app` rereads the list on `voiceschanged`.
- **Android and Linux.** There voicing is off until an adult turns it on for this device on the page "This device", having read why: the device's system chooses the voice, and whether it is a network one, the browser does not know.
- **No voice** — the phrase's language has no candidate, or on Android and Linux the adult has not turned voicing on. The speaker button is disabled, a line beside it says why, and the text stays. The page "This device" names only the languages that have a voice here, each with its choice; one without a voice it does not name.
- **Settings.** Whether voicing is on, and the speed — "Normal" or "Slow" — are part of the presentation style in the learner's profile on the server (О-23). Both the learner, by a button on the screen, and the adult change it. A new profile of the category `under_13` has voicing on, older ones off. It does not go into the AI call. The voice by language and the adult's permission on Android and Linux lie in the device registry: "Erase everything on this device" and the account gone or deleted erase them with the registry, a sign-in of another account and `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS` erase them, and the owner's sign-out leaves them.
- **One synthesis for the whole app**: a new text cuts off the former one, and the sentences of one text go in a queue one after another; `pause()` is not used; the tap after which a text will come lifts WebKit's ban with an empty utterance before the request; an error is silent.
- **The slice.** Voicing is in the first slice, stage 2, and its code after the first app, before the first child: the first app does not name it.

— *Why:* the reasoning is that of Т-13.
- A remote synthesis gets the text of the phrase from the learner's device, bypassing `api`. Chrome, if the voice is not named, takes any voice by language and does not prefer a local one, and its network voices "Google US English" and «Google русский» send the text to `www.google.com/speech-api` ([`tts_controller_impl.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/content/browser/speech/tts_controller_impl.cc), [`manifest.json`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/chrome/browser/resources/network_speech_synthesis/manifest.json) and [`tts_extension.js`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/chrome/browser/resources/network_speech_synthesis/tts_extension.js) of Chromium at commit 88a24da). Edge's voices with "Online" in the name send the text to Microsoft (sources 5.1). The tutor's words are written about the learner's work, and feedback by a rubric quotes the learner's words. Such services have no contract with the platform, and `app` speaks only with `api` and the sign-in service. The platform counts voicing as output, not data collection, only while the text does not leave the device.
- `localService` is what the browser knows, not the system. WebKit sets `true` for every voice of Apple's system ([`PlatformSpeechSynthesizerCocoa.mm`](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/72780a2/Source/WebCore/platform/cocoa/PlatformSpeechSynthesizerCocoa.mm) of WebKit at commit 72780a2). Chrome on Android builds a voice from the language and the system's engine and does not mark it as a network one ([`TtsPlatformImpl.java`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/content/public/android/java/src/org/chromium/content/browser/TtsPlatformImpl.java) of Chromium at commit 88a24da), although Android allows the engine to synthesize over the network, `Voice.isNetworkConnectionRequired()`. On Linux speech-dispatcher has a module of the cloud Edge TTS (the [README](https://github.com/brailcom/speechd/blob/8972b11/README.md) and [`edgetts-generic.conf`](https://github.com/brailcom/speechd/blob/8972b11/config/modules/edgetts-generic.conf) of speech-dispatcher at commit 8972b11, the [README](https://github.com/rany2/edge-tts/blob/bd44c1d/README.md) of `edge-tts` at commit bd44c1d), and Chrome does not count its voices as network ones. What Google's engine on Android does with the text, Google does not say (sources 5.1). The specification's "there is no guarantee that any of these implications are true" speaks of latency, price and quality, not of the truth of `localService` (sources 5.1). So where the browser cannot know this, the adult decides, having read why, not the browser.
- Devices' voices differ: one has no Russian, another no English, and the `voiceURI` of one voice differs on different devices. Chrome on Android does not choose voices within a language at all ([`speech_synthesis_impl.cc`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/content/browser/speech/speech_synthesis_impl.cc) and [`TtsPlatformImpl.java`](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/88a24da8ad5dbef9b947545887d4da2c33f45759/content/public/android/java/src/org/chromium/content/browser/TtsPlatformImpl.java) of Chromium at commit 88a24da). So the voice is on the device, and whether voicing is on is in the profile: the learner learns on other devices too, and the adult sets it once. The presentation style is how the platform speaks and shows: the length of phrases, voicing, the persona, ranks. Voicing is already named in it, and it changes no words and does not go to the model. The default by category — because the presentation style goes by the learner's age, and it can be chosen separately. Younger learners read slowly, and for older ones sound unasked would get in the way.
- Without a voice of the needed language the phrase is silent: a foreign voice would read an English text with Russian pronunciation. The note is needed so that the learner and the adult know that the device has no voice, not that voicing is broken.
- The first slice is children from 6, and they read slowly. Voicing by the device costs only code of `app`: no server, no money, no new data besides the setting. In the first app the author, an adult, solves the tasks, and the first app's code is kept narrow.
- The sign-out leaves the voicing: the same account's next sign-in finds it, and another account's adult decides anew.

— *Rejected:*
- a remote voice with the adult's consent — the reason above: the text goes to a third-party service bypassing `api`, and Т-13 closes exactly this;
- a voice of another language if the needed one is missing — the reason above; silently, without a note — the learner and the adult would not know why;
- only `localService`, as in the recommendation of Т-13 — on Android and Linux the text could go to the network without the adult knowing. No voicing on Android and Linux at all — tablets on Android would be left without a voice, although the adult can accept this themselves;
- the setting wholly on the device — on each device it would be set anew, and the adult would not know what the learner hears on another. The voice in the profile — a `voiceURI` from another device will not be found;
- the adult's default apart from the learner's choice, as for the solving mode: the solving mode holds what a task begins with, while voicing does not change the content, and there is nothing to guard the default from;
- voicing at stage 3, together with the voice (Т-13) — in the first slice only the adult beside would read the text to the younger learners. Voicing already in the first app — it would widen the first code, and the author does not need it;
- a text for speech from the model — `tutor` rejected it: a number written in words is not found by the search for the key;
- a button "listen" — `spec`'s glossary holds this word for an action of the text canvas;
- naming on "This device" each language without a voice, with how to add one in the system — the languages of the interface and the modules will be many, and the page would grow a list of what is missing; the line beside the speaker says it where the learner needs it.

— *Does not decide:* the persona's voice — tone and pitch — with the persona (О-13); "listen" of the text canvas — with it, by these same rules: `canvas` has no event `speak` yet; voicing by an AI provider and voice input — stage 3 (О-6, П-5); checking on devices what is derived from the browsers' code — Д5.

— *Consequence:* `src/voice/`; `src/storage/voicing.ts`; `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Own key

**R08. Only the owner profile of the category `adult` has an own key, and `app` sends it only with the requests of this profile that call the AI. On the device the key lies only if the adult so decided on this device — in the owner profile's database, — and otherwise it lives in the tab's memory while the owner profile is open. There is no encryption in the browser: `app` says plainly that the key is not protected from whoever holds the device, and advises a limit and an expiry at the provider. The owner profile on an account with a profile of another category opens with the same PIN as the section for adults, at each opening, and closes after a hidden page by the period the adult sets on this device. The key is not part of the export.** `spec` closed Т-9 and П-17 as this entry and `api` decide. The design and the key's path, step by step, are in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **Whose key.** Only the owner profile's as a learner, of the category `adult`. One account holds both the owner profile and the profiles of the owner's children, and `api` accepts a key only with a request of a profile `adult` and checks this itself (Т-9; О-4). An account without an owner profile as a learner connects no own key: the key is needed by whoever learns themselves. There is one key, at one provider.
- **The provider** the adult chooses themselves — from those that `api` opens to the own key for this profile by the admission function: OpenAI and Anthropic. `app` does not guess the provider by the look of the key.
- **Connecting** is in the section for adults, on the page "Own AI key", `#/adults/own-key`. In a group of its own under the form, the page says what will happen to it: it goes to the server of NovaTrail with each request of the owner profile that calls the AI, and lives there only in the request's memory; the provider links each request with the account of the key's owner; the PIN does not protect the key. And it advises a key of a separate OpenAI project with a hard limit, or a key bound to a separate Anthropic workspace with a monthly limit, with a lifetime (sources 6.1). The page works only for the owner profile: with no learner chosen it opens it by "Open my profile", without a PIN until the account holds child profiles.
