The list of `ListOwnKeyProviders` may be empty — on a deployment, until П-16 —, and the page then says that no provider takes an own key on this server and shows no field. `app` checks the key through `api`, and after that shows only the provider and the last four characters.
- **Where it lies.** By default only in the tab's memory, while the owner profile is open. The mark "Keep on this device" puts it into the owner profile's database. Whether the device is shared, only the adult knows, so the adult decides, as with the permission of voicing on Android and Linux.
- **What protects it.** There is no encryption in the browser. The PIN is a barrier of the interface, as MathTrail's canvas-api put it ([canvas-api](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/decisions.md) at commit 0901af0), and the browser's storage does not protect from whoever holds the device (sources 6.1). `app` says just that, and other measures hold the damage: the key goes only with the owner profile, by default it is not on the device, the provider sets the limit and the lifetime, and the key is revoked at the provider.
- **The request.** The key and the provider go with each request of the owner profile that calls the AI: with work to read, with the analysis and the hint, and with "explain differently", which calls the AI without work. With a request of a child's profile the key never goes.
- **A refusal of the key** — without a silent substitution. The owner as a learner gets what the platform requires for an exhausted limit, with a line of the cause, and the section for adults gets what to do. `app` moves to the platform AI only by the owner's action.
- **The owner profile** (П-17). If the account holds a profile of another category than `adult`, the owner profile opens with the same PIN as the section for adults, at each opening. The PIN field is `<input type="password" inputmode="numeric">` with pasting and without `autocomplete` hints. To a wrong PIN `api` answers `PinCheck` — how many wrong tries are left and, if this one locked the entry, when it opens —, to a locked entry the refusal `CAUSE_PIN_LOCKED` with the time, and the error names both. The profile closes — and the key leaves the memory — by "Switch learner", on a reload, and when the page has been hidden for the period, by the device's clock: 5 minutes by default, 15, 30 or 60, chosen on the page "This device". The period lies in the device registry, as the permission of voicing does, and goes with it: "Erase everything on this device", a sign-in of another account or `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS`, the account gone or deleted; the sign-out leaves it. The closing loses nothing: the work lies in the profile database. Without a profile of another category the owner profile opens without a PIN and does not close after a hidden page.
- **The key is erased by** "Remove key" — one action, there is no "Don't use own key" —, a replacement — when the new key has passed the check —, the mark taken off and the erasures of the storage on the device: the deletion of the owner profile, its data erased on "This device", "Erase everything on this device", a sign-in of another account or `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS`, the account gone or deleted. The sign-out leaves it, and the same account's next sign-in finds it. The other tabs of `app` learn of an erasure through `BroadcastChannel`. The key has no expiry of its own on the device.
- **The export** — without the key and without a mark about it.

— *Why:*
- The providers keep a key on the server, not in the browser: OpenAI — "Don’t share it with others or expose it in any client-side code such as browsers or apps", and Anthropic's SDK for the browser — "Any user with access to the browser can potentially inspect, extract, and misuse these credentials" (sources 6.1). Т-9 chose the device so that there would be no own keys on the server. On a shared tablet a key lying on the device can be got by anyone who holds the device. So by default the key does not stay on the device, and where the adult learns alone, the adult puts it into the database themselves. The providers' terms allow giving the own key to `api`: `spec` decided so without a lawyer.
- Encryption does not change this. A non-extractable key of Web Crypto closes only the export: "any application that shares the same scheme, host, and port have access to the same storage partition", and "Any application or user that has access to the device storage may be able to recover the key material" (sources 6.1). A password from the PIN is 4–6 digits, no more than a million variants. The secret of a passkey — the extension `prf` of WebAuthn — Safari does not give in `get()` ([MDN's compatibility data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/v8.1.10/api/CredentialsContainer.json) at v8.1.10: `prf` in `get()` — Safari `"version_added": false`, `webkit.org/b/259934`), so on an iPad there is none, and the code of a family tablet is often known to the child too. An honest caveat and a limit at the provider hold more than an appearance of encryption.
- A limit at the provider holds the key on all devices, even a leaked key of its own project or workspace, if the key is bound to it: an Anthropic key without a binding chooses the workspace by a header of the request. Neither OpenAI nor Anthropic has a limit on a single key — only on a project, a workspace or an organization (sources 6.1) —, so the advice is a separate project or workspace.
- A child who got into the owner profile would send the child's work to the provider under the adult's terms. OpenAI requires not processing the data of children under 13 without zero data retention, and a family's key does not have it (sources 6.1). So the PIN is asked at each opening, and the profile also closes by itself: a child who was given the tablet will not get back into the owner profile without the PIN. Without such profiles, the PIN and the closing hold nobody. While the page is seen, the profile does not close by itself: an idle timer would close it in the middle of a task, and the adult, handing over the tablet, presses "Switch learner".
- Closing for protection is a time limit by WCAG 2.2.1: "if a time limit is included in order to address security concerns, it would be considered to have been set by the content". The adult sets it, and 60 minutes is twelve default times: 2.2.1 allows changing the limit "over a wide range that is at least ten times the length of the default setting" (sources 8.1).
- "Remove key" alone: a key kept and not used would lie on the device for nothing, and the requests go on the platform AI either way.
- The sign-out leaves the key and the period: a re-sign-in of the same adult finds them, and the registry holds one account, so another account's sign-in erases them.
- The platform AI is other contracts and the platform's quota. Moving to it behind the owner's back would mean spending the quota and sending the work under other terms.
- The export is learners' data, and the key is the owner's secret. An export file is kept and forwarded, and a new key the owner issues at the provider themselves.

— *Rejected:*
- the key always on the device, behind the PIN: on a shared tablet it would always lie there, against what the providers advise; storing the key nowhere: on their own laptop the adult would enter it at each opening of their profile;
- encrypting the key with a non-extractable key of Web Crypto, a password from the PIN or the secret of a passkey — the reasons above;
- the key on the server, encrypted: the architecture has no own keys on the server (Т-9);
- the owner profile by a fresh sign-in — `api` rejected it: on a shared tablet each switch would become a sign-in through Google; the PIN once until a reload — a child who was given the tablet would get back into the owner profile without the PIN; the PIN also without profiles of another category — a barrier that holds nobody;
- a period "do not close": it would leave a child on a shared tablet in the owner profile, on the AI routes for adults and on the owner's own key;
- "Don't use own key" beside "Remove key" — the reason above;
- the key erased at the sign-out: the same adult would enter it anew; a device given away is cleared by "Erase everything on this device";
- the key in the export, or a mark about it — the reason above;
- moving to the platform AI on a refusal of the key without the owner's action — the reason above;
- the provider by the look of the key: a mistake would send the key to another provider;
- the key only with work: "explain differently" calls the AI without work;
- an expiry of its own for the key on the device: the provider sets the lifetime — at Anthropic it is chosen when the key is issued (sources 6.1) —, and a second expiry would only force entering the key anew.

— *Does not decide:* the admission table and which providers are open to the own key — `spec` and `tutor`; the spending limit — it is at the provider; the own key for minors — П-4; the first app — it has no own key, and how `api` and `tutor` name the path with the key from an environment variable — В64 `tutor`; the PIN of the section for adults as a whole; checking on devices that the screen lock hides the page and how the clock runs on a sleeping tablet — Д7.

— *Consequence:* `src/ownkey/`; `src/storage/ownkey.ts`, `src/storage/registry.ts`; `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Deployment configuration

**R09. `app` reads the deployment configuration at each start from the file `/deployment.json` at the root of its origin. The file lies beside the signed build, but not in it: at the platform the rollout of `infra` writes it from the rollout list, and in self-hosting the operator does, by the same format. The check is strict — an unknown field and a wrong value stop `app` before the first request to the network, and the build has no defaults and no address of the platform. The CSP is in two layers: the lower one in the signed `index.html`, and the hosting's header, assembled by a template from the same build, narrows it to exact addresses. Sign-in is by the popup window of Firebase Auth.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The settings** are format 1 of `spec`'s part `deployment/`: `format`, `api` and `signIn`, and in `signIn` — `service` with the value `firebase`, `apiKey`, `authDomain`, `projectId` and `appId`. The file has no secrets: the browser of everyone who opened the app gets it, and Firebase's keys "do not need to be treated as secrets" (sources 7.1). The domain of widgets will come with a new format at stage 4.
- **Where from.** `app` requests `/deployment.json` by an absolute path, first thing at the start, with `cache: "no-cache"`. The response is `application/json`, without a redirect and no more than 16 KiB. The release archive does not contain this file, and CI checks this.
- **The check.** The fields are exactly those the format names: an unknown, extra or missing field is an error with the field's name. `api` is only an origin, `https`, and `http` only on loopback, both for `api` and for `app` itself. The build has no schema of its own: it carries a copy of the schema `deployment.schema.json` from the release of `spec` it stands on; the rollout checks the file by it, and for `app` it is the same source of the rules.
- **What the learner sees.** Without a connection — "No connection" and a button to retry. Without a valid file — a static screen "This app isn't set up" and a line for the operator: what is wrong. `app` does not sign in and sends no other requests. If `app` is opened in a frame of another's page, it shows a static screen and stops. The failure screens are in the device's language, the line for whoever deployed the copy is in English, and the screen "isn't set up" does not address the child: instead of "tell a grown-up", an impersonal line that a grown-up's help is needed. In the built `index.html` — `<html lang="en">` and a `<noscript>` in English and Russian, and the Russian paragraph has `lang="ru"`.
- **CSP.** The lower layer is a `<meta>` in the `<head>` of the signed `index.html`, right after `<meta charset>`: its own scripts and Google's sign-in script, `object-src`, `base-uri` and `form-action` closed, `font-src 'none'` — `app` has no fonts of its own, only the system ones —, and the addresses that the build does not know — any `https:`. The hosting's header is assembled by `csp.json` from the same build: in it are the exact `api`, the addresses of Firebase Auth and `authDomain`, `font-src 'none'` and `frame-ancestors 'none'`. The canvas element's styles are set by `adoptedStyleSheets`, and from the CSP the element needs only `img-src blob:`. Where the hosting sends no headers of its own, only the lower layer acts.
- **Sign-in** is `signInWithPopup`, and `authDomain` is `<project>.firebaseapp.com`. The hosting needs neither `/__/auth/` on the domain of `app` nor Firebase's files beside the build. The price is a window that is sometimes blocked, and Google's script `apis.google.com` in the origin of `app`: on an iPad, in Safari and on mobile browsers the SDK loads it at each start. The device registry remembers the `projectId` and `apiKey` with which the sign-in went: if they are different in the file, `app` erases the session of Firebase Auth before the sign-in, otherwise the former record with the refresh token would stay in its database.
- **One build.** The build has nothing that depends on the deployment, so the platform and self-hosting roll out the same signed bytes, each with its own `deployment.json` and its own header, and the build and the file go out in one release of the hosting. The rollout list pins the build of `app` and the image of `api` together (Т-19), and the file has no versions. `app` offers to reload not at each version of the contract, but when `api` has answered `unimplemented` or sent an open view of a format version that the build does not read: within a major contract package an old build works with a new `api`.
- **Locally**, the development server and `vite preview` serve `/deployment.json` from `deployment.local.json`, which is not in git, and a proxy leads the paths of `api` to a local `api`.

— *Why:*
- A file beside the build does not touch the signed bytes and works on any static hosting. Vite replaces environment variables at build time, and copies the folder `public/` into the build "as-is" (sources 7.1), so everything the build knows is signed with it. `tutor`'s facts file is arranged the same way: «файл фактов в образ не вшивают: `api` читает его при старте, и self-hosting берёт ту же сборку со своим файлом» ([`tutor`'s architecture](https://github.com/altnovatrail/tutor/blob/be607e5/docs/architecture.md) at commit be607e5).
- The strict check is also as for `tutor`'s facts file: there «опечатка в имени факта не должна молча закрыть путь». Here a typo or a default could silently lead the learner's work to the wrong place: a self-hosting copy with the platform's address by default would send learners' data to another operator. The absolute path and the check of the type are needed because the usual fallback of an SPA serves `index.html` with the code 200 for a missing file, and a relative path from a lesson's page would land in it too.
- The signature holds the build's path, not what the browser gets: the browser gets `/deployment.json` from the same hosting as `index.html`, and does not check signatures. So other measures hold the file: the rollout checks it by the schema from the signed build, the rollout list names its digest, and the CSP header narrows where the page may go.
- The header is "the preferred mechanism for delivering a policy", and `frame-ancestors` does not work in `<meta>`. The policies of `<meta>` and of the header act together, and the second "can only further restrict" the first (sources 7.1). The lower layer is what the signed build guarantees itself: whatever hosting serves it, no script will run in it but its own and the sign-in script — neither a counter put in by the hosting nor an injected one. The template `csp.json` in the build is needed so that the list of directives is one for `app` and for the rollout.
- Sign-in by redirect requires `authDomain` on the domain of `app`: otherwise Safari on an iPad loses the user, as MathTrail's canvas-api found ([its decisions](https://github.com/mathtrail/canvas-api/blob/0901af0/docs/decisions.md) at commit 0901af0). So the hosting would have to proxy `/__/auth/` or keep Firebase's files beside the build, which Firebase tells to "periodically download and sync" (sources 7.1) — unsigned files that change without a release. The window works on any hosting, and canvas-api signed in with it too. The SDK loads the script `apis.google.com` both with the window and with the redirect, so it is named plainly, not hidden. It can be done without only by signing in at Google oneself and passing the result to Firebase.
- The canvas takes pictures by the function `media`, which gives a `Blob`, and shows them by `blob:` addresses ([`canvas`'s architecture](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/297849c/docs/architecture.md) at commit 297849c). So the media files go through `fetch`, `img-src` does not name the address of `api`, and CORS of `api` is needed on the paths without a token too.
- The failure screens are among the screens before the choice of a learner, which are in the device's language.

— *Rejected:*
- an answer of `api` at a constant path — the address of `api` would have to be known beforehand, that is, `api` kept on one origin with `app`, imposing this on the hosting;
- an insertion into `index.html` at the rollout — it edits a signed file, and an inline script needs a hash in the CSP;
- `/__/firebase/init.json` of Firebase Hosting — only this hosting has it, only for the SDK "version 8 and earlier", and without the address of `api` (sources 7.1);
- a build for each deployment, `define` and `VITE_*` — the card of `app` in `spec` requires one signed build for the platform and self-hosting;
- a soft check — an unknown field is skipped, and a missing one takes a default: the reason above;
- the CSP only by the header — on a hosting without headers of its own a copy would have no policy at all; only `<meta>` — the address of `api` cannot be named, and `frame-ancestors` does not work in `<meta>`; a `<meta>` that a script inserts after reading the file — "There must not be more than one meta element with any particular state", and the policy acts only after the insertion; a nonce — Vite tells to change it "for each request", and that needs a server (sources 7.1);
- sign-in by redirect — the reasons above.

— *Does not decide:* the build's hosting and the domains — `infra` (Т-17); whether `api` and `app` have one origin — also `infra`, and `app` works with both; what the rollout checks — `infra` with Т-19; App Check — `api`; the domain of widgets — stage 4; checking on devices and in browsers what is derived from the code of the SDK and from the specifications — Д8 and Д9.

— *Consequence:* `src/config/`; `src/boot/`; `scripts/deployment-plugin.ts`, `scripts/csp.ts`; `vite.config.ts`; `justfile`; `index.html`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Interface languages

**R10. The interface of `app` is in English and Russian. The whole interface is in the device's language, which the adult chooses on the device. The module's texts and the tutor's words stay in the module's explanation language, each with its own `lang`, and the legal texts are in force in English. Translations are code of its own on `Intl`, without a library, with TypeScript dictionaries, and the strings do not reveal the learner's gender.** The design and the glossary are in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The languages** are `en` and `ru`, by the recommendation of О-2. New interface languages are stage 5. A learner has three languages, and `app` decides only the interface language: the explanation language is set by the module manifest, and there is no target language in the slice.
- **The profile's language.** The contract carries a profile's field "interface language", any valid tag of BCP 47; `app` sends it only for a new owner profile, the device's language, and reads it nowhere. The interface language does not go into the AI call.
- **The device's language** is for every screen — the sign-in, the choice of a learner, the start of work, the failure screens, the section for adults and the learner's screens. It is the adult's choice on this device, and without a choice the first language of `navigator.languages` that the lookup of RFC 4647 finds — by truncation of the tag —, otherwise English. It is chosen by buttons where each language is named by its own name, on the sign-in screen and in the section for adults. The choice lies in a storage of its own, "the device settings", apart from the registry: the sign-out and the sign-in of another account do not erase it, "Erase everything on this device" does. The account has no language of its own.
- **Two languages on a screen.** The strings of `app` are in the interface language, and `<html lang>` is the same. The module's texts and the tutor's words are in the module's explanation language, and each of their blocks has its own `lang`, and the name of a module, a lesson or a skill inside a phrase `app` inserts as an element with its own `lang`. The module's name and explanation language before the first attempt come from the open view of the manifest of the current version. To the canvas element `app` gives the `lang` of the interface language: the buttons' captions are in it, and the manifest's language on the statement, the lines of the workbook and the parts of an example the element sets itself. A module's card names its explanation language if it is not the interface language. The names of anchor skills are English and not translated, with `lang="en"`. Voicing is in the language of each text.
- **Texts from `api`** — the legal texts and the consent — `app` asks for by `LegalService.GetLegalTexts`, without a token, naming the screen's language by the field `interface_language`, not by the header `Accept-Language`, as `spec`'s contracts say. The legal texts are in English, and the English text is in force; on a screen in another language `app` says in the screen's language that the text is in English. The gist of the consent — who the operator is, what data and why, to whom it goes, how to withdraw — `app` shows briefly beside the English text: in the request's language if the manifest of the texts carries it, otherwise in English, with the `lang` the answer names by the field `summary_language`. A version that has a translation into the screen's language `app` shows with it, below the line that the English text is in force and before the English text, with its own `lang`; the translation has no force of its own, and the consent names the version, not the translation. `spec` writes the translations.
- **Translations** are code of its own, without a library, by the common rules of UI packages. A dictionary per language is a TypeScript module: English is the source, with `as const`, Russian `satisfies` its shape, so `tsc` will not let through a key or a placeholder that does not exist. Placeholders are by name; there is no gluing of strings. The forms of a number are the categories of CLDR through `Intl.PluralRules`; `other` is always there, and a test checks the dictionary's categories against the language's categories. Numbers, sizes and dates are `Intl`, sorting is `Intl.Collator`. A code from the server that the dictionary does not know gets a generic string, not an error. A note to the translator is a comment at the key.
- **Rules of strings.** A string does not reveal the learner's gender, as the tutor's words do not: in Russian — without the past tense and short adjectives about the learner. On the learner's screens in Russian the learner is not addressed: on buttons and in the lines of a step — the infinitive, elsewhere — impersonal phrases. In the section for adults — «вы». The mode "self" on the button is "On my own". The pseudonym and the names stand where they are not declined. The AI does not speak of itself in the first person.
- **The name of a tutor's mark** `app` writes by the kind, the number and the place; the contract carries no link of the mark to a phrase of the tutor: the mark belongs to the words of the answer that brought it.
- **The words** are the glossary of terms in `en` and `ru` in [architecture.md](architecture.md). A word that more than one package shows is in `spec`'s table of shared words: the glossary takes the captions of the canvas's buttons from there, and a new common word goes there as a row «в реестр spec». The code writes the full phrases by these rules, and the author reads them in the pull request.

— *Why:*
- One language for the device: a language is set in one place and applies to everything, and the adult sets it once. The learner has no switch: a chance tap would take the child into a language they do not read.
- The device's language has a storage of its own, because the device registry belongs to the account — "Erase everything on this device" and a sign-in of another account erase it —, and the choice on the sign-in screen is made before the sign-in. The lookup is by truncation, not by "best match": in MathTrail the "best match" of CLDR gave Kazakh, Kyrgyz and Belarusian to Russian ([MathTrail standalone's decisions](https://github.com/mathtrail/mathtrail-standalone/blob/89986bc/docs/decisions.md) at commit 89986bc).
- Two languages on one screen were decided by the format: a module has one explanation language, and the format v0 has no translations. `lang` on each block is needed so that the screen reader reads the text with its pronunciation: "Screen readers can load the correct pronunciation rules" (sources 8.1). WCAG 2.2, 3.1.2 requires this too.
- The legal texts are in force in English alone: an accepted text has no translation in force that could diverge. The gist in the screen's language lets the adult understand what is accepted.
- Code of its own — because `Intl` gives everything two languages need: the forms of a number, numbers, dates, sorting, the names of languages. Libraries would bring their own parsing of messages into the build, or their own build plugin: FormatJS after precompiling leaves the parser in the build — "Precompiling skips runtime parsing but does not remove the parser from your bundle" ([its guide](https://github.com/formatjs/formatjs/blob/react-intl%4012.1.6/docs/src/docs/guides/advanced-usage.mdx) at `react-intl` 12.1.6), — Lingui without Babel works only by a plugin with native binaries ([its Vite plugin](https://github.com/lingui/js-lingui/blob/v6.11.0/website/docs/ref/vite-plugin.md) at v6.11.0), and its CLI pulls in Babel ([`@lingui/cli` 6.11.0](https://registry.npmjs.org/@lingui/cli/6.11.0)), and i18next has the forms of a number as suffixes of keys, and `tsc` does not check the keys. TypeScript modules, not JSON: from JSON TypeScript infers only `string`, so it will not check the placeholders, and JSON has no comments. The table of captions of `canvas` is arranged the same way — in code, with English by default ([`labels.ts`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/v0.3.43/src/element/labels.ts) of `canvas` at v0.3.43).
- The platform does not know the learner's gender, and in Russian the past tense, a short adjective and «сам» reveal it. Addressing an adult learner as «ты» and a child as «вы» is equally awkward, and the infinitive and an impersonal phrase suit everyone. «Я прочитал» is the masculine gender and a person where the AI reads (П-15).

— *Rejected:*
- FormatJS, Lingui, i18next — the reasons above; Paraglide — it takes its plugins from a CDN address ([`dist/cli/defaults.js`](https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@inlang/paraglide-js@2.29.3/dist/cli/defaults.js) of `@inlang/paraglide-js` 2.29.3); `messageformat` — it parses messages at run time. MessageFormat 2 is stable since [CLDR 47](https://cldr.unicode.org/downloads/cldr-47), and `Intl.MessageFormat` is at Stage 1 ([the proposal](https://github.com/tc39/proposal-intl-messageformat/blob/101996c/README.md) at commit 101996c): when it comes to the browsers, the dictionaries will move to it;
- JSON dictionaries, as in MathTrail standalone — the reason above;
- the language in each learner's profile, set on the profile's page — a second place for one setting; a switch for the learner — the reason above; the account's language on the server — the adult and the child of one account read in different languages, and the language in the request is enough for the texts from `api`;
- the device's language in the registry — the registry is the account's: "Erase everything on this device" and a sign-in of another account erase it, and the sign-in screen is seen before the account;
- the "best match" of languages — the reason above;
- the captions of the buttons of `canvas` from `app` — `spec` rejected this: the package has its own table;
- a link of a tutor's mark to a phrase of the tutor — `spec` did not take it;
- all the strings of the screens now — the words would set before the code of the screens, and the rules and the glossary hold them even then.

— *Does not decide:* the explanation languages of the slice's modules — `modules` and О-2; writing right to left — with the first such interface or module language; the full phrases of the screens — the code, by these rules.

— *Consequence:* `src/i18n/`; `src/storage/settings.ts`; `src/legal/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Accessibility

**R11. `app` meets WCAG 2.2 level AA and, beyond it, two AAA criteria: 2.5.5 — targets of 44 × 44 CSS px — and 2.3.3 — motion by `prefers-reduced-motion`. What it does not meet yet, its own and what the UI packages declare, it names on the page "Accessibility", in six places: an answer in handwriting, moving the sheet, the print in the lines of a workbook, the submission by "Check", a frame drawn by hand and solving a task from paper. The UI packages are bound by the same standard. It is checked by CI — its own checks in three engines, Biome and axe-core — and by a person on the slice's devices.** The print's 1.4.4 is met by the canvas's zoom. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and the sources in sources 8.1.
- **The standard** — WCAG 2.2, W3C Recommendation 12 December 2024, level AA. Beyond it — 2.5.5 Target Size (Enhanced): the targets of `canvas` are 44 × 44, and children aged 6–12 hit with a finger worse than adults. And 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions: motion that an action starts is turned off by `prefers-reduced-motion`. WCAG 3 is a Working Draft, and `app` does not look at it until it is a Recommendation (sources 8.1).
- **The six places** (the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md), with what stands instead and when each goes):
  - an answer in handwriting — 2.1.1, 2.5.1, 2.5.7: handwriting is a way of input, and the function is the input of an answer, so the exception of 2.1.1 does not fit it (sources 8.1). Choosing an option and everything else is done from the keyboard. It goes with a second way to answer, О-25;
  - moving the sheet — 2.5.7, which `canvas` declares: the sheet is moved with a single pointer by dragging alone — the hand, «Рука», under any pointer, one finger in pen mode, two fingers always —, a task from paper's tracing paper by its grip, and no button scrolls it. Instead: the wheel, the trackpad and the arrow keys in the sheet's region, across and down, and the arrow keys on the grip. No date: buttons come back only if `spec` changes the shared standard of the UI packages;
  - the print in the lines of a workbook — 1.4.12, which `canvas` declares: the print keeps its own spacing, its pieces' places set by `spec`'s page rule, `format/page.yaml`, not by the device's font. Instead: the statement above the workbook and everything else of `app` take the user's spacing, and «Крупнее» on the canvas's bar enlarges the print to twice the page's fit, which meets 1.4.4. No date: the spacing comes only if the page rule gives the lines room for it (В77 `canvas`);
  - the submission by "Check" — 3.3.4: a test's answer gets a verdict at once and goes into evidence, without confirmation and undo. A wrong answer does not end the attempt: the learner corrects the work and checks again. The learner model always counts a submission whose verdict the learner saw, and only one cancelled before the verdict may go uncounted, so it goes with a second way to answer if that gives a correction before the verdict (О-25);
  - a frame drawn by hand round a task the AI missed, and solving a task from paper — 2.1.1, with no date.
- **3.1.2 is not `app`'s.** `canvas` declares it for an element without the attribute `lang`: the name of the sheet's region then sounds in the page's language. `app` always sets the attribute, the screen's language, and the type holds it: in `CanvasProps`, `lang` is required and of type `Language`, the build's languages, so an element without it, or with a language not in the canvas's table of labels, does not compile.
- **The page "Accessibility"** is among the legal pages of `app`: the standard, the six places and what replaces them, how it is checked and whom to contact. Its words are the dictionaries'. The EAA expects such information "in the general terms and conditions, or equivalent document" (sources 8.1).
- **What a package declares is held on the element**, not taken from its documents: `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx` holds that «Крупнее» on the bar grows the print to twice the page's fit and «Мельче» takes it back (1.4.4, met), that the bar has the hand and no button that scrolls (2.5.7), that the print keeps its widths under 1.4.12's spacing while the statement takes it, and that the region's name is in the attribute's language (3.1.2). A canvas that changes any of it fails there, and the page's places are read again. `src/legal/LegalPages.browser.test.tsx` holds the six places, their criteria, and no 3.1.2.
- **The rules of `app`**, in [architecture.md](architecture.md): a visible label on each button, with the icon beside it, but for the closed list of buttons that `spec` lets show their icon alone, its caption its accessible name, with a tooltip of the same words that Escape dismisses; one blink per pause; a PIN field with paste and without `autocomplete` hints; the time after which the owner profile closes is set by the adult; what is irreversible is confirmed; status messages are `role="status"`; a heading and focus for each screen; zoom is not forbidden, orientation is not locked; the fonts are the system ones.
- **CI:** Biome's `a11y` rules by default; the screen tests search by role and name; its own checks in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox — targets, but for one not drawn and one inline in a sentence, 2.5.5's exception Inline, the `lang` of parts, forced colors in Chromium and Firefox, since WebKit has no such mode, reduced motion, layout at 320 px; the contrast of the theme's colors in Node; axe-core on each screen once every finite animation is over, except the subtree of the canvas element, which `canvas` checks in its own CI. axe-core is under MPL-2.0, a named exception `dev` in `licenses.json`, as `lightningcss` is: it does not ship and puts none of its own code into what ships.
- **A person** — on the slice's devices: VoiceOver on iPad and Mac, TalkBack on Android, NVDA on Windows, Switch Control and Voice Control, keyboard only, zoom 200 %, width 320 px, text spacing — Д10 in [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

— *Why:*
- this standard is every UI package's, with each package declaring what it does not meet and `app` gathering it;
- WCAG 2.2 AA is what EN 301 549 V4.1.1 equates itself to and what gov.uk tells the public sector to meet; WCAG 2.1 AA, which ADA Title II, EN 301 549 V3.2.1 and «ГОСТ Р 52872-2019» require, is covered by 2.2 AA but for 4.1.1 Parsing, "Obsolete and removed" (sources 8.1). The beta's markets are not chosen (П-2), so the standard is chosen by the learners: children from 6 and adults, on a tablet and a computer;
- W3C does not advise AAA as a whole: "It is not recommended that Level AAA conformance be required as a general policy for entire sites because it is not possible to satisfy all Level AAA success criteria for some content" (sources 8.1). The two AAA criteria are cheap and children need them;
- the nonconformances are named, not hidden: handwriting is the core of the slice, which checks the reading of handwriting, and a second way to answer is a decision of the format, the contract and `learner-model`, not of `app`;
- 1.4.4: Understanding 1.4.4 asks that "it should still be possible to get 200% text enlargement in some way" (sources 8.3). The page's fit caps the print at the element's width, and the canvas's bar zooms the sheet to twice it: the print is not declared;
- closing the owner profile is a time limit "included in order to address security concerns", which "would be considered to have been set by the content" (sources 8.1); 2.2.1 allows turning it off or changing it tenfold, and only the adult knows whether the device is shared;
- 3.3.8 names paste into the PIN field as a mechanism: "copy and paste to reduce the cognitive burden of re-typing" (sources 8.1). No hint to a password manager: on a shared tablet it would fill in the PIN for anyone who knows the device's code, and the section for adults advises not to save the PIN there;
- axe-core catches, in Deque's words, "on average 57% of WCAG issues" ([its README](https://github.com/dequelabs/axe-core/blob/v4.14.0/README.md) at v4.14.0), which only a person would catch otherwise; the canvas's subtree is `canvas`'s own CI's, and a violation of the package would fail others' pull requests here. A person is needed all the same: "Tools cannot check all accessibility aspects automatically" (sources 8.1).

— *Rejected:*
- WCAG 2.2 AA without AAA — `canvas` could shrink the buttons to 24 px; WCAG 2.1 AA — without 2.4.11, 2.5.7, 2.5.8 and 3.3.8; WCAG 2.2 AAA as a whole — W3C's argument above;
- handwriting as an exception of 2.1.1 — Understanding 2.1.1 says the opposite; a second way to answer before the first child — the slice would wait for a decision of the format, the contract and `learner-model`; a confirmation before each check — it would hinder a child;
- a scale of the page in `app`'s settings, to get the print to 200 % — the canvas's «Крупнее» gives it on every sheet;
- declaring 3.1.2 just in case — the page would name a nonconformance `app` does not have; taking the list from a package's documents unchecked — the element is what ships;
- closing the owner profile without the adult's choice — 2.2.1; without closing at all — a child would stay in the owner profile (П-17);
- the hint `autocomplete="current-password"` on the PIN — the argument above;
- IBM accessibility-checker — under Apache-2.0, but it pulls in puppeteer, chromedriver and telemetry, and takes its rules from a CDN ([`accessibility-checker` 4.0.34](https://registry.npmjs.org/accessibility-checker/4.0.34)); only its own checks — the argument above;
- a font of its own under OFL-1.1 — that license is not in the list of licenses, and the exception is only for tools that do not ship.

— *Does not decide:* a second way to answer — О-25; the accessibility laws by market — П-25, which the author decides by Claude's analysis; a dark theme; a font for children.

— *Consequence:* `src/i18n/en.ts` and `ru.ts`, `accessibility.*`; `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx`; `src/test/a11y.ts`; `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx`, `src/legal/LegalPages.browser.test.tsx`; `licenses.json`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [sources.md](sources.md).

## The app's skeleton

**R14. The skeleton of `app` is built by the framework and the tools chosen for it, with versions as of 2026-11-10: the lock file is built by `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, and `playwright` is 1.65.0, the version of the template's image. The screens live in the hash of the address, by its own code, without a routing library. `vite preview` and the development server do no SPA fallback. The build is checked by `ci-test`, and `NOTICE` names each package that goes into the build.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and the versions are `npm view` of 2026-11-10.
- **The versions** of third-party packages are the latest of those released no later than three days before the installation, as the framework and build decided: `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, and the date applies to the transitive ones too. The lock file has 172 packages, and the latest is `@biomejs/biome` 2.6.6 of 2026-11-06; the dates of all are checked against the registry (`npm view <package> time`, 2026-11-10). Against the table of versions as of 2026-11-02, these went up: `vite` — 8.3.6, `@vitejs/plugin-react` — 6.2.1, `@biomejs/biome` — 2.6.6, `vitest`, `@vitest/browser-playwright` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` — 5.1.0. The packages of `@altnovatrail` come the way `skill-map` installed them: first the third-party ones with `--before`, then `@altnovatrail` without a date — their version is set by the submodule's tag, and the signature is checked by `release-verify-npm`, — and the check of the lock file's dates against the registry, where only they may have later dates.
- **`playwright` is 1.65.0**, as the image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.65.0-noble` from which the stage `toolchain` takes, a `COPY` each, revision 1254 of Chrome Headless Shell, WebKit 2388 and Firefox 1572, with the libraries of the three engines and the fonts of Playwright's list `tools` from the snapshot `20261021T000000Z` (`.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, as `spec/repo-template/kinds/typescript.Dockerfile` has them): without them Playwright will not find the browsers. The stage `toolchain` is built `FROM toolchain-core`, and its apt reads a source of the snapshot alone, by the template of `spec` v0.18.4. WebKit and Firefox are named exceptions of `spec`'s license rule, as Chrome Headless Shell is, and the tag, the digest and the three revisions move together with `playwright` in `package.json`. The default font of the image is WenQuanYi Zen Hei, in Chromium too. Dependabot `npm` keeps `playwright` in `ignore`, and it is raised by a move to a tag of `spec` in which the template raised the image. So the devcontainer has no differences from the template.
- **The routes are in the hash of the address**: `/#/<screen>/<part>/…`, each part percent-encoded; parsing and building are `src/app/hash.ts`, without the DOM, and navigation and the current route for React are `src/app/router.ts`, on `hashchange` and `useSyncExternalStore`, and the same hash gives the same route object. Navigating to the route already shown adds no entry to the history. A hash that the build does not know is the home screen. The address holds only the ID of content, a lesson or a task, and never the ID of a profile, an account or an attempt.
- **The development server and `vite preview` are `appType: "mpa"`**: a path that does not exist gets 404, as on a hosting without rules of its own, not `index.html` ([`appType`](https://github.com/vitejs/vite/blob/v8.3.8/docs/config/shared-options.md#apptype) of Vite v8.3.8). So `/deployment.json` without the file is not replaced by the page either.
- **The build in CI.** The shared `typescript.yml` of `.github` calls five recipes, but not `build`: `ci-lint`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` in the stage `toolchain-core` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, without the browsers, and `ci-test` and `ci-engines` in `toolchain`, with them; its `documents.yml` runs the documents check in the core (both of `.github` at the commit `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins). `ci-engines` comes by the input `engines`, which came with v0.1.29: by it the tests in WebKit and Firefox are the check "TypeScript / engines". So `ci-test` depends on `build`, as in `canvas` and `skill-map`: a build that failed fails "TypeScript / test".
- **`NOTICE`** is the notice of `app`, a line `<name> <version> — <license> — <copyright>` for each package of the lock file without the mark `dev` — that is, for everything the bundler may embed, the transitive too: for `react-dom` that is `scheduler`, — and a line `Text of <license>` with its text for each license of these lines, now MIT. `scripts/notice.ts` in `ci-licenses` checks this, both ways: a missing line, another version, an empty copyright, a line of a package the build does not take, a license without its text, a package without a license in the lock file. The icon set and `@bufbuild/protobuf` go in here with the platform's packages.
- **`ci-licenses`** is also `release-verify-npm`: on a lock file without packages of `@altnovatrail` the recipe answers "no package of @altnovatrail to verify", and the platform's packages need add nothing to CI.
- **CI** — every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/` — `ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml` and `scorecard.yml` — is on the shared workflows of `.github` v0.1.34 by SHA `baa18cde370b3515e85b180d0563cb3eaa4955f2`, the tag's commit by `gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/git/ref/tags/v0.1.34`, read 2026-11-25: each move to a tag of `spec` also takes the latest release of `.github`. In `ci.yml` — the job "TypeScript" with `id-token: write` and `engines`, and a job of its own, Mockup, the comparison with the interface mockup, with `.github`'s action `toolchain` at the same SHA; in `codeql.yml` — the language `javascript-typescript` (Д9 of `spec`); Dependabot — the group `shared-workflows` (Д88 of `spec`), as `.github` tells its callers, and the ecosystem `npm` with the group of `vitest`, `@vitest/*` and `vite`. The cache of the image `toolchain` (Д146 of `spec`) came with v0.1.22.
- **What the releases of `.github` after v0.1.22 changed** in the shared workflows `app` calls asks nothing more of the calls than the input `engines` (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/compare/v0.1.22...v0.1.24` and `git diff v0.1.24 v0.1.30`, read 2026-11-16): v0.1.23 and v0.1.24 changed only comments; in v0.1.26 `typescript.yml` and `documents.yml` take one cache entry of the image `novatrail-toolchain`, which the job "TypeScript / toolchain" alone builds on a miss for the jobs after it, and only a push to `main` saves; in v0.1.28 `release.yml` signs and checks the files of a release at once; in v0.1.30 `codeql.yml` takes Go from go.dev's archive, for the language `go` alone, which `app` does not name; v0.1.31 brought the stage `toolchain-core`, with the same inputs and permissions; v0.1.32 changed none of them.

— *Why:*
- The hash works on any hosting without a rule "any path → `index.html`": one build both at the platform and at a self-hosting copy, whose hosting `spec` does not know. The browser does not send the fragment after `#` to the server — neither in the request nor in `Referer` (sources 2.2), — so the hosting's logs do not learn which screen the learner is on. The browser's "Back" goes through the screens. The own code is two small functions and a hook: a library would bring into what ships its own code, its own license in `NOTICE` and its own releases.
- `playwright` must match the image: another version expects another revision of the browser — 1.65.0 names revision 1254 of Chrome Headless Shell, 1.64.1 named 1248 (`browsers.json` of [v1.65.0](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/v1.65.0/packages/playwright-core/browsers.json) and [v1.64.1](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/v1.64.1/packages/playwright-core/browsers.json)), — and its Dependabot pull request would be red until the image is raised by hand.
- MIT requires that "the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software" (sources 2.2), and the build embeds `react`, `react-dom` and `scheduler` whole.

— *Rejected:*
- History API paths with its own code or `wouter`: each hosting needs a redirect rule, a row «в реестр spec» for `infra` and the self-hosting copies, and the screen's path is in the hosting's logs; no addresses at all — "Back" would lead out of the app, and a reload to the start; `react-router` 7.18.5 and `@tanstack/react-router` 1.170.46 — libraries of 4.8 and 1.1 MB for a few screens ([`react-router` 7.18.5](https://registry.npmjs.org/react-router/7.18.5) and [`@tanstack/react-router` 1.170.46](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tanstack/react-router/1.170.46), `dist.unpackedSize`);
- `playwright` ahead of the template's image — an image of its own and another revision in the Dockerfile, a difference from the template and a row «в реестр spec»;
- `NOTICE` only by `dependencies`: the transitive `scheduler` would go into the build without a notice;
- `build` as a job of its own in `ci.yml`: one more check in the ruleset, and `ci-test` builds before the tests anyway.

— *Consequence:* `package.json` and its lock file; `NOTICE`; `scripts/notice.ts`; `src/app/hash.ts`, `src/app/router.ts`; `vite.config.ts`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.claude/rules/code.md`; `.github/workflows/`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The platform's packages

**R15. `app` takes the platform's packages from npm at exact versions, which `package.json` holds: `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map`; `@altnovatrail/contracts` and its own copy of `@altnovatrail/spec`, both at the tag of the `spec` submodule; `@bufbuild/protobuf` at the version the contracts name; connect-es with the Connect transport for the browser; and Firebase Auth by the packages `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth`, those that `firebase` pins, not `firebase` itself. The canvas and the skill map may each keep a nested copy of `@altnovatrail/spec`, which type checks hold to `app`'s. The versions that another package or the submodule decides are held by a test, and a working copy of `canvas` comes to the development server by a recipe, without touching `package.json` and the lock file.**
- **Installation** — the third-party packages by the skeleton's cutoff date, `npm install --before`; the packages of `@altnovatrail` by `npm install --save-exact` without a date, the latest released when taken. `release-verify-npm` in `ci-licenses` checks every package of `@altnovatrail` of the lock file, the nested copies too, on each pull request, in the job "TypeScript / licenses": no job of its own is needed.
- **The platform's types and the contracts.** `@altnovatrail/spec` is in `devDependencies`, by a copy of its own, and `@altnovatrail/contracts` in `dependencies`; both are at the version of the submodule's tag, and the move to a tag raises them with `spec_tag` of the `justfile`, the release file of `parts/` and the line of the contracts in `NOTICE`. Dependabot `npm` keeps both in `ignore`. The contracts' code is imported by the path of its code file, and `@bufbuild/protobuf` is the version that the contracts name in `dependencies`, so that their code and the client share one runtime of protobuf. The client is `@connectrpc/connect` and `@connectrpc/connect-web`, `createConnectTransport`: `api` speaks Connect, and gRPC-Web is not needed.
- **The canvas and the skill map** are taken on one icon set of Material Symbols, which both name in `devDependencies`: `canvas`, `skill-map` and `app` raise the set together, only by a row of `spec`'s cross-repository plan, and Dependabot keeps it in `ignore`. Each names its own `@altnovatrail/spec` in `dependencies`; where that is not `app`'s version, the lock file holds it as a nested copy, types alone, which put nothing into the build. Two versions of one form are one type, and a form that drifted is a compiler error where `app` meets the package: `ElementHoldsContract` in `src/canvas/jsx.ts` holds the class of the canvas element to `CanvasElement` of `app`'s copy, member by member and both ways — `exportPicture()`, the picture of a work stored on the server, among them —, and `ElementTakesViews` in `src/skills/jsx.ts` holds the skill map's data so. A `.d.ts` file would keep them silent: `skipLibCheck` skips its errors. A trial test in Chromium holds the element's registration, its properties, the attribute `lang`, the event in lower case and a type error on a tool the contract does not know.
- **Firebase Auth** — `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth`, those that `firebase` pins, in one Dependabot group. `firebase` itself brings about 110 packages into what ships, and its `@firebase/firestore` holds `@grpc/grpc-js` ~1.9.0 ([`@firebase/firestore` 4.19.1](https://registry.npmjs.org/@firebase/firestore/4.19.1)), whose GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j (sources 2.3) fails `ci-vuln` though `app` does not import `firestore`. The modular API is the same: `firebase/auth` is `export * from '@firebase/auth'`, and `firebase/app` adds only `registerVersion("firebase", …)` ([`auth`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/firebase%4013.3.0/packages/firebase/auth/index.ts) and [`app`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/firebase%4013.3.0/packages/firebase/app/index.ts) of `firebase` 13.3.0).
- **The versions that another package or the submodule decides** are held by `scripts/pins.ts`, which a test in `ci-test` runs: `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are the version of `spec_tag` of the `justfile`; the icon set is the one in the `devDependencies` of the installed `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` (a package without the set is skipped); `@bufbuild/protobuf` is the one in the `dependencies` of `@altnovatrail/contracts`; each package of `@firebase` is in the lock file once, since `@firebase/auth` registers itself in the component container of `@firebase/app`; the lock file has no links to folders. Dependabot `npm` keeps `@bufbuild/protobuf` and the set in `ignore`, and `@connectrpc/*` and `@firebase/*` in groups of their own.
- **A working copy of `canvas`** is the recipe `just dev-canvas <folder>`: it sets `NOVATRAIL_CANVAS`, and only on the development server does `vite.config.ts` point each entry point that the copy's `package.json` exports to its built files in `lib/` and let the server into the folder. The copy is built by itself, `just build` in it. `package.json` and the lock file do not change, and the build and the tests do not read the variable: only the package that `release-verify-npm` checked goes into what ships and into CI.
- **`NOTICE`** is as for the skeleton — a line for each package of the lock file without the mark `dev`, and a line `Text of <license>` for each license of these lines —, and also: a line's license is the SPDX expression as it is, `(Apache-2.0 AND BSD-3-Clause)` for `@bufbuild/protobuf`, and each of its identifiers needs a line `Text of`; for a package that has a `NOTICE` of its own, its text goes as it is as a section `Notice of <name> <version>:` — so Apache License 2.0, clause 4(d), tells whoever distributes the work (sources 2.3) —, and a package without such a file has no section. The text of Apache-2.0 is the file `LICENSE` beside it, the same as that of `app`. The copyright of packages without a license file — `@bufbuild/protobuf`, connect-es and `@firebase/*` — is by the headers of their code. `NOTICE` names every package that the lock file does not mark `dev`: a nested copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` has its line, `app`'s own has none. `ci-licenses` installs the packages itself: the packages' `NOTICE` is read from `node_modules`.

— *Why:* Firebase by the packages `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth`, since `ci-vuln` failed on `firebase`. One icon set and the types of the agreement held to `app`'s copy keep the interface one and the meeting of `app` and a package checked by the compiler.

— *Rejected:*
- `firebase` — for the reason above; it with `overrides` of `@grpc/grpc-js` 1.14.5 — a version outside the range that `firestore` names, and about 110 packages whose next finding in Node code would fail `ci-vuln` again;
- `app`'s copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` at the version the canvas names, as the README of `canvas` tells: `spec` keeps it at the submodule's tag, and rejects `peerDependencies`;
- a second runtime of protobuf beside the one the contracts' code is built for, by the skeleton's cutoff date;
- a canvas whose icon set differs from the skill map's: two sets in one interface;
- the picture's type from the canvas's own `NovatrailCanvas` beside `ElementHoldsContract`: a change of `exportPicture()` in `canvas` would reach `app` unchecked by the agreement;
- `npm link` with the folder of `canvas` — it edits `package.json` and the lock file, and `release-verify-npm` lets a link through without a check; `resolve.alias` in a configuration file of its own outside `main` — a second file that is easy to forget to remove.

— *Consequence:* `package.json`; `package-lock.json`; `NOTICE`; `scripts/pins.ts`; `src/canvas/jsx.ts`; `src/skills/jsx.ts`; `src/works/picture.ts`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Storage on the device: the schema and the code

**R16. `app` keeps the data on the device in four kinds of IndexedDB databases — the device's settings, the registry, the shared cache and the database of each profile — behind a narrow interface of IndexedDB operations, for which an in-memory store stands in Node. The registry names a database before it is opened, and remembers an erasure until the browser has confirmed it; the storage's start repeats what was left unfinished, checks the sign-in project and keeps the 30-day limit.** It decides what the design of storage on the device left to the code — the names of the databases and stores and the schema version. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and the SDK's store is [`IndexedDBLocalPersistence`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/auth%401.13.10/packages/auth/src/platform_browser/persistence/indexed_db.ts) at the tag of the version `package.json` pins.
- **Databases and stores.** All stores have keys outside the records, and an index reads one field of a record. The names have the prefix `novatrail-`, so that among the origin's databases they are seen beside the database of Firebase Auth.

  | Database | Store | Key | Record | Indexes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `novatrail-settings` | `settings` | the setting's name | `language` — the tag of the device's language | — |
  | `novatrail-registry` | `registry` | `"account"`, `"signIn"`, `"voicing"`, `"ending"` | `{ id }`; `{ projectId, apiKey }`; the device's voicing — the permission of voicing on Android and Linux and the voice chosen for a language; `true` while an erasure of the account — "Erase everything on this device", the account gone — waits for a database that could not be erased | — |
  | | `databases` | the database's name | `{ kind, profileId?, erase }`, where `kind` is `profile` or `cache` | — |
  | `novatrail-cache` | `views` | `[digest, view]`, the view being `manifest`, `skill-graph`, `techniques` or `solving-steps` | `{ json?, version?, skillTiers?, usedAt }` — the JSON bytes as `api` gave them, the format version, and for the skill graph the tiers; a part the module does not have is a record without `json`, so as not to ask again | `usedAt` |
  | `novatrail-profile-<profile ID>` | `attempts` | attempt ID | `{ attemptId, digest, task?, steps?, changedAt, stored? }` | `digest`, `changedAt` |
  | | `history` | `[attempt ID, n]` | an event of the writing history, as the canvas gave it | — |
  | | `submissions` | `[attempt ID, n]` | `{ context, historyLength, work }`, an essay's without `work` | — |
  | | `correctWork` | attempt ID | the work of the correct submission, until the correct answer words | — |
  | | `lessons` | lesson ID | `{ lessonId, digest, passageId, view, place, demo, changedAt }` | `digest`, `changedAt` |
- **The schema version** is 1 for the settings, the registry and the cache; the profile database's is `PROFILE_SCHEMA` in `src/storage/schema.ts`, raised by its stores beyond the table — `ownKey`, the adult's own key kept on this device, and those of a page from paper — and by taking off the store `essays` of versions 3 and 4, with every essay in it. Opening at a higher version brings the stores and indexes to the schema and removes the extra ones, the work in the others kept, and a migration of data, if one is needed, comes with that version; a record that changes while the stores, keys and indexes stay keeps the version. A view of a format version outside the build's window does not go into the cache. A build that meets a database newer than its own offers to reload, as after a rollout: a newer build opened this database.
- **The pictures of the work** — the PNG of the sheet's crop and the crops of the workbook's boxes — lie as bytes, `{ bytes, type }`, an `ArrayBuffer` and its type, in `submissions` and `correctWork`: WebKit in an ephemeral session writes no `Blob` into IndexedDB — `put` fails with `UnknownError: Error preparing Blob/File data to be stored in object store` (measurements 2). `app` reads the bytes from the canvas's `Blob` before the transaction, and gives a `Blob` on reading. A `Blob` record written at schema version 1 is read as it is: a picture is bytes or a `Blob`, and a record of another kind gives "no work", stays in place and goes by the common rules — the attempt's end, the time limit or an erasure. An attempt's submissions are read by a transaction of their own, after the attempt and the history, so that a record the browser will not give back takes neither with it — the task screen would start the attempt anew and erase the history; the work of the correct submission is read before the transaction that erases the attempt, not in it, or such a record would break the erasure too.
- **The mark of the server.** `stored: { history, submissions }` of a record of `attempts` is how long a history and how many submissions the last `PutWork` that went through carried; a record without it is work the server keeps nothing of. `replaceHistory` takes it off, since a history of the same length may be another one; switching the storage of works on the server off, or `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF`, takes the marks off. The time limit also erases every history whose attempt the device keeps no more, as an essay ended under versions 3 and 4 left it in `history`.
- **The registry.** The record in `databases` goes before the database's first opening, and a profile database is opened only if the registry names it and it is not marked for erasure. An erasure sets `erase: true`, then calls `deleteDatabase`, and removes the row only on success. An erasure of the account erases the registry last, when everything else is erased; otherwise the registry stays with `"ending"`, and the next start finishes the job. The sign-out leaves all of it.
- **A lesson** lies under one lesson ID, and the digest and the run ID are in the record. A read with another digest or another run erases it: that is the same lesson on another package or another run under the same key.
- **The interface** is the IndexedDB operations that `app` takes: open with creation or without, delete a database with the answer `"deleted"`, `"blocked"` or `"failed"`, read and write transactions with `get`, `entries` by keys or by an index, `count`, `put` and `delete`. The tests in Node run the real rules of erasure, and only the engine is replaced — an in-memory store with the key order of IndexedDB, cloning of records and the rollback of a failed transaction. CI checks the real IndexedDB in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, and the reading of a `Blob` record in Chromium and Firefox alone: a context of Playwright is an ephemeral session, where WebKit writes no `Blob` (measurements 2 and 4). Each connection closes on `versionchange`; opening without creation does not create a database — in Chromium an aborted upgrade of a new database does not leave it behind; `blocked` is an answer at once, without waiting, and IndexedDB does not cancel the deletion itself: it will go through when the other connection closes. What CI does not check of IndexedDB — `close` when the browser clears the site's data, Safari on iPad — is Д13.
- **Firebase Auth** keeps the database `firebaseLocalStorageDb` and does not close its connection on `versionchange` — only on `pagehide` ([`IndexedDBLocalPersistence`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/auth%401.13.10/packages/auth/src/platform_browser/persistence/indexed_db.ts) at the tag of the version `package.json` pins; `src/storage/idb.browser.test.ts` checks `blocked` on such a connection). So erasing this database on another sign-in project, while another tab holds the former one, is `"blocked"`: the database is not erased yet — the deletion will go through when that tab closes, — and `app` counts the erasure as not done: it does not clear the web storage, the registry keeps the former project, the next start checks again, and the Firebase sign-in in this tab is not set up. Neither the sign-out nor "Erase everything on this device" erases the database of Firebase Auth: `signOut()`, which comes first in both, removes the session record (sources 3.1).
- **The storage's start** is after `/deployment.json` is read and before the app, one per tab: a repeat of the erasures from the registry — and of the registry's own, while an erasure of the account waits for one —, the check of the sign-in project, the work of correct submissions whose words were not waited for, and the time limit — attempts and lessons that did not change for 30 days, and the cache that was not accessed for 30 days, for each database separately. A step that failed holds neither the others nor the app. No registry — the start creates nothing; the registry could not be read — there is no check, and the start says just that, `"failed"`.
- **A connection taken away without `app`.** The browser closed a connection — `app` opens the registry again, without creation: no registry that the tab saw — the screen "The data on this device was erased"; the registry is there — "The data on this device can't be read", and nothing is erased. A `versionchange` came — `app` opens the same database again, without creation: another tab opened it at a new version — an offer to reload; erased it — everything erased, a deleted profile — nothing. Both screens have "Reload", and after the screen the tab sends nothing more to the storage. Its own registry, erased by this same tab, is no sign that the browser erased the data.

— *Why:* an interface at the level of operations, not of erasure events, keeps the rules of storage on the device in one code, which both the tests in Node and the tests in the browsers check. The registry before the opening and the mark before the deletion keep a database from staying without a row, and a tab from creating anew an empty database that another erased. A lesson's key without the digest holds no more than one state per lesson, as the design of the lesson player tells. Bytes, not a `Blob`: otherwise WebKit in an ephemeral session saves no work.

— *Rejected:*
- `fake-indexeddb` in Node: a new `dev` dependency, and the design of storage already tells to substitute an in-memory store, and the browsers check the real IndexedDB;
- a fake at the level of erasure events: the tests would check the fake, not the rules;
- random names for profile databases: the registry would link them to the profile ID anyway, and a name by the ID is checked against the profile list of `api` without an extra table;
- the writing history as one record per attempt: each stroke would rewrite it whole, up to a few MB;
- a migration of `Blob` records to bytes on opening the database: it needs a new version, and an upgrade transaction does not live through a read of a `Blob`, which is no IndexedDB request — the migration would be a pass of its own at the start, erasing or leaving a record whose `Blob` cannot be read: the same reading of two kinds, with a new version and an offer to reload in the tabs of the former build; such records live no longer than the attempt or 30 days, and an ephemeral session of WebKit has none;
- erasing `firebaseLocalStorageDb` on the account's erasure: the database is held by the SDK's own connection, and `signOut()` removes the session anyway;
- waiting for `deleteDatabase` after `blocked`: the start would wait until the other tab is closed.

— *Consequence:* `src/storage/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Dictionaries, the theme and the accessibility checks

**R17. The strings of the screens of `app` come from the TypeScript dictionaries `en` and `ru`, which `tsc` checks; the device's language is the adult's choice in the device's settings, by a pill and its pop-over on the sign-in and by buttons in the section for adults; the theme is the interface mockup's, the style its README gives, with the replacements the README names where a colour misses its contrast; in CI each screen passes `axe-core` by WCAG 2.2 A and AA, targets of 44 × 44 CSS px and layout at 320 CSS px, in three languages and in two browser modes.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and the sources in sources 8.1.
- **The dictionaries** are `src/i18n/en.ts`, the source, and `src/i18n/ru.ts`, `as const satisfies Translation<typeof en, "ru">`: the same keys and not one other, a string where there is a string, and the plural forms of its own language — `one`, `few`, `many` and `other` — where there are forms. The placeholders are checked by the type `SamePlaces` by the string literals, and a Russian string with another placeholder name is an error of `tsc`. The words of the failure screens are there too, and the string for whoever deployed a copy stays in English outside the dictionaries. A code from `api` that the build does not know gets the common string `common.unknown`. Both are outside SonarCloud's duplication search, `sonar.cpd.exclusions` of `.sonarcloud.properties`: the Russian repeats the English keys by design, which `tsc` checks, and their strings alone failed SonarCloud's threshold of 3 % in new code; it still searches the rest.
- **The device's language.** `Boot` reads the choice from the device's settings, `novatrail-settings`, together with the request of `/deployment.json` and draws the first screen when it has read it, but waits no longer than a second: a hung opening of IndexedDB will not leave the page empty. Not read — `navigator.languages`; a tag that the build does not know — lookup by truncation. In a frame the settings are not read. Each language is named by its own name in that language, "English" and «Русский», with its own `lang`, the chosen one `aria-pressed` and told not by colour alone, which forced colours take away. On the sign-in the choice is `src/i18n/LanguagePill.tsx`: a pop-over, `PopOver`, whose button shows the language icon, the chosen language's name and a chevron, named "Language: English", and opens the caption "Language" over a list of buttons, the chosen one tinted and ticked. In the section for adults it is `LanguageButtons`, buttons in a `fieldset` with the legend "Language", the chosen one underlined. A tap changes the screen's language at once — the pill's closes its pop-over too —, writes it into the settings and, if it was written, tells the other tabs through the tab's one persistent `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-settings`, which does not hear its own messages.
- **The theme** is `src/theme/theme.css`, the mockup's roles and colours, each role in a comment, its tokens in the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md); the font is the system one. "Not yet" and an error are one colour, as in the mockup; a tooltip is opaque. Where the mockup's colour misses its contrast, the replacement is the token: `#0071e3` as text misses on the page, 4.21:1, and on its tint, so text takes `accent-text`; white on `#248a3d` misses, 4.40:1, so "Correct" is darker. A second button is white with the accent's text, not the tint, which holds on a card alone, 4.38:1 on the page; a field is white with its edge, 3.26:1 against its fill. The way in's card on the sky takes the `--nt-sky-*` tokens in place of the generic ones, "Who is learning?" the `--nt-who-*` ones, and Google's button keeps Google's colours.
- **The pairs.** `src/theme/contrast.node.test.ts` reads the tokens from `theme.css` itself and checks each foreground on the layers it stands on — `contrast.ts` lays a translucent colour over the opaque one under it —: text 4.5:1, an edge, the focus ring and a mark 3:1. It fails on a colour token no pair names but a disabled button's — `disabled` on `grey-16`, `sky-off` on `sky-off-fill` on the way in's card, and `who-off`, "Add a learner" while it is closed —, which 1.4.3 exempts as an inactive control. Text over the sky of "Who is learning?" stands on `--nt-who-under-*`, the sky where it is brightest under it by the mockup's README, tokens no rule paints.
- **The rest of the theme.** A button is the mockup's pill — 44 high, radius 22, the accent's fill, white text of 600 —, and `.nt-main` its primary button, 52 high, radius 14, 16 px; a border of 1 px without a colour keeps a button's edge in forced colours, and a second button, an unchosen one and a segment's chip, white, take the accent's edge. The chosen one and the segment shown are told by an underline. A disabled one is `disabled` on `grey-16`, the last rule over every kind of button, and `GrayText` in forced colours, `aria-disabled` too. The body is 14 px, a field's text 16 px, under which Safari on iOS zooms the page on focus, and a `summary` a target of 44; bold is 600, and a heading 700, as the mockup's template draws every heading, though its README names 400–600 (В71). `.nt-glass` is the mockup's glass with `backdrop-filter`, `.nt-glass-panel` the tutor's 0.86. Each transition is under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, 0.15 s on `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`; `axeViolations` waits for every finite animation under way to end, since a button just enabled would be read halfway.
- **The packages' theme.** The canvas takes the text, the card, the tutor's words for its marks, and `--novatrail-canvas-accent` and `--novatrail-canvas-glass` — the tool in use the accent at 12 % with the accent's icon, the bar's fill the glass of 0.76 —, and `--novatrail-canvas-top: 0px`: the band in whose middle the bar stands starts below a sticky header, and `app` has none, while the mockup centres the bar in the whole screen; `src/theme/theme.browser.test.tsx` holds the three on the element itself. The skill map takes the card, the text and the accent.
- **The screen** is `src/app/Screen.tsx`: `<main lang>`, the heading `h1` and the document title in the screen's language — "{screen} — NovaTrail", and for a screen whose heading is "NovaTrail" itself, just "NovaTrail", — and the focus on `h1` when the screen replaced another, with no ring: the heading is read, not pressed, and Chrome rings a focus set by script after a key. Which screen is the first is decided by the screen instance's own token, not by a counter: under StrictMode a screen mounted twice is the same one.
- **The checks in CI**: `axe-core` 4.14.0 — in `devDependencies`, by the exception MPL-2.0 `dev` in `licenses.json` — by the rules with the tags of WCAG 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2 of levels A and AA, outside the subtree of the canvas element; targets — no smaller than 44 × 44 CSS px; at a width of 320 CSS px — no sideways scrolling and no content wider than its element; in `en`, `ru` and a pseudo-language; the checks by a person stay open, Д10. The Vitest projects `forced colors` and `reduced motion` — files of their own and a mode of the browser context from the very start, `contextOptions` of the Playwright provider, as in `canvas` ([`vitest.config.ts`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/canvas/blob/v0.3.43/vitest.config.ts) at v0.3.43).

— *Why:* `spec` makes the mockup the target of the look, and where its colour misses WCAG's contrast the documents win, so the replacement is a token the pairs hold. A dictionary type by string literals catches a missing translation and placeholder at `ci-lint`, not on the learner's screen; an edit of the code would not remove the dictionaries' duplication, which the form of the dictionaries makes. The mockup draws the sign-in's language as a pill (`signin-language`), and buttons in a pop-over keep a choice that changes nothing until it is tapped (WCAG 3.2.2); the section for adults has room for the buttons in its card. The check only by WCAG 2.2 A and AA is the standard `app` meets: the best-practice rules would fail the test on what the standard does not require.

— *Rejected:*
- a translation library — i18next, FormatJS: it would bring its own parsing of messages into the build, and the own code on `Intl` is a few functions;
- colours in TypeScript with generated CSS — a second source of colours; the test reads `theme.css` itself;
- a `<select>` list of languages — WCAG 3.2.2;
- the language buttons on the sign-in, as in the section — the mockup's pill, on a card that holds the sign-in alone;
- `axe-core` with all rules, best-practice too, — the argument above;
- the names of languages from `Intl.DisplayNames` — it writes «русский» in lower case;
- a theme of `app`'s own — the blue `#1d4ed8`, teal `#0f766e` or violet `#6d28d9` —: the mockup is the target;
- the tint of the accent under a second button on the page — 4.38:1; a red error beside "Not yet" — the mockup has one colour for both; headings at 600 by the README — the comparison with the mockup holds the weight the template draws;
- a rule of `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` with `!important`, as the template has — every motion of `app` is under the other query already, and Biome flags `!important`; the mockup's font stack — the system font stays, and the comparison draws both by FreeSans.

— *Consequence:* `src/i18n/en.ts`; `src/i18n/ru.ts`; `src/i18n/LanguageButtons.tsx`; `src/i18n/LanguagePill.tsx`; `src/i18n/LanguageContext.tsx`; `src/app/Screen.tsx`; `src/theme/theme.css`; `src/theme/contrast.ts`; `src/theme/contrast.node.test.ts`; `src/theme/theme.browser.test.tsx`; `src/theme/motion.browser.test.tsx`; `src/test/a11y.ts`; `.sonarcloud.properties`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The contract client

**R18. `app` calls `api` by its own thin layer: connect-es clients of the services of `novatrail.api.v1` behind three interceptors of the contract's rules, a failure of a call by one type, and `app` writes words only for those outcomes and refusals that any screen shows the same way.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The transport** is `createConnectTransport` of connect-web, Connect and binary protobuf, the call's deadline is 30 s, the limit of `api` itself ([`api`'s architecture](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/852f898af/docs/architecture.md) at commit 852f898af), and 90 s for `FindTasks` and `SubmitWork`, `api`'s limit of them. A refusal of `fetch` — a network failure — becomes `unavailable`: connect-es reads it as `unknown` ([`ConnectError.from`](https://github.com/connectrpc/connect-es/blob/v2.3.0/packages/connect/src/connect-error.ts) at the tag of the version `package.json` pins). The open views go by a Connect GET transport of their own, without a deadline. The eighth client, `Api.work` of `WorkService`, is for `PutWork`, the storage of works on the server.
- **The interceptors**, from the outside in:
  - the device's actions: `CAUSE_ACCOUNT_GONE` — sign-out and then the erasure of the whole account, once even with two refusals at once, `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS` — the erasure of what is left of the account that the registry names, `unimplemented` — an offer to reload, but for the methods an `api` may lack, `MAY_BE_ABSENT` of `src/api/interceptors.ts`, among them `PutWork`: an `api` without `WorkService` keeps no work, and the build is no older than it; a failure of these actions does not replace the refusal: the screen learns its cause;
