  - sign-in: the ID token in `Authorization` for every method except `GetManifest`, `GetSkillGraph`, `GetTechniques`, `GetSolvingSteps` and `GetLegalTexts`; `unauthenticated` without a `Refusal` — one token refresh and one retry, and a refusal with a cause gets no refresh: the cause is read first;
  - retry: `unavailable` without a cause, `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN` and a network failure — after pauses of 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 s, with the same message, and so with the same idempotency key; `CAUSE_STILL_GOING`, known by its cause whatever its code — `aborted`, and `unavailable` of an `api` before —, waits for the first request up to 30 s, or 90 s for `FindTasks` and `SubmitWork` — as long as `api` lets it run, — with the pause from `Retry-After`, otherwise, after the last pause, 4 s each, and past the wait it is "No connection". A call that its deadline or its caller cut off is not retried.

  `app` has no headers of its own, and connect-web sends only the allowed ones ([`request-header.ts`](https://github.com/connectrpc/connect-es/blob/v2.3.0/packages/connect/src/protocol-connect/request-header.ts) and [`connect-transport.ts`](https://github.com/connectrpc/connect-es/blob/v2.3.0/packages/connect-web/src/connect-transport.ts) at the tag of the version `package.json` pins) — a test holds this.
- **A failure** is an `ApiError` with the field `failure`: `refused` with the cause, its data and its code, `offline`, `sign-in`, `fresh-sign-in` with the allowed age of the sign-in, `account-gone`, `no-access`, `stale`, `invalid`, `forbidden` and `unknown` with the code. A cause that the build does not know, the client reads by the error code with any code — and with `unauthenticated` it refreshes the token, as without a cause, — and an unfamiliar outcome as an AI failure. A token that cannot be got is none: the request goes without it, and then comes the refresh.
- **Keys and clocks.** The idempotency key is `crypto.randomUUID()` per action; the screen holds it until the action is done. With a request to the platform AI goes the clock offset (`clock_offset_minutes` of `spec/contracts/novatrail/api/v1/attempt.proto`), and with the own key — the key and not the offset.
- **The words** are common to all screens: an AI failure and the platform AI's limit — one neutral phrase, the quota — with the day when it comes back, "today", "tomorrow" or a date, by the device's calendar, and the time when the next family day begins, in the screen's language, AI not admitted, the limit and the refusal of the own key, no consent, a version withdrawn, "This isn't there any more", an attempt closed, no access, "Sign in again" and "Sign in again to do this". The block "Tutor" names the quota and the platform AI's limit both under a rung's fallback text and after a correct answer without words. The causes of their own screens — PIN, invite code, country, the lesson's segments, "Explain differently" — the tasks of those screens supply with words, and until then it is the common string `common.unknown`.
- **The screens** take the client through `useApi()`, and a read through `useRequest()`: loading, an answer or a failure, and `retry()`. An action with a key the screen calls itself, on a tap. `Boot` makes the client once per `api` of the file, and takes the tokens and `fetch` from its properties on each call: new objects of them do not make a new client and do not repeat requests. Each method of the client is the same function on every read, and `useRequest` does not show the answer of a former call as the answer of a new one.

— *Why:* the contract's rules — the cause before the token refresh, one key for all retries, retrying "for a short while" — are its own, and a server-state library does not know them, and the cache of open views lies in IndexedDB by digest anyway. The words of a cause depend on the screen on which it comes, and writing them before the screen means writing them twice.

— *Rejected:*
- TanStack Query — a new dependency in what ships and in `NOTICE`, whose retries and cache would have to be turned off or made to obey the contract's rules;
- gRPC-Web — `api` speaks Connect; JSON on the wire — connect-es reads an unfamiliar enum value in JSON as empty, as `spec`'s contracts say;
- a retry without a limit — the contract's rules retry "for a short while";
- the words of all 38 causes now ([`cause.proto`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/spec/blob/v0.13.0/contracts/novatrail/api/v1/cause.proto) at the tag v0.13.0 of `spec`) — the argument above.

— *Consequence:* `src/api/`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Sign-in, the account and the network of the local run

**R19. `app` signs in by the Firebase Auth window behind the narrow interface of the sign-in service, which `Boot` sets up only after the sign-in project is checked against the registry. `app` reads the account by `GetAccount` at the start and after every sign-in, and by its answer erases on the device everything that does not belong to this account before anything opens; with no account, an invite code, then the statement "I am 18" with the acceptance of the texts; with no owner profile, a profile of the category `adult` with a pseudonym and a country; and signing out leaves the sign-in service alone and erases nothing. The container of `app` is also on the Docker network `novatrail-api`, and the Vite proxy reaches the local `api` by its container's name.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The sign-in service** is `src/signin/service.ts`: who is signed in — the user's ID at the sign-in service, `null` or "not read yet" —, the sign-in window, signing out, the client's tokens and the window's language. In the browser `firebase.ts` gives it: `initializeApp` with the file's parameters, explicitly, `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alone and `browserPopupRedirectResolver`, `signInWithPopup` with the Google provider and `languageCode`, the screen's language at each change of it. The window's error codes ([`errors.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/auth%401.13.10/packages/auth/src/core/errors.ts) of `@firebase/auth` at the tag of the version `package.json` pins): `popup-closed-by-user`, `cancelled-popup-request` and `user-cancelled` — the window was closed, with no line; `popup-blocked` — the browser did not open the window; `network-request-failed` — no connection; `unauthorized-domain`, `invalid-api-key` and `operation-not-allowed` — the project does not accept this copy, with a line for whoever deployed it; the rest — "The sign-in didn't go through". The local build signs in by `api`'s development token, with no window, when `just dev-app` gives one, and otherwise by the window.
- **The order of the start.** `Boot` makes the service once per page, and only when the storage's check of the sign-in project gives `same` or `erased`: `initializeAuth` would read the previous project's session as this one's. The outcome `blocked` is the screen "The app is open in another tab with the previous sign-in", `failed` is "The data on this device can't be read", and the sign-in is not set up in this tab. `Boot`'s property `signIn` is a factory of the service, in the tests one of their own.
- **The session** is `src/account/session.ts`, without the DOM. Once there is a sign-in, the registry remembers `projectId` and `apiKey`, then `GetAccount`:
  - an account — `accountSeen`: the registry's previous account is erased, and so is everything the registry still holds for erasure, and if another tab holds the database, the account does not open: the screen "Another account's data on this device can't be erased yet" and "Try again"; then `profilesListed`: the databases of profiles not in the list are erased too, but the app does not hold their `blocked`: the next start erases them; then the owner profile or its creation;
  - "no access" — the invite code; what was left of the registry's account, the client erased;
  - no connection — nothing is erased, and "Try again" asks again;
  - a sign-in that cannot be refreshed, and an account that does not exist — the sign-in screen;
  - another UID — everything anew, and the answer for the previous one is dropped, as is the answer of a session already stopped. A window that signed in as the same user asks for the account itself, and one that signed in as a new user waits until the service tells of it: one `GetAccount` per sign-in.

  The checks of packages, `CheckPackages`, begin once there is an account and end with it: before the sign-in `app` has no token.
- **The account and the profile.** The invite code is two steps: the code, then the statement "I am 18 or older" with the acceptance of `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use`, whose texts the screen shows; "Create account" sends them together. `CreateAccount` sends the code as typed, without spaces at the edges, `adultStated` only when the box is ticked — without the box there is no request —, and the versions of the texts the owner accepted. `CAUSE_CODE_NOT_ACCEPTED` is a line at the field, `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` is `GetAccount` again. `CreateOwnerProfile` sends a pseudonym of 1 to 30 Unicode characters after trimming the edges, which is checked before the request, an avatar of the set of `app`, optional, and a country, chosen in a pop-over, from a list of 249 ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes (sources 7.5), with the names of `Intl.DisplayNames` in the screen's language. Which countries the deployment does not accept, `api` decides: `CAUSE_COUNTRY_NOT_ACCEPTED` is a line at the country field, and `app` has no list of closed countries of its own.
- **Choosing the learner**: the owner profile, without a PIN: there are no children's profiles in E2. The chosen one is in memory alone; "Switch learner" goes back to the choice; Home opens under it (`useLearner()`). At the account's first sign-in on this device it asks the browser to keep the site's data, `persist()`, with no line of its own.
- **Signing out** is one press of "Sign out", with no screen: the sign-in service's `signOut()` alone. Everything of the account stays on the device for the same account's next sign-in; a sign-in of another account erases it. A failed `signOut()` leaves the session where it was, unsaid. "Erase everything on this device", "account gone" and the deletion of the account sign out first, then erase everything of the account; if the service did not sign out, nothing is erased — the same user would open the account again without its data. While the erasure runs, a new sign-in starts nothing. What the browser did not erase, the next start erases, and after "Erase everything on this device" the sign-in screen says so in a line. "Account gone" erases each time it comes, and two refusals at once erase once.
- **The words**: those of the screens of the sign-in, the invite code, the profile and the choice, and the button "Sign out"; and the choice of the language on the sign-in screen. The glossary is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The network of the local run.** `api` in its devcontainer is on the network `novatrail-api`, publishes no port outside and listens on all interfaces ([`server.go`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/02e22fb/internal/server/server.go), [`devcontainer.json`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/02e22fb/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json) and [README](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/02e22fb/README.md) of `api` at commit 02e22fb). The container of `app` is on two networks, `novatrail-app` and `novatrail-api`, the second `--network` in `runArgs` (sources 7.5), with `--network-alias=app` (Д195 `spec`); `host-network.sh` sets up both with one MTU, so `app` starts without `api` too. `api`'s container has the name `api` (Д31 of `api`), and the proxy goes to `http://api:8080` by default, `NOVATRAIL_API` naming another address (Д21). On loopback `api` is at the origin of `app` itself, behind the proxy, as the part `deployment/` of `spec` has it (Д177 of `spec`) — the answer to В46. `API_APP_ORIGIN` of `api` is the origin of `app` on loopback, `http://127.0.0.1:5173`, as README of `api` has it: behind the proxy the requests need no CORS, but the variable rejects nothing (Д237 `spec`). The container also mounts the volume `novatrail-dev-token` at `/home/vscode/.novatrail-token`, only to read it, and so differs from the template of `spec` by the second network and this volume.

— *Why:* a service behind an interface gives tests without Firebase and one place where it is decided when the session is read. Erasing by the answer of `GetAccount` before the app opens is the rule of the storage on the device and of the contract. A sign-out that erases nothing keeps the unfinished work and the essays the server does not keep for the same adult's next sign-in (П-8); the data opens only after a sign-in of the same account. The second network opens nothing beyond Docker and needs no change of `api`.

— *Rejected:*
- `getAuth` — it looks for the parameters and the emulator in the environment by itself ([`platform_browser/index.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/auth%401.13.10/packages/auth/src/platform_browser/index.ts) of `@firebase/auth`, [`postinstall.js`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/util%401.15.3/packages/util/postinstall.js) and [`defaults.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/util%401.15.3/packages/util/src/defaults.ts) of `@firebase/util`, at the tags of the versions `package-lock.json` pins);
- the sign-in service before the check of the project — the previous project's session would be read as the new one's;
- erasing everything of the account at the sign-out, with a screen naming the loss first — the adult who only signs out and in again would lose the unfinished work the server does not keep;
- the port of `api` on the host and `host.docker.internal` — a port published on `0.0.0.0` opens `api` to the local network, and one on the host's `127.0.0.1` cannot be reached from the container;
- a shared network of all NovaTrail containers — a change of the template of `spec` for one case; the name of the container on its network is the answer to В47 (Д195 `spec`);
- a list of closed countries of its own in `app` — the deployment holds it, and it would drift from the list of `api`.

— *Does not decide:* the sign-in window on the devices, iPad and Android — the rest of Д8, in E3: Chromium checks it under the CSP header, with the network log; the database `firebase-heartbeat-database` that `@firebase/app` keeps on the device and whose records it sends to Google with the requests to Firebase Auth ([`heartbeatService.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/app%400.16.6/packages/app/src/heartbeatService.ts), [`internal.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/app%400.16.6/packages/app/src/internal.ts) and [`indexeddb.ts`](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-js-sdk/blob/%40firebase/app%400.16.6/packages/app/src/indexeddb.ts) of `@firebase/app` at the tag of the version `package.json` pins) — the row «в реестр spec» В48; a fresh sign-in by the window — with deletions, E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/signin/`; `src/account/`; `src/boot/Boot.tsx`; `vite.config.ts`; `.devcontainer/`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

**R20. `app` opens the task screen from the start of an attempt, `AttemptStart`: by the open attempt that `GetAccount` named, "Continue the task" on Home, by "Try it again" after its end, and by the engine's choice, and the address `#/task` does not name the attempt. The screen is the mockup's task screen — the canvas over the whole window, the task's own top bar with "Back to Home", and a foot with the verdict's card and the row —, the options of the slots `choice` in the pop-over of "Choose an answer", and the tutor's panel over the canvas. The verdict stands at the chosen option and at the item, as a word and a sign, and "Correct" ends the attempt with the result: "Correct", the task's solution and "Try it again".** The way home is the one every learner's screen has, and the layout is the interface mockup's. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The entry.** `BeginAttempt` begins an attempt only by an engine choice, a place in a lesson or a past attempt, and only `ChooseNext` writes choices ([`BeginAttempt`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/42c2cb4/internal/learning/begin.go) and [the test of the methods of later tasks](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/42c2cb4/internal/learning/learning_test.go) of `api` at commit 42c2cb4). So the task screen is opened by an open attempt outside a lesson that `GetAccount` named — "Continue the task" in "Next up" on Home —, by "Try it again" with `previous_attempt_id`, and by a choice of the engine; the lesson player leads a lesson's attempt. The ID of the current attempt is in memory alone (`src/task/AttemptContext.tsx`), and the screen `#/task` without an attempt leads home. An attempt that ended stays on the screen with its result until the learner leaves, and Home no longer offers it.
- **The way home** is the top bar's Back, "Back to Home", while the attempt is open and once it is over, the result having none of its own; the read of the attempt that did not answer has "Back to Home" beside "Try again". Leaving keeps what a reload keeps: the attempt stays open, with its work as the device saved it, and Home offers "Continue the task".
- **What the screen reads.** `GetOpenAttempt` is the start of the attempt: the task's open view by `module_format_version` within the build's window, otherwise an offer to reload (`OpenViews.parseView`), and the package digest, by which the manifest, the solving steps and the technique catalog are taken from the shared cache. What this device wrote in the attempt — the writing history and the step state — comes from the profile's database; with no record, a new record with a clean sheet, and the step state with the options of the last submission, the number of wrong answers and the step's techniques, which `api` keeps. Each change is written at once, and a record that did not go in is the line "Your work isn't saved on this device".
- **Refusals.** `CAUSE_ATTEMPT_CLOSED` with a close reason is the screen "This task is already over" with the reason in words and "Try it again"; the attempt's work on the device is erased, and for "Not checked" `app` also asks `CheckPackages`. `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND` of `GetOpenAttempt` — home. No connection — a screen with "Try again", the rest — the common words of the contract client with "Try again".
- **The layout** is the mockup's: the canvas over the whole window, the top bar and the foot floating over it; the mode a pill at the bar's end with a pop-over; the options in the pop-over of "Choose an answer", where an item of the sheet is a `<fieldset>` — its text or "Item n" —, a slot `choice` is radio buttons, with "Answer n" in an item of several slots, and the keyboard is the native one. The tutor's panel floats at the window's right from 600 CSS px, over the canvas, which keeps its width, and below 600 is a sheet from the bottom; the lines and the foot keep clear of it.
- **"Check"** is `aria-disabled` while some slot holds nothing, with the line "Choose an answer in each item", which its press puts in the card; a task with handwriting is sent as the canvas gives it. The idempotency key is one per submission of these options, the same on its retry, and another option is another action and a new key.
- **The verdict** stands at the chosen option, outside its `<label>` and as the radio button's description, and at the item's text: "✓ Correct", "• Not yet", "• Can't read this", "• Not checked", and an unknown code is a common line. The summary "Not right yet: n items of m" is `role="status"`; with the panel closed the card above the row holds it. A changed option removes the verdict of its slot and item: it was the verdict of another answer.
- **"Correct".** The attempt is closed: its work on the device is erased — a choice has no work for the correct answer words —, the panel holds the result, the solution `SolvedTask` — paragraphs and pictures by `blob:` with their `alt` —, and the options can no longer be pressed. The screen does not show the keys of `SolvedTask`: for a choice the key is the option itself, already marked "Correct".
- **The steps** are led on the device, without the key and without AI, in the step's card of the panel: the list has `aria-current="step"` and the word of the place — "done", "now", "later" —, the steps done are buttons to go back; the step's name is an `h2` that takes the focus when the step changes; `description`, the chips — buttons `aria-pressed`, `givens` and `unknown` apart —, the cards of techniques, the line of the condition and "Step done" under `aria-disabled`. `BeginStep` once per step whose `gives` gives techniques, only in the mode "by steps"; a failure asks again on the return to the step. The step state is `src/task/steps.ts`, without the DOM: the mode, the place, the furthest step, the marks, the techniques, the chosen options and the number of wrong answers.
- **The blink** is `src/task/pause.ts`: 6 s with no stroke and no choice while "Check" is open — one blink, three flashes of 1 s each; again only after a new action; under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, a still outline.

— *Why:* without `ChooseNext` there is nothing else to begin an attempt on `api` with, and the open attempt is the very path by which the learner returns to the task after a restart. The canvas keeps its place and width while the panel opens and closes, and a verdict beside the option does not make the learner look for what it is about.

— *Rejected:*
- a debug entry with the ID of a choice — code of the development build that is not in the release, and a row put into the database of `api` by hand;
- the panel above the canvas — on a wide screen the sheet goes down, and the canvas would re-lay its page each time the panel opens;
- a separate screen of the outcome — the work on the canvas goes away from the solution;
- hidden headings "Tutor" and "Steps" — the panel and the list are named by `aria-label`, and the step's name is an `h2`;
- "Step done" and "Check" turned off by `disabled` — the focus would fall to the page.

— *Does not decide:* the canvas under the CSP beyond the built page in Chromium, where it was checked — the rest of Д9.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R21. `app` sends handwriting as the canvas gives it: on "Check", `exportWork()`, the submission into the profile's database with its context, and `SubmitWork` with the crop, with one key for this work and the same on a retry. The verdict of a handwriting slot stands at the item's text with "Read: …" or a reason for "can't read" — for a task's one item, in the verdict's card —, the tutor's words are a bubble of the tutor's panel's conversation, and the marks are boxes on the tutor's layer of the canvas. The tutor speaks of the work only while its panel is open: then "Not yet" asks at once for the rung's words with the same work, and "Correct" for the correct answer words with the work of the correct submission; with it closed the check ends at the verdict, and the panel's opening asks, once, the words of the last submission of the task on screen that gave "not yet" or "correct".** The words are asked only with the panel open, as `spec` has it: the work goes to AI once more for the tutor's words only while the panel is open, or once when the learner opens it. The card and the panel are the interface mockup's. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The submission.** "Check" for a task with handwriting is open when every slot holds something (`filled` of the element and the chosen options), and closed with the line "Write an answer on the sheet", on a workbook "Write an answer in each box"; no line about names stands above its row. On it: `exportWork()`, `addSubmission` with the length of the history — if the record did not go in, the line "Your work isn't saved on this device" —, and `SubmitWork`: for a sheet, the PNG of the crop, for a workbook, the crops of the boxes with their places, as bytes; with them the options and the clock offset, since the platform AI reads the work. A retry of an unanswered submission goes with the same key and the same work, and a new stroke or option is a new action and a new key.
- **The answer.** The verdicts are as for a choice, and at an item of handwriting "Read: {value}" or the reason: `blank`, `notation`, `illegible`, `ambiguous`, `no_final_answer`, `several_final_answers`; an unknown one is a common line. For the one item of a task they stand in the verdict's card; an item with text of a task of several keeps them under the canvas. On a workbook the list of lines under the canvas gives, for a line of more boxes than one, each box's verdict with what was read in it, and the summary counts lines. The outcome of the AI — a failure, the quota, the limit, "not admitted", a refusal of the key — is a line at "Check" in the common words of the contract client, and the slots the AI did not read are "Not checked".
- **The panel's opening.** By steps the panel opens as the task begins, its × closes it, and the step's pill or the tutor's round button opens it again; on my own the round button at the window's right edge opens and closes it, and "Hint" and the result's "Tutor" open it; a verdict never does. Back on my own from "by steps", the panel stays open while it holds the tutor's words or a hint. It opens on the tutor's words or the program's lines alone, with no line that the tutor is an AI. With the panel closed, the screen holds in memory the last submission of the task that gave "not yet" or "correct", with its work — the work of "correct" in `correctWork` — until the opening asks its words, or the learner leaves the screen, when the work of "correct" goes; the solution shown drops the rung's words unasked. While the panel is closed, words that come of an earlier request are neither said nor shown, nor are their marks, until it opens.
- **The rung's words.** A wrong answer — `wrong_answers` grew — and `app` calls `GetRungWords` with this number, the same work, the own key and the clock offset: at once while the panel is open, else at its opening. The words are a bubble of the panel's conversation, with the `lang` of the explanation language, and the live line says only "The tutor's words came". While the models' words are not admitted, `api` gives the rung's fallback text with the outcome "not admitted" ([`hints.go`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/94708bd/internal/learning/hints.go) of `api` at commit 94708bd): these are words, and then there is no line of the outcome; an outcome without words is a line in the conversation. The refusals "answer not current", "no rung" and "no more words" show nothing, a closed attempt is the end screen. After a restart the rung's words come from `OpenAttempt.rung`.
- **The marks** come only with the rung's words ([`hints.go`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/94708bd/internal/learning/hints.go) of `api` at commit 94708bd): `crop_box` for a sheet is a box `{ box }` in thousandths of the crop, `answer_slot` for a workbook is `{ cell }`; a mark without a place or of an unknown kind does not count. The name is "Tutor's mark n of m", and for a workbook also ": line l, box b". The element gets them with the context of that submission, which the database kept.
- **"Correct".** `attemptEnded` with the number of the correct submission: its work goes to `correctWork`, and the rest is erased. Then `GetCorrectAnswerWords` with this work, while the panel is open or once it opens, and when the answer has come or the request has ended, `wordsCame`. The words, if they came, go into the result with `lang`; for now `api` does not give them (В47 of `api`). Д29 holds: an answer without words drops the work.

— *Why:* the verdict beside what it judged, as for a choice; the words where the tutor speaks of the steps; the marks where the strokes they outline are. `app` asks for the rung's words because the server does not keep the crop, and only the device can send it once more. The tutor speaks only while its panel is open, so that what calls it is the learner's: the tutor's words take about 97 % of the money spent on AI, by the measurements of `tutor` ([its `docs/measurements.md`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/tutor/blob/df62bab/docs/measurements.md) at commit df62bab, the price of a check).

— *Rejected:*
- a strip of the outcome under the canvas — on a narrow screen it pushes the items away, and the words stand far from the steps;
- removing the marks with each new stroke — the canvas keeps a box on the strokes and dims it while the learner writes in it; a new submission removes the marks;
- giving `marks` as a JSX property — a mark that the element rejects would bring the screen down with a `TypeError` at rendering. `app` sets `marks` after rendering, when `task` and `history` are already on the element, and drops a rejected one;
- building the rung's fallback text on the device from the manifest's `rung_templates` — the trap text comes first, and traps do not come to the device;
- a button "Ask for words" after "Not yet" — an extra tap: the rung's question is part of the answer to a wrong answer;
- the words asked with the panel closed — the learner calls the tutor, and words nobody reads cost the same;
- the words held across a restart — the server keeps no crop, and the work stays in memory only while the screen is open.

— *Does not decide:* the size of the writing history by pen and by finger — the rest of Д4, with devices in E3: by mouse in Chromium it is measured (measurements 1).

— *Consequence:* `src/task/`; `src/storage/profile.ts`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R22. "Hint" stands beside "Check" in the task screen's row, before "Show the solution" and "Check", and also in the card of "Not yet" and, on a phone, at the foot of the tutor's panel, and asks `GetHint`: until the first wrong answer it shows, as a bubble of the panel's conversation, the givens and the unknown, the task's techniques and its leading question, and after it the words of the current rung once more, without the AI, or asks by `GetRungWords` those words that the closed panel left unasked. "Explain differently" is a button at the panel's foot, beside "Hint" on a phone, once per rung. "Show the solution" is visible to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, and the showing asks for confirmation: it ends the attempt.** What the buttons give and when, `spec` decided, and here is how it falls on the task screen and its tutor's panel. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **"Hint"** is `GetHint`, without a key and without the work. It stands in the row, before "Show the solution" and "Check", in the card of "Not yet", and at the panel's foot on a phone alone, where the open sheet hides the row: from 600 px the row's "Hint" stays in sight beside the open panel, and a second at its foot would only repeat it. Any "Hint" opens the panel.
  - Until the first wrong answer, the answer is the IDs of the task's techniques and the leading question. The hint is a bubble of the panel's conversation, not a block at its top: it shows as a list "What we know" and "What to find" from the open view, the cards of techniques from the catalog and the question, all with the module's `lang`. If there is neither a technique nor a question, the line "This task has nothing more to hint".
  - After it the answer is the words of the current rung, and they go into the panel's conversation, as the rung's words after a check do. "Hint" after a wrong answer whose rung's words the closed panel left unasked asks those words by `GetRungWords`, with the work, in place of `GetHint`.
  - The open attempt keeps what was given — `requested_technique_ids` and `requested_leading_question` —, and until the first wrong answer the screen shows them from there, without a request.
- **"Explain differently"** is `ExplainDifferently` with the number of the wrong answer whose words the learner is reading, a key and the clock offset. It stands at the panel's foot, beside "Hint" on a phone, shown only while it takes a press.
  - The new words take the place of the previous ones. The outcome `CAUSE_NO_OTHER_WORDS` gives the line "The tutor has no other words for this", and another outcome gives the words that any screen shows for it.
  - Any answer spends the right on the rung. The refusals "already asked", "answer not current" and "no rung" spend it too: the button is gone. The refusals of the quota, the limit and admission leave the right, and the button stays with the line of the refusal. A network failure is retried with the same key.
  - A new wrong answer is a new rung and again its own "Explain differently". The open attempt tells `explained_differently` of the current rung.
- **"Show the solution"** first asks, in a pop-over of the row, "Once the solution is shown, the task is over" with "Cancel" and "Show it"; the focus moves to "Cancel", and after "Cancel" it comes back.
  - Then `ShowSolution` as the learner's action with a key. The answer closes the attempt: its work on the device is erased, and the panel is the outcome "Solution shown" with the solution.
  - The refusal "adult needed" is the line "A grown-up needs to help"; "ladder not passed" is "The solution can be shown after a few more answers"; a closed attempt is the task's end screen.
  - The adult action behind a PIN is E4, with the section for adults.

— *Why:* what the buttons give and when, `spec` decided; where they stand is the mockup's task screen and tutor's panel. "Hint" and "Check" are actions on the task, and stand together; "Explain differently" is about the tutor's words, and stands at them. Showing the solution ends the attempt with no undo, so it is asked once more — sending an answer by "Check" asks for no confirmation, but its verdict can be put right by the next submission.

— *Rejected:*
- "Hint" at the panel's foot at every width: from 600 px it stood twice in sight, at the foot and in the row beside it;
- "Hint" only in the panel, none in the row: one more tap for the learner's first help, and none at all with the panel closed;
- «Объясни иначе» on the button — in Russian the learner's screens do not address the learner: «Объяснить иначе», as «Проверить»;
- showing the solution with one tap — the attempt would end from a chance tap on a tablet;
- a hint before a wrong answer without a request, from the open view alone — the technique and the question are closed, and `api` records what was given.

— *Does not decide:* calling an adult for a child, and showing the solution by an adult action — E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R23. `app` opens a lesson by a lesson run already begun — "Continue the lesson" in "Next up" on Home by `open_lesson_runs` of `GetAccount`, through `GetLessonRun` —, and a new lesson run by the engine choice, `OpenLesson`; the address `#/lesson` does not name the lesson run. The lesson's header is the mockup's lesson top bar: "Leave the lesson", the title and, under it, the line of the segment shown, which opens the segments in a pop-over, each row named by its words — the place, the kind and how far it is, or why it does not open. A lesson's task is the task screen under the lesson's bar, without "Try it again"; the place moves by the `next` of the answer about the task's end at once, and "Continue" on the last segment is `CompleteLesson`.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The entry.** The learner's lesson runs begun — `open_lesson_runs` from `GetAccount` — are held by the tab's memory per profile, as the open attempt is; a lesson run that ended leaves the list. "Next up" on Home names the first by the lesson's title from the state on the device, with the module's `lang`, and with no state as "A lesson: {module}", where the module's name is a placeholder element with its own `lang`, with "Continue the lesson", and the others under "Also begun:". With no lesson run chosen, `#/lesson` goes home at its own place in the history.
- **The key of the lesson's state** is the lesson's full ID, the package digest and, beside them, the ID of the lesson run (`learning.proto`). `LessonRun` does not carry the full ID: `app` builds it from the module ID of the manifest and the lesson's ID as [the format text](../spec/format/README.md) composes a full ID, and with no manifest takes `LessonRunRef.lesson`.
- **The segments** are the rows of the segments' pop-over under the lesson's top bar, by places; a row's name is its words — the place, the kind and "passed", "now" or "later", or on a segment that does not open, why: "This task is over" or "Not open yet" (WCAG 2.5.3) —, a tick in place of a passed one's number. A press on a row that does not open closes the pop-over and says why in the screen's line. The heading `h2` "Worked example · 2 of 4", to which the focus goes when the segment changes (WCAG 2.4.3), is out of sight on an example and a task.
- **A lesson's task** is `BeginAttempt` with the segment of the lesson run and a key, the same on a retry; only a refusal resets the key. An attempt opened at the place, `app` takes from `LessonRun.open_attempt`. The task screen stands under the lesson's bar without a bar of its own, "Try it again" and "Back to Home", its mode's pill at the bar's end, as on the task screen's own bar; a closed attempt is a line of the segment. An attempt closed by "left" or "abandoned" does not end the segment — "Begin the task again" —, and its other end is a repeated `GetLessonRun`.
- **What's next.** `next.segment` moves the place at once, while the learner still sees the task's outcome; `segments_ended` leaves the place on the last segment, and "Continue" on it is "Lesson complete"; an empty `next` is a repeated `GetLessonRun`. The refusal `CAUSE_SEGMENT_UNAVAILABLE` leads on if the segment ended by itself, and otherwise to the first task not ended, even if before the place; a refusal without places is a repeated `GetLessonRun`, not "nothing ended". An attempt that `api` no longer knows, the task screen in a lesson hands to the player and does not lead home. The quota, the limit, "not admitted" and an answer without an attempt are a line and "Begin the task again", and the lesson run stays open (Д26 of `api`).
- **The end.** `CompleteLesson` on "Continue" on the last segment; a network failure is a line, and "Continue" sends it again, while the client itself retries `unavailable`. `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED` opens the first task segment not ended with the line "The lesson has a task to finish". Any end of the lesson run — by an answer or by `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` — erases the lesson's state and removes the lesson run from the list; "Not checked" also calls `CheckPackages`. The end screen is the mockup's card of the lesson's end — the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" or a neutral phrase of the reason, and the button to what the engine chose — and "Back to Home".

— *Why:* a lesson run begins only by the engine's choice, and `api` itself names those begun. The segments behind the line keep the header one line at 320 px, and a row's words keep the kind and the state of each segment in its name. The place moves by `next` at once, since a lesson's place moves on as soon as its task has ended: otherwise a restart after a correct answer would open a task that has ended.

— *Rejected:*
- a column of segments with words — the header above each task of the lesson grows with the number of segments;
- a strip of chips under the title, a number and a tick on each — the mockup lists the segments in a pop-over from the line under the title;
- the address `#/lesson/<choice_id>` in the development build — code that is not in the release;
- a row named by its place and kind alone, its state its description — the visible "passed" would be out of the name (WCAG 2.5.3);
- the words of steps passed on the segments — «пройден» agrees with «шагом», while «звено» is neuter: the segments have words of their own, «пройдено», «сейчас», «дальше»;
- `NOT_SUITABLE` in the common table of the words of reasons in the lesson's words — a task outside a lesson gets this reason too.

— *Does not decide:* a lesson whose task by handwriting is not admitted to the AI, which is stuck before "Lesson complete" — Д26 of `api`; the screen says the refusal, and back and leaving work.

— *Consequence:* `src/lesson/`; `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`; `src/storage/profile.ts`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R24. `app` leads to what `engine` chose with one button: "Next lesson" or "Next task" by the choice that the answer about the end of an attempt outside a lesson, or of a lesson run, gave, and without it "What's next", which asks `ChooseNext`. With an attempt open outside a lesson and no choice at hand, the button is "Something else", which first says that the task in progress will close and cannot be gone back to; Home has "What's next" only while no attempt is open, and none beside "Continue the task". A lesson choice opens the player by `OpenLesson`, a task choice the task screen by `BeginAttempt`, and "nothing to give" is the line "There is nothing more to offer for now", with the outcome if the AI is closed.** What to do, `spec`'s contract of the path from a module to a task and the comments of `LearningService` decided, and here is how it falls on the task screen and the lesson player. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **Where the button is.** On the outcome of a task outside a lesson, under the solution, before "Try it again", which has become secondary; on the screen "This task is already over", secondary, after "Try it again"; on the end screen of a lesson, before "Back to Home"; on Home, at the right of the foot of "Next up", when there is no open attempt outside a lesson.
- **A choice from the answer.** `next.choice` of the answer of `SubmitWork` with "Correct" and of the answer of `ShowSolution`, `RunEnd.choice` of the answer of `CompleteLesson` and of the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` — a button by what the choice gives, without a request. A choice "nothing to give" from the answer is a line without a button. An end without a choice is "What's next".
- **"What's next" and "Something else"** are `ChooseNext` with the action's key and an empty skill: the skill map is E3. The answer leads at once to what the choice gives. An open attempt outside a lesson is closed, `CLOSE_REASON_LEFT`, both by `ChooseNext` and by beginning a task by a choice: before each such action, the question "The task in progress closes, and it can't be gone back to", wherever the button stands, and after it `app` erases the attempt's work on the device and forgets it as open, as at the end of an attempt. A lesson by a choice does not close the attempt and does not ask.
- **A task by a choice** is `BeginAttempt` with `choice_id` and a key of its own. The refusal `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` — the choice has already begun an attempt: `app` reads the profile's open attempt from `GetAccount` and opens it. The task screen `#/task` takes the new attempt even when it was begun from that screen itself, and the player `#/lesson` takes a new chosen lesson, with a new player.
- **The keys** are as for the hint's and the solution's actions: one key per action and the same on a retry, and only a refusal resets it.
- **The reason of the choice** the screen does not name: the answer to the learner does not carry it.

— *Why:* one button with the name of where it leads is clearer than a general "Next", which is, besides, a button of the canvas element. "Something else" closes the attempt with no way back, so it is asked once more, as showing the solution is, and "What's next" with no open attempt closes nothing and asks nothing.

— *Rejected:*
- one general button "Next" on every screen — it would clash with the "Next" of a worked example, and the learner would not know whether a lesson or a task is ahead;
- "Something else" inside a lesson too, beside its task — "Leave the lesson" is enough for the first slice, and the lesson goes on from the same place;
- moving on to the choice by itself after "Correct" — the learner is still reading the solution.

— *Does not decide:* the skill map with the choice of a skill, and consolidation in a lesson — E3.

— *Consequence:* `src/next/`; `src/task/`; `src/lesson/`; `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R25. A release of the build of `app` is one archive `app_<version>.tar.gz`, the only file of `dist/`: at its root `index.html`, `assets/`, `deployment.schema.json` and `csp.json` by the rollout layout of `infra`, without `deployment.json`, and also `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`. `scripts/release.ts` packs it with its own code, to the same bytes on every run, and the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.32 with `RELEASE_LINE` 0.1 signs it, the version worked out and the files built in the stage `toolchain-core` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`. A run after a green CI on `main` and on the button publishes the release; a run of a pull request is a dry run: it builds, signs and checks, publishing nothing.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The contents.** Everything from `build/` and, beside it, the repository's `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`. The bundle does not keep the packages' license comments, so the notices of third-party code — React, Firebase, `canvas` and others — travel in the same archive: a copy taken on its own carries them, and the site serves them at `/NOTICE` and `/LICENSE`. The rollout of `infra` does not reject extra files and puts them all out. `spec` records this layout, with `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` at the archive's root, in its decisions and in its card of `app`, and the rollout of `infra` takes it by Д208 of `spec`.
- **Packing.** ustar, entries of the form `./index.html` by name, the time, the owner and the group 0, the mode `0644` for files and `0755` for folders; a long path is split into the ustar fields at the first slash at which both fit. `gzip` of level 9 without a name and a time, and the byte of the system in its header "unknown", `0xff`: otherwise it would depend on the machine Node was built on. The recipe rejects a link in the build and a file of the build named `NOTICE` or `LICENSE`, and rejects a version not of the form `vX.Y.Z` already before the build.
- **The check**, before writing and in `ci-test`: only files and folders, with no path outside; at the root exactly five files and `assets/` with a file; `deployment.json` nowhere; the time, the owner and the mode as above; `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` byte for byte as in the repository; the lower layer of the CSP first in `<head>` after `<meta charset>`. `ci-test` also builds the tree a second time into its own folder and compares the bytes of the two archives: so a non-reproducible build itself is caught too, not only one of the packer.
- **`release.yml`** calls the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.34, commit `baa18cde370b3515e85b180d0563cb3eaa4955f2`, which works out the version and builds the files in the stage `toolchain-core` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` — Node, just, Go and buf, without the browsers, all that `release-files` needs. It runs on `workflow_run` of CI on `main`, only after a green push not from a fork, on `pull_request` from a branch of this repository and not from Dependabot, and on the button, without a path filter of its own: a run of a fork and of Dependabot gets no OIDC token, without which keyless signing does not go (sources 2.9), and the dry run checks Dependabot's changes on `main` after the merge; `paths` — the code without the tests, the page, `csp.json`, the part of `spec` in `parts/`, the scripts of the build and the archive, the configuration, the packages, `.npmrc`, `NOTICE`, `LICENSE`, the recipe, `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, of which `toolchain-core` is a part, and the file itself.
- **Publishing** is `publish: ${{ github.event_name != 'pull_request' }}` in `.github/workflows/release.yml`: a run after a green CI on `main` and on the button publishes the release, and a run of a pull request is a dry run that publishes nothing, as at `canvas` and `skill-map`. The first release, v0.1.0, goes out on the first run on `main` whatever `paths` says: `paths` cannot be compared with a past release, and the shared `release.yml` always puts out a first release.

— *Why:* the notices must go with the code that is distributed, and what is distributed is the archive, and the site comes from it as well. The license texts, read on 2026-11-11 in `node_modules` and in the repository's `LICENSE`: Apache-2.0, §4(d), — “If the Work includes a "NOTICE" text file as part of its distribution, then any Derivative Works that You distribute must include a readable copy of the attribution notices contained within such NOTICE file”; MIT (`react`) — "The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software"; ISC (`idb`) — "provided that the above copyright notice and this permission notice appear in all copies". A packer of its own does not depend on which `tar` is on the machine, and gives the same bytes: the check by sha256 in the rollout list and the signature rest on them. A release is published only because the repository keeps its releases immutable: by the GitHub API, read on 2026-11-12, `immutable-releases` is `"enabled":true`, and the ruleset `v-tags` (27046397, since 2026-11-11) has the target `tag`, `refs/tags/v*`, the rules `update`, `deletion` and `non_fast_forward`, and an empty `bypass_actors`. The dry run of a pull request builds and signs as a real run does, but creates neither a tag nor a release. The shared `release.yml` takes the same inputs and permissions on v0.1.32 as on v0.1.31: between them it did not change (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/compare/v0.1.31...v0.1.32`, read 2026-11-17).

— *Rejected:*
- `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` beside the archive in the release — a layout without a row in `spec`, but the site would serve the code without the notices, and a copy of the archive would not carry them;
- the notices only on the legal pages of E3 — until then the release would distribute the code without the notices;
- publishing from the first merge of the release workflow, as `canvas` did — v0.1.0 would have gone out before the repository kept its releases immutable;
- the system's `tar` with flags of reproducibility, as `sim` has — the recipe would depend on which `tar` is on the machine of whoever builds, and the check of the archive reads tar with its own code anyway;
- `SHA256SUMS` beside the archive — the rollout list of `infra` and the release's attestation hold the archive's sha256, and the file would only repeat them.

— *Consequence:* `scripts/release.ts`, `scripts/archive.ts`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `justfile`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `NOTICE`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The move to `spec` v0.15.0

**R26. `app` stands on `spec` v0.15.0: the submodule `spec/` and the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` are at this tag, the part `deployment/` is the file of its release, the rows В43–В49 are closed by the answers of `spec`, and what the tag decided for `app` is laid out across its documents by the same move, with the screen of the texts in force at account creation.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.13.0 v0.15.0`, read 2026-11-11). v0.15.0 took in v0.14.0 too: the end of epic E4 of `spec` and the start of E9:
  - the answers to В43–В49, all by the recommendations of `app`: В43 — the row of `spec`'s cross-repository plan that put the skill map in the epic of `app`'s code is rewritten; В44 — the README of `canvas` will name the consumer's copy of `spec` (Д186 of `spec`), which is the business of `canvas`; В45 — `spec`'s decision on the icons Material Symbols names a script on `@iconify/utils`; В46 — Д177 of `spec` and the reading of the file in [the part `deployment/`](../spec/deployment/README.md); В47 — Д195 of `spec`, the container's name by `--network-alias`, which is not there yet on v0.15.0; В48 — what `@firebase/app` sends to the sign-in service is recorded in `spec`'s privacy document, its architecture and the DPIA; В49 — `spec`'s decision on the deployment configuration lays the release archive out with `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` (Д208 of `spec`);
  - the texts in force: `privacy-policy` of version 2 with a translation into Russian, `terms-of-use` and `storage-consent` of version 1, and the family's texts — `parent-consent`, `teen-consent`, `child-notice` and `teen-notice`; `legal/` — versions 2, with translations; the operator in the texts is ALT EdTech LLC;
  - the consent contract: `CreateAccount` with the versions of the texts accepted, `SendConsentForm`, `CancelChildConsent`, `GiveOwnConsent`, `WithdrawOwnConsent`, `RestateAge`, `SetWorkStorage` and `AttemptService.ReportProblem`, the profile's consent in `Profile.consent`, and the causes 39–42 — `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS`, `CAUSE_AGE_NEEDED`, `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` and `CAUSE_LEARNER_DECIDES`;
  - the age of digital consent by country — the part `consent-age/` with the codes of `Country`, the export format — the part `export/`, the laws of the US states — in `spec`'s privacy document and its log;
  - the module format is still 0.4, and `RULES.md`, the template and `docs-check` are unchanged; the part `deployment/` is the same version 1, but the release file is another.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.15.0, commit 58d3f46; `gh release verify v0.15.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "Release v0.15.0 verified!". `spec_tag` in `justfile` is v0.15.0, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature is from the release v0.15.0: `release-verify` compares the commit in the archive's header with the tag;
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.15.0; `NOTICE` has the line of the contracts 0.15.0;
  - `.devcontainer/`: `diff -r` with the output of `just -f spec/justfile new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.15.0` gives only the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run, as on v0.13.0;
  - the tag in CLAUDE.md, README and RUN.md is v0.15.0; the plan's questions to the author are without В43–В49, with a note above them on where their answers are;
  - the code: the list of countries is the type `Country` (Д175 of `spec`), the words of the causes 39–42, `GetLegalTexts` by the open transport, as the open views are, and the screen of the texts at account creation;
  - the decisions on the framework and build (В45), the interface languages (the translation), the platform's packages, the sign-in and the network of the local run (В46, В47 and the texts) and the release of the build (В49) follow the tag;
  - what the tag decided for `app` beyond this — the rows Д144, Д166, Д168, Д175, Д180, Д183, Д190, Д195, Д201 and Д204 of `spec` and `spec`'s decisions on the legal texts, the consent and the laws of the US states — is laid out in [architecture.md](architecture.md) and by the epics E3 and E4 of the plan.

— *Why now:* the row Д190 of `spec`: while `app` sends no versions of the texts, `api` with texts in force refuses `CreateAccount`, and the first sign-in of the first app breaks; the rows Д144, Д166, Д168, Д175, Д180, Д183, Д195, Д201 and Д204 wait for the same move, and the answers to В43–В49 close all the open rows «в реестр spec» of `app`. The release v0.15.0 is the latest (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`, 2026-11-11).

— *Rejected:*
- the intermediate v0.14.0: v0.15.0 came out half a day after it and carries the translation of the policy, the operator and the family's texts in force, and the next move would have to be made at once;
- two tasks, as the move to v0.13.0 had: less is decided for `app` than on v0.13.0, and the screens of the consent, the teenager and the export stay for the epics E3 and E4;
- the screen of the texts in E3, with the legal pages: then the first build with `api` would go only with an `api` that does not yet refuse `CreateAccount` without the versions of the texts.

— *Consequence:* CLAUDE.md; README.md; RUN.md; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R27. With no account, `app` names on the invite code screen, on its step after the code, the texts `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use` that `GetLegalTexts` gives in force, each name in the line of the tick "I agree" a link that opens the text's sheet over the step, whose "Read the full text" opens its legal page, and asks for the tick; `CreateAccount` names the versions read, and on `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` the screen reads the texts anew and asks to agree again. The body of a text is Markdown, which the lexer of `marked` 18.1.3 reads, and code of its own builds the elements, with no string of HTML. `unimplemented` from an `api` without `LegalService` is "no text in force".** The step, its links and the texts' sheets are as the mockup's `agree`, `legal-terms` and `legal-privacy` draw them. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The screen.** Under "I am 18", the tick "I agree to the Terms of Use and the Privacy Policy", each name a link to `#/legal/<id>` that opens the text's sheet over the step — its version, its gist and its next version —, whose "Read the full text" opens the text's legal page, whose close comes back to the step with what was typed and ticked, and under the ticks the line that each text is in English, the one in force, with its gist in the screen's language. Without the tick, a field error and the focus on it, as for "I am 18". While the texts are not read, the button "Create account" is off; a failure of the reading is its words and "Try again". While the texts are read anew, the previous ones stay, so the tick loses neither its place nor the focus.
- **The text** on its page: the first-level heading of the body, `lang="en"`; the gist, with the `lang` of the field `summary_language`; on a screen not in English, a line that the text is in English and the English one is in force; the translation, if the version carries one, with its own `lang` and the note "for reading only"; and the English body, `lang="en"`.
- **The versions.** `CreateAccount` names `{id, version}` of each text the screen read in force, the versions its sheets and pages show; with no texts in force it names none, as the contract asks. The tick holds to the versions at which it was put: if others are read — after a change of language or a repeated reading —, it is removed; a next version does not remove it. `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` removes the tick and reads the texts anew, and when the screen had no text, says so in a line under the button, as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) asks of the versions of the texts.
- **The transport.** `GetLegalTexts` is a Connect GET without a token, without a deadline and without headers, as the package's open views are, with the screen's language in `interface_language`, but past the HTTP cache, `cache: "no-cache"`: the consent names the version read, and a version from the cache after a new one came into force would get a refusal, and the retry the same one again.
- **`unimplemented`.** For `GetLegalTexts`, `unimplemented` — an `api` before `LegalService`, or a proxy that does not route its path, whose 404 Connect reads so — is an empty list, without an offer to reload; for the other methods it is still the offer. An `api` with texts in force refuses `CreateAccount` without their versions, and the screen says so, so without the texts no account is created.
- **Markdown.** The part `legal/` writes CommonMark with GitHub tables, without HTML, pictures and links, as [the part `legal/`](../spec/legal/README.md) has it. The lexer of `marked` gives tokens, and `src/legal/Markdown.tsx` builds React elements from them; HTML, a link and a picture come out as their own text. Named character references are those of the markup and the typography of a legal text, from `&amp;` to quotation marks and dashes, and `marked` itself expands the numeric ones (`src/legal/Markdown.browser.test.tsx`); in inline code they stay as written, as CommonMark asks.

— *Why:* without the versions, `api` refuses `CreateAccount`, and the version that the consent names must be the one offered to the person: the step reads the versions in force, its links open their sheets, and a version that changed meanwhile is refused and read again. The links keep the step a card of the mockup's and each text a sheet over it, as the mockup's, in place of a long form; what is typed and ticked outlives the visit. Elements without a string of HTML give the text neither a script nor a link outside, and the CSP need not allow inline markup.

— *Rejected:*
- the texts in place below the ticks, each unfolding, with its next version — the screen grew past the card the mockup draws, and the mockup draws links;
- `mdast-util-from-markdown` with the extension of GitHub tables — [12 dependencies](https://www.npmjs.com/package/mdast-util-from-markdown/v/2.1.0?activeTab=dependencies) against [none](https://www.npmjs.com/package/marked/v/18.1.3?activeTab=dependencies) for `marked`, for the same CommonMark with GitHub's tables;
- HTML from `marked` and `innerHTML` — a string of markup on the page, which the part `legal/` does not write and which would have to be sanitized;
- taking `unimplemented` for an outdated build, as for the other methods: the invite screen on an `api` without `LegalService` would offer a reload, which changes nothing.

— *Consequence:* `src/legal/` — `texts.ts`, `body.ts`, `Markdown.tsx`, `LegalText.tsx`, `LegalPages.tsx`, `LegalSheet.tsx`; `src/account/InviteScreen.tsx`, `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`, `src/account/session.ts` — `createAccount`; `src/api/interceptors.ts` — `effects`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `src/i18n/ru.ts` — the keys `account.texts*` and `legal.*`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R28. `app` stands on `spec` v0.15.1: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` and the part `deployment/` are at this tag, and `.claude/settings.json` and `.devcontainer/` follow the tag's template: one task is one session, and the hook `UserPromptSubmit` holds it, compaction at 400 thousand tokens, the claude.ai connectors off, Go 1.27.4. The rest of what `spec` assigns to the repositories to save a session's tokens — the translation of `docs/`, a thin CLAUDE.md, the bodies of done tasks to the archive at once — `app` takes not by this move, but by the move to v0.16.3, with the restructuring of the documents, and the translation of `docs/` by tasks of its own.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.15.0 v0.15.1`):
  - RULES.md: "One task is one session" — the planning and the execution of a task run in one session, and the next task in a new one or after `/clear`; the hook `UserPromptSubmit` from `spec/tools/session/` stops a phrase that starts another task in the same session;
  - the template: `.claude/settings.json` — `autoCompactWindow` 400000, `disableClaudeAiConnectors` and this hook; the line about one session in RUN.md; Go 1.27.4 in place of 1.27.3 in `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` — 1.27.3 has the vulnerability GO-2026-6859 of the standard library. `app` has no Go code of its own, but the version of Go is one for all the repositories and comes from the stage `toolchain`;
  - the contract: the field `teaching_modules` of `AnchorSkillState` — the catalog's modules that teach the anchor skill and are not connected to the profile. `app` must show them when the anchor is "Unknown", with the name from the open view of the manifest and connecting by `ConnectModule` (Д211 of `spec`); this is the skill map screen, E3. The field is only added, and the build of `app` without the map skips it;
  - the module format is still 0.4; the parts are of the same versions; `docs-check` and RULES.md's rule on language are unchanged: `docs/` are still in Russian.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.15.1, commit 6a4ba48; `gh release verify v0.15.1 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "Release v0.15.1 verified!"; `spec_tag` in `justfile` is v0.15.1, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature is from the release v0.15.1: the contents are the same, but `release-verify` compares the commit in the archive's header with the tag;
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.15.1; `NOTICE` has the line of the contracts 0.15.1;
  - `.devcontainer/`: `diff -r` with the output of `just -f spec/justfile new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.15.1` gives only the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run; `.claude/settings.json` is the same as in the recipe's output; the container is rebuilt after the merge, for Go 1.27.4;
  - RUN.md: the line about one session, as in the template; the tag in CLAUDE.md, README and RUN.md is v0.15.1;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md): `teaching_modules` in the row of the skill map, and the epic E3 of the plan.

— *Why now:* v0.15.1 is the latest release (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`, 2026-11-11). A container on 1.27.3 would carry a vulnerable Go, and without the hook a session that leads one task would start the next one too, against the RULES.md of this same tag.

— *Rejected:*
- waiting for the tag with the rest of `spec`'s restructuring of the documents: Go and the hook are needed now, and the next move will be a task of its own anyway;
- the translation of `docs/` and the rest of what `spec` assigns to save a session's tokens already in this move: RULES.md on v0.15.1 still asks for `docs/` in Russian, and the tag has no checks of the translation, no rules of RUN.md about the archive and no thin CLAUDE.md — later tags of `spec` give them;
- showing `teaching_modules` in this move: there is no skill map in E2, and the detailing of `app` and `skill-map` decides the showing (Д211 of `spec`).

— *Consequence:* CLAUDE.md; README.md; RUN.md; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; `.claude/settings.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Section for adults

**R29. The section for adults, Settings, is the screens `#/adults` in the address's hash, in the device's language: its list — the account's profiles, This device, Language, the own key, and under them the quiet link Privacy — stands beside its page from 600 CSS px and is its first page below; each profile has a page of its own, `#/adults/profile`, whose profile is chosen in the section and lives in memory alone. The entry is "Settings" in the profile's menu. The profile page is one form: the avatar, the pseudonym, the default mode, the hints and voicing; "Save" in the page's bar sends `UpdateProfile` with only the fields that changed, and the other tabs learn of the profile through `BroadcastChannel`. The whole of `app`, the learner's screens too, is in the device's language.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **Routes.** `#/adults` is the first page, `#/adults/<page>` the others. The hash does not name the profile: the page's profile is the one chosen by its row of the list, in the section's memory. From 600 CSS px `#/adults` shows beside the list the page of the profile chosen, else of the learner chosen, else the owner's; below, the list alone. The profile page with no profile chosen — after a reload —, and a page the build does not know, lead to the first page at their own place in the history.
- **Where it is.** `AccountGate` shows the section while the account is open, in the place of the choice of the learner or of the learner's screens. The learner's screens lose nothing on leaving: what the tab learned about the attempt and the lessons stays with the profile, as after "Switch learner".
- **The section's frame** is `SectionScreen`: from 600 CSS px the list at the left under "Settings" and its close, and the page beside it under its bar — the heading in its middle, the way back to the page above where there is one, named "Back to {name}" by its words, and the page's action, a profile's Save, at its end; below 600 the list is the first page under "Settings" and its close, and each other page stands under its bar, whose way back leads to the list or to the page above. Close, "Close the settings", leads home — to the choice of the learner if no learner is chosen, otherwise to Home. The list is a third of the window between 280 and 375 px, and widens past that to its longest word with what stands beside it in its row — a label and its value, as "Language English" —: a word broken in the middle reads as two, and a language's word, as a German compound, may be longer than the column at its narrowest. A profile's name alone breaks where it must, since it may run long without a space and would push the page off the screen.
- **The list** is grouped rows, each named by what it shows: the profiles, each its avatar, its pseudonym and whose it is, the owner first, the others by the `Intl.Collator` of the screen's language; This device, Language with the device's language as its value, and Own AI key; under the last group the quiet link "Privacy", «Конфиденциальность», to the list of the legal pages, as Connecticut's law asks a link with the word in the settings — the legal texts and Accessibility are otherwise Help's. It tells of no next version of a legal text: Home's bell does. The row of the page shown beside the list is `aria-current="page"`.
- **Language**, `#/adults/language`, is a row for each language of the build by its own name and `lang`, the one in use ticked and `aria-pressed`, with a line that the whole of `app` uses it, the learner's screens too: buttons, not a choice of a form, since a press changes the screen at once (WCAG 3.2.2, sources 8.1).
- **How the section grows.** A page of the whole account or of the device is a part of the hash and a row of the list; a page of one profile is a part of the hash and a row of the profile page, for the profile chosen in the section, whose way back leads to it. The parts:
  - "This device" — `#/adults/device`, from the list: it is about the device, and the profiles are inside it;
  - keeping works on the server — a part of the profile page;
  - the legal pages, "Accessibility" and the notices of third-party code — screens of their own, visible before the sign-in too, which the quiet link Privacy and Help lead to;
  - own key — `#/adults/own-key`, from the list: the key is the owner profile's;
  - the platform AI quota — the part "Tutor time" at the bottom of the profile page: `GetQuota` asks for a profile and gives the shares of the profile and of the account together.
- **The entry.** "Settings" in the profile's menu, after "Switch learner" and with a chevron, as it leads to a page; the menu is the same on Home, Subjects, the map, a lesson and a task. In E3 the section opens without a PIN: the account has only the owner profile, of the category `adult`; the PIN, E4, will stand where `AccountGate` opens the section.
- **The profile page** is "Profile: {pseudonym}", the avatar, the pseudonym and whose it is above one form of grouped lists, and "Save" in its bar:
  - the avatar is changed in a pop-over and goes with the form, only once changed;
  - the pseudonym has the check of the profile's creation: 1 to 30 characters without spaces at the edges;
  - the default mode is "Step by step" or "On my own", a row of the group Learning, with a line that each task begins in it and the learner switches it on the task screen;
  - the hints are "Questions only" or "Show where", each with its line;
  - voicing is "Read the tutor's words aloud" and the speed "Normal" or "Slow". The page says that a voice of the device reads the words, chosen on "This device", and advises whoever listens to a screen reader to turn voicing off, which would speak at once.

  The choices are radio buttons — the mode in a pop-over of its row, which shows the value chosen, the hints as ticks, the speed as segments — and voicing a switch, which change nothing until "Save" (WCAG 3.2.2, sources 8.1). Save waits for a change, `aria-disabled`. `UpdateProfile` gets only the fields changed against how the form showed the profile. A value the build reads its own way — an unknown mode as "by steps", `ru-RU` as `ru` — goes only if the adult changed it: `app` does not write back what it does not know, as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) asks. Voicing on and its speed go apart, as `Voicing` carries them. With nothing changed there is no request, and the status line says "Nothing has changed"; saved, "Saved"; a refusal or a failure is its words under the form, and the choice stays. An edit made while the request goes stays an edit after the answer: the answer removes only those that went. A failure of the session moves the session, as in the other forms: a sign-in that cannot be extended, "account gone", and `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE` — the profile was deleted on another device —, on which `app` asks `GetAccount` anew: that erases the profile on the device, and the section returns to the first page without it.
- **Leaving with changes not saved.** While the form holds a change not saved — a pseudonym not checked yet among them —, a press that would leave the page asks first: the section's close, the page's way back, another row of the list and the link Privacy (`useLeave` and `useHold` in `src/adults/leave.tsx`). The question is a modal `<dialog>`, `alertdialog`, drawn as the grown-ups' PIN (`src/adults/UnsavedDialog.tsx`): "Save changes?", "The changes to your profile are not saved yet." — or "{pseudonym}'s profile" —, and Save, Don't Save and Cancel. Save sends the form as Save in the bar does and goes on once it has gone; a refusal or a failure stays on the page with its words. Don't Save drops the changes and goes on. Cancel and Escape stay, the focus back on what was pressed. The profile's own row asks nothing. A reload or a closed tab gets the browser's own question by `beforeunload`; the browser's Back is not held.
- **The answer and the other tabs.** The answer of `UpdateProfile` is the profile. `app` takes it whole but for the open attempt and the lesson runs, which stay as the tab knows them from `GetAccount`. The same profile goes to the other tabs by the `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-profiles` as a binary message `Profile`, and they do the same; a profile their account lacks, or a message that is not a profile, changes nothing. The fields the adult did not touch show what the other tab saved; the touched ones stay until the adult saves. Answers and messages have no order: the tab takes the profile that came last, so when two tabs save one profile at once, it may show not what `api` saved last until the next `GetAccount`, at the start or after a sign-in.
- **The language.** The whole of `app` — the section, the screens before a learner is chosen and the learner's screens — is in the device's language, chosen on the page Language, and `<html lang>` is it. The profile page neither shows nor sends the profile's `language`. A new owner profile gets the device's language in it: "On a new profile `app` sends the language of the section for adults", as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) and `ProfileSettings` in `account.proto` have it.

— *Why:*
- the address names no profile, so without it the profile is chosen on the first page again, as the attempt and the lesson are on Home;
- the section stands beside the learner's screens, not inside them, so that it opens with no learner chosen too;
- one form with "Save", because only the changed fields go and the adult changes them together: a write on each tap would give a request and a status line each, and a failure of one field amid editing others;
- all of the answer but the attempt and the runs: the tab knows those its own way from `GetAccount`, and the answer must neither bring back an attempt that ended nor lose one that began; the rest is `api`'s, and a field the contract adds comes without an edit of a list;
- `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE`, a new `GetAccount`: a profile `api` lacks is not needed on the device, and its form saves nothing;
- order without a version: `Profile` has no version or time of change, the contracts text does not say how `api` orders simultaneous `UpdateProfile` calls, and two adults saving one profile in one second are rare while the account holds one owner profile;
- `BroadcastChannel`, the same way between tabs as the own key's; a binary `Profile`, because a tab of another build reads it by its own contract and protobuf skips a field it does not know;
- the question before leaving: the form saves nothing by itself, so a close pressed after a change would drop it without a word; Save in the question saves the trip back to the page;
- `beforeunload` for a reload and a closed tab: the browser asks in its own words, and the page cannot ask in its own there;
- one language, the device's: a language is set in one place and applies to everything, and a second one on each profile would repeat the first;
- no notice in the list: Settings holds what an adult sets, and a notice is no setting;
- the legal pages by a quiet link, not rows: Help holds them, and Connecticut's law asks only that a link with the word "privacy" be in the settings.

— *Rejected:*
- the browser's Back held too — a change of the hash has already happened when the page hears of it, and a page put back by the history would leave a step of it behind;
- saving by itself on leaving — the adult did not press Save, and a change made by mistake would go;
- the entry on the choice of the learner too — the choice keeps the mockup's screen `who`, which has no such button;
- "Grown-ups" a place of the row, with the hint mode and This device in a pop-over of its own — it is no place, and the settings would stand in three parts: the pop-over, the section and the profile's menu;
- the profile in the hash, `#/adults/profile/<ID>` — the address names no profile;
- the section inside the learner's screens, under `App` — it would not open from the choice of a learner;
- a write on each tap, as the buttons of the device's language do — the reason above; those write only to the device;
- a `<select>` for the mode — radio buttons hold WCAG 3.2.2 as well;
- the language of the learner's screens on each profile, a row of its page — the reason above;
- the whole answer in place of the profile, with the attempt and the runs — the reason above;
- a list of the settings fields `app` takes from the answer — a field the contract adds would be lost;
- forbidding edits while the request goes — the form would freeze, and an edit during it stays anyway;
- in another tab, rereading `GetAccount` on the signal — its loading takes down the learner's screens, mid-task too;
- "Close the section" as the browser's "Back" — a section opened by the address or after a reload would leave `app`;
- "New versions of the texts" at the end of the list, or on the choice of the learner — the reason above, and the choice keeps the mockup's `who`; the bell of Home's frame tells of them.

— *Does not decide:* the section's PIN — E4; the learner's grade — with the first module whose manifest names grades; the children's profiles and "ask before showing" — E4.
