— *Consequence:* `src/adults/` — `AdultSection.tsx`, `SectionScreen.tsx` and `ProfilePage.tsx`; `src/account/settings.ts`; `Session.profileChanged` and `Session.profileFailed`, and `Session.createOwnerProfile` with the screen's language; `AccountGate` — the section and the channel; `src/app/LearnerMenu.tsx` — "Settings"; `setDeviceLanguage` in `src/i18n/page.ts`; `AttemptContext` and `LessonContext` — by the profile `GetAccount` gave; the route `ADULTS` in `src/app/hash.ts`; the keys `adults.*` in the dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R30. The adult turns the storage of works on the server on and off on the profile page in the section for adults. Its control is a switch, "Keep works on the server"; turning it on is a consent under the switch: the text `storage-consent` that `GetLegalTexts` gives in force, in the section's language, with the next version below it when there is one, the words `spec` gives the switch as a string of `app`, and the tick "I give my explicit consent", which holds to the version shown; `SetWorkStorage` is `ACTION_ADULT` with the current version, and on `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` the text is read anew and the consent is asked again, as at account creation. Turning it off is at once, without a question, with a line that the copies on the server are erased. While storage is on, `app` puts each work by `PutWork` as it writes it to the device, and "This device" warns at a profile that its works are only here until every work it keeps there is on the server.** `api` keeps the works by `WorkService`, and the canvas draws the picture by `exportPicture()`. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **Where.** Under the settings form on the profile page `#/adults/profile`, a part of its own, "Keeping works on the server". Not a field of the form: storage has its own consent, its own request and its own response, and the settings' "Save" does not send it.
- **What is visible.** Whether storage is on — by `Profile.work_storage`, as a line with the role `status`, so that a change from this tab or from another is heard by a screen reader.
- **Turning on.** The switch "Keep works on the server", off, opens the consent under it, the switch staying off, and gives focus to the consent's heading; pressed again, it closes the consent:
  - the text `storage-consent` through `LegalText` from `src/legal/` — the gist in the language that `api` found, a line that the English text is in force, a translation, if the version carries one, and the body —, and below it the next version, when there is one, with the day it applies from. `versionsOf` in `src/legal/texts.ts` gives both. The texts are read when the consent is opened, in the section's language — the device's language — past the HTTP cache, as at account creation;
  - the words of the switch: "A work may tell something personal, about health and family too. Nobody reads it" — the description of the tick, `aria-describedby`;
  - the tick "I give my explicit consent to keeping this profile's works on the server, with what they tell about health" — the explicit consent to what the work reveals about health (Art. 9(2)(a) GDPR, sources 3.4 spec). It holds to the version at which it was set: if another is shown, it is cleared;
  - "Switch it on" and "Cancel". Without the tick — the field's error and focus on it, no request, and a tick set clears the error; while the text is not read, the button is disabled, and a failure to read gives its words and "Try again". While the request goes, the button is `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`: focus does not fall off it, and a second press sends nothing.

  `SetWorkStorage` is `profile_id`, `ACTION_ADULT`, `enabled` and the `LegalTextRef` of the current version alone; with no text in force, without `text`, as the contract orders, and the tick with its words is needed all the same. `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` clears the tick, says that the text has changed, reads it anew and puts focus on the tick; while the text is being read, the previous one stays on the screen. Another refusal or failure — its words under the buttons, and the consent stays open and ticked. After the response the consent goes, and focus goes to the switch, now on; after "Cancel", to the switch, still off. Storage that another tab turned on while the consent is open here closes the consent — with focus on the switch — and the consent does not come back by itself afterwards.
- **Turning off** — the switch, on, at once, without a question and without a text: withdrawal is as easy as giving (Art. 7(3) GDPR, sources 3.3 spec). Its description while it is on: "It stops at once, and the copies of this profile's works on the server are erased. The works on this device stay". While the request goes, the switch is `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`: focus does not fall off it. It takes the marks of the works off, since the server erased its copies, and a `PutWork` still going marks nothing when it comes back. Each start takes them off every profile whose storage `GetAccount` names off, switched off elsewhere; one switched off and on again elsewhere between two starts keeps marks of copies gone, Д36.
- **The response** is the profile, and its path is the same as that of `UpdateProfile`: `Session.profileChanged` and the `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-profiles` to the other tabs. A failure that belongs to the session moves it too — `Session.profileFailed`.
- **When a work goes.** While `Profile.work_storage` is on, `app` puts a work whenever it writes it to the device: at each submission of a task and of an essay, and as its attempt closes — read before the device lets the attempt go, sent after, so that the screen waits for no network. Switching the storage on in this tab puts every work the profile keeps on this device but an empty one, each read in its turn. A tab sends its works one after another, in the order they were read, so that the last one read stands on the server, and a work it put already — the same history and contexts, by their SHA-256 — does not go again: the close after the last submission sends nothing.
- **What goes.** `StoredWork`, as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) has the storage of works, is `attempt_id`, the history as the JSON of its array in UTF-8, each submission as the JSON of its context with its `history_length`, in order, and the picture, which the canvas draws of the history on an element off the page; no `kind` and no `stored_at`. An essay's submissions go as any task's. The size is the sum of the bytes of the history, the contexts and the picture, and a work over 4,194,304 bytes does not go.
- **The answers.** Kept — the work's record marks how long a history and how many submissions the server keeps. `CAUSE_WORK_TOO_LARGE`, or `resource_exhausted` with no `Refusal` — the work stays on this device alone. `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF` — the profile is read again by `GetAccount` and goes to the session and the other tabs, as the answer of `SetWorkStorage` does, and the marks go; no other work of the tab goes until the profile it holds says off. `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN` of `PutWork` is no withdrawal, and asks no `CheckPackages`. `unimplemented` — an `api` with no `WorkService`: nothing is marked, and no reload is offered. Any other refusal, and a network that failed — no mark, and the next `PutWork` of the attempt carries the work again.
- **"This device"** lifts the warning that the works are only here, at a profile, only while its storage is on and every work the device keeps of it — each attempt — is marked as far as the device holds it, an attempt with no stroke and no submission needing no mark; or when the device keeps nothing of it. A work over 4 MiB, a refusal and a network that failed leave the warning. `copiedOnServer` in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx` decides it.
- **A learner with their own consent** — E4. Their storage they decide themselves, `ACTION_LEARNING`, and to an adult action `api` refuses with `CAUSE_LEARNER_DECIDES`: the section says this in the words of the refusal, and builds no view of its own for such a profile in E3 — the account has only the owner profile, `CONSENT_GIVER_OWNER`. The screen that tells a teenager an adult turned their storage on is E4's too.

— *Why:*
- the text of the consent is a legal text with a version, not a string of `app`; but the tick needs its own label, and `spec` names what the switch says. The label and the description of the tick say it as a string of `app`, and what binds is the text whose version the consent record writes. Without a text in force — a self-hosting copy without `storage-consent` — the words of the switch stay on the screen;
- the consent under the switch, not on a page of its own, like the disclosures of the texts on the invite code screen: the adult does not leave the profile page, and focus and return are as with the question before erasing;
- a tick and a button, not one button "I agree": explicit consent is a separate affirmative act, and the invite code screen is built the same way;
- the warning goes by works: a work the server does not keep exists only here, whatever `work_storage` says, and until it is kept the adult is warned of its loss;
- the picture of an element off the page, not of the one on the task screen: an attempt closed and a work sent as the storage is switched on have no screen, and the canvas draws the same strokes the same way.

— *Rejected:*
- the words of the switch instead of the text, as a string of `app` — the consent record does not hold the version of a string of `app`;
- the consent on a page of its own, `#/adults/storage` — leaving the profile page for the sake of one switch;
- a question before turning off — withdrawal must be as easy as giving;
- the warning by the profile, or none with storage on — a work that did not reach the server would lie only here with no warning;
- `app` drawing the picture by `replay(history)` — a second drawing of the same work, unlike the sheet;
- the crop of the last submission — not what the contract names, on a workbook not one picture, and before the first submission nothing to send.

— *Does not decide:* the list of a profile's works on the server and the erasure of one — Д26; going on with a work of another device by `GetWork` — Д27; a learner with their own consent and the teenager's screen — E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/works/`; `src/adults/WorkStorage.tsx` — the part and the consent; `ProfilePage` — the part under the form; `STORAGE_TEXT` and `versionsOf` in `src/legal/texts.ts`; `copiedOnServer` in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`; the keys `storage.*` in the dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Legal pages

**R31. The legal pages are screens of their own, `#/legal` in the address's hash, in the device's language, visible both before sign-in and after, each a sheet of the mockup's legal text: a list and a page each for the privacy policy, the terms of use and the consent to the storage of works — the texts that `GetLegalTexts` gives in force, open, with their next version —, for who runs this copy, for "Accessibility" and for the notices of third-party code. They are reached by "Read the full text" of the sheets of the privacy policy and the terms of use — which "Privacy and legal information" at the foot of the way in's card opens, as do the names in the tick at account creation and Help —, and by the button "Read the privacy policy and other texts" on the first page of the section for adults. The page of notices takes `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` from the bundle, where the build puts them, not from the hosting's `/NOTICE`. About accessibility, people write to the operator of the copy, whom the section Who we are of the privacy policy names, and a button of the page leads to it.** After sign-in the bell of Home's frame tells of a next version. The texts of "Accessibility" are proofread by the author line by line. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **Routes.** `#/legal` is the list, `#/legal/<page>` the pages: `privacy-policy`, `terms-of-use` and `storage-consent` by the text's ID, `#/legal/<id>/next` their next version, `operator`, `accessibility` and `notices`. A page the build does not know leads to the list in its own place in the history, like an unknown page of the section. The family's texts — `parent-consent`, `teen-consent`, `child-notice` and `teen-notice` — get no pages: the child consent screens show them, E4.
- **Where.** `AccountGate` shows the pages at any step of the session — before sign-in, on the choice of a learner and in place of the learner's screens — before the section for adults. They need only the client of `api`, which exists before sign-in: `GetLegalTexts` goes without a token. The language is the device's: the sign-in screen and the section that open them are both in it, and "Accessibility" takes the language of the screen it was opened from.
- **The sheet.** Each page's heading is in the sheet's head; "Back to the list" is a back before it on any page but the list, whose heading alone is "Privacy and legal information"; "Close" × at its other end leads to where the pages were opened from: from the sign-in screen, home, that is, to sign-in again, and from the section, to its first page. `AccountGate` remembers where only in memory, like the section's profile: after a reload "Close" leads home. It leads to the section only while its account is open: if the account signed out meanwhile — in another tab or by a revoked token — it leads home, or the next sign-in would land right in the section.
- **The button with the word "privacy".** Connecticut's law asks for a link with the word "privacy" on the start screen and in the settings (CGS §42-520(b)(2), sources 3.9 spec). The start screen before sign-in is the sign-in screen: under the sign-in button, "Privacy and legal information", «Конфиденциальность и правовая информация». The settings are the section for adults: under its list the quiet link "Privacy", «Конфиденциальность», to the list of the legal pages. A string of `app` is in the screen's language, so on a Russian screen the word is «конфиденциальность». It is a button, not an `<a>` link: so every move between screens in `app` is built — an HTML button, a target of 44 × 44 and a label on it.
- **A text page** has its version in a chip under the heading, "Version {version}, in force since {date}" by `in_force_since`, with `Intl.DateTimeFormat` of the screen's language — a day not in the format `YYYY-MM-DD`, or not in the calendar, like 2026-02-30, written as it came —; then, open, the gist with its own `lang` below its caption, the next version in a note, the line that the English text is in force, the translation "for reading only" where the version carries one, and the English body, `lang="en"`, its headings from the second level. It is the invite code screen's disclosure without `<details>`: `LegalContent` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx`. The text is read at each opening, in the screen's language and past the HTTP cache. No version in force gives "This copy of the app has no version of this text in force", as does an `api` without `LegalService`; a failure gives its words and "Try again".
- **"Accessibility"** is strings of the dictionaries of `app`, properties of the build, the same in all copies: the standard, WCAG 2.2 AA, and beyond it 2.5.5 and 2.3.3; the places where `app` does not meet it yet, each with its criteria, why, what there is instead and when this changes; how it is checked — by the automated checks of CI, while no checks by people with screen readers have been done (Д10); and whom to turn to — the operator of this copy, by the button "Who runs this copy and how to reach them" to `#/legal/operator`, with a request not to write a child's personal data, as the policy itself asks.
- **Who runs this copy**, `#/legal/operator`, is the section Who we are of the policy in force; the list leads to it by an item after the three texts.
- **The notices of third-party code** are the `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` of this build as they are, each in a `<pre lang="en">` with long lines wrapped, so that at 320 px the page does not slide sideways. Their copy `src/legal/notices.ts` takes them from the repository's files as text at build time — Vite's `?raw` import —; a test in Node compares it with the files byte for byte, and `scripts/release.ts` compares the archive's `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` with the same files. The page sends no request of its own.
- **Consent withdrawal for an owner without children.** The owner's own learning rests on the contract, Art. 6(1)(b) GDPR, not on consent (sources 3.3 spec), and accepting the policy and the terms at `CreateAccount` is no Art. 6(1)(a) consent to withdraw: leaving the contract is deleting the account, E4. The one consent such an owner gives and can withdraw in E3 is the storage of works on the server, which the profile page removes at once, without a question. So a new version of a text offers to switch storage off before it applies: a line on the next version's page and, while a profile keeps its works, in the notice of next versions; from E4, to delete the account too.

— *Why:*
- `#/legal` is outside the section for adults: the pages are needed before sign-in, and the section lives only while the account is open;
- a copy of `NOTICE` in the bundle, not the hosting's `/NOTICE`: `app` loads no parts of its own at run time — it gathers everything of its own into the build — and from the hosting reads only `/deployment.json`, which is not part of the build. `/NOTICE` is part of the build, and a page that read it would depend on the hosting: `vite preview` does not serve it — it is in the archive, not in `build/` —, a self-hosting copy might not put it out, and the browser checks no bytes: the rollout of `infra` compares `/NOTICE` with the build, and self-hosting is not bound to. The CSP would allow both paths — `connect-src 'self'` — but the bundle's copy needs no request. The price is about 20 KB of text in the bundle. Apache-2.0 names such a place itself, in clause 4(d): "within a display generated by the Derivative Works, if and wherever such third-party notices normally appear" (the repository's `LICENSE`);
- the operator's contact is the policy's section Who we are: the contract has no method that gives a contact (`legal.proto`), the operator's name and contacts are in the texts that a copy's `api` gives from its operator, and every version of the policy holds the section by the rule `legal.operator`; the terms refer to it too — "its contacts are in "Who we are" of our privacy policy" (`terms-of-use` of version 1). A field of its own in the build or in `deployment.json` would be a second home of the operator beside its texts;
- the list is its buttons without reading the texts: it does not wait for the network, and a text page itself says that there is no text in force;
- the family's texts have no pages: their place is the consent screens of E4, and a page with nothing to give yet would only confuse.

— *Rejected:*
- `/NOTICE` and `/LICENSE` from the hosting's origin — the reasons above;
- the pages inside the section for adults, `#/adults/legal` — they could not be opened before sign-in;
- a page for each text that `GetLegalTexts` gives — the family's texts without the screens of E4;
- the text as a closed disclosure `<details>`, as on the invite code screen — the page has no other business, and a closed text would only hide it;
- a link `<a href="#/legal">` instead of a button — the reason above;
- "Close" as the browser's "Back" — pages opened by the address would leave the pages of `app`, as the section's would;
- the English "Privacy" on a Russian screen — a string of `app` is in the screen's language; if the laws of the states require the English word in other languages too, the author will say so when proofreading the strings;
- showing a new version of the policy at the first start after it came into force — this is not "before it applies", as the part "Changes to this policy" of version 2 promises and §42-520(b)(3) orders (sources 3.11 spec).

— *Does not decide:* the words of the page "Accessibility" — the author proofreads them; account deletion and the family's consents — E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx` — the list, the pages and their sheet; `src/legal/notices.ts` — the build's `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`; `LegalContent` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx`; `PAGE_TEXTS` in `src/legal/texts.ts`; the route `LEGAL` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the pages at any step and where they were opened from; the button on the sign-in screen and on the first page of the section; `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` in `server.fs.allow` of `vite.config.ts`; the keys `legal.*` and `accessibility.*` in the dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R32. A module's license is a page of `app` of its own: the `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of the device part of one version as they are, in `<pre lang="en">`, with the module by its manifest above them — the name, the version and the license as an SPDX identifier. `app` takes the files from `api` by a request without a token by the package digest, and does not open them by a link. The page is reached by its address, `#/license/<digest>`, visible at any step, a sheet of the mockup's legal text whose × goes home. A card of Subjects names the license among the facts of its manifest under ⋯, as text.**
- **What is on the page.** The heading "The module's license and notices", in the sheet's head beside its ×; a line that the module's author gives it under this license; the module by its manifest — the name with its own `lang`, the version and the license; "License (LICENSE)" and "Notices (NOTICE)" — by the headings of the page of notices of third-party code, but the license first: the page is about it — each as text as it is. The page reads the manifest apart from the files: a manifest that takes long or does not come does not hold up the texts, and the module is then not named.
- **Responses**, as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) has the files of a device part. `200` is UTF-8 text, as the part carries it. `404` for `NOTICE` is "The module has no file NOTICE": `NOTICE` is not required in a package. `404` for `LICENSE` is "No file LICENSE came with the module": the format builds no package without it, and the page does not pretend it is there. `410` is "This version of the module is no longer given", and `CheckPackages` is asked, as at any `410`; the files are read one after another, `LICENSE` first, so that for a withdrawn version `410` comes once and `CheckPackages` is asked once. `503` with `Retry-After` — a retry after the named time, no longer than 30 s, and a network failure — and a response body broken off on the way — a retry after the client's growing pauses, and then its words and "Try again". This is `src/packages/files.ts` and its text call.
- **Without a link.** The file's address is the origin of `api`, another origin, and `app` has no external links; an `<a>` would also lead away from the pages of `app`. The response goes through the browser's HTTP cache, like media files, and `app` does not store it.
- **`#/license/<digest>`** is shown by `AccountGate` at any step, like the legal pages: the files and the manifest need no token, the page survives a reload, and the test of the built page checks it under the CSP header before sign-in. Its × goes to `#/home`, Home while a learner is chosen. The language is the device's. The package digest in the address is an ID of content, not of a learner; a route without a digest leads home in its own place in the history.
- **`lang="en"`** on the texts: a module's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` are in the language of its license, not its explanation language, as the module format in `spec/format/README.md` has it, and the official modules are Apache-2.0 in English. A third-party module under a license in another language waits for О-10, which licenses it may carry.

— *Why:*
- Apache-2.0, clause 4(a), orders giving the recipient a copy of the license — "You must give any other recipients of the Work or Derivative Works a copy of this License" (the repository's `LICENSE`) — and with the deployment the package travels to others. The page of `app` gives it without going to another origin;
- one route outside the section, not a page inside the learner's screens: it is visible by its address after a reload and before sign-in, and its sheet is that of the legal pages;

— *Rejected:*
- a link to `/packages/…/device/LICENSE` at `api` — the reason above;
- a copy of the modules' licenses in the bundle, like the build's `NOTICE` — the modules come from `api` after the build, and the build does not know their versions;
- "License" on the cards of Home — Home's cards are the learner's work, and the catalog's are where a module is connected;
- "License" on each card of Subjects — a button no learner presses, beside what the card says of the module; the license is a fact of the card's ⋯;
- a page in the learner's screens under their header — after a reload it would wait for the choice of a learner;
- `lang` by the manifest on the texts of the license — the module's explanation language is not the language of its license.

— *Does not decide:* a way to the page from a screen — it went with the profile's modules page in the section, Д57.

— *Consequence:* `src/packages/LicensePage.tsx` — the page, its sheet, `licenseRoute` and `LicensePages`; `src/packages/license.ts` — `readLicense`; `text` and `isDigest` in `src/packages/files.ts`; the license among the facts of a card in `src/subjects/SubjectsPage.tsx`; the route `MODULE_LICENSE` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the page at any step; the keys `license.*` in the dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Tutor — AI

**R33. That the tutor is an AI `app` says in the adult's Help, whenever it is open, and on the page on the tutor; the tutor's button at the top right of Home, Subjects and the skill map is shut until the tutor comes there, and opens nothing; the tutor's panel opens with no such line. The learning materials on safe use are a page of `app` of its own, `#/tutor`, "How to use the AI tutor safely", in `en` and `ru`, visible at any step, in the device's language, and without external links; no screen leads to it yet (Д46). "Report a problem" is a button under the tutor's words at each of its replies — a rung, "Explain differently", the correct answer words, the feedback by a rubric — and a form in the panel's pop-over: one of six kinds of problem, a text of up to 1000 characters with a request not to write names, the words as the panel showed them, `ReportProblem` with one idempotency key per report, and the answer "It will be reviewed by …" by `review_by`. The button is seen by a learner of the category `adult`; a profile of another category does not have it until E4 decides by П-26.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The disclosure — where and when.** Help's first item, "The tutor is an AI", at every visit, not once "at the first meeting": an "already seen" mark would lie on the device and would be lost with erasing, on another device and in a private window. The panels of a task, a lesson's task, an essay, the page to pick from and the screen of solving open with the tutor's words or the program's lines alone — the screen of solving on "Hints for this task come here, a question at a time." Whether this is enough for a child, by the providers' terms below, is to settle before the first child (Д287 `spec`). The providers require it: Anthropic's guidelines for organizations serving minors and its Usage Policy, that the users be told they are interacting with an AI rather than a human, and OpenAI's guidance for users under 18, age-appropriate disclosures to minors about AI tools and how to use them responsibly (sources 11.1).
- **The learning materials — the content.** The terms require materials but do not say what is in them: Anthropic's guidelines ask for educational resources and guidance for minors on safe and responsible use of the product, OpenAI's guidance the disclosures above, and Anthropic's child safety guidance for developers says nothing about materials for learners; OpenAI's usage policies could not be read, and the materials do not rest on them (sources 11.1). So the content is what the platform's documents already say about the tutor, in its own words for the learner, in six parts:
  - the tutor is an AI, not a person, and whether an answer is right a program decides by the task's key (`privacy-policy` of version 2, "Where AI comes in");
  - it can be wrong, and work that cannot be read it asks to write again (`terms-of-use` of version 1); "Explain differently" is once per rung;
  - what goes to the AI and what never goes, and the picture shows everything on the sheet — so no names and nothing else personal. Which providers the tutor calls the page does not name, but refers to the privacy policy: a self-hosting copy has its own texts, and with an own key there is its own provider;
  - it is for learning: it gives hints and does not solve the task;
  - it is not someone to talk to about personal things: about health, family and feelings, talk with a person one can trust, and if the tutor asks for something personal or says something frightening, stop and tell;
  - if something is wrong, "Report a problem"; the operator reviews within 3 days, the words and the text are stored as written, and in danger now, call the emergency service without waiting for the review. The part ends with the button "Who runs the app" to `#/legal/operator`, the section Who we are of the policy in force, in the device's language, whose close comes back to `#/tutor`, before signing in too.
- **One text for all learners**, while all of them are adults. In Russian it does not address the learner, like all the learner's screens, and the lines are short. Texts by age — OpenAI's "age-appropriate" — will come with the children's screens in E4, beside `child-notice` and `teen-notice`.
- **Where the page is** — a route of its own, `#/tutor`, like a module's license: `AccountGate` shows it at any step, before sign-in too — there is nothing about anyone in it — in the language of the learner's screens while a learner is chosen, otherwise the device's, as a sheet of the mockup's legal text, its heading "How to use the AI tutor safely" and its close × home. A part of the hash the page does not know leads to the page itself in its own place in the history. Which screen leads to it is open: the button "How to use the tutor safely" under the lists of Home went with its part — Д46.
- **The form** opens in the panel's pop-over, with the words quoted at its top and focus on its heading; "Cancel" closes it and brings focus back to the button. The kind is radio buttons in the contract's order, and without a kind the report does not go; "Something else" goes only with a text: "Something else, which the text says" (`ProblemKind` in `attempt.proto`). The text is `maxLength` 1000: the field counts UTF-16 code units, and a text has no fewer of them than the code points that the contract counts; spaces at the edges are trimmed. Under the field, a request to write no names, neither one's own nor others'; below, that the tutor's words will go with the report and that in danger now one must call the emergency service.
- **The tutor's words and reply** come from one state of the task screen: what the panel shows is what goes, with the reply that brought it. The words of `GetRungWords` and the hints after a wrong answer are `TUTOR_REPLY_RUNG` with the rung's number from the response; the words with which the attempt went on after a reload are too, with the number from `RungHint`: so the contract names them, although after "Explain differently" the attempt already holds its words; "Explain differently" is `TUTOR_REPLY_EXPLAINED_DIFFERENTLY` with the number of the rung whose words it replaced; after a correct answer, `TUTOR_REPLY_CORRECT_ANSWER`, without a rung; the feedback by a rubric, `TUTOR_REPLY_ESSAY_FEEDBACK`. A "Hint" that gave the same words once more leaves them their previous reply and the begun form; if "Explain differently" did not find the words of a rung on the screen, the number is by the count of wrong answers, from 1 to 3. Other words are another form: the previous one, open or sent, goes together with its words.
- **The key** is `idempotencyKey()` from `src/api/keys.ts`, one per report as the form holds it. A retry — by the client at `unavailable` or by the button after a failure — goes with the same key, and `api` answers with the same report, without creating a second, as [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md) has it. Another kind or another text is another report, with a new key: the same key with another request would get `CAUSE_KEY_ALREADY_USED`. A refusal ends the report, and the next "Send" is with a new key. `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` the client waits out itself, up to 30 s, and then the screen says "No connection", and the key stays.
- **The answer** is "Thank you: the report is sent. It will be reviewed by {date}": the day of `review_by` by the device's clock, in the long date of the screen's language; without `review_by`, without a day. Focus is on this line, and the button is no longer at these words; at new words it is there again.
- **Who sees the button** — a learner of the category `adult`: the "adult of the account" to whom `spec` gives the report. A profile of another category has no button, but has the disclosure and the materials. How an adult reports at a tutor's reply to a child, and whether a teenager reports themselves, E4 will decide together with П-26: the method is a learning request, so the answer is to whom `app` shows the button.

— *Why:*
- the disclosure and the materials are measures that `spec` assigns to `app` for all learners, and "report a problem" is a measure of the platform itself beyond the providers' terms; without them invite codes do not go to other adults;
- Help says it at every visit and stores nothing; the panel keeps its room for the work, the tutor being an AI plain to anyone;
- the materials say only what the platform's documents have already decided: facts about providers that cannot be checked against the primary source on that day do not appear on the page;
- the form over the task screen, not a page of its own: leaving the task screen would lose the words after a correct answer — the server does not store them — and the attempt is already closed;
- a key per report, not per press — the same technique as for the check with its work: a retry does not create a second report, and an edit is already another report.

— *Rejected:*
- the tutor's pop-over at the top right, with the disclosure and the ways to the work — the tutor does not come to Home, Subjects or the map yet, and a panel there would promise it: the button waits shut, as the cognitive map does;
- the disclosure once, by a screen at the first meeting with the tutor — a mark on the device gets lost;
- a part "The tutor is an AI" under the lists of Home, with the button to the materials — home stays uncluttered;
- the line "The tutor is an AI, not a person" as the first bubble of every panel — plain to anyone;
- the materials among the legal pages `#/legal/…` — those are in the device's language and close to where they were opened from by the tab's memory, while the materials are for the learner, in the language of the learner's screens;
- a button to the materials in the tutor's panel — it leaves the task screen, as a page for the form would;
- the form as a page of its own — the reason above;
- the button for all learners now — `spec` gives it to the adult, and who has it at a child's profile П-26 decides;
- words of its own for `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` — the client waits it out itself up to 30 s, and it reaches the screen as "No connection";
- counting code points instead of `maxLength` — a field with a limit is simpler and lets nothing extra through, and it is too strict only for a text made of characters outside the BMP alone.

— *Does not decide:* the texts of the materials by age — E4, with the children's screens; the button at the profile of a child and of a teenager — E4, by П-26; which screen leads to `#/tutor` — Д46.

— *Consequence:* `src/tutor/report.ts` — `Said`, `KINDS`, `TEXT_LIMIT`, `unsendable` and `reportRequest`; `src/tutor/ReportProblem.tsx` — the button and the form; `src/tutor/TutorPage.tsx` — the page and `TutorPages`; `src/app/Help.tsx` — the disclosure; `ShutTutor` of `src/app/Shell.tsx` — the tutor shut at the top right; `src/task/TutorWords.tsx` — the place for the button; `src/task/TaskScreen.tsx` — the property `report`; `src/task/Task.tsx` — the words with their reply and rung, `Said`, and the button for a learner `adult`; the route `TUTOR` in `src/app/hash.ts`; `AccountGate` — the page at any step; the keys `tutor.*` and `report.*` in the dictionaries; `.nt-report` and the field `textarea` in `theme.css`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R34. The learner's skill map is the screen `#/skill-map/<module ID>`, the module's map by the element `skill-map` with `view="learner"` in the language of the learner's screens, in the frame of the places, as a place of Subjects; `#/skill-map` alone goes to Subjects. The ways in are the skill map's button on a module's card on Home and "Skill map" on a connected card of Subjects, each at a module with a current version, and on a map the module beside its heading, whose pop-over chooses another. "Practise" on the map sends `ChooseNext` with the skill by the same path as "What's next", through the shared hook `useEngine`, and while an attempt is open, it first asks as "Something else" does. The modules that teach an anchor "unknown" `app` shows beside the map — by the part "Modules to connect" above it, as one list without repeats, with the name from the manifest of the current version and "Connect". The mark "sequential" is by the `skill_tiers` of the current version. The map's theme is the background, the text and the primary color of the theme of `app`.** The screen is the mockup's `map`. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **The version of `skill-map`** is in `package.json`, on the icon set of `canvas`. The element's own `@altnovatrail/spec`, which the lock file nests under it while the element names another version than `app`'s, is types alone: `ElementTakesViews` in `src/skills/jsx.ts` compares the element's properties with the types of `app`'s copy both ways, and a type that diverged is an error of `ci-lint`.
- **The screen** (`MapScreen` of `src/skills/SkillMapScreen.tsx`): "Subjects" back above the heading "Skill map", and beside it the module — its tile and its title in its own language, a pill named "Module: {module}" —, whose pop-over lists the modules connected with a current version, the one shown ticked; below them a white frame, a region named by the title "Skill map: {module}", which scrolls inside, the keyboard by the focus of the buttons in it, with the hint how to choose a skill, the line of the choice, the modules to connect, the level set by an adult and the element, the headings of its tiers as `h2`; a skill chosen on the map lights its chain, and its card stands at the foot of the window, over the tab bar on a phone. The module ID is an ID of content, not of a learner; `#/skill-map` alone, or with more parts, goes to Subjects in its own place in the history.
- **The map's data** is `GetSkillMap` with `ACTION_LEARNING`, and the graph with tiers and the manifest are open views from the shared cache by the digest from its response, so that the map and the state are on one version. A module without a graph has no map, and the screen says so. Above the element, "The level was set by an adult on {date}", by `level_set_at`, when an adult set the module's level, for every learner.
- **Choosing a skill** is `ChooseNext` with the skill's full ID, as the event `skillchoose` sends it, and then as with "What's next": the lesson opens, the task begins. The code of the choice is the hook `useEngine` in `src/next/`, shared with `NextStep`; the idempotency key is one per skill until a response or a refusal. While an attempt is open, "Practise" first asks in the same words as "Something else", and "Cancel" and "Go on" return focus to "Practise". While the choice goes, the status line says "Opening what's next…", and another press does nothing: `app` cannot disable a button inside the element. `api` holds the skill; "What's next" and "Something else" send the choice without a skill, and it returns to the engine. A module's card on Home sends it too, by its "Continue", when the module has neither a lesson begun nor the task open: the skill is the one its map gives the card — a chain's skill to work on, else the first begun, due, or ready to start —, and the task the engine gives opens; with an attempt of another module or of paper open, no such skill, nothing given or a failure, the card opens the module's map, which asks and says why. Rejected: the map on every "Continue", a step more before a task, and the choice without a skill, which may give another module's task.
- **Modules for an anchor — beside the map**: `spec` leaves showing them to `app`; the element does not read `teaching_modules`, and `app` does. The part is above the map, under the hint and the line of the choice, so that it is seen before a long map. In it are the modules of all anchors whose mastery is "unknown" or a value the build does not know (so the contract orders) — each once, by name. The name is from the manifest of the current version by the digest from `ListCatalog`, as Subjects names it. A module that the catalog does not give the profile is "Not available now" without a button: `ConnectModule` would reject it. After "Connect", the line "Connected: it is on Home now" with focus, and the map's state is read anew in place.
- **The theme** — `--novatrail-skill-map-paper`, `-ink` and `-accent` — is `--nt-background`, `--nt-text` and `--nt-primary`: their pairs already hold a contrast of 4.5 : 1 in the theme's test, and the map looks like the screens of `app`.
- **The mark "sequential"** is `isSequential`, by the `skill_tiers` of the open view of the current version's graph, from the shared cache — the line "In order" under the title of a card of Subjects and of the section for adults, and the kind "Sequential" of a card on Home. The cards do not wait for the graphs: the mark appears when the graph has come, by the hook `useSequential` or the card's counts — and a graph that has not come gives no mark.

— *Why:*
- the ways in on the cards of Home and Subjects: a module's map is one press from its card, on the learner's home screen and the catalog, and the module beside the heading changes the map without going back;
- one code of the choice for both entrances: `ChooseNext` with a skill and without differs by one field, and the start of a task and of a lesson, the retry with a key and `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` are shared;
- the modules beside the map do not wait for a new release of `skill-map` and a decision of `spec`, and the learner sees the anchor on the map too, in its band of anchors, with the mastery "unknown";
- the name from the manifest of the current version is what the learner will see on Home too after connecting.

— *Rejected:*
- npm `overrides` of the element's `@altnovatrail/spec` to `app`'s version: against what the package names: the element is checked by its tests on its own;
- a list of the maps of all modules at `#/skill-map` — Subjects lists the modules, each with its map, and the pill beside the heading changes the module;
- modules by anchors — the anchor's heading and its modules: `app` has no names of anchors — they are in the shared taxonomy of the format's release and built into the element, not exported — and an anchor's ID is not a word for a learner;
- a row «в реестр spec» so that the element itself shows the modules for an anchor: the element, without the network and without `ConnectModule`, would call `app` anyway, and `spec` already gives this to `app`;
- the part under the map — a long map would take it off the screen, and at 320 px off several;
- choosing a skill without a question with an open attempt — `ChooseNext` would close it silently.

— *Does not decide:* a line for a child that an adult sees their progress — E4; ranks on the map — О-13 and `skill-map`.

— *Consequence:* `src/skills/` — `jsx.ts`, `map.ts`, `SkillMap.tsx`, `SkillMapScreen.tsx`; `src/next/useEngine.ts`; `src/subjects/ModuleCards.tsx` — the cards of the modules with one button each, `ModuleCardList` and `useConnectedCards`; `isSequential` and `sequentialOf` in `src/subjects/modules.ts`, the hook `src/subjects/useSequential.ts`, `skillToWork` in `src/subjects/states.ts`; the route `SKILL_MAP` in `src/app/hash.ts`; the keys `skillmap.*` and `subjects.sequential` in the dictionaries; the theme's variables in `src/theme/theme.css`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Progress in the section for adults

**R35. Settings has no page of a profile's progress: the attempts, the repeating traps, the words of the reason codes and a module's map in the adult's view are not shown, and `app` calls no `GetProgress`. A test in Node holds that no source of `src/` reads `GetProgress` or the field `reason_code`.** The adult's view of progress returns as a place of its own, by its mockup, Д56.
- **What went with it.** The pages `#/adults/progress` and `#/adults/progress/<module ID>`, the profile's modules page `#/adults/modules` with its licenses, and their rows on the profile page; the words of the nine codes; the adult's correction of a module's level on its map.

— *Why:*
- Settings holds what an adult sets, as its mockup has it: a page of reading is no setting;
- the reason code stays the adult's alone: a learner's screen never named it, and with no screen of the adult's no screen does;
- the code went with the pages: kept with no screen, nothing a user reaches would test it.

— *Rejected:*
- the rows "Progress" and "Modules" kept on the profile page — the reason above;
- the pages kept by their addresses alone, with no row — a page no screen leads to.

— *Does not decide:* where the adult's progress returns, and the line telling the child that the adult sees it — Д56, E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/adults/progress.node.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

**R36. Voicing in code is the module `src/voice/`: one synthesis for the app, the voice by the rules of voicing, and the platform by `navigator.userAgentData.platform`, without it by the user agent string. The "Slow" speed is `rate` 0.75 until a check on devices finds the number (Д5). The permission of voicing on Android and Linux and the voice chosen for a language are the record `voicing` of the device registry. The learner turns voicing on and off with the button "Voicing" in the head of the tutor's panel, or above the canvas while it is closed — `SetVoicing` — and the session takes the profile from the response. The check's verdict and the neutral phrase, the rung's words, the correct answer words, the line of the quota or of the platform AI limit, "Explain differently" and the hint sound by themselves, and "Read aloud" stands at the task statement, the tutor's words, the hint, the step, the explanation and the worked example. Signs sound as words by the table for `en` and `ru`. Where a text will not sound, the button is inactive, and a line beside it describes it and does not serve as a status.** The author proofreads the words `voice.*` in `en` and `ru` and the table of signs. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md); the speed's number rests on the Web Speech API (sources 5.1).
- **Speed.** "Normal" is `rate` 1: the specification calls it the voice's default rate, "which should correspond to a normal speaking rate". "Slow" is 0.75, a quarter slower. `VOICING_SPEED_UNSPECIFIED` and a value the build does not know are "normal". The number is not checked: each engine reads `rate` its own way, and the specification speaks of it only relatively (sources 5.1). A check on devices finds it — Д5.
- **Platform.** The values "Android", "Chrome OS" and "Linux" of `userAgentData.platform` are these platforms. "Windows", "macOS" and "iOS" wait for no permission. "Fuchsia", "Unknown", an empty value and a browser without `userAgentData` go by the user agent string: "Android" before "CrOS", "CrOS" before "Linux" or "X11". iPadOS calls itself Mac, and there is no need to tell it apart: an empty utterance goes with each tap after which the tutor's words will come — "Check", "Hint", "Explain differently" — on all platforms, while voicing is on and permitted, and "Read aloud" speaks by itself within the tap.
- **The device's record.** The key `voicing` of the registry is `{allowed, voices}`, where `voices` is a `voiceURI` per language tag in lower case. Reading does not create the database, and a record that cannot be read means "nothing chosen and no permission". "Erase everything on this device" and the account gone or deleted erase it with the registry, and a sign-in of another account — `accountSeen` and `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS` — erases the record itself: the other account decides for itself; the owner's sign-out leaves it. A failure to write gives the line "The choice wasn't kept on this device", and what is shown stays as before. The page "This device" announces the record to other tabs by `BroadcastChannel`, and the learner's screens there read it again: a permission taken back holds at once. Until the record is read, nothing sounds, and the buttons are inactive without a line — the line would only flash.
- **The learner's button** is "Voicing", `aria-pressed`: in the head of the tutor's panel, beside the tutor's name, while the panel is shown, and in the lines above the canvas while it is closed. A button on the tutor's panel alone would not do: in the mode "self" the panel is closed before the solution, and the learner must turn voicing on before the words come. `SetVoicing` sends only `enabled`, and the speed stays as it was. The session takes the profile from the response as the profile page's save does, and tells other tabs about it. While the request is under way, the button is `aria-disabled`; a failure gives its words, `role="alert"`. Turning it off stops speech at once — by this button and by any other path: the profile page, another tab, a permission taken back. If voicing is on and the language does not sound, the line under the button says why.
- **What sounds by itself** — only when voicing is on, and on Android and Linux also permitted:
  - after "Check" — "Correct" or "Not right yet: …" and the neutral phrase of the AI call outcome, in the screen's language; after them, without cutting them off, the rung's words or the correct answer words, in the explanation language;
  - the line of the quota or of the platform AI limit, in the screen's language: under the rung's fallback text after the words, and after a correct answer without words after the verdict;
  - on "Hint" — what it gave: the givens, the unknown, the cards of techniques and the question, or the rung's words;
  - on "Explain differently" — the new words.

  Not sounding by themselves:
  - the lines at items — what was read and why it cannot be read: they stand at the item, not on the tutor's panel, and the screen reader reads them;
  - the task's solution;
  - the words with which the attempt opened after a reload: no tap called them.
- **"Read aloud"** is at the task statement above the work, at the tutor's words, at the hint, at the step, at the lesson's explanation and at the worked example. What sounds is listed in [architecture.md](architecture.md). In an example, a box the working has already filled sounds as its value, and a hidden working does not sound at all, with the boxes of its lines. A second tap while its text sounds stops it, a tap on another button cuts the earlier text off, and a button that leaves the screen — with a lesson's segment, with a hint — stops its own. A task screen that leaves stops what it said itself, and the words that came after it do not sound at all. The button sounds also with voicing off, and on Android and Linux only after the permission. The icon is `volume-up`, without fill, as it is from right to left: this is a value of `app`'s own, `read-aloud`.
- **The line "will not sound".** The button is not `disabled` but `aria-disabled`, so as not to lose focus. The line beside it — "This device has no voice for …" or "On this device, an adult turns voicing on first" — is the button's description, `aria-describedby`, not `role="status"`.
- **Signs in words** are a table for `en` and `ru` in [architecture.md](architecture.md), by the first subtag of the explanation language:
  - a sign is read as an action when there is an operand beside it on either side — a digit, a box or a bracket — and without an operand the sign is named;
  - a hyphen between numbers with spaces is a minus;
  - a fraction with a denominator from 2 to 12 is read by the name of its parts, otherwise "a over b" and «a делённое на b»;
  - a language without a table sounds with its signs as they are.

  The screen shows the text as it is.
- **The page "This device"**, the section "Voicing" after the profiles:
  - the languages are the device's and the explanation languages of the modules whose manifests the cache holds;
  - each language has a group of radio buttons: the candidates, with the voice taken now marked; a choice is written at once, without "Save";
  - the sample is in English or in Russian, in the voice's language: only for the build's languages, and on Android and Linux only after the permission;
  - a language without a voice has, in words, how to add a voice in the system;
  - a browser without Web Speech API has a line that it does not read aloud.

— *Why:*
- 0.75 is markedly slower, but not twice as slow: "half as fast", 0.5, is the edge, not the middle, and without a check on devices there is no reason to take the edge;
- `userAgentData` first: the specification names its values (sources 5.1), while the user agent string names no values of its own and is read by substrings. The string is a fallback for a browser without `userAgentData`;
- the button above the canvas while the panel is closed: it is seen in both modes before the first words, while the tutor's panel appears and disappears;
- the line is a description, not a status: it stands from the start, and a status at each voicing button would be read on every screen together with the text;
- the device's language, not each of the build's: the build will hold many languages, and only the device's speaks on its screens;
- the cache's languages, not those of the connected modules: only `api` knows the connected modules, profile by profile, and the device page speaks of what is on this device. A module the cache does not hold yet will appear in the list once it is opened;
- the sample only after the permission: before it, the sample's text could go to the network just as the tutor's words could;
- the choice of a voice without "Save": it is a setting of the device, it sends nothing and changes no context (WCAG 3.2.2, sources 8.1);
- a hidden working does not sound with its boxes: the demo is hidden whole, its open parts stay open but are not seen.

— *Rejected:*
- "slow" as 0.5, or a number of its own for each engine before the check on devices: without a check any number is a guess, and one number is simpler to correct;
- the platform by features of the API: no feature says whether the system synthesizes on the device;
- the learner's button only on the tutor's panel — the argument above;
- the line "no voice" as a status — the argument above;
- a group for each language of the build — the list would grow with the build, for languages no screen speaks;
- the languages of each profile's connected modules through `ListConnectedModules` — a request per profile for a page that speaks of the device;
- SSML instead of the table: Web Speech API allows it, but how the engines read it was not checked, and the table is plain text, the same for all;
- "Read aloud" at the task's solution — the design of voicing does not name it; the word «Послушать» for the sample — it is close to «прослушать», and `spec`'s glossary keeps that word for the text canvas.

— *Does not decide:*
- the number of the "slow" speed and the check of the code on devices — Д5;
- how the engines read signs that are not in the table — `+`, the `:` of division "300 : 100" beside the time "4:50", `%` and the decimal point — Д18;
- the texts of modules in `ru`: the first slice has none, and the table for `ru` is checked with synthetic texts;
- the "listen" of the text canvas — with it, through the same synthesis;
- the persona's voice — О-13.

— *Consequence:*
- `src/voice/`:
  - `voices.ts` — the voice, the platform and the speed;
  - `spoken.ts` — the table of signs and the sentences;
  - `texts.ts` — what the button reads;
  - `speech.ts` and `browser.ts` — the synthesis;
  - `VoicingContext.tsx` — the voicing of the learner's screens;
  - `ReadAloud.tsx`, `VoicingToggle.tsx` and `DeviceVoices.tsx`;
- `src/storage/voicing.ts` — the device's record; `src/test/speech.ts` — the synthesis of the tests;
- voicing in `src/task/Task.tsx`, the buttons in `TaskScreen.tsx`, `TutorWords.tsx`, `Hint.tsx`, `StepsPanel.tsx`, `src/lesson/Explanation.tsx` and `Example.tsx`;
- `VoicingProvider` in `src/app/App.tsx`, the learner's `onSaved` and `onFailed` in `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`, `useOptionalPackages` in `src/packages/PackagesContext.tsx`;
- the section "Voicing" in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`, a new line in `ProfilePage.tsx`;
- the icon `read-aloud` in `scripts/icons.ts`, the keys `voice.*` in the dictionaries, `.nt-read-aloud` in `theme.css`;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Platform AI quota on the screens

**R37. When the platform AI quota comes back, `app` writes it by the device's calendar: "today at {time}", "tomorrow at {time}", and later "on {date} at {time}", and when access to the own key comes back by the same three phrases. The tutor names what closes the AI of the attempt — the quota, the platform AI limit, a refusal of the own key and its limit reached — by a line both under the rung's fallback text and in the place of the correct answer words, while an AI failure and "not admitted" give no line there.** The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md); the refusals and the outcomes are those of [the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md) and of the comments of `Cause`, `Refusal`, `Outcome` and `EngineChoice` in `cause.proto`.
- **What has been there since E2** and does not change:
  - the outcome of "Check" is a line at the button, and the answer slots the AI did not read are "Not checked";
  - the refusals `CAUSE_QUOTA` at the start of a task — "What's next", "Try it again" and a lesson's segment — and at "Explain differently" are a line, and the right to "differently" stays;
  - "nothing to give" is "There is nothing more to offer for now" with the choice's outcome;
  - the clock offset goes in `SubmitWork` with handwriting, `GetRungWords`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords` and `ExplainDifferently` (`aiFields`), as the contract requires: "with a request on platform AI alone", and in `SubmitEssay`; `BeginAttempt` and `ChooseNext` have no such field.
- **When it comes back.** The day `next_family_day` is compared with today by the device's calendar, in its time zone:
  - the same day is "today at {time}". So is a time of today that the device's clock has already passed: the clock runs fast against `api`'s clock;
  - the next one is "tomorrow at {time}";
  - later is "on {date} at {time}", the day and the month in words, without the year. This happens after a change of the offset: the first family day by the new one lasts up to 44 hours. A day before today, for a clock off by more than a day, is also written as a date: "today" about a day gone by would be untrue.

  The time is `timeStyle: "short"`. There are three strings, each a whole phrase of the dictionary, not a concatenation, by `dayText` of `src/api/words.ts`. When access to the own key comes back is written by three of its own — "Access comes back today at {time}", "… tomorrow at {time}", "… on {date} at {time}" — by the same `dayText`.
- **Under the rung's fallback text** is the quota line or the neutral phrase of the platform AI limit, in the tutor's panel, under the words. Voicing says it after the words, in the screen's language.
- **One line, not two.** If the check has already said it — as a line at "Check" — the words of the same check do not repeat it. A refusal of "Explain differently" with the same text is not repeated under the line, and the right to "differently" stays, and the button too: with the start of the next family day it gives words again. When the model's words come, the line goes.
- **After a correct answer without words** the same line stands in the tutor's panel where the words would, above the solution; voicing says it after the verdict. The line of the earlier rung's words goes with the correct answer.
- **What closes the AI of the attempt** is `closesAI` in `src/api/outcome.ts`: the quota, the platform's limit, and with the own key its refusal and its limit reached — `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED` and `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED`, with the cause of the limit and when access returns, if the outcome names them. Each is a line at the words. An AI failure and "not admitted" give none — neither under the fallback text nor after a correct answer.
- **The quota's share** is `GetQuota`: for the learner a line at the foot of "Next up" on Home, for the adult the section "Tutor time" on the profile page, which writes the day the quota comes back by the rules above, through `dayText`.

— *Why:*
- "tomorrow": `api` leaves the text to `app`, which names no numbers but may promise "tomorrow". A time alone, without a day, on a family day of up to 44 hours does not say which day is meant;
- the device's calendar: the offset by which `api` counts the family day is sent by this same device, and `Intl` writes the time in its zone. A family day after a change of the offset does not end at midnight, and only the day on the device's clock is the one the learner sees;
- the line under the fallback text: in a run with `api` the fallback text came without it, and the learner would not learn why the tutor speaks in general words and why the next check is "Not checked";
- the quota and the limit, and the own key's refusal and limit, are what closes the AI for longer than one call: until the next family day or until the operator's command. An AI failure is a matter of one call, and "not admitted" is a lasting state of the deployment. `api` does not admit the correct answer words now (Д25 `api`), and the line "The tutor isn't available for this profile" would stand at every correct answer, telling nothing.

— *Rejected:*
- a date always, "on 13 November at 00:00": longer for a child of 6, and "tomorrow" is right almost always;
- "in N hours": the line stays on the screen, and the number ages with every minute;
- a line at each outcome of the words, as at "Check" — the argument above about "not admitted";
- the day by the offset from the request, not by the device's zone: it is the same number of the same device, and `Intl` writes the time by the zone too.

— *Does not decide:*
- a line shown before midnight stays until the next response. A tablet left overnight would say "tomorrow" in the morning about today, until the next check brings a new response;
- a run with `api` on the tariff `probe`.

— *Consequence:* `calendarDays`, `dayText` and the quota words in `src/api/words.ts`, and an optional `now` for `outcomeText` and `failureText`; `closesAI` in `src/api/outcome.ts`; the line at the rung's words and after a correct answer in `src/task/Task.tsx`; the keys `api.quota`, `api.quota-today` and `api.quota-tomorrow` in the dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The move to `spec` v0.16.3

**R38. `app` stands on `spec` v0.16.3: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0, and the part `deployment/` are at this tag, and `.devcontainer/` and `.claude/settings.json` follow the tag's template: Playwright 1.65.0, and the rules of `spec/.claude/rules/` do not load in `app`'s sessions. The rows «в реестр spec» В50 and В52–В60 are closed: `spec` has recorded them. What the tag decided for `app` is laid out so: the structure of the documents — by this same move, and the translation of `docs/` — by two tasks of its own; the names in the progress and the platform AI quota share — by the code of this same move; the own key in every engine choice — by the own key's code; the path in the browser without the author and the checks on devices — one task with the move to v0.17.1; the revocation of the Google token at account deletion — E4.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.15.1 v0.16.3`). v0.16.3 took in v0.16.0–v0.16.2 — the restructuring of `spec`'s documents and an addition to the contract:
  - RULES.md: the reader of `docs/` is a session of Claude Code; `docs/` are in English, and a document in Russian stays so until its own translation task; an external fact is a record of `docs/sources.md`, the results of measurements go to `docs/measurements.md`; a decision that changes another entry of the log puts an amendment on it in the same change;
  - the template: Playwright 1.65.0 and Chrome Headless Shell 1254; the line `spec/.claude/rules/` in `claudeMdExcludes`; `DEBT.md`, `docs/sources.md` and `docs-budgets.json`, the rules of RUN.md about a done task's body, and the step of the measurement at an epic's archive; `docs-check` — the rules of `.claude/rules/`, the budgets of size by `docs-budgets.json`, and the register of debts in `DEBT.md`, and for a repository without it in table 4 of RUN.md;
  - the contract is only added to, and a build that does not know the additions works as before: the own key's route is `own_key_provider` in each request that gives an engine choice; `lesson_title` and `skill_titles` in `AttemptRecord`; `AccountService.GetQuota` — the quota share of the profile for the learner, and of the account too for the adult;
  - the answers to the rows «в реестр spec» В50 and В52–В60 of `app`, each recorded in `spec`'s plan or decisions; most of the tasks of `spec` that carry them out are open in the tag, and `app` takes them by a move to their tag;
  - the answer to the rows В76 and В77 of `canvas`: `app` with canvas 0.3.15 already had it so, and Д15 is closed;
  - the account deletion screen revokes the Google access token — the deletion screen of E4; the learner sees that the words after the verdict are still coming — `app` has it so already: "The tutor is writing…" in the block "Tutor" (`src/task/TutorWords.tsx`);
  - `spec`'s rule of checks without the author — the executor checks the path in the browser alone, the local build signs in by a custom token, the AI calls go to a stub and under a limit and a cap, and on real devices nothing: the check is the three engines in CI. Rule 4 of RUN.md takes this exception with the move to v0.17.1;
  - the module format is still 0.4; the parts `deployment/` and `legal/` are of the same versions; `types/canvas` and `types/formatv0` have not changed since v0.15.0, and `types/admissionv0` — the admission table 0.3 — `app` does not read.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.16.3, commit 664398e; `gh release verify v0.16.3 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "Release v0.16.3 verified!"; `spec_tag` is v0.16.3, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature comes from the release v0.16.3: `release-verify` gives "deployment-1.tar.gz: spec v0.16.3, signed by spec's release.yml";
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.16.3, `@bufbuild/protobuf` is 2.17.0; `playwright` is 1.65.0; `release-verify-npm` accepted all six packages of `@altnovatrail` in the lock file; `NOTICE` has the lines of the contracts 0.16.3 and `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0;
  - `.devcontainer/` — `diff -r` with the output of `just -f spec/justfile new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.16.3` gives only the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run; `.claude/settings.json` is as in the recipe's output;
  - the tag in CLAUDE.md, README, RUN.md and the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts` is v0.16.3;
  - table 3 is without В50 and В52–В60, with a note above it saying where their answers are; table 4 is without Д15, and Д9, Д13, Д14, Д16 and Д17 wait for the tasks of `spec` that will close them; the plan takes in the own key's route, the checks on devices and the path in the browser without the author.

— *Why now:* v0.16.3 is the latest release (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`, 2026-11-12). It holds the contract that the own key's code, the quota share and the names in the progress wait for, the answers to all the rows «в реестр spec» of `app`, and `spec`'s rules of the documents' structure, without which RUN.md grew to 287 KB.

— *Rejected:*
- the intermediate v0.16.2: without the addition to the contract, and v0.16.3 came out an hour later;
- waiting for a tag with the tasks of `spec` still open: the contract and the answers are needed now; WebKit and Firefox in CI, the kept works, the next text version and the language of the license come with the next move, v0.17.1, where the kept works wait for `WorkService` in `api`, the next text version is a task of its own, and the language of the license needs nothing;
- the restructuring of the documents as a task of its own after `spec`'s pilot — the move brings `spec`'s rules of the structure, and the documents follow them by the same move, the translation by tasks of its own;
- the code of the quota share and the names after the move of `api` — the code for the new contract comes with the move, ready when `api` gives it.

— *Consequence:* CLAUDE.md; README.md; RUN.md; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; `package.json`; `.claude/settings.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The structure of the documents

**R39. `app`'s documents are laid out by `spec`'s rules of their structure: table 4 is `run/DEBT.md`, and tables 2 and 3 are `run/EPICS.md` and `run/QUESTIONS.md`; a done task's body and its summary sentence go to its epic's archive at once, and RUN.md keeps neither; external facts are records of `docs/sources.md`, the results of measurements are in `docs/measurements.md`, and `docs/architecture.md` points to them; CLAUDE.md follows the template, and the checks of the code are the rule `.claude/rules/code.md` by paths. The translation of `docs/` into English is two tasks, and `docs-budgets.json` comes with the last of them.**
- **`run/DEBT.md`** is table 4 word for word, with its opening and its next number, by the template, in English. A text that names a row of table 4 of RUN.md names the same register, and is not edited for it.
- **The archive at once.** A done task's body goes to its epic's archive, `docs/plan/<epic>.md`, word for word, with its relative links corrected, and its summary sentence with it; RUN.md keeps neither. A done task outside the epic goes to the archive's section «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные за его время». The first such move took RUN.md from 287 to 63 KB.
- **Sources and measurements** follow `spec`'s: a record is a numbered subsection of its topic, a citation is `sources 3.1`; the results of measurements — the writing with a mouse and the one-time run in WebKit and Firefox — are in `docs/measurements.md`; [architecture.md](architecture.md) keeps a pointer to the records in its section on sources.
- **CLAUDE.md** follows the template: `docs/sources.md`, `docs/measurements.md` and `run/` in the list of documents, `docs/` in English. `app` has one part with checks of its own — the code: its checks are the rule `.claude/rules/code.md` with the paths of the code's files, and CLAUDE.md names it in one line.
- **The translation** follows `spec`'s [order](../spec/tools/translation/README.md): one task — `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/sources.md` and `docs/measurements.md`, ≈ 620 KB of Russian; the other — the log, ≈ 480 KB, and with it `docs-budgets.json` and the measurement "after". `spec` translated ≈ 460–580 KB per task.
- **The measurement.** "Before" is the row `-workspaces-app` of measurements 1.9 spec; "after" is `just -f spec/justfile tokens-report --since 2026-11-12T20:23 --row` over the sessions of the restructuring and the translation.

— *Why:* RUN.md, the debt and the bodies of done tasks are read by every session, though it needs only the live rows; `spec`'s table of differences names for `app` a RUN.md of 102 KB, of which table 4 is 71 KB, and 9 sections of `docs/architecture.md` over 24 KB. The translation is separate because ≈ 1.1 MB cannot be checked in one task.

— *Rejected:*
- the translation with the restructuring — more than one check, and the blind reading of 1.1 MB takes several subagents;
- three or four translation tasks — `spec` translated more per task, and the log is read by entries and in pieces of 15–30 KB;
- `docs-budgets.json` before the translation — the limits would have to be set on the Russian text, which the translation will shrink: only what stays over after the translation is raised;
- no rule for the code — the template's CLAUDE.md names no parts, but the checks of the code would then live only in table 1 of RUN.md, which the session reads whole, while a rule loads when the session touches the code.

— *Consequence:* `run/`; `docs/plan/`; `docs/sources.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `.claude/rules/code.md`; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Names in the progress and the quota share

**R40. `app` takes the platform AI quota share by `GetQuota`: for the learner, as a line with its bar at the foot of the card "Next up" on Home and in the head of the tutor's panel, the share of the learner's own profile, and when the cap is reached, the words of the quota refusal; for the adult, as the group "Tutor time" at the bottom of the profile page, a bar for each share: the shares of the profile and of the account, that the cap is reached, and when the next family day begins. From an `api` without `GetQuota`, neither the line nor the section.** The code for the new contract is there before `api` gives it; the line's place and bar are the mockup's. The design is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and the contract in [the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md).
- **Reading the share** is `readQuota` in `src/account/quota.ts`: `GetQuota` with `ACTION_LEARNING` or `ACTION_ADULT`. `unimplemented` from an `api` on a tag older than v0.16.3 is not a share and offers no reload: `GetQuota` is in `MAY_BE_ABSENT` of `src/api/interceptors.ts`, as `GetLegalTexts` is. A response without `share` is not a share either. The percentages are `Intl.NumberFormat` with `style: "percent"` of the screen's language: "40 %", "40%".
- **For the learner** — a line `<p>` at the foot of the card "Next up" on Home: "Tutor time today: {percent} used", with a bar of the share beside it, full once a cap is reached — the account's too —, hidden from assistive technology, since the line's words name the share. The share is read when the screen opens, and again on the lists' "Try again": it may not have come with them. With `cap_reached` — the words of the quota refusal, "Today's tutor time is used up. It comes back …", and without `next_family_day` — the neutral phrase, as for the refusal: the contract says that `cap_reached` for a learning action means the same as the refusal `CAUSE_QUOTA`. Without a percentage — the tariff has no caps, the platform AI is closed — there is no line; on a failure there is none either, and the lists stay as they are. The line does not show the account's share, even if it came. A child's profile, which comes with E4, gets the same line.
- **In the tutor's panel** — its head shows the learner's share as well, "Tutor time today: 20% used", by `shareText` of `src/account/quota.ts`, "100% used" once the cap is reached, the conversation saying why the tutor is silent; it is read by `GetQuota` as the panel opens.
- **For the adult** — the group "Tutor time" on the profile page, the last one (`src/adults/QuotaSection.tsx`), its note "How much of today's tutor time — the platform's AI, which checks work and words the tutor's replies — this profile and the whole account have used":
  - a row for each share, "This profile" and "The whole account", "{percent} used" at its end and under it a bar, `role="meter"` named by its row and its value the same words (`MeterRow` of `src/adults/rows.tsx`): red once a cap is reached, orange from four fifths;
  - a row with the clock's tile, "Tutor time renews today at {time}", "… tomorrow at {time}" or "… on {date} at {time}" — by the device's calendar, as the quota's return is written: `dayText` in `src/api/words.ts` writes the quota refusal too, here with the group's three phrases;
  - with `cap_reached`, in the note — "Today's tutor time is used up: until it renews, the platform's AI checks no work of this profile";
  - without both percentages — the one row "The platform's AI has no tutor time on this account now: it is closed to it".

  While the request is under way, a skeleton; from an `api` without `GetQuota`, no group: an empty heading would say nothing. A failure gives its words and "Try again" in the group.

— *Why:*
- the learner's line is where the learner begins work, and the panel's head where the tutor is called: so the contract names the place of a learning action, and the learner sees the share before going to the tutor. A plain line, not a live region: no action of the learner called it;
- the group is on the profile page, not on the section's first page: `GetQuota` asks for a profile, and the account's share stands beside the profile's share, because the account's cap is shared by all the profiles;
- the words of the quota refusal when the cap is reached: one state — one phrase on all screens;
- without a percentage the learner sees no line: "closed" on the first screen gives a child nothing, and the call that meets the closed AI will say its own.

— *Rejected:*
- waiting for the move of `api` to v0.16.3 — the code is ready before `api` gives it, and from an `api` without `GetQuota` nothing shows;
- bars, not lines of percentages: the two shares and the cap read at a glance, and the words stay for assistive technology;
- the share on the section's first page or on a page of its own: the request is a profile's, and the page would have three lines;
- the account's share for the learner if `api` sends it: "A child never sees what the family spends", as [the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md) has it.

— *Does not decide:* the check with a real `api` — with its move to the tag.

— *Consequence:* `src/account/quota.ts` — `readQuota` and `shareText`; `src/adults/QuotaSection.tsx` and the section in `src/adults/ProfilePage.tsx`; the line and its bar in `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`; the panel's head in `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`; `MeterRow` in `src/adults/rows.tsx`; `dayText` in `src/api/words.ts`; `percentText` in `src/i18n/intl.ts`; `GetQuota` in `MAY_BE_ABSENT` of `src/api/interceptors.ts`; the keys `quota.*` in the dictionaries; tests; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## RUN.md: only what a session acts on

**R41. RUN.md has no section «Задачи вне эпика», and what in the current epic's part has stopped being live goes to the epic's archive before the epic ends.
