The body of an open task outside an epic is in `run/TASKS.md`; a done task's body and its summary sentence go to the archive — of the current epic, or, when there is none, of the last done epic, into its section «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные после него» —, at the start of the section that receives them, and RUN.md keeps neither. The summary sentences of done tasks are short: the gist and the decision, the detail is in the body in the archive.**
- **Moved to [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md)**, word for word, with the links corrected, into the sections «Закрытые строки таблицы 3» and «Из описания эпика в RUN.md»: the history of the closed rows В50–В60, the reconnaissance of the neighbours in the planning of E3 on 2026-11-12, the decision on В51, the points of «Почему такой порядок» about done tasks, the lines about how the epic was cut into tasks, and the summary sentences of E3's done tasks as they were before they were shortened. A task that relies on the reconnaissance checks the neighbours again on the day of its planning.
- **The template.** The template's RUN.md has no section «Задачи вне эпика» either, and a task of analysis by `spec`'s threshold of measurements goes to `run/TASKS.md`, as any task outside an epic.

— *Why:* every session reads RUN.md whole: a section without a task, the history of answered rows and a snapshot of the neighbours that newer tags have overtaken cost tokens in each of them and help none. The archive is not read whole, so what moved there costs nothing until a task needs it.

— *Rejected:*
- deleting the moved text — the archive must keep the epic's description word for word, as RUN.md asks, and what moved early is part of it;
- shortening the rules of RUN.md — they are the template's, and a difference in them makes every move to a new tag harder; the bodies of the open tasks — each is planned and rewritten in its own session.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; `run/TASKS.md`; `docs/plan/`.

**R42. The deployment of `app` is checked by `app` itself, by the recipe `just site-check <origin> <version>`: the release's archive, accepted by `release-verify`, against what the site serves to anyone — every file of the archive byte for byte, `/deployment.json` as the code of `app` reads it, the headers [architecture.md](architecture.md) asks of the hosting, and the page in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox under the site's own header up to the sign-in window, which it closes before anyone signs in. The sign-in with an account, the invite code and a task on the deployment are not walked again: the first rollout of `infra` walked them. The platform's domain is `novatrail.web.app`.** `infra` rolled out `app` v0.1.0 with `api` v0.1.22 on 2026-11-12, and the author signed in there with Google and made the account by a code of `api invite`. The site gets the pair `app` v0.1.3 with `api` v0.1.35 or newer by a task of `infra`, and the run `just site-check https://novatrail.web.app v0.1.3` comes after that rollout; a session of `app` changes no other repository.
- **What the rollout of `infra` checks and what `app` checks.** The rollout holds the archive to the list's digest and to its layout, `/deployment.json` to the schema and to `api_origin`, and after the release of the site the page at `/` and at a path of the SPA, one file of `assets/`, a 404 under `/assets/`, `/deployment.json`, `/NOTICE` and `/LICENSE`, with their headers (`tools/deploy/app.mjs` of `infra`, `main` on 2026-11-13). `site-check` adds:
  - every file of the archive, `csp.json` and `deployment.schema.json` included;
  - the file read by the code of `app` itself, `loadDeployment`;
  - the CSP header built by `csp.json` of the release, by `scripts/csp.ts`;
  - what only a browser shows: no violation of the CSP under the real header with Google's real script and iframe, only the origin of `app` asked before the tap of "Sign in with Google", after it no host beyond those the CSP allows, the window on `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/handler` and the hidden iframe of `authDomain` in the page, and the site's host among the authorized domains of the project, by the rule of the SDK: a name also within a domain, an IP address only exactly.

  It reads the site as anyone does and signs nobody in, so it needs neither the author nor an account. The same recipe checks a self-hosted copy: its operator names the copy's origin.
- **Three engines.** The check opens the page in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox and names each problem of the page by its engine. In WebKit, which the SDK takes for Safari, it allows gapi, the hidden iframe and `www.googleapis.com` before the tap, and `www.googleapis.com` after it too (measurements 3 and 4); in Firefox, `www.googleapis.com` after the tap, where the hidden iframe loads (measurements 11), and takes `connect-src` refusing gapi's ping, `https://apis.google.com/js/gen_204`, for no violation of the CSP (sources 7.6); any other refusal, and this one in Chromium and Firefox, fails it.
- **The domain.** `novatrail.web.app`, which the rollout of `infra` sets; an own domain waits for О-16. That it is among the authorized domains of the Firebase project, the recipe sees in the answer of `identitytoolkit.googleapis.com`.
- **The archive** is taken from the release's public address with its signature, by `curl`, not by `gh`: so the recipe needs no token, and a copy's operator can run it too.

— *Why:* the rollout's check is the rollout's: it does not open the page in a browser, and a self-hosted copy has no rollout of `infra` at all, while the hosting's headers and the CSP are the design of `app`. A recipe and not a one-off script, so that it runs again after each rollout.

— *Rejected:*
- putting the check off until `infra` rolls out v0.1.3: that rollout itself waits for the check, and the two would wait for each other;
- a one-off script — no code to keep, but nothing to run again after the next rollout;
- walking the sign-in, the invite code and a task again by hand — the author walks nothing by hand, and on the deployment the sign-in is the author's Google account.

— *Consequence:* `justfile`, `site-check`; `scripts/site.ts` and its test; `scripts/google-stub.ts`, Google's stand-ins out of `scripts/page.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## The move to `spec` v0.17.1

**R43. `app` stands on `spec` v0.17.1: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0, and the part `deployment/` are at this tag, and `.devcontainer/`, CLAUDE.md and the rules of RUN.md follow the tag's template: WebKit and Firefox beside Chrome Headless Shell in the stage `toolchain`, the container's name `app` on its own network, rule 4 with the exception for checks without the author, and rule 9 for a task outside an epic when there is no epic. The container also mounts the volume `novatrail-dev-token` of `api`'s development token, only to read it. The row «в реестр spec» В61 is closed: `spec` has recorded it. What the tag brings for `app` is laid out so: the path in the browser without the author and the three engines in CI — by this same move; the next version of a legal text and «Who we are» — a task of its own right after it, before 2026-11-27; the stored works — a task of its own once `api` answers `WorkService`; the export of version 2 — E4; the proxy to `api` by its name — once `api` has the alias.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.16.3 v0.17.1`). v0.17.1 took in v0.16.4–v0.17.0:
  - the template: WebKit 2388 and Firefox 1572 from the same image of Playwright 1.65.0, a `COPY` each, and 78 packages from the snapshot `20261021T000000Z` — the libraries of the three engines and the fonts of Playwright's list `tools` without `xvfb`; the browsers are named exceptions of `spec`'s rule of licenses; the default font becomes WenQuanYi Zen Hei instead of Liberation, in Chromium too; `--network-alias=<repository>` right after the repository's own network; rule 4 of RUN.md with the exception for checks without the author; rule 9 — where the body of a task outside an epic goes when there is no current epic; a sentence in «Environment» of CLAUDE.md on the parts that `.gitignore` keeps out of git;
  - the contract is only added to, and a build that does not know the additions works as before: `GetLegalTextsResponse.next_texts` — the next version of each text before it applies, and `in_force_since` is now the day from which a version applies, which may be later than the day it was approved; `WorkService` — `PutWork`, `ListWorks`, `GetWork` and `DeleteWork`, a stored work up to 4 MiB, and the causes `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF` and `CAUSE_WORK_TOO_LARGE`;
  - the part `legal/`: version 3 of `privacy-policy`, which applies from 2026-11-27, and the passport of format 3; the types `legalv3` and `exportv2` instead of `legalv2` and `exportv1`, which `app` does not import; `types/canvas` — the comments of `SheetSubmission` and `WorkbookSubmission`: they are sent only within a stored work, while the storage of the profile's works is on;
  - `API_APP_ORIGIN` of `api` in the local run is the origin of `app` on loopback;
  - the answer to В61: the site gets the pair `app` v0.1.3 with `api` v0.1.35 or newer, by a task of `infra` outside an epic;
  - `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of a module are in the language of the license, as `spec/format/README.md` has it: `app` already shows a module's license as `<pre lang="en">`, which holds while the official modules are Apache-2.0 in English and the licenses of third-party modules are О-10;
  - the part `deployment/`, the module format 0.4, `types/formatv0` and `types/admissionv0` are as at v0.16.3.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.17.1, commit 54c5f83; `gh release verify v0.17.1 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "✓ Release v0.17.1 verified!"; `spec_tag` is v0.17.1, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature comes from the release v0.17.1: `release-verify` gives "deployment-1.tar.gz: spec v0.17.1, signed by spec's release.yml";
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.17.1, `@bufbuild/protobuf` stays 2.17.0, which the contracts 0.17.1 name; `release-verify-npm` accepted all six packages of `@altnovatrail` in the lock file; `NOTICE` has the line of the contracts 0.17.1;
  - `.devcontainer/` — `diff` with the output of `just -f spec/justfile new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.17.1` gives only the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run and the volume of the token: `source=novatrail-dev-token,target=/home/vscode/.novatrail-token,type=volume,readonly`, whose folder the stage `devcontainer` makes as `vscode`, so that a volume Docker makes for it is `vscode`'s, as `spec` and `api` have it. WebKit and Firefox are `app`'s, as Chrome Headless Shell is. The later moves keep these two differences alone: the base image, the Dockerfile's opening comment, its three stages — `toolchain-core`, `toolchain` over it with the three engines, and `devcontainer` —, its apt reading a source of the snapshot alone, the heading of the stage `toolchain` and `"shutdownAction": "none"` of `devcontainer.json` are the template's;
  - CLAUDE.md, README, RUN.md and the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts` name v0.17.1; rule 4 of RUN.md has the template's four points of the exception word for word, and rule 9 the template's sentence for when there is no epic: the summary sentence goes to the archive with the body;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) says `API_APP_ORIGIN` as `spec` has it, and that the proxy goes to `http://api:8080` once `api` has its alias (Д31 of `api`);
  - table 3 is without В61; table 2, E4, has the export of version 2; table 4 — Д16 waits for `WorkService` of `api`, Д17 is a task of its own now, and the new Д21 is the proxy by name.
- **Without the new methods.** Nothing of `app` calls `WorkService` or reads `next_texts` yet, and the list of methods that `api` may not have gets nothing: the task of the next version reads `next_texts`, and from an `api` without it shows no next version, as the line of the quota shows nothing without `GetQuota`.

— *Why now:* v0.17.1 is the latest release (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`, 2026-11-14). It holds what the path in the browser and the checks on devices waited for — WebKit and Firefox in the template, rule 4 with the exception for checks without the author —, and `api` on `main` has the stub of `tutor` and the development token. Version 3 of `privacy-policy` applies from 2026-11-27, and `app` must show it before that: the contract of the next version is in this tag.

— *Rejected:*
- the intermediate v0.17.0: the contract of the next version and version 3 of the policy are in both, but v0.17.1 is the latest and came out four hours later;
- the next version of a text in this move — one more check in a task kept whole; it is a task of its own right after;
- the stored works now — `api` does not answer `WorkService` on `main`, so there would be nothing to check them against;
- the proxy by name `http://api:8080` now — `api`'s container has no alias until its own move.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; `run/`; [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md); CLAUDE.md; README.md; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; `package.json`; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The path in a browser without the author

**R44. The local build signs in by `api`'s development token, and the path of the first slice runs in a browser with no one at the keyboard: `just walk`, by hand and not in CI, on Chromium, WebKit and Firefox against `api`'s `just dev-app`. Its AI calls go to the stub of `tutor`, and no real one is made; a 4xx of the origin fails it unless it is `api`'s refusal with a cause the build knows. The proxy of the development server goes to `api` by its name.**
- **The sign-in of the local build.** «Sign in with Google» on the development server first asks it for `/__novatrail/custom-token` and, with a token there, signs in by `signInWithCustomToken`, with no window; with none — `just dev-app` is not running —, by the window as before. The branch stands behind `import.meta.env.DEV`, which Vite replaces with `false` in the build, so `src/signin/local.ts` is not in the build at all; the plugin `scripts/local-token.ts` is `apply: "serve"` and serves the file of the volume `novatrail-dev-token`, which the container mounts only to read, read again at each request, since `api` writes a new token every half hour, or 404; `vite preview` has no such path, and the test of the built page holds that no file of `build/` names it. The path is a constant of `src/signin/token-path.ts`, shared by the app and the plugin.
- **The proxy** goes to `http://api:8080` by default: `api`'s container has the name `api` on the network of the local run, and `NOVATRAIL_API` names another address. Д21 is closed. Plain HTTP, which SonarCloud flags (S5332) and the line marks `NOSONAR` with the reason: `api`'s development server has no TLS, the requests stay on the Docker network of the machine, and a release's build has no proxy.
- **The walk** — `walk/path.test.ts` with its own `walk/vitest.config.ts`, outside `src/` and `scripts/`, so that `ci-test` and the coverage do not take it. It starts the development server by Vite's API with `vite.config.ts` and walks, in each engine `just walk` names, in a new context: the sign-in and Home with the line of the tutor's time; a lesson; a task of the skill map — in handwriting through the stub's reading of the crop's shape, by tap otherwise —; the own key; the section for adults; the sign-out; «Erase everything on this device». The steps are listed with the tests in [architecture.md](architecture.md). A request to a host the sign-in does not name, or an error in the console, fails it.
- **A response of 4xx of the origin** is an error of the walk in every engine unless it is `api`'s refusal with a cause the build knows. `walk/path.test.ts` reads each as Connect's error and takes its `Refusal` by the app's own `refusalOf`, as the contract carries a refusal: a refusal with a cause the build knows is noted by its cause, and any other — no error of Connect, no refusal, a cause the build does not know, a body that could not be read — is an error that says which. Chromium and WebKit log such a response as an error at the resource's address, Firefox not at all: the line of a 4xx of the origin is left to its response, so that Firefox finds the same as the other two.
- **The lesson** step reads the profile «Dev» of the last `GetAccount`: with a lesson open, «Continue the lesson» of "Next up" on Home; with none, «What's next». It goes back by the bar to the segment the lesson opened at, not to the first, and begins again a task an earlier walk left, by «Begin the task again». A beginning of a task refused for the quota of the day — the screen's «Today's tutor time is used up» — stops the walk with the message of its first step, where it would wait 30 s for a sheet: in a lesson, outside one and at «Try it again».
- **The own key.** The walk connects an own key on `api`'s stub, which answers the check by the key's ending: a key ending in `-invalid` is refused with its words, then a synthetic key is accepted and kept on this device, the task outside a lesson goes on it and through its reload, and "This device" removes it. It holds that the key's bytes go with no request but `CheckOwnKey` and those that call AI, and that `ChooseNext` and `BeginAttempt` name its provider. An `api` whose dev mode lists no provider stops the walk with what to run — `api` v0.1.41 or newer.
- **The stub and the quota.** The stub answers by the crop's shape — wider than tall correct, taller than wide not, square illegible —, and the crop has a side of 200 px at least: the walk writes 25-unit upright dashes, one for a square crop, eight in a column for a tall one and eight in a row for a wide one. Each call spends the quota of the profile's day as the real one would: so the full sequence runs once per engine, on the skill map's task, and a lesson's tasks are ended by their solution. With the quota spent, the walk stops at its first step and says so; restarting `just dev-app` with another `API_DEV_APP_UID` gives a new account.
- **What it found** (measurements 3): two sentences of a status ran together with no full stop — fixed here, on the screen of what's next and the skill map; in WebKit, Google's page in the hidden iframe asks `www.googleapis.com`, which spec's list of the hosts of the sign-in does not name — row В62 «в реестр spec», and [architecture.md](architecture.md) names the host; in WebKit, once of three runs, the heartbeats' database was still there 15 s after the tab was closed (Д13).
- **DEBT.md:** Д14 is left with the task by tap, which the engine gave in no run; Д20 is closed — the line of the tutor's time and the lesson's name in the progress were seen against `api` with its `GetQuota`; Д13 has the heartbeats in WebKit.

— *Why:* the executor has no Google sign-in, so the path of the first slice against a real `api` waited for the author; `api` gives its development token for just this, and `spec` has the path walked in a browser by Playwright. The same button in the same app keeps what is walked what the learner has. A refusal is how `api` answers by the contract, and the app shows it: Chromium logs the 400 of `CAUSE_NO_MORE_WORDS` all the same, and the walk failed where nothing was wrong. A walk that failed mid-lesson leaves the lesson open; on such an account «What's next» gave a task four times in a row, so every later walk of it failed, and the way back the app gives a learner to a lesson begun is «Continue the lesson». At 90% of the quota «What's next» gave tasks alone with no lesson open too: whether the quota was a part of it is open, and the walk's message names the line of the tutor's time (measurements 6).

— *Rejected:*
- a button or a global hook of its own for the walk — lines and a path no learner has;
- the Firebase Auth emulator — `just dev-app` signs in on the real dev project;
- the walk in CI — it needs `api` with its stub, the dev project and the token, which CI has none of;
- the walk on `vite preview` — the build has no path of the token, by design;
- `NOVATRAIL_API` as a requirement — `api`'s name makes it the exception;
- the full stub sequence on every task — a day's quota would not hold three engines;
- ignoring every line of a 4xx — a 4xx with no refusal is a fault of the build or of `api`, which the walk is there to find; the engines' lines alone, matched to the refusals by address — Firefox logs none, and a 4xx with no refusal would pass there; a window of time between a line and a response — the response itself says what it was;
- a new test account per run — the dev project would gather accounts, and a lesson left open is a state a learner has too.

— *Consequence:* `src/signin/` — `local.ts`, `token-path.ts` and the branch of `firebase.ts`; `scripts/local-token.ts`; `vite.config.ts`; `walk/`; `justfile`; `src/next/NextStep.tsx`; `src/skills/SkillMapScreen.tsx`; `tsconfig.node.json`; `biome.json`; [.claude/rules/code.md](../.claude/rules/code.md); README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [measurements.md](measurements.md); `run/DEBT.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`.

## Three engines in CI

**R45. The tests of the screens, the test of the built page and the site check run in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: each Vitest project of the screens has an instance per engine, `forced colors` in Chromium and Firefox alone; `ci-test` runs the build, Node and Chromium with the coverage, and `ci-engines`, a check of its own, WebKit and Firefox without it. A test that holds an engine's own behaviour holds each engine's, and says why. In WebKit the test of the built page and the site check take one refusal of the CSP for no violation: gapi's usage ping to `https://apis.google.com/js/gen_204`, which the CSP still refuses.**
- **`vitest.config.ts`.** The helper `screens(name, include, { contextOptions, exclude, engines })` gives a project an instance per engine, named `<project> (<engine>)`: `browser` and `reduced motion` in the three, `forced colors` in Chromium and Firefox — WebKit has no forced colours (measurements 2).
- **`ci-test` and `ci-engines`.** `ci-test` keeps the build, `vitest run --coverage --project node --project '*(chromium)'` and the release's archive built twice: Vitest 5.1.0 refuses its v8 coverage on any engine but Chromium that `--project` leaves in ([Vitest v5.1.0](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/blob/v5.1.0/packages/vitest/src/node/projects/resolveProjects.ts#L989-L1029)), and Codecov gets the coverage of Node and Chromium. `ci-engines` runs `vitest run --project '*(webkit)' --project '*(firefox)'`, without coverage and without `build`, as "TypeScript / engines": `ci.yml` gives the job TypeScript the input `engines: true` of `typescript.yml` v0.1.30, and `just ci` runs all five recipes. `ci-engines` builds nothing: no test in a browser reads `build/` — the test of the built page, which opens it in the three engines, is the Node project's, and stays in `ci-test`.
- **The test of the built page** opens the page in each engine in turn; it records the page's own requests apart from Google's, and in WebKit, which the SDK takes for Safari, expects gapi and the hidden iframe at the start, as [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists them; each engine leaves its own trace in a frame it refuses. **The site check** opens the page in the three engines at once, each engine read from the browser of its context, names each problem of the page by its engine, and allows in WebKit, before the tap and after it, what the SDK loads for Safari and `www.googleapis.com`, which Google's page in the iframe asks there (measurements 3; В62), and in Firefox that host after the tap (measurements 11); the test of the built page takes the same lists from it. Its test of a wrong header opens one engine: the header is the site's, and three would each wait out a step of 30 s for a button that cannot come.
- **The ping of gapi in WebKit.** The refusal taken for no violation is `connect-src` refusing `https://apis.google.com/js/gen_204`, with any query, as the event gives it and as WebKit writes it in the console. Any other refusal, and this one in Chromium and Firefox, fails the two checks. `scripts/site.ts` holds the rule, `expectedRefusal`, and the one recorder of violations both take, `recordViolations`; `scripts/site.test.ts` holds what passes and what does not, and the page test that the header refuses the ping in every engine and lets it by in WebKit alone. gapi pings wherever it runs, so on a computer too once "Sign in with Google" is tapped; the page test gives Chromium and Firefox a stand-in for gapi, but the site check runs Google's own, and there the same refusal can fail it now and then — row В64.
- **What the engines showed** (measurements 4), and what the tests hold now: WebKit cannot be given a `Blob` of schema version 1 to read — the test runs in Chromium and Firefox; WebKit lists the four English voices of flite, none of them local by its own word, so `app`'s path is "no voice" in every engine, and the test holds that no local voice is listed; Firefox matches no `:lang()` inside the canvas element's shadow tree — the test holds the region's name alone there, and what NVDA takes is Д10's; Firefox widens the box of each piece of SVG text by some 2 px — the zoom's test measures the print from its leftmost piece to its rightmost. No code of `app` changed for an engine.
- **DEBT.md:** Д5, Д8, Д9, Д10, Д13 and Д18 say what the engines checked and keep what a device alone shows; the checks by hand of Д5 and Д18 go with those of Д10, before the first child, in E4. Д4 and Д7 are devices' alone and stay. The ruleset of `main` requires "TypeScript / test" but not yet "TypeScript / engines": a check enters it once it has come to `main`, the author's step in GitHub's settings — Д32.

— *Why:* WebKit and Firefox are in the container since the move to `spec` v0.17.1, and `spec` gives the browser checks to CI where no device is needed. The earlier tests of the screens, run once in WebKit, had found three failures there, and the later ones had not been run in another engine at all. Two jobs side by side: "TypeScript / test" was the longest job of `app`'s CI, 278 s, of which Chromium with the coverage 88 s and WebKit with Firefox 120 s, one after the other (measurements 3.1 spec); side by side, the job waits for the longer of the two, and what the checks prove does not change — the same tests run in the same three engines, in the same container with `--ipc=host`. The ping: gapi, which the SDK loads at every start in WebKit, sends a usage counter there in one flush of a hundred (sources 7.6), and the test failed on `main` twice for it alone, holding back a release (measurements 5). Refused, the ping costs the sign-in nothing, and Google learns less of the device.

— *Rejected:*
- istanbul in place of v8, for the coverage of all three — the instrumentation changes the code the screens run, and the coverage of Chromium is what Codecov had;
- `forced colors` in WebKit — it has no such mode, and the project would only test the absence;
- skipping a test in an engine without saying why — each skip and each engine's expectation names its cause and the measurement;
- a job of WebKit and one of Firefox — two loads of the image and two `npm ci` where one serves, and `typescript.yml` has no such input; `build` before `ci-engines` too — a second build in another job, which no test of it reads;
- `https://apis.google.com/js/gen_204` in the header's `connect-src` — the device would send Google its address and counters for nothing `app` needs; skipping the WebKit check of violations altogether — a real refusal there would pass unseen.

— *Consequence:* `vitest.config.ts`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `scripts/page.test.ts`, `scripts/site.ts` and `scripts/site.test.ts`; `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx`, `src/voice/voice.browser.test.tsx`, `src/storage/idb.browser.test.ts` and the opening comments of the tests in the browser; `.claude/rules/code.md`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [measurements.md](measurements.md); [sources.md](sources.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

## The next version of a legal text and the operator

**R46. `app` shows the next version of a legal text before the day it applies from, beside the current one: the text's page `#/legal/<id>` names it with its day and leads to a page of its own, `#/legal/<id>/next`; the consent to keeping works shows it in place below the current one, and a text's sheet, from the invite code's screen or Help, names it and leads to its page; after signing in, the bell of the frame of Home tells of it, with a red dot until this device has shown it. A consent names the current version alone. Who runs the copy is a page of its own, `#/legal/operator`: the section Who we are of the privacy policy in force, to which "Accessibility", the list of the legal pages and the page on the AI tutor lead. From an `api` without `next_texts`, no next version.** The contract is that of `spec` v0.17.1: `GetLegalTextsResponse.next_texts`, which `api` gives since its release v0.1.38.
- **Reading.** `readLegal` in `src/legal/texts.ts` gives the current versions and the next ones of one `GetLegalTexts`, and `readTexts` is its current half. From an `api` on a tag older than v0.17.0 the field does not come, and the list is empty, as protobuf reads a field the answer does not carry; `unimplemented` is both lists empty, as it is no texts for the current ones. A text's next version is the one `next_texts` gives for its ID (`nextOf`); the notice takes those of the texts that are legal pages, in the order of `PAGE_TEXTS` (`pageNext`): a family's next version waits for its screens, E4.
- **The text's page.** Under "Version 2, in force since …", the line "Version 3 of this text applies from November 27, 2026" and the button "Read version 3", which the line describes. The next version's page, `#/legal/<id>/next`, titled "{text}: the next version", says "Version 3, applies from …" and "Until then, version 2 is in force", that a consent given in the app can be withdrawn before it applies — in E3 keeping works on the server, switched off on a profile's page —, has "Read the version in force", and shows the version as `LegalContent` shows the current one, open, its headings from the second level. Without a next version it says "This text has no next version now" and leads to the current one; without a current version there is neither the line "until then" nor the way back. Each page is read when it opens, past the HTTP cache, as the current text's page is.
- **In place.** In the consent to keeping works `storage-consent` is followed by its own next version; the invite code's screen names the texts of the account as links to their pages, which show theirs: `NextLegalText` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx` — a disclosure titled with the text's title and "version 3, applies from …" in the screen's language, and in it "Until then, version 2 is in force" and the text. The tick, `CreateAccount` and `SetWorkStorage` name the current versions alone: the tick's key is the current versions', and another next version does not untick it. A next version with no current one shows on its page, with no tick: nothing in force is there to accept. A request that named the next version before its day would get `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` by the contract.
- **After signing in.** `Bell` in `src/app/Shell.tsx`, at the top right of Home, Subjects and the skill map, is the notice: for each next version of a page's text, a tile of a page, "{text}: version 3", "Applies from …. Until then, version 2 is in force.", and "Read version 3", named "Read {text}, version 3" — two texts may have next versions of one number —, which opens its page with its bar back home. While a profile of the account keeps its works on the server (`Learner.storing`), a line that this can be turned off in Settings before that day: in E3 it is the one consent an owner without children can withdraw. A red dot on the bell, and its description "1 new", while a notice is one this device has not shown; once the pop-over has shown it, its text's ID and version go to `localStorage` under `novatrail.bell.seen`, and the dot goes — a device that keeps nothing shows the dot again next time. No next version, an `api` without the field and a failure of the read leave "Nothing new yet" and offer no "Try again": the texts' own pages say the rest, and the bell reads again when the frame next opens. The choice of the learner reads no text.
- **Who runs this copy** — `#/legal/operator`, titled "Who runs this copy of the app": "From the Privacy Policy, version 2, in force since …"; on a screen not in English, the line that the text is in English and the English one is in force; the section in English, `lang="en"`, from its heading `## Who we are` to the next heading of the first or the second level — `sectionOf` in `src/legal/body.ts`, by the tokens of `marked` —, its heading at the second level under the page's; and "Read the whole privacy policy". The rule `legal.operator` holds that every version of the policy, in a copy's folder too, has a heading of the second level that reads exactly `Who we are`; a translation needs none, and the page takes none. A policy without the section gives "This copy's privacy policy has no section on who runs it", and a copy without a policy in force "This copy of the app has no privacy policy in force". The pages that name the operator lead to it: "Accessibility" by "Who runs this copy and how to reach them", in place of "Open the privacy policy"; the list of the legal pages by an item after the three texts, since its opening line names the operator; and the page on the AI tutor by "Who runs the app" in its part "If something is wrong", which names the operator who reviews a report, with the bar's "Close" back to `#/tutor`, before signing in too, since that page shows at any step. The line for whoever deployed the copy on the screens of a failed start (`operatorLine`) gets none: it stands before `api` is known, and there is no policy to read.

— *Why:*
- "Changes to this policy" of version 2 promises "The app shows you a new version before it applies to you, and you can withdraw a consent then", and CGS §42-520(b)(3) asks to notify and to give "a reasonable opportunity … to withdraw consent", by "all reasonable electronic measures" (sources 3.11 spec); version 3 applies from 2026-11-27;
- the bell: the notifications, the policies' changes among them, go to a bell of the main menu beside the user's icon, which every screen of the frame shows, so an adult meets the notice without opening Settings; Settings holds what an adult sets, and the choice of the learner keeps the mockup's `who`;
- the dot until shown, remembered on the device: the notice stays in the bell until its day, and the dot says only that something new is there;
- a page of its own for the next version: each page holds one version, so the headings stay at the levels the legal pages give them, the notices lead straight to the version they tell of, and the current version is one button away;
- a page of its own for Who we are: the operator's contacts are what a reader of "Accessibility" looks for, and on a Russian screen the English heading stands after the whole translation;
- the consent names the current version: the contract says so, and a request that named the next one before its day is refused.

— *Rejected:*
- both versions on one page, each under its heading — the next version would stand under the whole current text and its translation, and the body's headings would go one level lower than the legal pages have them;
- the policy opened at the heading Who we are — on a screen with a translation the heading stands after it;
- the notice on Home — from E4 a child sees it there, and the notice is the adult's;
- the notice on the choice of the learner, under the profiles — the screen keeps the mockup's `who`;
- the notice at the end of the section's first page — the reason above;
- a notice the adult closes, remembered on the device — one more setting for a line that goes by itself on the day; the dot alone is remembered;
- what was seen kept by `api` — the contract has no field for it, and a device that shows the dot once more costs nothing;
- the operator's name and contacts as a field of the build or of the contract — a second place of the same truth, which can part from the text;
- "Try again" under a notice whose read failed — a line for a read its next opening makes anyway.

— *Does not decide:* account deletion, the second thing an owner can withdraw — E4, and then the line of withdrawal names it; the next version of a family's text — with its screens, E4; a notice outside the app, such as an email — `app` sends none, and the operator's means are the operator's.

— *Consequence:* `src/legal/` — `readLegal`, `nextOf`, `versionsOf`, `pageNext` and `dayText` in `texts.ts`, in place of `storageText`, `sectionOf` and `OPERATOR_SECTION` in `body.ts`, `NextLegalText` and `LegalOriginal` in `LegalText.tsx`, `NextTextPage`, `OperatorPage`, `nextPage` and the routes in `LegalPages.tsx`, and `NextVersions.tsx`; `InviteScreen` in `src/account/InviteScreen.tsx` and `openLegal` in `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`; the consent in `src/adults/WorkStorage.tsx`; the button in `src/tutor/TutorPage.tsx`; the keys `legal.next-*`, `legal.operator*`, `accessibility.operator` and `tutor.operator` in the dictionaries; tests; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

## Own key: the code

**R47. One module with no DOM, `KeyHolder` in `src/ownkey/holder.ts`, one per tab, holds the own key of the owner profile of the category `adult` while that profile is the learner chosen, and it alone gives the key out: `own_key` with each request that calls AI, and `own_key_provider` alone with each request that may give an engine's choice and carries neither a key nor a work. The page "Own AI key" is `#/adults/own-key` in the section for adults, and a key kept on this device is the record `key` of the store `ownKey` of the profile database, of version 2. "Don't use own key" and "Remove key" are one action, "Remove key". The list of providers may be empty, and the page says so. A limit of the key reached says when access comes back, where `api` names it, and the page says what to do by which limit ran out. `just walk` connects a key on `api`'s stub.**
- **The holder.** Its state is closed, opening — the key kept on this device being read — or open, with the provider, its name, the last four characters and whether it is kept, and beside them a refusal of the key or its limit. The key itself is a private field beside the state, never in it: the screens read the state by `useSyncExternalStore`, and the field of the page is not controlled, so the key is in no state of React. `aiFields` gives the key, and so no clock offset, or on platform AI the offset, as `src/api/keys.ts` builds them; `routeFields` the provider alone, or nothing. A new key comes in by `connect` only once `CheckOwnKey` accepted it; `setKeep` writes it to the device or erases it there; `remove` takes it from memory and the device, or from the device alone with no owner profile open.
- **Its life.** `AccountGate` opens the holder when the session's learner is the owner profile `adult` and closes it on every other step — "Switch learner", the sign-out, another account, a reload. It renders the learner's screens only once the holder is open, so that the first `ChooseNext` after a reload already names the provider of a key kept here. The section for adults and the learner's screens take the holder by `useKeyHolder()`; outside the gate, as in a screen's own test, a holder that never opens.
- **Which requests.** `own_key`, by `aiFields`: `SubmitWork` with a work, `GetRungWords`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, `ExplainDifferently` and `SubmitEssay`. `own_key_provider`, by `routeFields`: `ChooseNext`, `BeginAttempt`, `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun`, `CompleteLesson`, `ShowSolution`, `Done` and `SubmitWork` without a work: beside a work or a key `api` refuses it as `invalid_argument`, by the contract.
- **A refusal.** `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED` and `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED` on a call with the key close the AI of the attempt as the quota does, with a line of the cause — the owner's limit, the tier, the balance or the payment, if the outcome names it. When `own_key_limit` carries `access_returns`, the line goes on by the device's clock and in the screen's language: "Access comes back today at {time}", "… tomorrow at {time}" or "… on {date} at {time}", by `dayText`, as the quota does; with no time, or one already past — a repeat of an earlier answer, or a refusal kept beside the key —, the line of the limit alone. `app` takes the time whatever the cause and adds no reckoning of its own. The holder keeps the refusal beside the key, and the page says what to do; a call on the key that gave its words takes the refusal away. Nothing moves to platform AI but "Remove key". `CAUSE_KEY_NOT_FOR_PROFILE` has its words.
- **The page.** Grouped rows, as the rest of the section: the four rows [architecture.md](architecture.md) gives the page, in a group under it all; the provider from `ListOwnKeyProviders`, chosen already when it is the only one; the key in `<input type="password" autocomplete="off">` in its row, "Connect" grey until a key is typed; "Keep on this device", a switch, off. "Connect" gives `CheckOwnKey`'s five ends: accepted — the key in place of the former one, in memory and, with the mark, on the device —; wrong, no rights and the provider closed, each with its words tied to the field by `aria-describedby`; and a failed check for `UNSPECIFIED` and a value the build does not know. A sixth end is the refusal beyond the checks `api` lets the account make — `CAUSE_KEY_CHECKS_EXHAUSTED`, code `resource_exhausted`, with `Refusal.next_key_check`: `failureText` of `src/api/words.ts` words it, so the page says it in its alert below the form, as it says a check that did not go through — when the next check opens by the device's clock, today, tomorrow or on its date, three whole phrases like those of the quota, or, with no moment beside the cause, to try later. A refusal that carries `next_key_check` is this one whatever its cause says, since the moment comes beside this cause alone; `app` counts no checks of its own, which would part from `api`'s. A key not accepted changes nothing. Connected, the page shows the provider and the last four characters, the switch, "Replace the key" and "Remove key", asked first. With a limit reached it says beside the key the task's line of the limit, with its time, and then, in a paragraph of its own, what to do by which limit ran out: the owner's spending limit — raise it at the provider; the tier's ceiling — replace or remove the key; the balance — top it up; the payment — check its details; none named, or one the build does not know — raise the limit or top up the balance; each with "replace the key, or remove it to go to the platform's AI". With no learner chosen it says the key is the owner profile's and opens it by "Open my profile".
- **On the device.** The profile database of version 2 has the store `ownKey`, the key `key` and the record `{provider, name, key, last4}`, written only with the mark: the mark taken off, or a write that failed, erases it there. The other tabs hear of each change by `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-own-key`, the message `{profileId}`: each reads the database, and the key kept there takes the place of the one in memory in one step — with none kept, the one in memory goes —, unless a change made in that tab meanwhile outran the read. Writes to the device go one after another, and a refusal is noted only beside the key the call was sent with. "This device" has the line of a kept key at the owner profile, with "Remove key" — the same action —, and "Erase the profile's data" removes the key in memory too.
- **What `api` admits** (`api` v0.1.57): in the dev mode, Anthropic at the category `adult`; on a deployment the list is empty until П-16. `api` sets `own_key_limit`, and `access_returns` in it beside the ceiling of the provider's tier alone — 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month by the adapter of Claude of `tutor` v0.1.42 —, never beside the owner's own limit, the balance or the payment. The stub answers `CheckOwnKey` by the key's ending and calls with a key as without one, so it never gives `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`, and `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED` with the tier's ceiling only on a key ending in `-limit-tier`.
- **Tests.** `src/api/words.node.test.ts` — today, tomorrow and a date in both languages, the tier with no time and the owner's limit with none; `src/task/Task.browser.test.tsx` on the routing transport — the tier with a time on the reading of a sheet, the owner's limit beside the rung's words with none; `src/adults/OwnKeyPage.browser.test.tsx` — the line and what to do for each limit. `just walk` has a leg on `-limit-tier` — Д22.

— *Why:*
- one module gives the key out, so its tests hold every request: a screen asks the holder for fields and never sees the key;
- the key out of the state of React: the state goes to snapshots, the tools of React and the messages of errors;
- the gate waits for the holder: otherwise the first choice after a reload would go by the platform's route, and the attempt it begins with it;
- one action: a key kept and not used is a key on the device for nothing, and the requests go on platform AI either way;
- the empty list has its own line: on a deployment it is empty until П-16, and a form with no provider would ask for a key no one takes;
- the three phrases of the time: on the 30th or the 31st the ceiling comes back tomorrow, and west of UTC today, so a date alone would read oddly beside the quota's "today" and "tomorrow"; the time whatever the cause — the contract's "only when the provider says so" is `api`'s;
- what to do by the cause on the page and not on the task: the task's line is the learner's, and the page is where the owner acts on the key.
- the page in grouped rows: the section is drawn as a device's settings, and a form of native fields under four paragraphs stood apart from it; the rows under the form keep what the paragraphs said, each a line and the line under it.

— *Rejected:*
- the key in the session or in a context of React — the reason above;
- "Don't use own key" as a switch of its own that keeps the key — two actions with one result for the requests;
- the walk's leg as a change of its own — the code and its walk go in one change;
- the refusal of the checks tied to the key field like the check's answers — it is about the account's checks, not about this key; waiting until `api` sets its cause — the contract names it already, and a refusal without it reads as the common phrase;
- a time of `app`'s own beside the tier, with none from `api` — it would promise what the provider did not say; the date alone, without the time — 00:00 UTC is not midnight on the device.
- the four paragraphs before the field — a wall of words before one field, drawn apart from the rest of Settings.

— *Does not decide:* the PIN of the owner profile and its closing after a hidden page — E4, with the PIN of the section for adults; which providers a deployment opens — П-16.

— *Consequence:* `src/ownkey/holder.ts` and `OwnKeyContext.tsx`; `src/storage/ownkey.ts`, the store in `schema.ts` and `ownKey`, `keepOwnKey` and `dropOwnKey` in `profile.ts`; `src/adults/OwnKeyPage.tsx`, its route in `AdultSection.tsx` and the line in `DevicePage.tsx`; the gate in `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`; the fields in `src/task/Task.tsx`, `src/lesson/Lesson.tsx` and `src/next/useEngine.ts`; the words of the limit and of the checks in `src/api/words.ts` and `closesAI` in `outcome.ts`; the keys `ownkey.*`, `device.own-key`, `api.key-not-for-profile`, `api.limit-*`, `api.access-returns` and `api.key-checks*`; the leg of `walk/path.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

## The move to `spec` v0.17.9

**R48. `app` stands on `spec` v0.17.9: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0, and the part `deployment/` are at this tag. `.devcontainer/`, `.claude/settings.json`, CLAUDE.md and the rules of RUN.md stay as the move to v0.17.1 made them: the template did not change. The tag changes no contract, type, format or part; what it brings `app` is the look of the app by the interface mockup, and `app` builds it in an epic of its own, E5 «Интерфейс по эталону», after E3 and before E4, once the mockup's rules of style and its row of `spec`'s plan are written. The page "Accessibility" names the nonconformance 2.5.7 for moving the sheet once `canvas` has no buttons of scrolling: `canvas` 0.3.26 and newer, and `app` takes 0.3.28. The rows «в реестр spec» В62, В63 and В65 are closed by the next move, which `spec` recorded; В64 is no row «в реестр spec».**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec diff v0.17.1 v0.17.9`). v0.17.9 took in v0.17.2–v0.17.8 — the plan of the board module of `tutor`, an epic of `spec` for later, and the first tasks of `spec`'s epic «the app's interface by the mockup»:
  - the look of `app` and of the tool panel of `canvas` follows the interface mockup in `draft/mockup/`, and where the mockup and the documents differ, the documents win; the first slice is for a phone, a tablet and a computer; the tutor's panel at the side, with the solving steps at its top; an avatar on every profile; the adult's Help; the module card with the states of the skills by `./states` of `skill-map`; "Take from my paper" and "Add a learner" shown disabled; the sign-in through Google alone, and Apple is Т-41;
  - a closed list of buttons that show an icon alone, with the caption as the accessible name and a tooltip; the buttons "Up", "Down", "Left" and "Right" leave the row of `canvas`, the sheet is moved by dragging, and `canvas` declares the nonconformance 2.5.7, which `app` names on the page "Accessibility";
  - the words of the states of a skill are "Ready to start" and "Locked": they are the words of `skill-map`, and `app` has no string of its own for them;
  - the template, `tools/`, the contracts, `types/`, `format/`, `deployment/` and `legal/` are as at v0.17.1; `draft/mockup/` holds the mockup's sources, and its README with the rules of style is not in the tag yet.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.17.9, commit 2070352; `gh release verify v0.17.9 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "✓ Release v0.17.9 verified!"; `spec_tag` is v0.17.9, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature comes from the release v0.17.9, the same files as before: `release-verify` gives "deployment-1.tar.gz: spec v0.17.9, signed by spec's release.yml";
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.17.9, `@bufbuild/protobuf` stays 2.17.0, which the contracts name; `release-verify-npm` accepted all six packages of `@altnovatrail` in the lock file; `NOTICE` has the line of the contracts 0.17.9;
  - `.devcontainer/` — `diff` with the output of `just -f spec/justfile new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.17.9` gives the same as at v0.17.1: the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run and the volume of `api`'s development token; `.claude/settings.json` is as in the recipe's output;
  - CLAUDE.md, README, RUN.md and the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts` name v0.17.9; [architecture.md](architecture.md) names the packages 0.17.9 and the phone, the buttons that leave the row, and the epic by the mockup.

— *Why now:* v0.17.9 is the latest release (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/spec`, 2026-11-14). It brings the decisions on the look of `app` that its next epic stands on, and a session of `app` on v0.17.1 would read neither the mockup's decision nor the phone.

— *Rejected:*
- the intermediate tags v0.17.2–v0.17.8: each holds part of `spec`'s epic by the mockup, and v0.17.9 holds the whole of what is done of it;
- waiting for the tag with the mockup's README and its row of `spec`'s plan: neither is written yet, and until then a session of `app` would read v0.17.1, which has neither the mockup's decision nor the phone;
- the epic by the mockup in this move — without the rules of style and the row of `spec`'s plan its tasks have nothing to stand on, and its place among E3 and E4 is the author's;
- the nonconformance 2.5.7 on the page at this move — `canvas` 0.3.15 still has the buttons, and the page would name what does not happen with `app`.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; `run/DEBT.md`; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; `package.json`; the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [sources.md](sources.md).

## The plan's files and language

**R50. `app`'s work plan is laid out and worded by `spec`'s layout of the plan, as the template of `spec` v0.18.3 has it: tables 2, 3 and 4 are `run/EPICS.md`, `run/QUESTIONS.md` and `run/DEBT.md` — the former `DEBT.md` —, the open tasks outside an epic are `run/TASKS.md`, and RUN.md holds its rules and the current epic. The rules, the tables' openings and rows and the headings are in English, terse, for the session; the bodies of tasks, the epic's description and the rows of table 1 stay in Russian. A done task's summary sentence goes to the archive with its body. A task outside an epic keeps its row in table 1, a difference from the template.** The layout is that of the output of `just new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.18.3` in a clone of `spec` at v0.18.3 outside the repository.
- **The files.** Each opens with the template's rules and its next free number, without the record of closed rows: that is in the archives' sections «Закрытые строки таблицы 3» and in the «Сделано» of the task that closed a row. The rows are translated whole, the decisions recorded in them kept. Relative links lead from `run/`. `run/TASKS.md` holds the open tasks outside an epic.
- **RUN.md.** The rules are the template's, numbered as before, and the opening names the four files. The summary sentences of E3's done tasks went to the start of «Задачи эпика» and «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные за его время» of [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md), without their «Тело — …», which there leads to itself; the pointer «Таблица 4» left. RUN.md went 60,592 → 32,765 bytes, table 4 32,506 → 22,684.
- **What differs from the template**, each held by an entry or by the epic:
  - a task outside an epic has its row in table 1, with or without an epic: the epic's archive lists them with its own, and «Зависит от» cites them;
  - rule 7 names E3's word, in the epic's description, that the executor also merges in a session the author started; in an orchestrator's run the orchestrator merges;
  - under table 1, what «Зависит от» and «Ждёт» mean and the checks every code task ends with, from the plan of E2 and E3, translated; rule 6 points to them;
  - Epics names the archives of the done epics, since the done epics' list left with the history.
- **Older texts** are not edited, but for links in the archives: «Как пользоваться» is Rules, «Как устроен план: эпики» is Epics, «таблица 2, 3, 4» or «Д… в таблице 4 RUN.md» names the file of `run/`, and `DEBT.md` in the log and the archives is `run/DEBT.md`. The links of [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md) and of [architecture.md](architecture.md) to `DEBT.md` lead to `run/DEBT.md`.
- **CLAUDE.md and README.md**, Documents, are the template's: RUN.md and `run/` in place of `DEBT.md`; CLAUDE.md keeps its line of `docs/measurements.md`, which `app` has.
- **The checks.** `docs-check-repo` reads «В» and «Д» from `run/`, and a table left in RUN.md or `DEBT.md` beside it would be a second copy. The limits of `run/` are those of `spec`'s block `repository`; `docs-budgets.json` needs none of its own.

— *Why:* `spec` asks each repository to move its plan with its move to a tag that has the new layout, and every task reads RUN.md whole: tables 2 and 3, the summary sentences and the Russian rules were bytes no task needs. The row in table 1 stays because `app`'s epics list their tasks outside an epic there, and the table of the archive keeps them with the epic.

— *Rejected:*
- dropping the rows of tasks outside an epic from table 1, as `spec`'s own RUN.md has none — the epic's description cites one in the order of its tasks, and E3's table of tasks would lose its tasks outside an epic when it goes to the archive;
- translating the bodies of tasks or the archives — `spec` keeps them in Russian: the author writes and reads them;
- keeping «Сделанные задачи» in RUN.md until the archive of E3 — `spec`'s layout moves them, and they are 6 KB in every task.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; `run/EPICS.md`, `run/QUESTIONS.md`, `run/DEBT.md`, `run/TASKS.md`; `DEBT.md` removed; [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md); CLAUDE.md; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [measurements.md](measurements.md); the comment of `scripts/page.test.ts`.

## The move to `spec` v0.18.4

**R51. `app` stands on `spec` v0.18.4: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0, and the part `deployment/` are at this tag, and `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` has the template's three stages — `toolchain-core` with Node, just, Go and buf; `toolchain`, built `FROM toolchain-core`, with Playwright's three browsers and their system packages; `devcontainer` —, its apt reading a source of the snapshot alone. Every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/` is on a release of `.github` from v0.1.31 on, whose `release.yml` builds the release in `toolchain-core`, and whose `typescript.yml` and `documents.yml` run "TypeScript / lint", "TypeScript / vuln", "TypeScript / licenses" and the documents there, and "TypeScript / test" and "TypeScript / engines" in `toolchain`; the job Mockup of `ci.yml` takes the image of the stage `toolchain` by the action `toolchain` of `.github`. `toolchain` holds only what the tests need: the browsers, their libraries and fonts.**
- **What the tag brings `app`** (`git -C spec log v0.18.3..v0.18.4`, four commits, and `git -C spec diff v0.18.3 v0.18.4`):
  - a release builds the stage `toolchain-core` from the Dockerfile, with no cache; `toolchain` is built over it with what the tests need — in a copy of the kind `typescript`, the browsers and their packages —, and `toolchain.sh build` takes the stage from `TOOLCHAIN_STAGE`;
  - apt reads a source of its own, `https://snapshot.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/<ID>/` with the suites and components of the base image's `ubuntu.sources`, and no longer `--snapshot`, which read the live archive's indexes too; the ID and the list of packages are as they were;
  - the rest is `spec`'s own CI and its plan;
  - `app`'s card in `spec`, RULES.md, `run/DEBT.md` of `spec`, the contracts, `types/`, `format/`, `deployment/` and `legal/` did not change, and of the template only `kinds/typescript.Dockerfile` and the Dockerfile's core stage.
- **What changes here:**
  - `spec/` is at v0.18.4, commit 5867a6c; `gh release verify v0.18.4 -R altnovatrail/spec` gives "✓ Release v0.18.4 verified!"; `spec_tag` is v0.18.4, and `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature comes from the release v0.18.4: the same files, the times of the entries alone differ: `release-verify` gives "deployment-1.tar.gz: spec v0.18.4, signed by spec's release.yml";
  - `@altnovatrail/contracts` and `@altnovatrail/spec` are 0.18.4, `@bufbuild/protobuf` stays 2.17.0, which the contracts 0.18.4 name; `NOTICE` has the line of the contracts 0.18.4;
  - `.devcontainer/` — `diff` with the output of `just new-repo-draft app typescript 3.1 v0.18.4`, run in a clone of `spec` at v0.18.4 outside the repository, gives only the second network `novatrail-api` of the local run and the volume of `api`'s development token; `.claude/` is as in the output, but for `rules/code.md`, which is `app`'s own;
  - `uses:` is on SHA `93c848fb51a10a245968cf730b99cc709a7748bf`, the commit of the tag v0.1.31 by `gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/git/ref/tags/v0.1.31`, the latest release by `gh release list`, published 2026-11-16. Between v0.1.30 and v0.1.31 (`git diff v0.1.30 v0.1.31`, read 2026-11-16) the calls gain no input or permission: `typescript.yml` and `documents.yml` read from the caller's Dockerfile whether it has a core that holds less than `toolchain` and keep its image by a file, a key and an artifact of its own, and `release.yml` sets `TOOLCHAIN_STAGE=toolchain-core` for the version and builds that stage for the files. v0.1.32, commit `1704f78`, which took its place later, changed no shared workflow `app` calls;
  - CLAUDE.md, README, RUN.md and the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts` name v0.18.4; the comments of `justfile`, `ci.yml` and `release.yml` name the stages; [architecture.md](architecture.md) and `.claude/rules/code.md` say which stage holds what.
- **What each stage holds**, by `.github` v0.1.31. The core is all that `ci-lint` — Biome, `tsc` and the scripts on Node —, `ci-vuln` — `npm audit` —, `ci-licenses` — `spec`'s recipes on Node and `scripts/notice.ts` —, `release-files` — `vite build` and `scripts/release.ts` — and `docs-check-repo` need: `app` adds nothing of its own to the template's core. The browsers and the packages apt installs for them are needed by `ci-test` and `ci-engines` alone, and stay in `toolchain`. The step "Toolchain stages" of `typescript.yml` reads the Dockerfile so: `FROM toolchain-core AS toolchain` with instructions beyond `SHELL` — a core that holds less.

— *Why now:* v0.18.4 is the latest release of `spec` (`gh release list`, 2026-11-16), and v0.1.31 the release of `.github` that uses its stage. On `main` the step "Work out the version" built the whole stage `toolchain`, three browsers and 341 packages of apt, for `release-version` alone: 176 s in a run on `main` (measurements 7), 391 s in measurements 3.1 spec.

— *Rejected:*
- the release and the checks without a browser kept in `toolchain` — the cost above, and a Dockerfile behind the template is a difference no decision holds;
- a tool of `app`'s own in the core — none of the recipes the core runs needs one beyond Node, just, Go and buf;
- apt by `--snapshot`, as before — twice the download, from a source that gave from 259 kB/s (measurements 3.1 spec).

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `justfile`; `parts/`; `NOTICE`; `package.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.github/workflows/`; the comments of `src/canvas/jsx.ts` and `src/skills/jsx.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [sources.md](sources.md); [measurements.md](measurements.md); `.claude/rules/code.md`.

## The move to `spec` v0.18.6

**R52. `app` stands on `spec` v0.18.6: the submodule `spec/`, the packages `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0, and the part `deployment/` are at this tag; `.devcontainer/` is the template's of v0.18.6 with `app`'s two differences — the second network of the local run and the volume of `api`'s development token —, and so has `"shutdownAction": "none"`. The rows «в реестр spec» В66 and В67 are closed. The adult's correction of a module's level, which the card now gives `app`, comes as a change of its own once `api` has its method; this move builds nothing of it.**
- **What the tags bring `app`** (`git -C spec log v0.18.4..v0.18.6`, eleven commits, and `git -C spec diff v0.18.4 v0.18.6`):

The hook comes with the submodule, and `.claude/settings.json` is as it was;
  - the heading of the stage `toolchain` of the template's Dockerfile holds in `spec` and in a copy alike, and `new-repo` suffixes `spec`'s sections, `measurements 3.2 spec`, as it does decisions — the answer to В67, option (a);
  - `canvas` gives the picture of a work, `StoredWork.picture` of `PutWork`, by a method of its element, by a row of `spec`'s plan — the answer to В66, option (a); the stored works wait for that row;
  - `docs-check-repo` reads the plan's tables in `run/` alone, under their English headings — where `app` has had them since its plan moved to `run/`;
  - three rules in place of calibrating the learner model. `app`'s card in `spec` gives `app` "Easier" and "Harder" at a module's level in the section for adults, a band in one step by `ProgressService`, with the account owner's right, and "the level was set by an adult" with the date in every learner's progress. In `spec`'s plan it comes after `api`'s part and after `spec` writes the method, and the family texts name it in their next versions before it reaches a child. Until then `app` has no method to call;
  - RULES.md: table 3 is `run/QUESTIONS.md` alone, apt reads "a source of its own whose URI names the snapshot's ID" — what the move to v0.18.4 already took —, and the sentence on translating a plan is gone. `app` has its tables in `run/` and its plan in English, and nothing here named the old place;
  - `spec`'s `run/DEBT.md` gains a row on the outage of the snapshot that held the trial Releases of `canvas` and `app` on 2026-11-16, and one on the family texts; no row of it asks `app` to act now;
  - of `repo-template/` only the Dockerfile of the kind `go` changed, which `app` does not take; `contracts/`, `types/`, `format/`, `deployment/` and `legal/` did not change, and the archive `deployment-1.tar.gz` of v0.18.6 unpacks to the files of v0.18.4.
- **What changes here:**

## Essays

**R53. An essay — a task whose answer slot is bound `sheet` — has a screen of its own beside the task screen: "Check" sends the crop by `SubmitEssay`, the tutor's panel holds in its conversation the feedback of the last submission or why there is none, and "Done", open after the first submission, ends the attempt. No reminder about names stands before "Check", on an essay or on any other sheet: the camera's line before a photo of paper and Help's line for an adult are the reminders. The device keeps an essay as the work of any task, until the end of its attempt: there is no store `essays`, no screen "My essays" and no essay on "This device".** `api` closes the rubric path on its stub and not yet on models: the row of `spec`'s plan for models has no date, and the essays close on the stub and do not wait for it.
- **The screen.** `Task.tsx` reads the attempt as for any task and, for an essay, how many submissions `api` counts, `OpenAttempt.essay_submissions`; then `Essay.tsx` and `EssayScreen.tsx` take the place of `OpenTask` and `TaskScreen`, with the same layout, steps, history of writing, "Hint" and voicing and the task screen's foot: the row Hint · Done, a line, Check. Above the row stands the task screen's card: "Reading your work…" while a check is under way; "Feedback", which says the feedback is in the tutor's panel, with a Done of its own, while the last submission gave words; why Check or Done does not open, on its press, with OK. The panel opens by steps and with a hint, not with the feedback or the reading. There is no "Show the solution" and no rung: a rubric has neither.
- **"Check"** is open when the sheet holds a stroke; "Write an answer on the sheet" is its description while it is not. It sends `exportWork()`'s crop with the own key or the clock offset, one key for this work and the same on a repeat; a stroke is a new action. The answer's three ends, each a bubble of the panel's conversation: feedback — its words with the module's `lang`, or, without words, "No feedback this time"; unreadable — "The essay can't be read: {reason}. Write it again and send it", by the words of `blank` and `illegible`; unchecked — the outcome's words, or "The essay wasn't checked this time". A repeat whose answer was lost, `repeated`, brings no words: "The feedback was lost on the way. Send the essay again to get it". The text read stands under the canvas as "What was read". A line of status says "The feedback came", and the voicing says the feedback or the line. The screen holds the end, the text read and the words in memory alone: a page reloaded shows no feedback of an earlier submission, as the server keeps neither the text nor the words. `closed` beside an answer — `api` closed the attempt while it went — writes the submission to the attempt first and then shows the screen "This task is already over".
- **"Done"** is `Done` with `own_key_provider` by `routeFields`, `aria-disabled` until the first submission, by `api`'s count and those sent from the screen since, with the description "Done opens once the essay has been sent". Its answer is the result "Essay finished": the last feedback, "What the feedback looked at" — `SolvedTask.criteria`, without marks —, the solution if the task has one, the engine's choice, "Try it again" and the way back; in a lesson, `next` goes to the player.
- **"Report a problem"** stands at the feedback with words for a learner of the category `adult`: `TUTOR_REPLY_ESSAY_FEEDBACK`, rung 0, the words as shown.
- **No reminder before "Check".** A handwriting sheet with a slot written by hand, `sheet` or `anywhere`, sends the whole ink of the sheet to AI, an essay too, and no line about names stands above its card or row, nor in the description of "Check". Where names are kept out: the camera's line "Keep names out of the photo", before the photo of a page; Help's "Your name is made up" — write no real names in the work, an essay either — for an adult; and the page on the tutor, `#/tutor`. Whether this is enough for `spec`'s rule that the platform show the reminder is to settle in `spec` (Д288 `spec`). `english-essays` of `modules` leaves the reminder out of its statements to `app` (Д9 `modules`), so an essay has none.
- **On the device.** The essay's attempt is a record of `attempts`, its history in `history` and each submission in `submissions` as `{ context, historyLength }` — the context as the canvas gave it and the history's length then, for `PutWork`, with no crop, which nothing on the device reads again. Its end, the term of 30 days and a withdrawal go as for any attempt. Schema version 5 takes off the store `essays` of versions 3 and 4 with every essay in it, and the time limit erases the histories those essays left past their attempts; the essays the server holds as stored works stay there.
- **On the server.** While the profile's storage is on, the essay goes as every work does: at each submission, at "Done" and when the storage is switched on, its history and the contexts of its submissions.
- **Not checked yet.** The manual check on `api`'s stub and the leg of `just walk` wait for `api`'s container, Д34.

— *Why:*
- a screen of its own: an essay has no verdict, no rung and no solution shown, and its "Done" no counterpart on a task with a key; branches through `OpenTask` would touch every path of the task screen, its handwriting and its hint. What the two screens do alike is one code: the layout — the mode, the canvas, the panel with the hint and the steps, the result's end and "Hint" — `src/task/TaskLayout.tsx`, and the techniques of a step, the voicing of the screen, the work kept on the device and a closed attempt, `src/task/shared.ts`;
- no reminder before "Check": the reminders stand where names come in — the photo of a page, where the learner chooses what goes to the AI, keeps the camera's line, and Help keeps the adult's;
- the essay kept as any work: no screen to read essays again was ordered, and an essay kept on the device with no screen for it would be data with no use; one rule of erasure for every attempt leaves no count or erasure that has to tell an essay apart.

— *Rejected:*
- "My essays", `#/essays`, the store `essays` with the crop, the text read and the feedback of each submission, and the essays on "This device" with "Erase the essay" — the screen was not ordered, and the store goes with it;
- the screen and its button alone gone, the store kept for the server and "This device" — the store without its screen is data with no use too;
- restoring the feedback after a reload from the device — it would need the store above, and the server keeps no text of it;
- a standing line above the row of "Check" on every handwriting sheet, as the description of "Check" — the reminders stand where names come in;
- a confirmation before each submission — one more press on every check, and a learner would learn to press it unread.

— *Does not decide:* help for a learner under 18 at a work with a rubric — E4, with the section "Help"; the export of the device, whose file of version 4 has its field `essays` written empty — E4.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/Essay.tsx`, `EssayScreen.tsx`, `essay.ts`, `TaskLayout.tsx` and `shared.ts`; `addEssaySubmission` in `src/storage/profile.ts` and version 5 in `schema.ts`; the keys `task.done*`, `task.essay-*`, `task.feedback-*` and `task.criteria`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

## The workbook

**R54. A task on a workbook goes through the task screen as handwriting does: "Check" is open when each box holds a stroke, and closed with the line "Write an answer in each box"; it sends `exportWork()`'s crop of each filled box with its place, and nothing of the draft. After the check the list of lines under the canvas gives each line's verdict, and a line of more boxes than one each box's verdict with what was read in it, each line one row that wraps; the summary counts lines, "Not right yet: {count} lines of {total}".** `api` begins and checks a workbook and admits it on its stub where it admits a sheet, but on models only with the admission records of `tutor`, whose row of `spec`'s plan has no date: the workbook closes on the stub and does not wait for it. `just dev-app` of `api` gives the stub a synthetic child of 9: `novatrail/grade-3-math` is only for learners under 13.
- **What was there.** The task screen took the workbook with the sheet: `filled` by box opens "Check" and `answer_slots_filled`, `workOf` sends the canvas's cells as `WorkbookWork`, the marks stand on a cell by `answer_slot`, named with their line and box, and the list of lines under the canvas gives each line's verdict and readings. The lesson player shows a worked example on a workbook. No test had opened a workbook on the task screen.
- **The verdict of a box.** The verdict belongs to each answer slot, and the item's is made of its slots', by the module format, so a line of two boxes — "47 ÷ 6 = □ r □" — says which of them is not right yet: under "Line 5 • Not yet", "Box 1 ✓ Correct", "Read: 7", "Box 2 • Not yet", "Read: 11", as a nested list. A line of one box has the line's verdict alone: the box's is the same. Each line is one row that wraps, at 13 px — the line's verdict, then each box's with what was read, the nested list kept —, and the canvas keeps at least `min(20rem, 50dvh)` of the window, what stands under it scrolling with it: six lines of two boxes had hidden the canvas.
- **The words.** "Write an answer in each box", «Записать ответ в каждое окошко», in place of "Write an answer on the sheet", whose sheet the learner of a workbook does not answer on, and of the step's "Write an answer in each slot" at `answer_slots_filled`; "Not right yet: {count} lines of {total}", «Пока не верно: {count} строки из {total}», in place of items, which a workbook shows as lines; "Box {n}", «Окошко {n}», as the voicing and the marks name a box.
- **Not sent.** The draft — what falls in no box — and an empty box go nowhere: the canvas exports the filled boxes alone, and "Check" is not open until every box holds a stroke (`Workbook.browser.test.tsx`: the crops are the same with a draft stroke and without it).
- **Not checked yet.** The manual check on `api`'s stub with the synthetic child and a leg of `just walk` wait for `api`'s container, Д38.
- **Beside it.** The stand-ins for Google of the page and site tests, `scripts/google-stub.ts`, take an answer that the closing of its context cut off, Playwright's `TargetClosedError`, as none, not as an unhandled rejection (`scripts/google-stub.test.ts`): a run of CI on `main` had failed on it now and then.

— *Why:* the verdict where its slot is, as the task screen has it for an option and an item, and the canvas shows no verdict in a box: it takes the tutor's marks by `marks`, and no verdict. A box's verdict in the list under the canvas is the one place `app` owns beside the box.

— *Rejected:*
- each box's verdict on the canvas — a change of `canvas`, whose tutor's layer holds the marks of the rung, not verdicts; and the list is read in order by a screen reader, while a box on the page is not;
- a box's verdict on every line, one box too — "Line 1 ✓ Correct" and "Box 1 ✓ Correct" say the same thing twice;
- keeping the words of the sheet — a learner told to write on the sheet would not know the draft is not the answer.

— *Does not decide:* the workbook on models — the admission records of `tutor`, without a change of `app`; the module of the workbook on a deployment's catalog — `spec`'s, with the catalog.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/Items.tsx` (`Boxes`), `TaskScreen.tsx`, `summaryText` in `attempt.ts`; `exitLine` in `StepsPanel.tsx` and `onWorkbook` in `steps.ts`; the keys `task.check-boxes`, `task.summary-lines` and `task.box`; `src/task/Workbook.browser.test.tsx`; `scripts/google-stub.ts` and `scripts/google-stub.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `run/DEBT.md`.

## The adult's correction of a module's level

**R56. — *Why:*
- the correction is the adult's, beside the map whose skills it moves; with no adult's map, it has no place.

— *Rejected:*

## The way back to Home

**R57. Every learner's screen but the places of the shell's frame — Home and Subjects — has a way back. A module's skill map has "Subjects" back above its heading. A screen with the mockup's top bar has its Back, `back` of `IconButton`: "Back to Home" on the task screen, during the attempt and after it, `aria-disabled` while a check or the solution is under way, the result having none of its own; "Leave the lesson" in a lesson, and a row of the segments' pop-over, its task having no way of its own. A screen without one has "Back to Home", `HomeButton`, one element: beside "Try again" when the read of `#/task` or `#/lesson` did not answer, a lesson not read with the profile's pill above it, and at the end of a task and of a lesson, which a task closed by `api` shows too. The legal pages, a module's license, the tutor's page and the section for adults keep their bar's close.**

— *Why:*
- a learner's screen that showed the bar — "Switch learner", "For adults", "Sign out" — and its heading and line had no way to leave it: on `novatrail`, `app` v0.1.21, it was a trap;
- the skill map and the end screens already had this button, its words naming where it goes: a learner finds it on every screen alike;
- the task screen was the same trap while its attempt was open: its way back stood in the result alone. Leaving keeps what a reload keeps — the attempt stays open, with its work as the device saved it, and Home offers "Continue the task"; while a check or the solution is under way, the way is `aria-disabled`, as "Try it again" is, so that their answer is seen;
- a read that did not answer left "Try again" alone, offline possibly for long;
- the top bar's Back where the mockup draws one: the icon with its caption as its name reads as the way home only in a top bar, and `HomeButton` keeps its words where there is none.

— *Rejected:* a way home in the learner's bar — the mockup's top bar has Back on the screens it draws (its README, `Main.dc.html:424`); the browser's Back alone — an address opened directly has no history before it; a button beside the result's as well as the top bar's Back — two ways alike on one screen; an icon under a heading with no top bar — it would not read as the way home.
