— *Consequence:* `src/app/HomeButton.tsx` — `HomeButton` and `HomeWay` —, in `src/task/Task.tsx`, `src/lesson/Lesson.tsx` and `ReadFailed` and `Offline` of `src/boot/Screens.tsx` with `home`; "Subjects" back in `src/skills/SkillMapScreen.tsx`; the top bar's Back in `TaskLayout.tsx` and the lesson's player; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R58. A session compares `app`'s screen with the interface mockup itself, by `just mockup-compare`: the mockup of `spec/draft/mockup/` — or of another spec folder named, a clone of a newer `spec` — in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, at its two sizes. One run holds every state of the mockup's first slice across the three engines, and compares each of `app`'s screens with its state region by region — a box and a computed style, by the thresholds of epic E5 —, with the snapshots and the reports of both sides in `mockup/out/`, an artifact of the run. The browsers of the comparison draw `system-ui` and `sans-serif` by FreeSans, by `mockup/fonts.conf`. CI runs it as the job Mockup of `ci.yml`, the repository's own, with the `spec` submodule and the image of the stage `toolchain` by `.github`'s action `toolchain`; every `uses:` of `.github` is on v0.1.32.**
- **One run, both sides in the same browser.** `mockup/vitest.config.ts` has two projects. "mockup engines", in Node, opens each state of `tools/mockup/states.mjs` not under "Later", on the iPad, 1194 × 834, and the phone, 390 × 844, with `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, in the three engines at once: each has to reach `data-ready="true"` with no error in the console and no uncaught exception, and the shapes of its `#frame` — each button, field and glass and each element with a fill or a radius — agree with Chromium's in count, in box within 2 CSS px read to a tenth of a pixel, and in computed colours, radii, sizes and weights. "mockup", in the browser, an instance per engine, draws each screen of `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx` as the screen tests do — synthetic data, no `api`, no token —, at each size by `page.viewport`, and asks the command `mockupCompare` of `mockup/compare.ts`, which runs in Node: it opens the same state of the mockup in a new context of the same browser, reads each region on either side by Playwright — the test's frame by `ctx.frame()` —, compares them, writes the snapshots of both and the differences, and returns them.
- **A region** is a pair of places, `Find` — a role and its accessible name, whole; a part of a text; or CSS — in `mockup/regions.ts`, which the test in the browser and `scripts/mockup.ts` both take. Each is read in its page by `readElement`: its box, and its background laid over what stands under the centre of its box in the order of painting — `elementsFromPoint`, or its ancestors when it takes no pointer —, all over white; its edge, a border or else a ring of its shadow — no offset, no blur, a spread —, as the mockup draws the edge of "Continue with Google"; its text's colour over its background, its radius, its font's size and weight. `parseColour` reads `color(srgb …)` too, which an engine computes of `color-mix()`, as canvas fills the tool in use. `followsText` compares a region whose width follows its caption by its height, its y and the x of the edge it names; `contrast` holds a colour the README's «Contrast» replaces, the mockup's on one side and `app`'s on the other; `differs` gives, in words, the reason — a row of the mockup's «Where it differs from the documents», or a choice of `app`'s — under which a difference is reported and not held. It names a number of the box too — `x`, `y`, `width` or `height`, the x of the edge `followsText` names among them —: the box's difference is reported, not held, when every number of it beyond the threshold is named, and held when another is. An entry may name `devices`, the sizes it is compared at.
- **A screen whose task has not come yet** carries `until`, naming the task of epic E5 that brings it to the mockup: its differences are reported, not held. The task of a screen drops `until` and adds its states, and from then on each difference but those of `differs` fails the test.
- **What is compared**, each difference held but those its `differs` names:
  - the canvas's bar — the bar, the pen in use and "Zoom in" — in `task-pill` and `task-empty` at both sizes and in `task` on the iPad, and on the iPad `example` — the row of "Next", "Demo" and "Continue", "Next" and "Continue" —, the page scrolled to the element's top, where the mockup's sheet stands. Held: the widths, the radii, the glass and the accent; named: x 16 px further right, the screen's padding; y and the bar's height, since `canvas` shows "Draw with finger" only in pen mode; the font of a caption out of sight, and of the row, which has no text of its own;
  - the eleven states of the way in, `signin` … `who`;
  - the home screen's states `home` and `home-legend`, at each size: the heading, the card "Next up", "A task from my paper", the first module and its bar, and on the iPad the legend standing, on the phone its sheet. Named: the places and heights that the heading with the learner's bar above it, "What's next" with no lesson and "Disconnect" and "License" on a card give, the phone's tab bar, glass over the cards they move, and the font of a card or a bar, which has no text of its own;
  - `home-switch`, `home-help`, `map` and `map-essays`, at each size: the profile's pill; the profile's menu and its rows, "Switch learner", "Settings", "Help" and "Sign out"; Help in the menu's place, its way back, its first thing and its first page; the map's top bar, its Back and title, and the frame. Named: the font of a button of an icon alone, of a glass, a pop-over or a frame, which have no text of their own; Help's height, by its line that the AI checks a task from paper; the heading of the home stands 30 below its bar from 600 CSS px, as the mockup's greeting, so that on the iPad its places hold;
  - `lesson`, `lesson-segments`, `example` and `example-demo` on each size, and `lesson-end`, the example's bar among them; named: what the lesson player lets differ from the mockup;
  - `legal-privacy`, `legal-terms` and `legal-access`, at each size: the sheet, its heading, the chip, the close and "Read the full text"; the gist's caption, the gist and the line that the English text is in force; the next version's note; Accessibility's caption and standard. Named: what the legal pages let differ from the mockup.
  - `settings`, `settings-profile` and `settings-device`, at each size: the list's heading, its close, the owner's row and the groups of the device and of the texts; a profile's page — its heading, Save waiting and lit, the avatar and the group of hints —; This device's heading. Named: the close of `app`'s closed list of icon buttons; Save waiting in the colour of text, 4.5:1; the place of the hints, below `app`'s own words and lines; "Profile: Sam", whose page it is, for the mockup's name;
- **The fonts.** The container draws `system-ui` by WenQuanYi Zen Hei, a CJK font, and Firefox lays its `line-height: normal` a pixel taller than Chromium and WebKit at most sizes: the mockup's boxes parted by up to 7 CSS px between the engines, though its tree and styles were the same (measurements 9). FreeSans, which `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` installs for Playwright with `fonts-freefont-ttf`, agrees in the three; `mockup/fonts.conf` keeps the system's configuration and prefers FreeSans for both families, and the browsers of the comparison alone read it, by `FONTCONFIG_FILE` in their launch, so the tests of the screens draw as before. Neither side's stack names FreeSans, so it draws both, and the mockup's fonts are not compared anyway. GNU FreeFont is GPL-3.0-or-later with the font exception: a tool of the checks that never ships, an OSI license, named here as the exception `spec`'s rule of licenses asks.
- **Where it runs.** Not in `ci-engines`: no job of `typescript.yml` but the licenses' checks out the submodule, and the mockup is in it. The job Mockup is `ci.yml`'s own: `actions/checkout` with `submodules: true`, the image `novatrail-toolchain` of the stage `toolchain` by the composite action `.github/actions/toolchain` of `.github` v0.1.32 — from the cache of GitHub Actions that `typescript.yml` fills by the same key, or built —, `just mockup-compare` in it as `ci-engines` runs, and `mockup/out/` uploaded as the artifact `mockup` for 7 days whatever the end. The action's Go caches stay empty, and its save is not called. Every other `uses:` moves from v0.1.31 to v0.1.32 with it, one release for all as Dependabot's group `shared-workflows` keeps them: between the two (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/compare/v0.1.31...v0.1.32`, read 2026-11-17) no shared workflow `app` calls changed, and the tag v0.1.32 is commit `1704f782e76cee50ea5392cc0ea0d4b76c4b3672` (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/git/ref/tags/v0.1.32`). The job is not yet a required check of the ruleset `main`: that is the author's setting, as Д32 has it for "TypeScript / engines".
- **A failure of the mockup** — a state that does not open, or engines that part — fails "mockup engines" with its state, device and engines in `mockup/out/<state>.<device>.engines.json` and the three snapshots beside it; the session writes it as a row «В реестр spec» of `run/QUESTIONS.md` naming the run, and edits no screen of `app` for it (Д244 spec).
- **Not in the coverage.** `mockup/` is outside `src/` and `scripts/`, as `walk/` is: the logic is `scripts/mockup.ts`, which `scripts/mockup.test.ts` holds in Node and in Chromium on synthetic pages, since the job of the tests has no `spec/`.

— *Why:* `spec` gives the checks in a browser to the session and CI; the snapshots by which the author approved the mockup are in no repository (Д244 spec), so the engines are held to each other. Drawing `app`'s screen as its screen tests already do needs no server of `app`, no `api` and no token, and the command reads both sides in the very browser of the test, so an engine's own rounding is on both sides at once.

— *Rejected:*
- `app`'s screen on the development server or the build, its pages opened as the mockup's — a route of its own to each state, or the walk's `api`: what the screen tests draw already is the screen;
- the comparison in the page of the test, the mockup in an iframe beside it — the mockup's page takes the whole viewport at its size, and the epic's description puts the comparison in Node;
- the agreement of the engines by the regions of the compared screens alone — the states no screen compares yet would wait for their tasks, and Д244 spec asks for every state;
- the container's fonts — the engines part by up to 7 CSS px on text alone; a font of its own in `.devcontainer/` — FreeSans is there already; Liberation Sans — it parts as WenQuanYi Zen Hei does (measurements 9);
- a step of `ci-engines` — its job has no submodule, and checking the submodule out is the shared workflow's, for every caller; `.github` v0.1.31 with the image's steps copied into `ci.yml` — the action exists for a caller's own job, and a copy drifts;
- a run that fails on every difference of every compared screen from the start — E5's tasks bring the screens to the mockup one at a time.

— *Consequence:* `mockup/` — `vitest.config.ts`, `compare.ts`, `regions.ts`, `fonts.conf`, `engines.node.test.ts`, `screens.browser.test.tsx`; `scripts/mockup.ts` and `scripts/mockup.test.ts`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/`; `.gitignore`, `biome.json`, `tsconfig.app.json`, `tsconfig.node.json`, `.claude/rules/code.md`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); [measurements.md](measurements.md).

## The layout from the width and the pop-over

**R59. `app` lays every screen out from the width of the window, as the mockup's README has it: below 600 CSS px the phone's layout, one column of the whole width; from 600 the tablet's — a column of reading of 40rem; the frame of Home, 1120 at most, centred, for Home, Subjects and the skill map, the catalog's cards two in a row at any width and Home's modules one column; a step's card of the way in on the sky; and the whole window for a screen with a canvas. A list or a choice opens in one component, `PopOver`: a modal `<dialog>` that from 600 hangs from its button and grows from its corner, and below 600 is a sheet from the bottom with ×; Escape, a tap outside and a choice close it, and the focus goes into it and back to its button.**
- **One boundary, by the width.** `600px` in each media query, not `37.5rem`: the README names CSS px, and a query in `rem` moves with the browser's own font size. A zoom of 400 % of 1280 is 320 CSS px, the phone's layout.
- **The catalog's cards** stand two in a row from 600 CSS px, as the mockup's `subjects`, 16 apart, and one column on the phone, 12 apart; a module's card on Home is a row of the column of the whole frame.
- **`Screen`'s layouts** are those [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists, one for each kind of screen: `column` by default, `reference` the frame, `canvas`, `task` and `lesson` the whole window, `map`, `end`, `welcome`, `profiles` and `who`, `adults`, `sheet` and `failure`.
- **`PopOver`'s options:** `caption`, what its button shows in place of its words, which still name the dialog; `buttonLabel`, its button's name where its caption is not all of it — the profile's pill —; `icon`, a button of the closed list of buttons without a caption in place of its words; `centred`, hung below its button's middle — the avatars —; `gap`, between the button and the pop-over where it is not `GAP` of `hang.ts` — the frame's pop-overs hang 6 below its row —; `anchor`, the closest ancestor of its button it hangs from — a lesson's segments hang from the pill —; and `holds`, nothing closing it while a report is under way.
- **A bar above a screen** — the learner's, `LearnerBar` — keeps the width of the screen under it, by `:has()`, so that its edges are the screen's.
- **A tap outside** is a press and a release both outside the dialog's box: a drag from inside that ends outside, or a press on its edge or scroll bar, leaves it open.

— *Why:* the README makes the width the rule, not the device, and `spec`'s rule of accessibility asks WCAG 1.4.10 at 320 CSS px. A modal `<dialog>` gives the inertness of the rest of the page, Escape and the top layer of the browser itself, in the three engines; the pop-over's place is a function without DOM, `hang.ts`, so a test in Node holds each case.

— *Rejected:*
- the Popover API, `popover="auto"`: light dismiss of its own, but the rest of the page is not inert, and it moves no focus by itself; CSS anchor positioning — the place it gives depends on what each engine supports, where `hang.ts` gives one place that a test in Node holds;
- the kind of device, by `pointer` or the user agent — the README lays out by the width;
- four cards in a row where the frame holds four — the mockup's `subjects` has two at any width;
- a pop-over of `--nt-card` on the tablet — the mockup's pop-overs are glass of 0.9–0.96, within 2 of the panel's 0.86 over the page, which the pairs of text on `glass-panel/page` hold; on the phone it is white, `--nt-card`, within 2 of the mockup's 0.98 over the dimmed page.

— *Consequence:* `src/app/PopOver.tsx`, `hang.ts`, `Screen.tsx`; `src/theme/theme.css`; `common.close` in both dictionaries; the tests `src/theme/layout.browser.test.tsx`, `src/app/PopOver.browser.test.tsx`, `src/app/hang.node.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Buttons without a caption

**R60. A button of `app` shows its icon alone only if it is of `spec`'s closed list of buttons without a caption — back, close, the previous and the next task (‹ ›), the speaker at a text and the switch of voicing, Help, the tutor's button, the grabber of the phone's sheet, and the buttons of a task from paper: the camera's "Take photo" and "Choose from photos", a frame's "Remove the frame" and "Resize your frame", the notifications at the top right of Home's frame, and beside them the ring of tutor time where it shows its sign in place of the share — none of today's used, or all of it —, the skill map on a module's card on Home, "About the module", ⋯, on a module's card of Subjects, and the microphone and Send of a request about the page, and "Remove all frames" and Retake of the pick screen's top bar where it does not fit one row with their words, and Ask, the tutor's button of the row of solving a task from paper, and its Next task, once the task is over, where the row does not fit one line with their words, and in the head of its tutor's panel grown-up mode's key, pressed while the mode is on, and the delete key of the grown-ups' PIN it asks (В77 of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md)) —; and it is `IconButton`: 44 × 44, the grabber 88 × 44, but for those four, `BarButton`, round, 48 in the top bar and 44 in the row, their captions hidden to the eye and still their names, its caption from the dictionaries its `aria-label`, and a tooltip of the same words of its own, white on an opaque `#1d1d1f`, that comes on hover and on keyboard focus, stays while the pointer is over it, and that Escape dismisses (WCAG 1.4.13). A button of the list takes a caption in place of the list's only where it names where the button goes, one of the closed list `INSTEAD`. The places of the frame's row show their icon alone from 600 CSS px to 72em, by the same list, with the same tooltip of their words; the cognitive map, shut, has the same tooltip at every width, its name with why it waits, "Cognitive map — comes later". Its icons are app's own choice from Material Symbols Rounded, in [architecture.md](architecture.md). Every other button shows its caption.**
- **The icons**, of the platform's set: back — `arrow-back-ios-new`, the mockup's chevron; ‹ › — `chevron-left` and `chevron-right`, not canvas's `arrow-forward`; the three mirrored right to left; close — `close`; the speaker — `volume-up`, the switch filled while voicing is on, as the platform draws a mode turned on, where the mockup draws `volume-off` while off; Help — `help`, filled while its pop-over is open, as "Demo"; the tutor — `sms`, the mockup's bubble with three dots; the grabber — no icon, a bar. "Take photo" — `photo-camera`, the camera of the home screen's card, in the mockup's white ring of 72; "Choose from photos" — `photo-library`, where the mockup draws a thumbnail of the last photo, which app does not read; "Remove the frame" — `close`; "Resize your frame" — `open-in-full`, where the mockup draws a blue dot. The notifications — `notifications`, filled; the skill map — `account-tree`; "About the module" — `more-horiz`, filled while its pop-over is open; the microphone — `mic`, grey while off; Send — `arrow-upward` in the accent's circle; Next task of the row of solving — `arrow-circle-right`, mirrored, the mockup's arrow in a circle after its words, as the lesson's Continue.
- **The captions in place of the list's** (`INSTEAD`): back in a lesson is "Leave the lesson"; back of the task screen and of a task from paper "Back to Home", the one way home of those screens; the section for adults' back and close "Back to the section" and "Close the section"; a legal page's back "Back to the list". The close of a sheet and of the camera is the list's own "Close".
- **The tooltip** is `useTooltip` of `src/app/Tooltip.tsx`: `IconButton` holds it, `PlaceLink` of `src/app/Shell.tsx` for a place, and `PopOver`'s `tip` for a button that opens a pop-over with its words hidden, and `BarButton` of `src/paper/BarFit.tsx` for the two buttons of the pick screen's top bar and Ask and Next task of the row of solving, each only while its words are hidden. It sits in the top layer by `popover="manual"`, so that a dialog or a scrolling box does not clip it and the page does not scroll sideways; it comes after 0.3 s, the mockup's `.tip`, below the button or above it where only above has room, 8 px inside the window (`tip.ts`); its gap to the button is its own padding, so that the pointer crosses no gap onto it. It is `aria-hidden`: its words are the name already. A tap shows none, a finger having no hover; a click's focus shows none, only the keyboard's. Escape dismisses it, and a new keyboard focus brings it back; with the focus on its button Escape goes no further, so that a pop-over it stands in stays open until the next Escape, and with the focus elsewhere, under a tooltip of hover, it goes on there too. `IconButton` takes a `ref` to its button beside its own, so that a pop-over gives the focus back to it.
- **The check:** `captionlessOffList` of `src/test/a11y.ts`, in each screen's test in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, fails a button with no text whose name is off the list; it reads `INSTEAD` too, and passes a caption of it only on its button's icon. `src/app/IconButton.browser.test.tsx` holds the names, the sizes, the fill, and the tooltip — on focus, on hover, over it, Escape, at 320 px and in a pop-over — in the three engines.

— *Why:* `spec` closes the list and asks WCAG 1.4.13 of the tooltip, which the mockup's CSS alone does not give; one component holds the rule for every screen that comes, and the test of the screens holds that none goes around it.

— *Rejected:* `aria-labelledby` on the tooltip, kept in the page hidden — two copies of the words for a name that `aria-label` already gives; the native `title` — Escape does not dismiss it, and touch never shows it; a tooltip on a tap — it would flash under the finger; Escape passed on to the pop-over too — the tooltip would close with the pop-over, and the focus move, which 1.4.13 asks not to need; `volume-off` for voicing off — the platform draws a mode turned on as the same icon filled; the shutter as a bare ring, as the mockup's — a target with no picture in it, where the camera of the card says what it does.

— *Consequence:* `src/app/IconButton.tsx`, `Tooltip.tsx`, `tip.ts`, `Shell.tsx`, `Help.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `PopOver`, `ReadAloud`, `VoicingToggle`, the screens' top bars and the screens of `src/paper/`; `src/theme/theme.css` and the pairs of `contrast.node.test.ts`; the captions in both dictionaries; `.sonarcloud.properties` leaves `src/app/icons.ts` out of SonarCloud's duplication search, as the dictionaries: the script writes an entry of one shape for each icon; `src/test/a11y.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The way in by the mockup

**R61. The sign-in, the invite code, the owner's profile and "Who is learning?" are the mockup's states `signin` … `who`, but where the documents say otherwise: the sign-in, the code and the statement each on a dark card on the sky of the trail to the spark; Google's button Google's own, its light theme and its "G"; the invite code in two steps — the code in four boxes of four characters, then the statement "I am 18" and the acceptance of the texts in force, each name a link to its legal page, the ticks round —, sent together; each profile with an avatar of the set of `app`, eight codes of pictures of Material Symbols Rounded under Apache-2.0 on gradients of the mockup's, chosen in a pop-over and optional, the set's own shown for none and for a code the build does not know; the countries — every code of ISO 3166-1 —, chosen in a pop-over; the owner's profile a dark card on the same sky; and "Who is learning?" on it too, each profile a dark card that its one button covers, "Start" its look, and "Add a learner" a card of glass, shown, disabled, with its line why.**
- **The sky and the cards** — `WelcomeScreen` of `src/signin/Welcome.tsx`, the layout `welcome`: the sky over the whole window — `--nt-sky` under four radial glows, the stars, the trail of maths, + × ½ x² √x π ∫, to the spark —, drawn and hidden from assistive technology; the name NovaTrail at its top left, and on the sign-in the language's pill at its top right. Each step stands on a dark card, inside which the generic tokens are the `--nt-sky-*` ones of [architecture.md](architecture.md), so a step's screen keeps its own rules. From 600 CSS px the iPad's composition — the name, the pill, the trail at the left, the card of 440 at the right — stands in a middle of 1194 × 834 centred both ways, on a computer too, as the mockup's `desktop` has it; from 600 to 900 the trail is left out and the card centred; below 600 the name and the pill in a row, the trail small above the sign-in's card alone, that card at the bottom, and a later step's card right under the name. "Privacy and legal information" stands at the card's foot, the one way to the legal pages; the note for grown-ups has a round "18", hidden, as its words say it.
- **"Who is learning?"** — `ChooseScreen` of `src/account/`, the layout `who`: the sky of the sign-in with the mockup's stars over the whole window, the bar the name in its middle and "Sign out" at its end, a pill of glass with its icon, the heading at 48 and its line, and a row of cards of 300 × 380, radius 36. A profile's card is `--nt-sky-card`: the avatar at 140 in a ring of white at 0.16, the pseudonym at 26 bold, the line whose it is and "Start", a pill of `--nt-who-start`. Its one button holds the avatar and the pseudonym, named by the pseudonym alone, the line its description, and its `::after` covers the card, so a tap anywhere on it chooses; "Start" is hidden from assistive technology, and the focus ring, white on the sky, rings the card. "Add a learner" is a card of glass, white at 0.12 blurred, its dashed ring and plus, its name dimmed while it is disabled, and its line why in white at 0.9. No "For grown-ups" and no line at the foot: the design leaves them out, and the section opens from the profile's menu. Below 600 CSS px the cards stand two by two, 244 high. The design's `#2f6bff` and white at 0.55 miss 4.5:1 over the sky's glows: the mockup takes the replacements its README gives for contrast, and so does `app`.
- **The owner's profile** — `OwnerProfileScreen`, the layout `profiles`: the sky and stars of "Who is learning?" (`src/account/Stars.tsx`), the name in the bar, and a card of `--nt-sky-card` of 520, radius 36: the heading at 40, the avatar at 120 in a ring of white at 0.08, its button and "Choose a country" `--nt-own-link`, the rows white at 0.07 with a ring at 0.1, the hint white at 0.55, and "Continue" a pill of `--nt-who-start` while it is on. The design's ring at 0.1 misses 3:1 for the field's edge, so the field has an edge of its own at 0.5, as the sign-in's. The pop-overs stay light. No line under the heading: the design leaves it out, and the hint under the rows still says the name is made up.
- **Google's button** is Google's light theme (sources 4.8 spec): white, a stroke of 1 px inside, Google's text colour — the `--nt-google-*` tokens —, the words "Sign in with Google", in Russian as the rules allow, and Google's "G", `src/signin/google-g.svg`: the light pill icon of Google's pre-approved assets, `signin-assets.zip`, its white circle left out, unchanged in size and colour. It is Google's artwork and trademark, not under the Apache License, by the exception `spec`'s rule of licenses makes for it: `NOTICE` names it so, and Biome leaves the file as Google drew it.
- **The avatars** are those [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists for the way in: `peak`, `rocket`, `leaf`, `star`, `moon`, `wave`, `sun`, `bolt`, in that order, `peak` the set's own. A code names its picture for good: a new picture is a new code, so that a profile a newer build gave stays as it was chosen wherever the build knows its code. The avatar and its pop-over are `AvatarChoice` of `src/account/`, which a profile's page in the section for adults draws too, saved there with the form.
- **The countries** are every code of ISO 3166-1, all 249: neither the deployment configuration nor the contract names the countries a deployment takes; `api` says it of the one chosen, by `CAUSE_COUNTRY_NOT_ACCEPTED`, and its row takes the focus with the line. A list of the deployment's is В72 of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md), «В реестр spec».
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: the headings of the owner's profile and of the choice keep the words [architecture.md](architecture.md) gives — "Your learner profile", "Who is learning?" —, and `app`'s words have no full stops; the code's boxes four of four characters, not eight of one, as `api`'s codes are sixteen; one button to the legal pages; no "Free, by invitation" — the price is undecided (О-14); every country, not the mockup's five; no "Code accepted" before `api` answers, which it does only with the account.
- **The code and the ticks.** One field lies under the four boxes, its text and caret transparent: a paste fills them all, and a screen reader hears one field, "Invite code". The box the next character goes to is ringed in the link's blue, and the focus rings the boxes. A tick is the browser's checkbox with no look of its own, a target of 44, over a ring of 24 that fills with the accent and its tick; in forced colours, the browser's own. "Back" on the code's step signs out, as the mockup's goes back to the sign-in.
- **A step's button** looks off, by `aria-disabled`, until its step is given — the code all sixteen characters —, as the mockup draws it, and keeps the focus, as "Check" does: pressed, it says what is missing — the code, the statement, the acceptance, the country — and focuses it. What is typed and ticked on the invite code's screen is kept in `AccountGate` while the legal pages stand in its place, and dropped once the step is over.

— *Why:* `spec` makes the mockup the target of the look and keeps the documents' behaviour; a card that is its own button, or a button "Start" of its own, would give voice control and a screen reader a name unlike the label they see (WCAG 2.5.3), or several buttons "Start" alike for one choice each; the contract keeps the avatar a code of the client's set, optional, and asks the set's own picture for a code unknown. Google's rules ask the standard "G" on any button of one's own (sources 4.8 spec). One step for the code keeps a mistyped code apart from the texts, and sending both together keeps `api`'s one call that makes the account.

— *Rejected:*
- a "G" drawn by paths of `app`'s own, or no "G" — Google's rules ask its standard "G";
- Google Identity Services' rendered button — more of Google's script in the page, while `app` signs in through Firebase Auth's window;
- the whole card as the button, named by `aria-labelledby` — axe finds its name unlike its visible text (2.5.3); a button "Start" of its own — one choice with two targets, and as many "Start" as profiles;
- the line "This is how you learn here. Use a made-up name, never your real one" under the owner's heading — the design leaves it out, and the hint says it;
- the pseudonym's initial in the avatar, as the design drew it — the contract gives the avatar of the set;
- uploaded pictures or initials — `spec` allows the set alone, and a picture of the learner is data the repository and `api` do not take;
- emoji for the avatars — they draw differently on each system, where Material Symbols is built in at its exact version;
- the mockup's five countries, or a list of `app`'s own of what a deployment takes — the deployment's list is no field of the configuration, and one of `app`'s would part from `api`'s;
- the code checked by `api` at its own step — the contract has no such call, and `CreateAccount` is one;
- the statement and the texts on the code's step — the mockup's two steps, and a shorter screen on the phone;
- one field for the code, the browser's own checkbox, no "Back" on the code's step and the texts in place below the ticks — the mockup draws its boxes, its round ticks, its "Back" and its links; a box for each character — sixteen of 16 px on the iPad's card;
- `disabled` on a step's button — a button that takes no focus says nothing of what is missing.

— *Consequence:* `src/signin/Welcome.tsx`, `SignInScreen.tsx`, `google-g.svg`; `src/i18n/LanguagePill.tsx`; `src/app/Brand.tsx`, `PopOver.tsx`, `hang.ts`, `Screen.tsx`; `src/account/InviteScreen.tsx`, `OwnerProfileScreen.tsx`, `ChooseScreen.tsx`, `Stars.tsx`, `FormFailure.tsx`, `avatars.ts`, `Avatar.tsx`, `AvatarChoice.tsx`, `AccountGate.tsx`, `session.ts`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css` and the pairs of `contrast.node.test.ts`; both dictionaries; `NOTICE`; `biome.json`; `src/test/a11y.ts`; the tests; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md) and `docs-budgets.json`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`.

## The home screen by the mockup

**R62. The states of the skills of a module connected, on its card on Home, are what the function `./states` of `skill-map` gives, the rule its map draws by, and `app` counts them: the card and the map never part. Its counts are the squares of its skills, "{m} of {n} mastered" and the skills due for review — or, for a module whose skills have a rubric and no verdict, its mark —; the legend of the five states and the two marks stands below the cards from 600 CSS px, and on a phone behind "What the marks mean", as a sheet. A module's tile is the first letter of its title on one of the mockup's three gradients, by its ID.**
- **`skill-map`** is the version `package.json` pins, on the icon set of `canvas`: `./states` is in the package since 0.1.11.
- **The counts.** `useSkillCounts` reads `GetSkillMap` by the learner's action for each module connected with a current version, and the skill graph of the version its answer names, as the map does, and counts the statuses of `skillStates` at the device's time (`src/subjects/states.ts`); from the same answer and graph it takes a chain's place and the skill a card's Continue works on. Each map is asked once for its profile, module and version, and one that failed again with the next lists read — a retry, a connection —; a card of another profile never takes its counts. A map that does not come, a module with no skill graph or a graph with no skill leaves the card with no squares and no foot; the cards of Subjects have none.
- **The squares** are one for each skill, in the colours the mockup's README gives for contrast, in the legend's order, and `role="img"` named by every state and mark with its count; the words beside them are "{m} of {n} mastered", and the skills due for review a chip, "{r} skills to review". A module with no skill of verdict is "Feedback by criteria", with the count of the skills with a rubric.
- **The icons** of the states and marks are `spec`'s table of them, in the legend as on the map; the camera of "Tasks from my paper", `photo-camera`, the "i" of the legend's button, `info`, and the card's own are `app`'s, in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **A module's tile** is decoration, which WCAG 1.4.3 excepts: the format gives a module no picture of its own, and CSS draws its letter, hidden from assistive technology. It is `src/subjects/ModuleTile.tsx`, shared by Home, Subjects, the skill map's module and the end of a lesson.

— *Why:* `spec` gives the card the rule of `skill-map` by `./states` and the legend to `app`; a count from that function and from that answer is the map's own, which a test holds on the element itself.

— *Rejected:* a state of `app`'s own from the answer — the card and the map could part; the legend inside each card — four lines on a phone where the mockup has one button.

— *Consequence:* `src/subjects/` — `states.ts`, `useSkillCounts.ts`, `Legend.tsx`, `HomeCard.tsx`, `MySubjects.tsx`, `ModuleTile.tsx`, `modules.ts`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries; the tests `states.node.test.ts`, `useSkillCounts.browser.test.tsx`, `HomeCard.browser.test.tsx`; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx` — `home` and `home-legend`; `package.json`, the lock file and `NOTICE`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Help, the profile's menu and the skill map by the mockup

**R63. The profile's menu is the pill of the learner chosen — the avatar, the pseudonym and a chevron — at the top right of Home's frame and at the end of a lesson's and a task's top bar, and behind it a pop-over that names who is learning now, with no tick, and holds "Switch learner"; after a line "Settings", the section for adults, and, for a learner of the category `adult`, "Help", each with a chevron; after a line "Sign out". It is the same on every screen that has it. Help opens in the menu's own place, with "Profile" back to its rows, and says three things — the tutor is an AI, a program checks every answer, and the work the AI read wrongly is written again or reported by "Report a problem"; the name is made up, so no real names go into the work — and leads to three texts of `app`: Privacy Policy and Terms of Use, each a sheet over the screen, and the page Accessibility, with no external link and no helpline. `LearnerBar` stands on no screen with a pill of its own.**
- **The pill on every screen of the profile:** [architecture.md](architecture.md) has "Switch learner" seen on every screen of the profile, so that an adult who hands the tablet over switches where they are — in the frame, in a lesson, on a task, and by `ProfilePill` on a lesson's or a task's screen with no top bar of its own.
- **The pill's name** is "{pseudonym}, profile menu", its words in its name (WCAG 2.5.3), and its menu is the dialog "Profile menu": the mockup's "Sam, switch learner" names a menu of one row. Below 72em in the frame it shows its avatar alone, still so named, by `spec`'s rule of buttons without a caption.
- **Help's words** are the mockup's, in the second person and with no first person of AI, in `en` and `ru`; "Report a problem" is the button's own words. A learner of another category has none: the Help of a learner under 18, with whom to turn to, is E4's.
- **The pop-overs of the frame** hang 6 below its row, as the mockup's, by the `gap` of `PopOver`, `BELOW_BAR` of `hang.ts`. A button of the closed list shows no tooltip while what it opens is open, so that the tooltip in the top layer never stands over its dialog.
- **The bar by the screen:** Home's frame on Home, Subjects and the skill map, none on the lesson, the task and a task from paper, which have their own, and the bar of buttons on any other screen, so that a screen yet to come keeps a way to switch.
- **Help in the menu.** The menu shows Help in its rows' place, its heading taking the focus; "Profile" brings the rows back, the focus on "Help"; a text pressed closes the menu first. The dialog keeps its name, "Profile menu", where the mockup's takes Help's.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, named by its region of the comparison: Help is taller by its line that the AI checks a task from paper.

— *Why:* the row holds only places, and what is not a place — the learner, the settings, Help and the way out — is the profile's, behind its pill, as the mockup's `home-switch` draws it; the tick named a choice in a menu that holds none; the settings are in one place, the section, and not split between pop-overs; [architecture.md](architecture.md) asks the switch on every screen of the profile, and the documents win. One component, `LearnerMenu`, holds the pill and its menu on each screen that has it.

— *Rejected:*
- "Settings" and "Sign out" on the screen beside the pill — the menu is where the profile's actions go;
- no pill on a screen the mockup draws without it — "Switch learner" would leave a screen of the profile;
- Help as a page of its own, or for every category — `spec` puts it behind its button, and a child's Help names helplines;
- Help and "Grown-ups" as places of the row — they are no places;
- the hint mode in the menu of a lesson and a task — a setting, it stands on the profile's page with the others.

— *Consequence:* `src/app/LearnerMenu.tsx`, `Help.tsx`, `App.tsx` — `LearnerBar` by the screen —, `Shell.tsx`, `PopOver.tsx`, `IconButton.tsx`, `hang.ts`; `src/account/AccountGate.tsx` — `openLegal` of the learner —, `Avatar.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries; the tests `src/app/Help.browser.test.tsx`, `LearnerMenu.browser.test.tsx`, `hang.node.test.ts`, `src/account/AccountGate.browser.test.tsx`, `walk/path.test.ts`; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The lesson player by the mockup

**R64. The lesson is the mockup's `lesson`, `lesson-segments`, `example`, `example-demo` and `lesson-end`. The whole window lies under a top bar: its glass pill holds Back, captioned "Leave the lesson", the lesson's title and, under it, the line of the segment shown, "Worked example · 2 of 4" with a chevron, and the profile's pill stands at the bar's other end, with a place after it where a task segment draws the pill of its mode: the task segment has no bar of its own. The line opens the segments in a pop-over, hung 6 below the pill: the lesson's title and its module, the rows by places — the number in a circle, a tick for a passed one, the kind and how far it is — and "Leave the lesson". An explanation is a card in the middle, its heading its caption, "Read aloud" at its corner; a worked example and a task fill the window under the bar, the canvas's statement, sheet and row its own. "Continue", its icon after its word, stands in a row of glass at the bottom, or in the canvas's row on an example. The end of a lesson is a card in the middle — the module's tile and title, the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" after a filled star, and the engine's way —, the profile's pill above it.**
- **Back of the lesson** is `back` of the closed list of buttons without a caption, with the caption "Leave the lesson" in place of "Back": the mockup's `aria-label`, and its words name where it goes. `IconButton` takes such a caption only from `INSTEAD`, a closed list too, each caption for one button of the list, which `captionlessOffList` reads by the button's icon.
- **The line** under the title is a button over the whole heading, 44 high, so that the target holds WCAG 2.5.8; its name is "{line}: segments of the lesson", its words first (2.5.3). The pop-over hangs from the pill, not from the line, as the mockup's, by `PopOver`'s `anchor`; `Screen`'s top bar takes the line by `under`.
- **A row's name is its words** — the place, the kind and "passed", "now", "later" or why it does not open: axe's `label-content-name-mismatch` fails a name other than the visible text. A passed row shows a tick in place of its number, the number out of sight beside it. The shown row's kind is bold, as the mockup's, its state in the accent of a segment, `--nt-segmented`, which holds 4.5:1 on the glass. A press on a row closes the pop-over, and one that does not open says why in the screen's line.
- **The explanation's caption** is the `h2` "Explanation · 1 of 4": the format gives a segment no title, where the mockup shows one. On an example and a task the `h2` stays, out of sight, for the focus on a change of segment (2.4.3).
- **The profile's pill** at the end of the lesson's bar and above its end, where the mockup draws none: "Switch learner" is on every screen of the profile, as on the skill map; above "Try again" of a lesson not read too.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: the statement and the sheet are canvas's own, with no card and no speaker of their own, which `app` cannot draw in their place (В74); "Read aloud" of an example stands above the canvas, not at the statement; the segments' pop-over has no switch "DEMO", which only the mockup has; on a phone canvas's row keeps its line, so Next and Continue stand 10 inward; Next is paled by its colour, not its opacity, as canvas draws it; the accent as text is `#0066cc`, the replacement the mockup's README gives for contrast; the tile's letter is CSS's; the bar and the row have the body's 14 px, `app`'s size of text, where the mockup has 16.

— *Why:* `spec` puts every list or choice behind a small button, and the mockup the lesson's segments behind the line under its title; the documents win where they say more — the switch on every screen, a segment without a title, Label in Name.

— *Rejected:*
- a strip of chips under the title, one for each segment — the mockup's `lesson` has none, and its list is a pop-over;
- a row named by its place and kind, its state its description, as the chips were — the visible "passed" would be out of the name (2.5.3);
- the pop-over hung from the line — the mockup hangs it from the pill's left edge;
- the shown row's kind underlined — the line ran into "now" under it;
- any caption for a button of the list — the closed list would hold any words;
- a card of the example's statement in `app` — canvas draws the statement itself, and two statements would stand on one screen.

— *Consequence:* `src/lesson/LessonScreen.tsx`, `SegmentBar.tsx`, `Lesson.tsx` — `LessonEnd` with the module, the pill above a lesson not read —; `src/app/IconButton.tsx` — `caption` and `INSTEAD` —, `PopOver.tsx` — `anchor` —, `Screen.tsx` — the layouts `lesson` and `end`, the top bar's `under` —, `App.tsx` — no `LearnerBar` on the lesson; `src/subjects/ModuleTile.tsx`, out of `MySubjects.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts` — `lesson-complete`; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries — `lesson.segments-open`; `src/test/a11y.ts`; the tests `src/lesson/LessonScreen.browser.test.tsx`, `Lesson.browser.test.tsx`, `src/app/IconButton.browser.test.tsx`, `PopOver.browser.test.tsx`, `LearnerMenu.browser.test.tsx`, `src/a11y/screens.browser.test.tsx`; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The task screen by the mockup

**R65. The task screen, with the tutor's panel closed, is the mockup's `task-pill`, `task-mode`, `task-empty`, `task-reading`, `task-not-yet`, `task-correct`, `choice`, `choice-wrong` and `solution-ask`. The canvas fills the whole window, and over it float a top bar — Back, captioned "Back to Home", the task's title, and at its other end the profile's pill and the pill of the mode — and a foot in the middle, the canvas keeping the room they cover as `--novatrail-canvas-pad-top` and `-bottom`: the screen's lines, the verdict's card and a row of glass, Choose an answer · Hint · Show the solution, a line, Check, with the tutor's round button at the window's right edge. The mode's pill opens a pop-over with the two modes and their lines; "Choose an answer" a pop-over of the options, 14 above the row; "Show the solution" a pop-over of its question with Cancel and "Show it". The card, 460 wide above the row: "Reading your work…" with a turning ring while a check is under way; the lock of Check, on its press, with its line and OK; "Not yet" or why the work could not be read, with what was read in the one item or the summary, Hint and "Try again"; "Correct"; "Solution shown". In a lesson the lesson's bar stands in place of the task's, and the mode's pill at its end. No reminder about names stands above the row.**
- **Back** is `back` of the closed list of buttons without a caption, with the caption "Back to Home", `INSTEAD`'s second: the way home in the task's own bar, `aria-disabled` while a check or the solution is under way. The result keeps no way home of its own.
- **The mode's pill** is named "Mode: Step by step" (2.5.3: its words first), and its pop-over "Mode"; a choice closes it. The pill is drawn only while the task has steps and its attempt is open, as the switch before it.
- **"Choose an answer"** shows, on a task's one slot bound `choice`, the option chosen and its verdict's sign, and is then named "Your answer: 20. Change it", once checked "Your answer: 20, Not yet. Change it" — the verdict in a word, which the sign alone does not give; a tap or Enter on an option closes the pop-over there, the arrows walking the group. Once the attempt is over the row keeps it alone, its options taking no tap. The options keep the browser's radio buttons and their verdicts by a word and a sign, as [architecture.md](architecture.md) has the verdicts; the pop-over takes the focus on its first option.
- **The card on a phone** stands 16 from the window's left and, where the tutor's round button stands at the right — the foot's `data-tutor-side`, a task of a module with the tutor —, 72 before the right edge; on the screen of a task from paper, whose Ask is in the row, 16 from both, in the middle, as the mockup's `solve-*` (`src/theme/theme.css`).
- **The card** holds what the line "Not right yet: …" and the reading under the item held — the reading of a task's one item with one slot on the sheet; other readings stay under the canvas, and the summary stays `role="status"`, out of sight. The card of Check's lock comes on a press of Check while it is locked, as the mockup's `task-empty`, and goes with OK or once Check opens; "Not yet" stays till "Try again", Hint or the next check; Hint in it is the row's Hint. Check, locked, is the muted text's grey, as the mockup's; while the work is read it is grey too, where the mockup keeps it blue: it takes no press till the verdict comes.
- **The question of the solution** takes the focus on Cancel, the answer that keeps the task.
- **In a lesson** the task has no bar of its own: `LessonScreen` gives a place at the end of its bar, and the task draws its mode's pill there by a portal, `AtTopEnd` of `src/app/TopEnd.tsx`. The learner's bar is gone from the task screen, its pill in the task's bar, `ProfilePill`, as on the lesson; the screens "This task is already over" and a read that failed have the pill above them.
- **The essay** has the same row: Hint · Done, a line, Check.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: the statement is canvas's own, inside its shadow root, with no card, no speaker of its own and no fold "Show less / Show all" on a phone: app cannot hide it, nor draw a card in its place — the row В74 of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md), «В реестр spec»; "Read aloud" and the voicing's switch stand in a line above the canvas; the mockup draws the task only inside a lesson, and the task's own bar is app's; the way on after "Correct" is the result in the panel, or the lesson's row; Check grey while the work is read; the accent as text is `#0066cc`, the replacement the mockup's README gives for contrast; the row, the card and the pop-overs have the body's 14 px, `app`'s size of text, where the mockup has 16.

— *Why:* `spec` puts every list or choice behind a small button — the options, the mode —, and the verdict in a card above the row while the panel is closed; one bar on a screen, as on the lesson and the skill map; the card gives a screen reader nothing new, the status line keeping what it said.

— *Rejected:*
- the options under the canvas — the mockup has them behind "Choose an answer", and the column under the canvas took its height from the sheet;
- the bar and the foot over the sheet with no room kept for them, as the mockup floats its row — a target of the sheet would lie under them (2.4.11): the canvas's padding keeps it clear;
- a statement card of app's own over canvas's — two statements on one screen;
- the reading left under the item of a one-item task beside the card — the same words twice;
- Check blue while the work is read — a button that looks open and takes no press;
- "Back to Home" in the result too — two ways home on one screen;
- a reminder about names above the row before each Check — the camera's before a photo and Help's line for an adult are enough.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/TaskScreen.tsx`, `TaskLayout.tsx` — `Frame`, `ModePill`, `VerdictCard`, `HintButton`, `RowLine` —, `Items.tsx` — `ChoiceGroups`, `hasChoices`, `onlyChoice` —, `EssayScreen.tsx`, `Task.tsx`; `src/app/TopEnd.tsx`, `IconButton.tsx` — `task.home` in `INSTEAD` —, `LearnerMenu.tsx` — `ProfilePill` —, `Screen.tsx` — the layout `task` —, `App.tsx` — no `LearnerBar` on the task; `src/lesson/LessonScreen.tsx`, `Lesson.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries; the tests of the task, the essay, the workbook, the lesson, the account and the learner's bar; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; `walk/path.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The workbook and the essay by the mockup

**R66. The workbook and the essay are the mockup's `workbook`, `workbook-not-yet`, `workbook-shown`, `essay`, `essay-feedback` and `essay-done`, on the task screen of the mockup. On a workbook the list of lines under the canvas gives each line one row that wraps, at 13 px — the line's verdict, then each box's with what was read —, and the canvas keeps at least `min(20rem, 50dvh)` of the window: what stands under it scrolls with it. The essay's row is Hint · Done, a line, Check; Done is semibold, in the accent's words once it opens and the muted text's till then. Above the row the essay has the task screen's card: "Reading your work…" while a check is under way; "Feedback" — "The feedback is in the tutor's panel. Write again, or press Done." — with a Done of its own, while the last submission gave words; why Check or Done does not open, on its press, with OK — Done only while nothing was sent, since after a submission it waits only for the device to keep it. The lines why are the buttons' descriptions alone.**
- **The lines of a workbook.** The mockup ends each line of the page with what was read, "Read: 6 r 8 · Not yet", and rings the box not right; canvas draws the page, and a verdict in it is no part of its element, so the list stays under the canvas. As a column of nested items it took more than the window's height on six lines of two boxes, and the canvas, the rest of a grid row, came to nothing: now one row a line, `Line 5 • Not yet  Box 1 ✓ Correct Read: 4  Box 2 • Not yet Read: 7`, the nested list kept for a screen reader.
- **The card of the feedback** says where the feedback is, not its words: they are a bubble of the panel's conversation, and the same words twice on one screen are what the task screen's card leaves out too. A note — no feedback this time, the essay unreadable, unchecked, the feedback lost — stays in the conversation alone: the mockup draws none of them. The feedback does not open the panel.
- **No reminder about names** stands above the card or the row, where the mockup has one at the foot of the statement's card: the camera's before a photo and Help's line for an adult are enough.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: canvas draws the workbook on its own white paper, where the mockup lays a white page on the page's `#F2F2F7`, which the row's glass lets through; after Done the way on is the lesson's row, or the result in the panel, which names the criteria without the marks the mockup's panel shows — the marks are the adult's, in the progress —; the statement, its speaker and the line "Task 2 of 2 · …" are canvas's.

— *Why:* the mockup's states, by the task screen's rules for the card and the row; a canvas that the list under it can hide is no task screen.

— *Rejected:*
- the readings of a workbook on its page — a change of `canvas`, whose element holds no verdict;
- the list of lines in the tutor's panel — a learner on my own with the panel closed would see no line's verdict;
- a cap on the list's height with a scroll of its own — a scroll region within the scroll of the work, which a keyboard reaches only by a stop of its own;
- the feedback's words in the card — the panel holds them, and a card of 460 cannot hold a feedback of several sentences;
- a card for each note of the essay — the same words as the panel's conversation, twice on a screen.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/EssayScreen.tsx`; `Essay.tsx` — the reading ends with the answer, not once the device kept it; `TaskLayout.tsx` — the card's kind `feedback`; `Items.tsx` — the line's head; `src/theme/theme.css` — the token `--nt-rubric`, in `contrast.node.test.ts` too, and a locked Done `GrayText` in forced colours; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts` — `feedback`, `chat-bubble`; the keys `task.feedback`, `task.feedback-line` and `task.done-locked-line` in both dictionaries; `src/task/Essay.browser.test.tsx`, `src/theme/layout.browser.test.tsx`; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The tutor's panel by the mockup

**R67. The tutor's panel is the mockup's `task`, `task-steps`, `task-pill`, `task-hint`, `task-rung`, `task-alt`, `task-report`, `task-full` and `quota`. From 600 CSS px it is glass, 400 wide at the window's right, as the debug window, from under the top bar to 16 above the foot, over the canvas — on the page to pick from, over the photo —, which keeps the window's width, and slides in from the right; below 600 a sheet from the bottom at half the window's height, whose grabber takes it to the window's top and back — tapped, or dragged: the sheet's top follows the finger, from a third of the window up, and let go it stands at the nearer of half and whole (`src/app/grabber.ts`) —, the canvas's tools (`tools-hidden`) and the row hidden while it is open. Its head: the tutor's sign, "Tutor", the profile's share of the quota, the switch of voicing and ×. Under it: by steps the step's card, "Step n of m" unfolding the list; the conversation — the verdict as a line, the hint, the tutor's words with "Report a problem", whose form is a modal pop-over —; at its foot Hint and "Explain differently". Closed, the tutor's round button of 56 at the right edge — on a phone 172 above the foot — and, by steps, the step's pill above the row open it. By steps it opens as the task begins; on my own it is closed till called; a verdict never opens it, nor an essay's feedback. No text field and no microphone (О-6), and no line that the tutor is an AI: Help, the tutor's pop-over of Home's frame and the page on the tutor say it.**
- **The place.** The lines above the canvas and the foot end 400 + 16 from the window's right edge, `data-beside="panel"`, while the panel is shown, so that they stay in view: the row stands in the middle of what is left, as the mockup's, and the canvas keeps the window's width, its right part under the glass. The panel is fixed, `z-index` 5, as the pop-overs' bars.
- **When it is open.** By steps from the task's start, and when an attempt goes on with a hint or a rung's words; on my own it closes when the learner chooses it, unless it holds the tutor's words or a hint. Once the attempt is over it holds the result, with no × and no foot, and the round button is gone. The essay's panel opens by steps and with a hint, and its × or the round button holds till the next hint; the card "Feedback" says where the words are.
- **The head.** The share is `shareText` of `src/account/quota.ts`: "Tutor time today: 20% used", the profile's share alone, "100% used" too once the cap is reached — why the tutor says nothing then is the conversation's line —, read by `GetQuota` with `ACTION_LEARNING` as the panel opens; no line without a share. The switch of voicing stands in the head while the panel is shown, and in the lines above the canvas while it is closed.
- **The conversation** is a region "Conversation", scrolling from its foot, where its newest words stand: the verdict a line in its middle, "Reading your work…" and "Not yet" alike, in place of the card while the panel is open; the hint a bubble on the hint's yellow labelled "Hint"; the rung's words the same yellow, other words on grey; "The tutor is writing…" a bubble. "Report a problem" stands under the newest words. Hint and "Explain differently" are the last of its scroll, held in sight at its foot: a scroll with a target of the keyboard inside needs no stop of its own, and a region takes no `tabIndex` (WCAG 2.1.1; SonarCloud's S6845); the step's card has its buttons.
- **The step's card**, white, 330 high at most and 172 on a phone, scrolls inside: "Step n of m" is a button of 48, `aria-expanded`, over the step's title, an `h2`, in one cell; the list folds again when the step changes. The step's pill shows "Step 1 of 4" and the title, named "Step 1 of 4: Understand the task. Open the tutor", and its "Step done" but on the last step.
- **"Report a problem"** opens its form in `PopOver`, modal: 440 wide, 40 below the window's top in its middle, the words quoted on grey, the hints at 11.5; on a phone the pop-over's sheet. The form is the one of the reports; its options keep app's radio buttons of 44.
- **The phone.** `useNarrow` of `src/app/narrow.ts` reads `(width < 600px)`, the one width of the layouts, for what CSS cannot do: the grabber, and `tools-hidden` on the canvas.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: the grabber shows the icon of what it does, Full screen or Half screen, of the closed list of buttons without a caption, where the mockup draws a bar; the round button is its own sign, the gradient in an edge of glass, where the mockup's sign is a span in it, and has no mark of unread words; "Explain differently" is hidden while it takes no press, where the mockup greys it; the form of the report is taller, its options 44 high as radio buttons; on a phone the options of a choice and the question of the solution open from the row, with the sheet closed, where the mockup opens them under its open sheet; the ends of the row of a choice stand 4–5 further out, each of its buttons a pixel or two wider; the statement, its speaker and the lesson's line "Task 1 of 2 · …" in the conversation are not app's (В74 of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md)).

— *Why:* `spec` puts the steps, the hints and the tutor's words in one panel over the work, and the mockup draws it; the canvas keeps its width, so that a sheet's grid and a workbook's page do not change size as the panel opens; `spec` has the tutor speak only while the panel is open, so what opens it is the learner's.

— *Rejected:*
- the panel as a column of the task's grid — the canvas would shrink and re-lay its page each time the panel opens;
- the verdict or the essay's feedback opening the panel — the learner calls the tutor;
- "Explain differently" greyed while spent — a button that looks open and takes no press, as Check would be, blue while the work is read;
- the grabber as a bar alone — a button without a caption off the closed list;
- the form of the report in place of the button — `spec` opens it in a pop-over, and the conversation keeps its words;
- radio buttons of 20, the mockup's — targets under 44.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/TaskLayout.tsx` — the panel, `VerdictLine`, `TutorButton` —, `TaskScreen.tsx`, `Task.tsx`, `StepsPanel.tsx` — the step's card and `StepPill` —, `TutorWords.tsx` — `HintAct`, `ExplainButton` —, `Hint.tsx`, `EssayScreen.tsx`, `Essay.tsx`; `src/tutor/ReportProblem.tsx`; `src/account/quota.ts` — `shareText`; `src/app/narrow.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; the keys `steps.of`, `steps.card`, `steps.pill` and `tutor.conversation` in both dictionaries; `src/task/TutorPanel.browser.test.tsx` and the tests of the task, the essay, the workbook, the lesson and the quota; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The section for adults by the mockup's style

**R68. Settings, the section for adults, is drawn as the mockup's `settings`, `settings-profile` and `settings-device`, as a device's settings: grouped lists on the page's grey — a group white, radius 12, its caption above it at 13 in capitals and its note under it at 13 in `#6E6E73`; its rows 44 high at least, the words at 17, a hairline between them from the words' start, a tile of 30 or an avatar before them, the value and a chevron after a row that leads further. From 600 CSS px the list is a column of 17.5 to 23.4rem at the left, sticky, under "Settings" at 34 bold and its close, the page beside it under a bar of 56 — the heading at 17 semibold in its middle, the way back, "‹ {page above}", and Save in the accent at its ends — and its groups in a column of 37rem; below 600 the list is the first page. A profile's page has its avatar of 88, its pseudonym at 24 bold and whose it is above its groups. A module's map in the progress stands in the white frame of the learner's map.**
- **The parts.** `src/adults/rows.tsx` draws a group and its rows: a row that leads further, a tick, a switch, segments and a choice in a pop-over. The inputs stay native and unseen over their whole row or segment, the target — a segment's 44 high though it shows 28 —, and what shows is the tick, the switch or the segment after them, the row ringed while its input has the keyboard's focus. A row is named by what it shows, a space between its parts.
- **The frame.** `SectionScreen` of `src/adults/SectionScreen.tsx` is each page's `Screen`, of the layouts `settings` and `settings-list`; the section tells it its place — the first page or another, the list, the page above — by a context, and a page drawn outside the section, in its tests, has neither the list nor the way back. The way back and Save never shorten; the heading wraps where they leave it no room.
- **The avatar on a profile's page.** `avatar` of `ProfileSettings` is the contract's field, and the form sends it only once changed, as each field (`changes`); a code the build does not know shows as the set's own and is never written back. `AvatarChoice` of `src/account/` draws the avatar and its pop-over for the owner's profile and a profile's page alike, the name between them on the page.
- **What stays.** This device, Own AI key, Modules and Progress keep their cards, in the section's frame: their lists of modules, traps and attempts stay Home's cards on the page's colour under their headings, not inside a group, since a card in a card loses its edge. The license page in the section leads back to Modules, the progress map to Progress; the legal pages and the license outside the section are sheets of the mockup's legal text. A module's map keeps the top bar of the map.

— *Why:* the section is where an adult looks for settings, and a device's settings are what an adult knows already; the mockup draws it so. A choice in a pop-over shows its value in its row, so nothing chosen is hidden, and it changes the form alone till Save (WCAG 3.2.2).

— *Rejected:*
- the section as cards of buttons, its first page a column of them — untidy, and not what an adult knows from a device;
- a choice as a card of radio buttons on the page — a group of two rows grows into a card each;
- Save the primary button of the column's width under the form — a device's settings keep the page's action in its bar;
- every part of the progress page as a group — a list of cards in a card;
- the avatar saved at once by its own `UpdateProfile` — every other field waits for Save, and two ways to save would part.

— *Consequence:* `src/adults/rows.tsx`, `SectionScreen.tsx`, `AdultSection.tsx`, `ProfilePage.tsx`, `WorkStorage.tsx`, `QuotaSection.tsx`; `src/account/AvatarChoice.tsx`, `AccountGate.tsx`; `src/app/Screen.tsx`, `icons.ts` by `scripts/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; the tests of `src/adults/`, `AccountGate.browser.test.tsx`, `LicensePage.browser.test.tsx`, `src/a11y/screens.browser.test.tsx` and the comparison `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The legal pages and the failure screens by the mockup

**R69. The legal pages, a module's license outside the section for adults and the page on the tutor are each a sheet of the mockup's legal text (`legal-privacy`, `legal-terms`, `legal-access`), drawn as a screen of its own, `SheetScreen`: from 600 CSS px a white card of 560, radius 28, its top where the mockup's card of 580 in the window's middle has it, on the page's colour; below 600 the whole screen, its text 52 from the top. Its head is the page's heading at 24 bold, a chip under it — a text's version, or "WCAG 2.2 · level AA" on Accessibility —, back before the heading on a legal page but their list, captioned "Back to the list", and close × on the page's grey at the other end. A text's page has the gist below its caption, the next version in a note after it, then the line that the English text is in force, the translation and the body, open. The failure screens are a white card of 460, radius 28, in the window's middle, the heading at 22 bold, what to do the primary button of its width. The privacy policy and the terms of use, opened from a screen — the way in or Help —, are first the mockup's sheet itself over it, `LegalSheet`: the head, the gist scrolling inside 300, the next version's note, and at its foot the line that the English text is in force and "Read the full text", which opens the text's page.**
- **One sheet for three kinds of page.** `src/app/SheetScreen.tsx` takes the heading, the chip, the back and what close does, and draws `Screen`'s layout `sheet` with its `head`. The legal pages give their place — the list or another, and where they close to — by a context, as the section for adults does, so a page drawn alone in its test has no back and no close. The license and the page on the tutor close home, as their bars did; the license in the section keeps the section's top bar.
- **A text's page:** the version leaves its line for the chip; the gist is a region named by its visible caption, "In short" in capitals at 12 semibold, the mockup's "THE GIST"; the next version's line and "Read version {n}", a link, are a note of the owner's grey `#f5f5f7` at 13 in the colour of a segmented control, where the mockup has its note; the line that the English text is in force, on a screen not in English, at 13 in the secondary colour, where the mockup's foot has it. The body stays open below: no "Read the full text". The page Accessibility has its four headings in the gist's caption style, the places it does not meet the standard at 17 bold under them.
- **The texts' sheet** (`src/legal/LegalSheet.tsx`) is a modal `<dialog>` in the page's body, not in the screen whose colours, as the sky's, are not its: from 600 the white card of 560, radius 28, where the page's card stands, over the window dimmed by 0.28, as tall as its text down to 16 above the window's foot and scrolling whole beyond it; below 600 the whole screen, scrolling, the gist filling what is left. The gist is no box that scrolls: Safari scrolls one from the keyboard only once it takes the focus (О-24), and a region that takes it is no interactive element (SonarCloud, typescript:S6845); the card holds ×, and scrolls with it. It opens once its text is read — the steps of the way in give theirs, the sign-in and Help read it —, so nothing stands half drawn; Escape, × and a tap outside close it, and the focus goes back to what opened it. "Read version {n}" in its note opens the next version's page; no line that the English text is in force on a screen in English. With no version in force its foot is "Privacy and legal information", to the list, so that the other pages stay a press away from the sign-in.
- **The list** `#/legal` is the sheet with "Privacy and legal information" for heading, its pages as links one below another, as Help has its three.
- **The failure screens** of `src/boot/Screens.tsx` — in a frame, no connection, a read that failed, the copy not set up, the storage erased or unreadable, another tab, and the account held, `Held`, which `AccountGate` drew apart before, as it did a read of the account that failed, now `ReadFailed` — take `Screen`'s layout `failure`: the card in the middle of the whole window, or below the bar above it; Try again and Reload are `nt-main`, 52 high, of the card's width, "Back to Home" below Try again, 48 high; the line for whoever deployed the copy at 13 in the secondary colour. The offer to reload over a screen and a read that failed inside a screen, `ReadFailure`, stay as they are.
- **The colours.** The cards that stand alone — the end of a lesson, a sheet, a failure screen — share one shadow, `--nt-panel-shadow`. The chip is "Correct" `#1e7a35` on its tint, `rgba(36,138,61,0.12)`, the mockup's: 4.86:1 over white, a token `--nt-correct-tint` with its pair in `contrast.node.test.ts`, with the note's `#3a3a3c` on `#f5f5f7` and the close's grey under the pointer's wash.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region in `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`: the way back to the list before the heading, which moves the heading and the chip right, and on a phone wraps the chip and lowers the gist — the mockup has no list; the sheet's height and no "Read the full text" — the whole text open; the gist's height and the note's place — the texts come from `api`, as the mockup's README says where it differs from the documents, and the note holds the way to the next version's page; no line that the English text is in force on a screen in English; the size of the sheet's own text, 15, as its gist's; the close's icon, of the closed list.
- **PageBar** is gone: no screen has a bar of captioned buttons above it now; the learner's bar of buttons, `LearnerBar`, keeps its own until E4.

— *Why:* `spec` has the screens the mockup lacks drawn by its rules of style, and the mockup draws the legal texts as this sheet; the license and the page on the tutor are texts of the same kind, read and closed, and one sheet keeps them alike. A text opened from a screen is read over it and closed back to it, as the mockup's, its gist enough for the moment and its page a press away. A failure screen has one thing to say and one thing to do, as the end of a lesson, whose card it takes.

— *Rejected:*
- the legal pages as a sheet over the screen alone, as the mockup's — they are pages of their own by the hash, opened before sign-in and after a reload, and nothing stands under them then; a screen that opens a text opens its sheet first;
- the texts' sheet opened at once, the gist waiting in it — the screen would show a sheet half drawn;
- the way back after the heading, beside the close, so that the heading stands where the mockup's does — a back at the end of a line reads as the line's last action, not as the way back, and every other top bar has it first;
- no way back, close going to the list from a page opened from it — a page closes to where it was opened from, and Help opens a page alone;
- "Read the full text" closing the body — a page's body stays open, never folded in `<details>`;
- the failure screens as a sheet — they have nothing to close.

— *Consequence:* `src/legal/LegalSheet.tsx`, opened by `InviteScreen`, `SignInScreen` and `Help`; `src/app/SheetScreen.tsx` and `SheetScreen.browser.test.tsx`; `src/app/Screen.tsx` — the layouts `sheet` and `failure` and `head`; `src/app/PageBar.tsx` removed; `src/app/IconButton.tsx` — `legal.back` in `INSTEAD`; `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx`, `LegalText.tsx` — `captioned` and `note` of `LegalContent`; `src/packages/LicensePage.tsx`; `src/tutor/TutorPage.tsx`; `src/boot/Screens.tsx` — `Held`; `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`; `src/theme/theme.css` and `contrast.node.test.ts`; the keys `accessibility.level` in, and `legal.close`, `license.bar`, `license.close`, `tutor.bar` and `tutor.close` out of, both dictionaries; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx` — the three states; the tests of the legal pages and of the license; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R70. Scorecard's alert #10, `VulnerabilitiesID`, stays open: GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf in `picomatch` 4.0.7 has no fixed release, and `picomatch` comes only with the dev tools `vite` and `vitest`, which never ship.**
- **The finding** (sources 2.7): a ReDoS of `picomatch` by single-branch repeated extglobs, every version to 4.0.7, the latest on 2026-11-18. In `app` it is `vite` 8.3.8 with `tinyglobby` and `fdir`, and `vitest` 5.1.0: the build holds none of it, `npm audit --omit=dev` of `ci-vuln` finds nothing, and the globs they read are `app`'s own.
- **When it closes:** Д42 of [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md) — once `picomatch` releases a fix.

— *Why:* there is no version to move to, and the vulnerable code runs only on the developer's machine and in CI, on the repository's own patterns. A finding a change cannot close stays open, its reason recorded here.

— *Rejected:* dismissing the alert in the Security tab — a dismissed alert does not come back when a fix comes out, and the reason belongs here, read with the log; an `overrides` entry to another glob library — `vite` and `vitest` call `picomatch`'s own API; dropping `vite` or `vitest` — they are the build and the tests.

— *Consequence:* `run/DEBT.md`; [sources.md](sources.md).

## A task from paper

**R71. A task from the learner's paper has a screen of its own, `#/paper`: `app`'s own camera — the device's camera at the back by `getUserMedia`, "Take photo", "Choose from photos" and "Close" —, with no finding of the page in the viewfinder, no flash and no "Auto"; the line "Keep names out of the photo" on the camera's screen; the photo, encoded anew as a JPEG of at most 4 MiB, sent to `FindTasks` once; the mockup's pick screen, on which the learner frames a task the AI missed by a rectangle dragged corner to corner or by two taps, a corner and then the one opposite; Start, which cuts the crop of each task picked, and its instruction's, on the device and begins their attempts by `BeginPaperAttempts`; and the mockup's screen of solving, each task begun a sheet in dots under its crop, the tracing paper of `canvas`, with the list of the tasks and the row Hint · Check. The page, its photo, its crops and the work on them are in the tab's memory, and from Start on in the profile's history too.**
- **The camera** (`src/paper/Camera.tsx`): `getUserMedia` with `facingMode: "environment"` and an ideal of 4096 px, in a `<video>`; its tracks stop when the screen goes, and a camera that answers after it went is stopped at once. With no camera, or none the browser may use, the screen says so and "Choose from photos" is the way in. The picker takes any picture the browser decodes, drawn upright by its own orientation (`createImageBitmap`, `imageOrientation: "from-image"`).
- **The photo** (`src/paper/photo.ts`, `capture.ts`): drawn on a canvas with its long side at most 3 200 px, a JPEG at 0.9; one over 4 MiB is drawn again at 0.8 of the size, six tries at most. The canvas takes nothing of the file's metadata, its place and time among them: the platform has no geolocation, and its picture is sent and kept re-encoded. `tutor` fits the photo to its model; 3 200 is past what a model takes unshrunk and leaves the crops their detail. Crops of a task and its instruction are PNGs within the work crop's bounds, 1 568 px a side and 1 568 patches of 28 px (`cropFitted`), drawn at 0.8 while over 4 MiB: a camera frame at full size cost each model of the Check about three times the tokens, and outgrew the 4 MiB api takes. Rejected: bounds in `tutor` alone, at the model's larger limit; JPEG crops, no fewer tokens.
- **The turn** (`turnedPhoto` of `capture.ts`, `uprightPage` of `page.ts`): frames found with a `turnDegrees` other than 0 are on the photo turned upright, so `app` turns its photo the same way and encodes it as any photo, the finding still shown meanwhile; the page then takes it, the frames on it before turned too, and a request, the crops and the history take it. An answer about a photo the page no longer holds is dropped. With tasks begun on the page, or a photo that could not be turned, the photo stays and each frame found is the rectangle that holds it turned back.
- **Finding the tasks:** each try of `FindTasks` is an action with a key of its own. api keeps no frames, and a repeat of one key answers `repeated` with none (the contract of `PaperService`): `app` then offers "Find the tasks again", not "No tasks were found". The frames come picked, in the order of the page; one that is no rectangle in the photo, or one past 20, is dropped.
