With 20 found, "20 tasks is the most at once" says the limit at the head of the bar, in a ring, with how to take another, for the page may hold more; with more than 20 picked, Start is shut and "At most 20 tasks start at once" says why, with "Leave some out" (`limit` of `page.ts`). A frame found may bring its instruction's — the words above a group of tasks —, turned with it and dropped where it is no rectangle in the photo; a frame drawn has none. An outcome with no frames is its words, and the learner frames the tasks by hand. The own key goes by `aiFields`, and a refusal or a limit of it stays beside it.
- **AI closed:** a refusal of `FindTasks` or `BeginPaperAttempts` for the quota, the platform's limit or no model admitted, or an outcome of the own key's limit or refusal, shuts Start, with the line "Without the AI a task from paper can't be checked": the AI checks a task from paper, at every Check. Retake stays open.
- **The pick screen** (`src/paper/Pick.tsx`, `page.ts`, `frame.ts`): the photo as large as the screen leaves it, each frame a button with `aria-pressed`, "Select task n" or "Deselect task n" by its place on the page; the top bar — Back, "Back to Home", which drops the page, its icon alone in its pill, with "Your page" the screen's heading unseen and the document's title; in its middle, while the tasks are found, a turning ring and "Finding the tasks on the page…", a line of light running down the photo and back, and then a tick and "n tasks found · k framed by you"; at its end "Remove all frames", while there is one, which clears the photo for the learner's own frames, and Retake —, in one row by `BarFit.tsx`: where the captions do not fit, each button shows its icon with a tooltip; below Back only where icons do not fit; each frame's mark of 22, a tick while picked, else its number, and its cross of 24 in a target of 44; the note with the lock; the glass bar of "Select all" or "Select none", "x of y selected" and Start. A stroke past a box of 40 × 30 CSS px is a drag: the rectangle from the press to the pointer follows it, back too, as a picked drawn frame, and at the lift is the frame, with no margin, none under 40 × 30; a smaller stroke is a tap, which draws nothing, and two taps are opposite corners. Every frame's cross removes it, the AI's too; a frame drawn has a dashed line inside. Every frame, the AI's too, resizes by its corner, a dot of 15 in a target of 44, at least 40 × 30 and never past the photo, and moves when dragged by its middle, kept in the photo; a press of 6 CSS px or more is that drag, its click picks nothing — the mockup's `pick-adjust`, as AI frames cut long lines; a task begun keeps its crop. A frame is in whole thousandths of the photo, as a crop box is, and the attempts begin in the order of the page, top to bottom and the left first, as `tutor` orders the frames found.
- **Start** cuts each frame picked and not begun yet, and its instruction, from the photo as PNGs, then sends `BeginPaperAttempts` with the page's ID, the number of tasks and `routeFields` — the same page after "Add" closes none of its tasks begun before —, its key kept while the photo and the tasks picked are the same, so that a press again repeats it; with every task picked begun it only goes back to solving. The attempts, the frames, the crops and the work on each stay in `PaperContext`.
- **The screen of solving** (`src/paper/Solve.tsx`, `solve.ts`): the mockup's `solve` — the top bar's pill with Back alone, the task's name — "Task n" or "Your frame, task n" by its place on the page — the screen's heading unseen and the document's title; at its other end ‹, the counter — a list's sign and "n / N", tinted while open —, which opens the list of the tasks begun in a pop-over, as the mockup's `solve-tasks`: at its head "Tasks", "n of N done" and "+ Add"; each task its number in a circle, the one shown in the accent, its name with its state under it — "Solved", "Not yet", "So far correct", "Cannot be checked", "Closed" or "Not checked yet", by its last Check (`taskState`) — and the state's sign at its end; and ›. The page finds no statement's text, so the name stands where the mockup has the statement. "Add" goes back to the page: a task left out there leaves the list, and comes back with its work when picked again. Each task is a `<novatrail-canvas>` of its own, keyed by its task, with `paper` its crop, its `history` and `tracing` — the paper's place and scale — kept on `historychange` and `tracingchange`. Recognise on the paper, `tracingread`, calls `ReadStatement` with the task's crop as printed and its own key, kept till an answer so a press again repeats it, and gives the element `reading` — `"busy"`, then `"done"` with the statements, or `"ready"` again with the outcome or the failure at the foot; the page keeps each task's reading while it is open. Which of the photo and the text the paper shows is the element's, and the photo comes back with each mount. Its sheet runs up under the floating top bar, `--novatrail-canvas-pad-top` 0: a task from paper has no statement to keep clear of the bar, and the learner writes between its pills and moves the tracing paper there; a task of a module keeps the pad, its statement below the bar. The row is the debug button and a line, for an account with debug, Ask, Hint, a line and Check: Check with nothing written is the card "Write your solution first"; otherwise `exportWork()` gives `ExportedPaper`, and `SubmitWork` takes its `PaperWork` — the task's crop, the instruction's if any, and the work's —, with the own key's fields and a key kept while the work is the same, so that a press again repeats the answer, but for a failure of AI, after which a press again asks anew; nothing goes to the device's database, for the server keeps no work of a task from paper. While the AI checks it the card is "Reading your work…"; then the verdict of each task of the frame, an item for each, kept on the task in the tab's memory with the context of the work the element gave (`paperVerdictOf`, `paperCard`): "Correct" when every task is, the task marked solved in the list; "Not yet" when one is, or one is correct and another not read, with Hint and "Try again"; "So far correct" when none is wrong and one has no answer yet, a quiet tick on the accent's tint, no answer read, no mark, and Check open; of one task with "Read: …", and of several as their count, "Correct · 4 of 4" or "Not yet · 3 of 4 correct", of the tasks checked, the line naming the tasks whose answer was not read. By each answer correct or not yet whose box the Check gave, the element draws a tick or a cross, the mark `verdict` of `canvas`, named "Task n: correct" or "Task n: not yet" (`verdictMarks`), which goes with the card and with other work; a work not read with why — no final answer "No answer found", more than one "More than one answer", each with its line on writing one answer or circling one option, as `app` knows no kind of the answer —; "Not checked" with the outcome's words; and `CAUSE_NO_KEY`, nothing read, "Couldn't check this one", with Hint and "Next task", its sign the question of Help, that Check's alone. Other work puts any card away; once solved, "Next task" is the row's main button, Check plain, the card's button "OK". The first action needing `api` on a closed attempt begins a new one on the page, the work kept; so does a refusal "closed". The Check's words go to the tutor's panel.
- **The page on the screens:** the page and its crops live in `PaperContext` while the learner is chosen; Retake, Back, "Open camera" again, another learner and a reload drop them from the screens, and from Start on the profile's history keeps them.
- **Accessibility:** two taps frame a task with a single pointer and no path, so WCAG 2.5.1 and 2.5.7 hold; a frame can't be drawn from the keyboard, and the page "Accessibility" declares 2.1.1 for it, the AI's frames and Retake being what there is instead. A task from paper is solved in handwriting alone, with no options to choose among, and the page declares 2.1.1 for it with no date; the tracing paper's drag is `canvas`'s 2.5.7, its arrow keys beside it.

— *Why:* the camera of the browser's file input would leave the reminder out of sight while the photo is taken, and its shutter is not `app`'s; finding the page in the viewfinder needs a model on the device, which the first slice has none of, and a flash on paper glares. Two taps give a learner with one finger and no steady hand a frame without a path; a new key for each try costs a call of AI, where a kept key would give a repeat with no frames. Back stands alone in the top bar of both screens, the screen's name its heading unseen: on the sheet a pill with the name ran over the popovers of `canvas`'s pen and eraser, and the pick's bar leaves its row to the words of its end.

— *Rejected:* a loop round a task, its box the frame — a drag across a task drew a line, the frame seen only at the lift; the file input with `capture` alone — no line before the shutter, and no way to the photos on some browsers; "Page found · hold still" and the auto shutter — a model on the device; the flash — glare, and the torch's API is not in every engine; a frame from the keyboard by arrow keys — a pointer on the photo that the screen would have to read aloud, place by place; the platform's words for a choice, "Circle an answer first" — `app` knows no kind of the answer, so its words fit both; "Couldn't check this one" ending a task's checking — each Check reads the work anew; one sheet for every task, the crops changed on it — a task's work would mix with another's, and the tracing paper's place is a task's own (`TracingState.at`); the list as a panel always open — the mockup's pop-over keeps the sheet whole; keeping the photo on the device's database now — its store, its erasure and its export come as one; the screen's name beside Back, hidden only where the pick's bar does not fit one row — the solving screen's name ran over the pen's popover, and the name is the heading anyway.

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/` — `Camera.tsx`, `Pick.tsx`, `BarFit.tsx`, `PaperScreen.tsx`, `PaperContext.tsx`, `Solve.tsx`, `solve.ts`, `capture.ts`, `photo.ts`, `page.ts`, `frame.ts` and their tests; `src/canvas/jsx.ts` — `paper`, `tracing` and `tracingchange`; `src/task/attempt.ts` — `workOf` of `ExportedPaper`; `src/storage/profile.ts` — a submission of a module's task alone; `src/app/hash.ts`, `router.ts`, `App.tsx` — the route `#/paper` with no learner's bar; `src/app/Screen.tsx` — the heading `unseen`; `src/api/client.ts` — `paper`; `src/api/outcome.ts` and `words.ts` — `CAUSE_NO_KEY`; `src/task/TaskLayout.tsx` — the card `nokey`; `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`; `src/app/IconButton.tsx`, `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx` — the places "framing" and "paper"; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R72. The debug mode of the models of a task from paper, in every build, for an account with `api`'s debug right: a debug button in the bottom bar of the page picked of a task from paper and of the screen of solving, in every profile of the account, which shows once `api` answers `GetDebug`, and its window, Models · Log · Performance, as the mockup's states `pick-debug` and `solve-debug-log`. The choice is `api`'s; the calls and the session's sum are the tab's memory alone; a Check of the log exports as a case of `tutor-eval`, a `DebugCase`, with the truth the developer corrects.**
- **Who sees it.** `src/api/client.ts` makes `Api.debug` in every build. `src/debug/gate.ts`, in the main chunk, asks `GetDebug` once an account — `src/debug/slot.tsx` asks it on its mount —, and `Boot` tells it the user of the sign-in service, so that another account signed in asks again. The slot loads the window, `DebugButton.tsx` with `debug.ts` and `debug.css`, by a dynamic import, a chunk of its own, only once `GetDebug` answered, and `src/debug/watched.ts` loads `debug.ts` only then, a call made while `GetDebug` is asked waiting for its answer. Any refusal shows nothing and is no error to the learner: `permission_denied`, an account without the right, and `unimplemented`, a run beside the stub of `tutor`, turn the mode off for the account; `GetDebug` is a method that may be absent, never a stale build; any other failure leaves it to the next mount. The button shows in a child's profile too, as `spec` has it.
- **The button and the window** (`src/debug/DebugButton.tsx`, `debug.css`): 44 round, Material Symbols' bug in orange on a light orange, beside the tutor's button, ringing while a call is under way; it stays in the bar while its window is open, expanded, so that the row does not move, and a press of it closes the window; the window of the tutor's panel's size, drawn in the page's body, since the bar's glass would hold a fixed window inside it, over the canvas as the panel: 400 wide at the right, and on a phone a sheet from the bottom at half the window's height, with its grabber, the tutor's panel's, tapped or dragged to the window's top. Models: one model for every call of a task from paper — finding the tasks, reading the statement, the Check and "Explain differently" —, "As admitted", each call its models of the list as `GetDebug` gives them, in the order of their attempts, or one of the catalog with its effort and thinking, which stays on where the model or the effort keeps it, under the warning that the choice bypasses the admission list, in every profile. Log: the actions, newest first among the lines of the app's log, each call with its model, cost and time — each attempt of a call a row of its own, after the first by its number —, opening to its tokens, time to the first token, retries and what it returned, with "Copy"; the session's total, "Copy" and "Clear". Its words are English alone and its button is none of the closed list of buttons without a caption: it is the developer's, never a learner's.
- **The calls** (`src/debug/debug.ts`): `watched` wraps `FindTasks` and `SubmitWork` of a task from paper, and takes `TakeDebugCalls` once each ends, however it ends; the actions join the session's in the module's state, which a reload and another account forget. A Check passes `watched` the `PaperWork` it sent, its instruction too, and the verdict of each task of its answer, which the state keeps by the key of its action.
- **The export** (`exportOf`, `truthOf`): Export beside a Check of the log opens a form of its truth — for each task of the frame its statement, value read and verdict, and a note, under what the Check answered, its verdict and its reading —, filled in from the calls of the attempt: the statement of its latest reading up to that Check, and for each task the reading and the verdict of that Check's own call, by the last attempt of it that answered with no failure, matched by the task's number, and the verdict `api` gave each. Save case downloads the JSON of `DebugCase`, with `check_verdict`, `check_read` and the work with its instruction, `case-<time>-<attempt>.json`, which the developer moves to `eval/paper-cases/` of `tutor`, where `tutor-eval -paper` reads them; a case with no task, or a task with no verdict chosen, saves none.

— *Why:* the author and the users the author names see and change which models check a task from paper, what each returned and what it cost, on a local run and on the deployment, without editing code. The right is `api`'s, so the build asks and `api` decides; the window's chunk loads only for an account `GetDebug` answers, so the others fetch none of it.

— *Rejected:* the mode in the development build alone, under `import.meta.env.DEV`, which left the deployment without it; the window in the main chunk, which every learner would fetch; an error shown on a refusal of `GetDebug`, which an account without the right always gets; the window as a `PopOver`, which hangs from its button and holds the rest of the page inert, while the developer watches the screen beside it; the dictionaries, for words no learner reads; the log in the device's storage, which would outlive the session and keep the readings of the work; an export of what the calls gave alone, which would leave the case with no truth to measure against; a second reader of the work beside the first, and a model of its own for the Check — one model checks a task from paper, and the Check's own reading is the one a case measures.

— *Consequence:* `src/debug/` — `gate.ts`, `slot.tsx`, `watched.ts`, `DebugButton.tsx`, `debug.ts`, `debug.css` and their tests; `src/api/client.ts` — `debug`; `src/api/interceptors.ts` — `GetDebug` may be absent; `src/boot/Boot.tsx` — the user told to the gate; `src/paper/PaperScreen.tsx` and `Solve.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R73. The three attempts of a call of a task from paper in `app`: `FindTasks` and `SubmitWork` name a deadline of 90 s, `api`'s limit of them, and a repeat waits as long for a first one still going; the debug log shows each attempt of a call as a row of its own, and the admitted models of a call in the order of their attempts.**
- **The deadline** (`src/api/interceptors.ts`): `PAPER_TIMEOUT_MS`, 90 s, is the `timeoutMs` of the calls of `FindTasks` in `src/paper/PaperScreen.tsx` and of `SubmitWork` in `src/paper/Solve.tsx`; every other call keeps the transport's 30 s, `GetOpeningWords` too, which on paper runs a Check, while `api` gives it no more. `stillGoingMs` gives the repeat of `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` the limit of its method, by the service's and the method's names, as `api` gives the idempotency of the two methods its own window.
- **The log** (`src/debug/`): a row's key is its kind and attempt, and its title names `attempt N` after the first; `truthOf` takes, of a call's attempts, the last that answered with no failure, which is what the call gave; Models lists, after "As admitted", each call with its models joined by an arrow.

— *Why:* a call of a task from paper may now ask Haiku, Sonnet and Opus in turn, and `api` gives the two methods 90 s: the client's 30 s would cut `api` short at its second model, and a repeat that waited 30 s would give up on a first request `api` still runs. The log shows what each attempt returned and cost, which is what the author looks at.

— *Rejected:* 90 s for every call, which would leave a call of any other method waiting three times as long as `api` lets it run; the deadline by a second transport, where the two calls name theirs; the attempts of a call folded into one row, which would hide which model did and what each cost.

— *Consequence:* `src/api/interceptors.ts`, `client.node.test.ts`, `transport.node.test.ts`; `src/paper/PaperScreen.tsx` and `Solve.tsx`; `src/debug/debug.ts`, `DebugButton.tsx` and `debug.node.test.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R74. The request about the page in `app`: the pick screen has the tutor's button and panel, the shell of the task screen's in `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, with the field of a request; a request goes to `FindTasks` with the frames on the page, its frames take the place of those found, and Undo brings back those before.**
- **The panel** (`src/paper/PageTutor.tsx`): the tutor's line of what it takes, each request a bubble of the learner's at the right and the program's line of what it came to — "Found N tasks", "No tasks found for that", "The frames are back as they were" —, Undo while it can, and the field of 200 code points, a rounded box in a blue edge that grows down with its lines to 7.5rem and then scrolls, Enter sending and Shift+Enter breaking a line; under it the microphone, off until epic E17 of `spec` brings voice to every chat of the tutor, and Send, each its icon alone, named "Voice comes later" and "Send", as the mockup draws them. No reminder of names above the field and no line that the tutor is an AI, as on the task screen's panel. The field and Undo wait while the finding runs, and the field is off while AI is shut. The debug button stands above the tutor's, and while the tutor's panel is open, on the page or on the screen of solving, it is not shown, by `debug.css`.
- **The request** (`src/paper/page.ts`): `requestOf` trims it, makes each control character a space and cuts it to 200 code points; `framesOf` gives the frames found and drawn, each in the order of the page, at most 20 and 40; `asked` puts the frames found in the place of the AI's before, the learner's own staying, and with none found leaves the tasks as they were.
- **The page** (`PaperContext`): `talk`, the conversation, and `before`, the tasks before the last request that gave frames, which any change of the learner's to the tasks takes away; a failure keeps the request, and "Find the tasks again" asks it again. A new photo forgets both.
- **The debug log** takes the request of a finding by its action from the tab (`watched`, `take`), for `api` keeps none.

— *Why:* the panel is the one the learner knows from a task, so a request about the page goes where a word to the tutor goes; the frames now go with it, so that it can name them, and Undo makes a wrong answer cost a tap.

— *Rejected:* a panel of the pick's own, apart from the task screen's; a field of one line, which would hide a longer request; a request with the photo alone; the conversation kept beyond the tab.

— *Consequence:* `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, `TaskLayout.tsx`; `src/paper/PageTutor.tsx`, `PaperScreen.tsx`, `PaperContext.tsx`, `Pick.tsx`, `page.ts` and their tests; `src/debug/watched.ts`, `debug.ts`, `DebugButton.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/i18n/en.ts` and `ru.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R75. The screen of solving a task from paper has the tutor's button and panel, the shell of the task screen's in `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, as every canvas has them: Hint and a verdict do not leave the screen for a line at its foot. The panel's head holds the learner's share of the quota and the switch of voicing; its conversation, the Check's verdict and words while it is open and the hint asked for; its foot, Hint, "Explain differently" and the next task; the tutor's words are those of a task from paper by AI, with their own opening question.**
- **The panel** (`src/paper/SolveTutor.tsx`): closed at first; Ask, the tutor's button of the row, and any Hint — the row's, the card's, the panel's — open it, a verdict does not, × closes it, and it stays as the learner left it from task to task. Its head: the share of the quota, `GetQuota` by the learner's action, read at each opening and after each answer to a Check or a Hint (`useQuotaLine`), and the switch of voicing. Its conversation: the mockup's line "Hints for this task come here, a question at a time.", without its "A program checks every answer", for the AI checks a task from paper; the reading and the verdict as lines — the card's words, by `paperCard` —, the Check's words and the tutor's words. Its foot by `tutorActs` of `src/paper/solve.ts`: Hint while the task is not solved, after a Check that read nothing too, and "Next task" once the task is solved or over, or a Check read nothing.
- **The screen** (`src/paper/Solve.tsx`): Hint stays in the row beside Check till the task is solved, and the card above the row has the panel's buttons, with its own Try again or OK. While the panel is open the reading and the verdict are lines in it, "Write your solution first" stays a card, and beside it the sheet and the foot end where the panel begins; on a phone its sheet hides the row and the canvas's bar of tools, `tools-hidden`. While it is closed the verdict is the card above the row. The tutor's words stand till another task or Check — no longer till the next stroke — and are said by themselves while the voicing is on and the panel open, in the language of the interface, for a task from paper is in no module; another task or the panel closed stops it, and a hint that comes to the panel closed meanwhile is told to a screen reader. What a Check or a Hint failed with is said in the open panel, which the phone's sheet does not hide. The focus goes to the panel's heading as it opens and back to the tutor's button — Ask on the screen of solving — as it closes (`usePanelFocus`, the task screen's and the pick's too).
- **The Check's words**: the `tutor_words` of an answer, when there are any, are a bubble of their own, not marked a hint, above the hint's; they stand from the Check till another task or Check and are said by themselves while the voicing is on and the panel open. An adult reports them as `ReportProblem` takes a reply — `TUTOR_REPLY_CORRECT_ANSWER` after a correct answer, `TUTOR_REPLY_RUNG` at the count of wrong answers, three at most, after one, else `TUTOR_REPLY_OPENING` (`checkReply` of `solve.ts`) —, for the contract has no reply of the Check's own.

— *Why:* the tutor's panel is there on any kind of canvas, so a task from paper speaks where a task of a module does. The share of the quota stands in the head as on the task screen, for every Check of a task from paper spends it; the task's name is in the top bar already.

— *Rejected:* the task's name in the panel's head, as the mockup draws it; "Explain differently" drawn and off, as the mockup's; the mockup's red dot on the tutor's button after a Check with the panel closed, which the task screen has not either; the hint as a line at the screen's foot; the Check's words in the verdict's card, which would hide them while the panel is open and leave them no "Report a problem".

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/Solve.tsx`, `SolveTutor.tsx`, `solve.ts`, `PaperScreen.tsx` and their tests; `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, `TaskLayout.tsx`, `Task.tsx`, `Hint.tsx` (`HintBubble`), `shared.ts` (`hush`); `src/i18n/en.ts` and `ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R76. The tutor's words of a task from paper and the hint mode: Hint before the first wrong answer asks the opening question, `GetOpeningWords` with the work as it stands, which the AI checks to ask it; after Not yet the rung's words come by `GetRungWords`, a rung of the hints the Check kept, with no work, with the panel open or once it opens, and in the mode "Show where" with the wavy line under the first mistake; "Explain differently" once on a rung; "Report a problem" for an adult. The hint mode is a field of the profile's page in the section for adults, `UpdateProfile`, which "Settings" of the profile's menu opens.**
- **The words** (`src/paper/Solve.tsx`): Hint asks `GetHint`; its rung's words are shown, and no techniques — a task from paper before its first wrong answer — ask `GetOpeningWords` with the work as it stands — the sheet's export, or with nothing written the task's crop as the statement and as the work —, the instruction's crop with it, and a key kept till an answer while the work and the paper's place are the same. A Check that is Not yet asks the rung's words at once while the panel is open, else keeps the request for the panel's opening or Hint, as the task screen does. "Explain differently" names the wrong answers of the Check, `BegunTask.wrongAnswers`, and its key is kept till an answer. Each call is `watched`, so the debug mode shows it, and each reads the share of the quota again. The words are `Said`, with their reply — `TUTOR_REPLY_OPENING` at rung 0, the rung's, or "explained differently" — and `TutorWords` shows them in the panel, marked a hint, with `ReportProblem` below them for an adult and the note of an outcome. An answer to another task or an earlier Check shows nothing.
- **The wavy line**: `wavyOf` takes the box of `MARK_KIND_WAVY_LINE` of the rung's words, and `verdictMarks` draws it beside the ticks and crosses of a Check that is Not yet, on the context of that Check, named "Tutor's mark: where the mistake is". It stands while the panel is open, beside the words it underlines, and goes with other work, the tracing paper moved, another Check or another task.
- **The mode**: two radio buttons of the profile's page, each its title and its line, saved with the form (`src/account/settings.ts`); a child's would be set there behind the PIN. A profile that names none is "Show where".

— *Why:* a task from paper speaks as a task of a module does, by AI and with its own opening question from the work as it stands, and its mark is the profile's choice. The mockup's `later-grownups` puts the mode on the profile's page, behind "Settings" of the menu and a child's behind the PIN; the line beside the words it underlines, as the mockup's `solve-not-yet`, and gone with the work it stood under.

— *Rejected:* the mode in the profile's menu or a pop-over of the row, by `SetHintMode` — a setting, it stands with the others, and a child's is a grown-up's; the line with the panel closed, away from its words; the line kept across other work, whose strokes it may no longer underline.

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/Solve.tsx`, `SolveTutor.tsx`, `solve.ts`, `PaperScreen.tsx`, `src/app/App.tsx`, `src/account/settings.ts`, `src/adults/ProfilePage.tsx`, `src/tutor/report.ts`, `src/debug/debug.ts`, `src/theme/theme.css`, `src/i18n/en.ts` and `ru.ts`, and their tests; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The history of pages from paper

**R77. A page from paper is the profile's history from Start on: the profile's database keeps its photo, its frames and each task begun on it with its crop, its writing history, where its tracing paper lies, the context of each Check answered with the history's length and whether its attempt is over — never a verdict, a key or a hint —, until the profile is erased. "Your pages" on the card "A task from my paper" lists them, newest first, in a pop-over of its small button, and a page opens on the screen of solving with its work, and a task whose attempt is over is solved on, in a new attempt begun at its first action. `src/paper/export.ts` writes a page as `paper/<id>/<page>.json` of the export's version 4; the rest of the export and its button are epic E4's (Д180 spec, Д233 spec).**
- **The stores** (schema version 4, `src/storage/schema.ts`): `pages` by page ID, its time and frames, apart from `photos`, so that the list reads no photo; `paperTasks` by the attempt ID the task holds now, with the index `page`: a task begun anew moves to its new attempt with its work and its place on the page. Neither the end of an attempt nor the 30 days take them: an erasure of the profile does, as it takes the database, and a new page past `MAX_PAGES` of `profile.ts` takes the oldest with its photo and its tasks, in the same transaction, never the page Start keeps. The line under the list names the number from the same constant.
- **When it is written.** Start writes the page, its photo once, and the tasks it began, after those before; a task begun anew, its frame resized, takes its earlier attempt's place, as on the screen. A task's work is written half a second after it changes, with whatever came meanwhile, and at once on a new photo, Close, a page opened, the tab hidden or the screens gone; the writes go one after another, so that no work comes before its task. A write that fails leaves the history as it was and tells nothing: the work is still in the tab.
- **What is not kept.** The verdict, the ticks and crosses, the wrong answers and the tutor's words stay in the tab: the platform's rules of privacy have the device keep the photo, the frames, the crops and the work. The instruction's crop is not kept either: the file of the export has no place for it, so a page opened again, and a task begun on it after "Add", is checked with none. Whether a task is over is kept, so that the first action on a page opened again begins a new attempt before it goes, not after `api` refuses it; a correct answer sets it, and so does a "closed" of an attempt begun anew. Whether it was solved is kept too, `solved`, so that the list of a page opened again marks it as the tab did: a correct answer sets it, and so does a "closed" of reason correct — the fact the list shows, not the verdict, whose card and marks stay in the tab.
- **The Check.** Each Check answered adds its context and the history's length at its press, once for its key: a repeat after a lost answer adds none. The repeat of the Check that solved a task gets `solved` alone, its verdicts erased with the close, and is correct.
- **The list**, by the design `spec/draft/Vashi-stranicy-1a.html` and the mockup's states `home-pages` and `solve-kept`: each row a thumbnail of the page's photo, 44 × 56 CSS px and straight, beside when it was taken and, under it, how many tasks it began, the row named by both. The list still reads no photo: each row's is read apart by `Profile.photo`, one after another, and `thumbnailOf` in `src/paper/capture.ts` draws it in memory as a JPEG that covers 132 × 168 px, three device px to a CSS px; its object URL is made once and revoked when the list goes. A skeleton of the thumbnail's size stands while it is made, and a blank sheet where the photo cannot be read; the thumbnail is decorative, its `alt` empty.
- **The file of the export**: the page's frames in the order of the page, and after them the frame of a task begun before the learner resized it, so that each task names its own by its place; the tasks in the order of their attempts; a tracing paper never moved where canvas starts it, at [180, 60], shown.

— *Why:* the contract: the history of a task from paper is the device's, and the export holds it. A store of its own keeps the rules of attempts — their end and their 30 days — off the history, which has rules of its own.

— *Rejected:* keeping the pages in the stores of attempts, `history` and `submissions`, which the end of the attempt and the expiry erase; writing the work on every stroke, a photo and a crop rewritten each time; keeping the verdict to show again, which the platform's rules of privacy do not list on the device; the whole export now, which is epic E4's with its button and its other files; a task that is over only looked at — the learner could not solve it again, nor go on with a page another Start closed; a button "Solve again" — a step the learner does not need; a thumbnail kept beside the photo — a field of its own would change the device's records, which go into the profile's export as they are, and so its format, for what a read of the photo makes in a moment; the thumbnails tilted by turns, as the mockup drew them — the rows keep each photo straight, as it was taken; the whole photo in each row — megabytes held for a picture of 44 px.

— *Consequence:* `src/storage/schema.ts`, `src/storage/profile.ts`, `src/paper/PaperContext.tsx`, `src/paper/Solve.tsx`, `src/paper/solve.ts`, `src/paper/YourPages.tsx`, `src/paper/capture.ts`, `src/paper/export.ts`, `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`, `src/app/App.tsx`, the dictionaries and their tests; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The wait between screens

**R78. Where `app` does not know yet which screen comes, it draws the mockup's wait (`wait`, `wait-home`), `src/app/Waiting.tsx`, never a white page: the ground of the screen to come at once — the sky of "Who is learning?" with its stars at the start and while the session reads the account, or the page's grey while the owner's key opens — and after 0.3 s the spark of the name in a halo of 72 in its middle, pulsing in 1.4 s, with "Loading" a status for a screen reader. Where the screen is known, its frame stands and what it reads is a skeleton.**
- **Where.** `Boot` until the storage, the sign-in service and the settings are there — under the offer to reload, which stays its own status; `AccountGate` at the steps `starting` and `loading`, and on `learning` until the key is in memory — the screen after it is the route's, and its frame needs the learner's context, which the key opens. Every other read draws its screen's frame and the skeleton of what it brings (`Sk` and `Loading` of `src/app/Skeleton.tsx`): Home, Subjects, the skill map, a lesson (`LessonLoading`) and a task (`TaskLoading`), the section's pages, the legal texts and their sheet, the license page.
- **The spark** is the name's mark, `Spark`: white aglow on the sky, the name's indigo `#5b5fe0` on the page, each on a halo of its own; under "Reduce motion" it stands still. It is drawn and hidden from assistive technology, so its colours need no pair of contrast.

— *Why:* a white page between the sky's screens flashes, and a wait drawn in its place does not. The ground alone for the first 0.3 s keeps a quick read free of a flicker of the spark.

— *Rejected:* keeping the screen before until the next is read — the screens are drawn by the session's step and the route, and what stands before the start and the sign-in has no screen before it; a spinner of the browser's or a bar — the mockup draws the spark; the spark at once — it would blink on every quick read; the spark over a lesson or a task being read — their frame is known, and a skeleton stands there.

— *Consequence:* `src/app/Waiting.tsx` and its test; `src/boot/Boot.tsx`, `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`; `src/theme/theme.css`; the key `wait.label` in both dictionaries; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx` — `wait` and `wait-home`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Home and its frame by the author's design

**R79. Home (`#/home`), Subjects (`#/subjects`) and a module's skill map (`#/skill-map/<module ID>`) stand in one frame, `ShellBar` of `src/app/Shell.tsx`, by Home's design: from 600 CSS px a row above the screen — at its start a pill of the places, Home, Subjects and the cognitive map shut with "Soon"; at its end a pill of the tutor, shut until it comes there, the notifications and the profile's menu —, and below 600 the three places a tab bar at the bottom, the row holding the mark and the pill. Home is one card at the top — "Next up", with the tutor time, and "Tasks from my paper", with "Your pages" — and under it a card of each module connected: its kind, its counts, its skill map, Continue, a square for each skill and a foot. The catalog is Subjects, and a module's map opens from either, under "Subjects" back.**
- **The places.** A place is a link of the hash, `aria-current="page"` on the page shown, its icon filled — a module's map is Subjects' (`placeOf` of `src/app/App.tsx`). From 600 CSS px to 72em, where the words of every place do not fit beside the pills — Russian's are the longest —, a place shows its icon alone, but the page's, its words its name, hidden to the eye only, with the tooltip of those words, by the rule of buttons without a caption, and the profile's pill its avatar alone. The cognitive map is a button with `aria-disabled` and the title "Cognitive map — comes later", in the keyboard's order, so that its "Soon" is read. The frame has no `LearnerBar`.
- **The top right.** The tutor, `ShutTutor`, shut as the cognitive map is: its sign in the grey of a place that waits, `aria-disabled`, in the keyboard's order, named "Tutor", its tooltip "Tutor — comes later", and it opens nothing; the tutor helps in a task, a lesson and a task from paper, and that it is an AI Help says. The notifications, `Bell`: the next versions of the legal texts, with a red dot until shown, else "Nothing new yet". The profile's menu, `LearnerMenu`, as on every screen of the profile.
- **"Next up"** names what is next in line: the attempt open outside a lesson by its task — "A task: {module}" and the first paragraph of its statement, by `GetOpenAttempt`, which only reads (`src/task/openTask.ts`), or "A task from paper", which that call refuses —, with "Continue the task"; else the first lesson begun, "Continue the lesson", and the others under "Also begun:"; else "The tutor picks the next lesson or task", or "Nothing to begin yet" with no module connected. At the foot the tutor time and its bar, and at its right, while no attempt is open, "What's next", secondary beside a lesson begun; no "Something else" stands on Home.
- **"Tasks from my paper"**: "Camera" above its heading, its line, "Open camera" and "Your pages". The pictures beside both halves — a sheet in squares, a page of a book — are drawn and hidden from assistive technology, and dropped on a phone and in forced colours.
- **A module's card** (`src/subjects/HomeCard.tsx`): its tile, its title in its explanation language, its kind — "Sequential" for a chain, "Feedback by criteria" where no skill has a verdict, else "Adaptive" —, a line of "{m} of {n} mastered" or the skills with a rubric, the explanation language when it is not the screen's and "Not available now"; the skills due for review a chip; the skill map, a button of its icon described by the title; and Continue, which goes on with the module's lesson begun, else its task open, else asks `ChooseNext` with the card's skill, else opens its map. Under them a square for each skill in its state's colour, in the legend's order, `role="img"` named by every state and mark with its count. A chain's foot is the place "n/N", the skill to work on, the share mastered with its bar, and the next skill; an adaptive module's, "The tutor chooses", "The next skill, as the work goes", the skills due and "Any module" — a stub: `ChooseNext` is an action, and what it gives is known only once it is asked. While its map comes the card's kind, counts, squares and foot are shapes, a skeleton. The legend stands below the cards.
- **Subjects** (`src/subjects/SubjectsPage.tsx`): "Connected · {n}" first, then "More on this server", each list in the order of the screen's language, the focus going with a card to its new place — a disconnected card's Connect, a connected card's ⋯. A card is its tile; its title in its explanation language; a line of its subject, whom it is for — its grades, else its ages, "Any age" for all three —, its kind once its graph has come — "On a sheet", "Feedback by criteria" where no skill has a verdict, "In order" or "Adaptive" — and its explanation language where it is not the screen's; "Skills · {n}", three by title in the order of their tiers and a chip of the rest, which opens every skill by tier, with "Open the skill map" on a connected card; on a connected card a square for each skill and its counts, as Home's, by `GetSkillMap` of the learner's action, and "Connected" with "Skill map"; on another, Connect and how many skills it has. ⋯, "About the module", opens the facts of the manifest — the version, the authors, whom it is for, the explanation language and the license, as text — and on a connected card Disconnect. The facts and the skills come from the current version's manifest and graph (`aboutOf`, `skillListOf` of `src/subjects/modules.ts`); a manifest without its audience or authors gives a card without the facts.
- **Where it differs from the mockup**, each named by its region of the comparison: a card of Subjects has no line of what its module teaches — the manifest has no such field — and no line of the anchor skills it builds on — `skill-map` keeps their titles to itself —; "What's next" names no lesson; the adaptive card's foot names no skill.

— *Why:* the design; a card of Subjects says what its module is before it is connected — half of the old card stood empty, and its License served no learner —, and its actions that are seldom pressed wait under ⋯; one frame for the three places keeps the places, the tutor and the profile's actions where the learner finds them on each; the catalog on a page of its own leaves Home the learner's own work; the places as icons on an iPad keep the row one line. Continue on a skill is one press to a task, where the map is one more.

— *Rejected:* a line of what a module teaches — the manifest has no such field, and adding one is a change of the module format —; Disconnect and License on the card — a press that is seldom made beside the ones made each day, and a page no learner reads; a menu of the places down the left side, the first design's, and Help a bubble at the top right — the last design puts the places in the row; "Grown-ups" and Help in the row — they are no places, and the profile's menu holds them; the catalog and the skill map on Home — Home keeps the learner's own work; Pip for an adult (О-13); the skill the engine will give named in the adaptive foot — `ChooseNext` is an action that closes the attempt open; "Something else" beside "Continue the task" — it would close the attempt, and a module's card goes on with its own work.

— *Does not decide:* a look at the engine's next choice that records nothing, and `ChooseNext` within one module, which a card's Continue would need with no skill of its own — О-27.

— *Consequence:* `src/app/Shell.tsx`, `NavCaption.tsx`, `Tooltip.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `LearnerMenu.tsx`, `Help.tsx`, `IconButton.tsx`, `Screen.tsx`; `src/subjects/MySubjects.tsx`, `HomeCard.tsx`, `SubjectsPage.tsx`, `states.ts`, `useSkillCounts.ts`, `modules.ts`; `src/task/openTask.ts`; `src/skills/SkillMapScreen.tsx`; `scripts/icons.ts` and `src/app/icons.ts`; `src/theme/theme.css`; both dictionaries; `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R80. On the two screens of a task from paper — the page to pick from and the screen of solving — the tutor's button and the debug button stand in the bottom bar, not at the right edge of the screen, as the author's mockup 1b draws them. On the page: at the bar's start, before a line, the tutor's button, 44 round, the chat's sign in the accent's words on its tint, and the debug button beside it, then "Select all", the count and Start. On the screen of solving the row is the debug button and a line, Ask — the tutor's button as a pill of the chat's sign and its word, in the accent's words on its tint —, Hint, a line and Check. The debug button is 44 round, its bug in orange on a light orange. Without the debug right neither the debug button nor its line is drawn; while the tutor's panel is open the tutor's button and the debug button leave the bar, and so does the page's line after them.**
- **The fit.** The row of solving keeps one line by `fitRow` of `src/paper/BarFit.tsx`, as the pick's top bar keeps one row: with its words where they fit, Ask its icon alone where they do not — its word still its name, with a tooltip of it —, and only then wrapped; a button that comes or goes in it, as the debug button once its code is loaded, fits it again. On a phone of 360 CSS px the row without the debug button keeps one line, in Russian Ask an icon. The page's bar wraps on a phone, as it did.
- **The colours.** The accent's words `#0066cc` on its tint keep 4.9:1, text's 4.5:1 (WCAG 1.4.3, sources 8.1); the bug `#c25400` on `#fff3e0` keeps 4.2:1, past the 3:1 of a control's sign (WCAG 1.4.11, sources 8.1), where the mockup's lighter orange `#e37400` keeps 2.8:1. Sizes are the bar's: 44, the target, where the mockup draws 32–35.
- **The windows** open as before: the tutor's panel at the right and the phone's sheet, the focus to its heading and back to the button; the debug window at the right, drawn in the page's body.

— *Why:* the buttons at the right edge stood over the sheet the learner writes on and over the photo; in the bar they stand with the actions of the screen, and on the screen of solving Ask stands beside Hint, both a way to help, which the learner looks for at the same moment. The debug button leaves with the tutor's, so that its window never opens over the tutor's panel.

— *Rejected:* the two buttons as a pair at the top right, beside the buttons there, which makes the pick's top group longer; one "⋯" menu at the top right in their place, which puts the chat a level deeper, where it is found less; the chat in the bottom bar and the debug button as a small icon in the title; the mockup's orange of the bug, below 3:1; the bar's buttons at the mockup's 32–35, below the target of 44.

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/Pick.tsx`, `Solve.tsx`, `PaperScreen.tsx` and `BarFit.tsx`; `src/debug/slot.tsx`, `DebugButton.tsx` and `debug.css`; `src/theme/theme.css`; `src/i18n/en.ts` and `ru.ts` — "Ask"; their tests; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R81. The settings of the pen and the eraser — the element's `pens`, which its `penchange` tells — pass from element to element of one learner in the tab's memory alone: `src/canvas/pens.ts` keeps what the learner chosen last changed them to, and every new element of that learner takes them — a task's, an essay's, a lesson's example's, a task from paper's. Nothing of them is stored: a reload starts with canvas's, and so does another learner chosen on the device. The writing history passes through as the element gave it — each stroke with its style, a cut with its pieces — to the device's database, `PutWork` and the export, and a history kept before strokes had a style opens as it did.**
- **Whose.** The settings are the learner's, by the profile chosen: "Switch learner" to another profile gives that learner canvas's, and the first learner's are gone once the second changes theirs. A screen outside the learner's gate, as in a screen's own test, keeps those of no learner.
- **How.** The element is given back what its own `penchange` told, which changes nothing and tells nothing; an element that comes before any change is given none, and keeps canvas's.

— *Why:* the platform has the pen's settings kept across one visit's tasks and lessons and stored nowhere: the learner sets the pen once and it holds for the next task, with no new kind of the profile's data on the device, in its export or on the server. The history is the element's, carried unchanged, as the device always kept it: a new field of a stroke or a new event needs no change of `app`.

— *Rejected:* the settings with the profile's database on the device, which the platform rejects — a new kind of the profile's data, in its export too, for a choice of two presses; one setting for the tab whatever the learner, which hands one learner's pen to the next learner of a shared tablet; each learner's settings kept side by side for the tab's life, which keeps more than the next element needs; giving the element its own settings back on every render as a new object, which would be a change with each render.

— *Consequence:* `src/canvas/pens.ts` and `jsx.ts`; `src/task/TaskLayout.tsx`, `src/lesson/Example.tsx`, `src/paper/Solve.tsx`; `src/test/browser.setup.ts`, `src/test/ink.ts`, `src/test/pens.ts`; the tests of the pens, the storage, the picture, `PutWork` and the export; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R82. The debug window has two tabs more of the mockup's `solve-debug-errors`, `solve-debug-filter`, `solve-debug-perf` and `solve-debug-hud`: Log is the app's own log, with the actions of AI among its lines, and Performance is what loads the device. The log begins with the tab for every account, in its memory alone, its last 500 lines; Performance measures only while it is open, its strip is on the screen or the flash of what a frame paints is on.**
- **The log** (`src/debug/journal.ts`, in the main chunk; `src/main.tsx` installs it): the window's errors and the promises nobody caught, what the console says as an error or a warning, each call to `api` by the outermost interceptor `journaled` of `src/api/interceptors.ts` — its method, how it ended, how long it took and the bytes its fields of bytes held —, each attempt of a call that the interceptor `repeat` repeats — how it ended, what the browser said of a network that failed and how long the attempt had gone, at the level warning, and "still going" as info, one line counting its repeats —, the page put away and back and the network gone and back, the photo and each crop as `src/paper/capture.ts` makes them, and a long frame while Performance measures. A line names what happened, never the message of a call or the learner's work; a line is cut to 300 characters and its detail to 4000. An error is a failure alone: a refusal the calling screen names as an answer by `expecting` of `src/api/interceptors.ts` — the words of a rung there are none of, `CAUSE_NOT_CURRENT`, `CAUSE_NO_RUNG` and `CAUSE_NO_MORE_WORDS`, and "Explain differently" spent, `CAUSE_ALREADY_ASKED` beside the first two — is info, and the browser's word of a ResizeObserver loop, whose notifications only went on to the next frame, is debug, so that "N errors" counts what broke.
- **Log** (`src/debug/Log.tsx`, the runs in `runs.ts`): the lines and the actions newest first, the lines that follow one another on one card, each opening to its detail with Copy; two info lines or more in a row on a card one row, as the mockup's `solve-debug-runs` and `solve-debug-run-open` — the names counted, how many, from where and when —, which opens to them, a card and a run known by their oldest line, so that a line coming keeps a run open; a filter by level — errors, warnings, info, debug — and source — the AI's calls, `api`, the canvas, the app with the camera — in a pop-over of a small button, "N errors" beside it showing the errors alone, the tab's count of the errors in red; at its foot the session's total, Copy of what is shown as text, and Clear of the lines and the actions.
- **Performance** (`src/debug/perf.ts`, `Perf.tsx`): a loop of animation frames and a timer every quarter of a second, in buckets of half a second over 30 s — a second's frames, the p95, those over 50 ms and the worst of each bucket as bars; the main thread's busy share, from the long tasks where the browser tells them, else from what late frames ran over 16.7 ms; the timer's lateness, the wait of a touch; the canvas's `paintStats`, summed over the elements on the screen; memory estimated from the canvases' buffers, the decoded pictures and videos, the strokes' points and the heap where the browser gives it; the browser, the view and the cores. Pause, Copy as JSON, the strip — a dark line at the top left of the screen that takes no touch — and the flash, the element's `paintFlash`, given to each canvas every half-second.

— *Why:* the iPad that heated up on a full sheet showed that the developer needs to see the device's load and the app's errors where they happen, on the device, without a computer beside it. An error before `GetDebug` answers is one the developer needs too, so the log begins with the tab; it costs a list of 500 lines in memory and goes nowhere. A browser gives a page no CPU load, heat or battery, and Safari no memory, so the frames and the busy time stand in for them, and the window says so. A loop of animation frames wakes the device each frame, so it runs only while someone looks. A Check from paper on an iPad was repeated 48 s into its 90 s with the call's line alone saying it answered: the repeat's line and the page's and the network's show what cut the first attempt, which only the device knows. Each screen calls `api` a few times, so its info lines in a row hid the warnings, the errors and the AI's calls among them; folded, they take one row.

— *Rejected:* `app` asking no words of a rung past the third wrong answer, which would leave the other quiet refusals red; a run of info across a warning, an error or an AI's call, which would hide where it came; the log only once the debug mode is known, which loses the errors of the start; the log in the device's storage or sent to `api`, which would outlive the session or leave the device; the message of a call in its line, which may hold the learner's work; the monitor always on for a debug account, which keeps the device awake; the Long Tasks API alone, which Safari lacks; `performance.memory` as the memory, which Chromium alone gives; a meter of the CPU by a busy loop, which itself loads the device.

— *Consequence:* `src/debug/journal.ts`, `Log.tsx`, `runs.ts`, `perf.ts`, `Perf.tsx`, `DebugButton.tsx`, `debug.css` and their tests; `src/api/interceptors.ts`, `client.ts` and `journaled.node.test.ts`; `src/paper/capture.ts`; `src/main.tsx`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R83. A module solved on the sheet, the check board, opens from Continue on its card on the screen of solving of a task from paper, whole, at `#/paper/module/<module ID>`: `BeginPaperAttempts` with the module's ID begins its tasks, and each statement's text is drawn on the device as the picture the tracing paper shows and the Check sends. The page is the tab's alone.**
- **The route** names the module, an ID of content, never a page or an attempt; each beginning is a new page's ID, so `api` closes the attempts of any page before, and its key is kept for the try, so that a second run of the effect repeats it, and a try again after a failure is a new one.
- **The picture** is a PNG of the statement's parts of text joined, 1200 px wide, the system's sans-serif at 44 px on lines of 1.4, dark on white with a margin of 48, left-aligned and wrapped by the words; `canvas` sizes the paper by it, as by a crop.
- **The page** is a `ModulePage` of `PaperContext`: its tasks rows one under another, which `numbers` names "Task n" in the module's order, beside the tasks begun, with no photo; a photo taken, a page of the history opened or Close takes its place. Nothing of it is written to the profile's database: `keepPaperWork` and `renewPaperTask` are not called for it.
- **The screen** is `Solve` with `source` `"module"`, which gives the element no `reading`, and with no `onAdd`, which leaves "Add" out of the list.

— *Why:* the author's word, one canvas of solving for a task from paper and the check board, the statement the one difference; a picture keeps the Check of a task from paper as it is, and one picture is what the learner and the model both see. The database and the export hold pages from paper, each with its photo: a module's page in them would be a new kind of page in both, for one module, and its tasks begin anew from the module at any time.

— *Rejected:* the module's page kept in the database as a page from paper with a white photo — "Your pages" would list it and the export hold it as a photo of the learner's; the text laid out by `canvas` — two drawings of one statement; the route naming the page's ID — a reload would name a page the tab no longer holds; a spinner while it begins — the skeleton is the rule.

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/module.ts`, `statement.ts`, `PaperContext.tsx`, `PaperScreen.tsx`, `Solve.tsx`, `ModulePage.browser.test.tsx`; `src/subjects/modules.ts`, `MySubjects.tsx`, their tests; `src/theme/theme.css`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

**R84. While a Check of a task from paper goes, the screen of solving asks api's `GetCheckProgress` 2 s after the work is sent and 2 s after each answer, and once the Check has gone on to a later model the card, the mockup's `solve-reading-closer`, says "Looking more closely…" and "The first look wasn't sure — now a closer one.", its ring on a tinted disc that pulses, with the track of the models under its line: dots, or for an account with the debug mode whose Check goes as admitted, the models' names from `GetDebug`, the mockup's `solve-debug-reading-closer`, without the version on a narrow screen. The open panel's line says the same.**
- **The questions** (`watchProgress`): one at a time, from the attempt the work was sent on, with a deadline of 4 s, stopped by the answer of `SubmitWork`, another task and the screen gone; a question under way then finishes unheard, since one cut short is a cancelled request, an error in api's log. A question that fails leaves the card as it was, and the next asks again; an answer of a place past the list, or of a Check not going, is no step.
- **The names** (`trackNames`): read from the debug mode's own module, loaded by the Check as it already is for that account, only while the account's choice is "As admitted" and the list of the Check has as many models as api counts; otherwise dots. The track is `aria-hidden`, and the screen's status line says "Looking more closely…".
- **Motion**: each mark steps in after the one before, the one that reads pulses; none where the browser asks for less.

— *Why:* a Check whose first model was not sure may run for over a minute, and one card all the while reads as a stuck screen; the author approved the mockup's second step. Questions of a light method keep the main path of a submission — its key, its repeat and its deadline — as it is, and api, which alone knows when the Check goes on, answers from the record of the request. Only the debug mode names models, so the names come from what that account's `GetDebug` gave.

— *Rejected:* a stream of `SubmitWork`, which would change its transport and its repeat; a step guessed by the time gone, which would say "more closely" of a slow first model; the names for every account, which the debug mode alone shows.

— *Consequence:* `src/paper/progress.ts`, `Solve.tsx`, `SolveTutor.tsx`, `progress.node.test.ts`, `PaperScreen.browser.test.tsx`; `src/task/TaskLayout.tsx`; `src/theme/theme.css`; `src/i18n/en.ts`, `ru.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The microphone of the tutor's field

**R85. A press of the microphone stops what the app is saying first, and where the browser has nothing to ask first — no `available`, as WebKit — starts the recognition within the press itself; a listening with no sound `NO_AUDIO_MS` after it began ends as one that heard nothing, but only while the browser has the microphone granted, and so does one whose sound or speech gave no word `NO_WORDS_MS` after it began.**
- **The voice:** `useSpeech().stop()` before the recognition is made, whether the voicing is on or not; a reply that comes while it listens is said, as at any other time.
- **The listening:** continuous, till Stop, through the learner's pauses.
- **The start:** a browser with `available` — Chrome — is asked first whether it recognises the language on the device, and starts once it has answered; a browser without it starts in the press, with no promise between.
- **The timers:** the first armed before `start()` while the microphone is granted, and `audiostart` and `speechstart` each put the second, `NO_WORDS_MS`, in its place afresh, which a result with a word clears — a result with no word leaves it on; `navigator.permissions` tells whether the microphone is granted, read when the field comes and on each change; a browser that does not say counts as not granted, and its listening waits for its sound. `NO_WORDS_MS` leaves a learner time to think before speaking.

— *Why:* the voice speaking over the microphone gives the recognition the app's own words, so a press stops it. A promise before `start()` is a step out of the press, which WebKit may hold against a start. The timers turn a listening that hangs into the line the field already has, "Nothing was heard. Try again, or type.", so no new words and no state of the mockup; a prompt for the microphone waits for the learner, however long, so the first timer stays off until the grant is known; WebKit gives a result with no word where it hears sound, so only a word clears the second. On an iPad a listening after the first of a page hears sound and no word: a bug of WebKit since iOS 27, open (sources 5.7 spec), WebKit's to fix — the timer ends such a listening, and the app keeps no way round the bug.

— *Rejected:* asking `available` when the field comes, so that every browser starts in the press — in headless Chromium the call crashes the page, and the field would ask on every screen; a timer whatever the grant — it would cut a learner reading the prompt; a new message for a deaf microphone — a state of the mockup for a case the existing line covers; a voice that ends the listening when it begins — a reply arriving would cut the learner short mid-sentence; ways round WebKit's bug on an iPad — the voice held quiet while it listens, a microphone that gave sound on the page counted as granted, a listening till a pause or after a word of the synthesis, chosen in the Debug window, and each event of a listening a line of its log — code and tests kept for a browser's bug its maker will fix, and the voice held quiet keeps a reply from being said.

— *Consequence:* `src/voice/dictation.ts`; `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, `TutorPanel.browser.test.tsx`; `src/test/recognition.ts`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).
